# 18. Legal, tax, banking and payments

## Business Structure: Sole Proprietor, LLC, S-Corp and Entity Strategy

### Why a Legal Entity Matters

- An LLC (Limited Liability Company) is a separate legal entity that protects personal assets from business liabilities and looks more professional to suppliers than a sole proprietorship. Form an LLC unless you genuinely have nothing to protect. [L0118.001]
- A sole proprietorship has no legal separation from its owner, so personal assets such as bank accounts and property are exposed to business liabilities. A separate legal entity limits liability to the business assets only. [L0698.003]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* A sole trader is personally liable for business debts. In a dispute, personal assets such as a house could be claimed to settle them. [L0702.003]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* A proprietary limited company or LLC is separate from its owner. The owner is usually both director and shareholder. If the company cannot pay its debts, it can be wound up without the director generally being liable. [L0702.004]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Limited liability does not cover illegal conduct. A director can still be held liable if the company or the director acts illegally. [L0702.005]
- Liability protection holds only if business and personal funds are not commingled. [L0429.009]
- Commingling funds across unrelated LLCs makes separate businesses look like one entity. This weakens the LLC's liability protection and makes a later sale of one business much messier. [L1092.011]
- Lawsuits against the business target the business and not the owner personally. [L1085.076, L1274.047]

### Choosing a Structure

#### Structure Overview (US Terms)

| Structure | Separation from owner | Setup and cost | Suits | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sole proprietorship | None. Personal assets are exposed to business lawsuits. | No formation filing. The owner can trade under their own name immediately. | People with little to lose. Owners with a home, several vehicles, retirement savings or significant cash should avoid it. | [L0703.001, L0703.002, L0698.005] |
| LLC | Separate legal entity. A lawsuit reaches only assets the LLC owns, such as business bank funds and equipment. | Inexpensive and easy to set up. Commonly about $100. | The most common first structure for online sellers. | [L0703.003, L0270.027, L1085.076] |
| LLC taxed as S corp | Same liability protection as the LLC. The S corp is a tax election, not a separate entity type. | Election is filed with the IRS. The one-time setup cost is small once the business is profitable. | Once profit justifies payroll setup and accounting costs. | [L0703.004, L0703.012] |
| C corp | Separate legal entity. | Used when bringing in shareholders, seeking investment or going public. | Businesses seeking outside investment. Most small business owners use an LLC, the most popular structure. | [L0969.034, L0272.042] |

#### Sole Proprietorship

- Trading under your own name is the fastest and cheapest way to start. It is a reasonable starting point but is usually replaced by a company structure once revenue grows. [L0698.005]
- A sole proprietorship is not a separate legal entity, so all business profit is reported on the owner's personal tax return. Choose the structure with this in mind before you start selling. [L1344.001]
- The owner's name is the business name, even if the website domain differs. [L0969.033]
- A sole proprietorship has a higher tax burden. The owner cannot use as many write-offs and cannot split pay between salary and distributions. [L0698.004]
- Some states require registration even for sole proprietors. Upfront cost is lower, but there is no liability protection. [L0270.065]
- *Older advice (2021):* A sole proprietor operating under a business name may not need a DBA on day one. Many stores launched without one and later formed business entities as they grew. This is general experience, not legal advice. [L0968.024]
- *Older advice (2020):* A new store can start as a sole proprietorship with an EIN and no state sales tax certificate. When sales grow, the owner can form an S corporation and obtain the state sales tax certificate at the same time. [L1111.180]
- *Older advice (2019):* Starting as a sole proprietor using personal accounts is a valid option while you test the business. Once sales come in, a business structure can be set up with an accountant. [L1275.062]

#### LLC

- Forming an LLC typically costs about $100. [L0270.027, L1085.076]
- *Older advice (2021):* An LLC can be formed in a state other than where you live, through an accountant or yourself using an online filing service such as LegalZoom. [L0968.020]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* In the US, the LLC is the most commonly chosen structure. It is quick to form, often possible entirely online, and separates the company's assets from the owner's personal assets, unless the owner breaks the law. [L1405.009]
- *Older advice (2022):* Lean approach to entity setup in the US: form a cheap online LLC and optionally elect S-corp tax treatment. A sole proprietorship can be used first and an LLC formed later. Consult an accountant or tax professional for a personal situation. [L0969.005]
- An LLC is optional at launch, and a sole proprietorship can work initially. Once the business is making money, consult an accountant about forming a separate entity. [L0247.103]
- Form an LLC early and elect S-corporation tax treatment, which gives asset protection and tax benefits. Start as a sole proprietor only if the business is brand new, and form the LLC as soon as sales begin. [L0667.076]

#### S Corp and C Corp Elections

- An LLC can be elected to be taxed as an S corporation. This decision affects tax obligations. [L0249.074, L0469.051]
- Confirm the S-corp election with a tax professional before filing. [L0469.051]
- An S corp is not a separate entity type but a tax election. An existing LLC is taxed as an S corporation by filing an election form with the IRS. The owner keeps the same liability protection while gaining the S-corp tax treatment. [L0703.004]
- An LLC taxed as an S corporation offers tax advantages and separates personal liability from the business. This structure is commonly used by US-based dropshippers. [L0471.060]
- Consider an S-corp election after formation to reduce self-employment taxes. [L0270.027]
- Under an S corp or LLC, different pay structures are possible that can reduce taxes. [L0698.004]
- In the US, the main difference between an LLC and an S corp is how business profits are taxed. An accountant should compare both for the owner's personal situation. [L1405.010]
- *Older advice (2019):* An S corporation is the structure the presenter uses for protection and tax benefits. The right legal entity depends on your situation, so confirm it with an accountant or attorney. [L0663.060]
- *Older advice (2022):* Form your business as an LLC elected to be taxed as an S corp. Use a C corp only if you plan to seek outside investment. [L0272.042]
- *Older advice (2022):* An LLC can be elected to be taxed as an S corporation, which the lesson presented as a way to save on taxes. This is an example structure only and not tax or legal advice. [L1092.007]
- *Older advice (2022):* A C corporation is generally used when bringing in shareholders, seeking investment or going public. Most small business owners use an LLC, which is the most popular structure. [L0969.034]

### When to Start Simple and When to Upgrade

#### Timing Advice Compared

Sources agree that a sole proprietorship is a valid start and that the structure can be upgraded later. They differ on how early to form the LLC.

| Advice | Ids |
|---|---|
| Start as a sole proprietor for speed, then move to an LLC or corporation as soon as the business makes money. | [L0698.006] |
| Sole proprietorship can be used at the start. Set up a corporation with an accountant once the business earns money. *(Older, 2021.)* | [L0962.122] |
| An LLC is optional. Many people start as a sole proprietor, then form an LLC or S corporation once the business is making money. *(Older, 2021.)* | [L0671.109, L0671.246] |
| A business can start as a sole proprietor, and an LLC can be formed later once the business is established and earning money. *(Older, 2022.)* | [L1092.014] |
| Start as a sole proprietor and form an S corp only after the business is profitable. Entity formation is optional and not needed before contacting suppliers. *(Older, 2020.)* | [L1088.077] |
| Common path: start as an LLC and later elect S-corp taxation once the store earns enough profit to justify the payroll setup and accounting costs. | [L0703.012] |
| Form an LLC early and elect S-corp taxation. Start as a sole proprietor only if the business is brand new, and form the LLC as soon as sales begin. *(2025.)* | [L0667.076] |
| *Older advice (2021-22):* In the US, do not run a store as a sole proprietor. | [L1405.008] |

- The timing decision depends on personal risk and sales level. Whether to form an LLC or S corp early or wait is a personal choice. *(Older advice, 2018.)* [L1274.047]
- The sales level and risk exposure decide when a sole proprietorship stops being suitable. Owners with significant assets should avoid it from the start. [L0703.002]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* The right structure depends on personal circumstances such as existing self-employment, other jobs, investments and personal assets. A local accountant or lawyer should confirm the choice, especially outside the US and Australia. [L1405.007]

#### Revenue and Profit Thresholds

| Measure | Threshold | What applies | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Annual profit | Below about $50,000 per year | Operating as a sole proprietor under your own name is simplest. | [L1126.027] |
| Annual profit | From about $100,000 per year | Look at tax-saving structures. At a 30% rate, tax on $100,000 would be about $30,000. | [L1126.027] |
| Monthly store revenue | Above roughly 5,000 to 10,000 USD | A company setup is recommended. It also helps with funding, business credit, banking and tax handling. *(Source C.)* | [L1512.009] |

- Profit thresholds and revenue thresholds are different measures. Use the profit figure for tax-structure decisions and the revenue figure for the company decision in the Source C source. [L1126.027, L1512.009]

#### Upgrade Procedures

*Older procedure (2020): Business setup path used by the coach.*

1. Start as a sole proprietor. [L1277.104]
2. Get an EIN. [L1277.104]
3. Once revenue arrives, consult an accountant. [L1277.104]
4. Set up an S-Corp with that accountant, and consider separate LLCs or S-Corps for each unrelated niche network. [L1277.104]
5. Confirm all of this with your own accountant or lawyer, since the coach is not one. [L1277.104]

*Source C procedure for a beginner's company path.*

1. Check whether Shopify Payments can be opened with personal documents, which may make a company unnecessary at first. [L1512.038]
2. If a company is needed, form a basic company in your home country, not abroad. [L1512.038]
3. After you have proof of concept, plan a better overseas setup for scaling. [L1512.038]

- *Older advice (2018):* You can start as a sole proprietor and later convert to an LLC or S-Corp, typically once the business is profitable. The expert did exactly that and paid an accountant to set up the S-Corp after sales started coming in. [L1274.046]

### Structuring Multiple Stores

#### Related Versus Unrelated Stores

- Related stores can sit under one LLC when they may later be sold as a network, especially when they cross-promote and share a remarketing pixel. Unrelated niches should be placed in a separate LLC. [L0471.059]
- Stores are related if they target the same audience, share suppliers, or compete directly or indirectly. Unrelated niches should each sit in their own LLC. *(Older, 2022.)* [L1092.009]
- Run separate niches under separate businesses. A closely related niche can share an LLC, while an unrelated niche should get its own LLC so finances stay separate. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0803.063]
- Building a network of stores under one LLC works for stores selling related products. Keep stores in separate LLCs when they operate in unrelated product types, so funds are not commingled. *(Older, 2021.)* [L0801.146]
- Put related stores that are cross-promoted or would be sold together under one LLC or S corp. *(Older, 2022.)* [L1113.046]
- Test: ask whether you would compete with store A if you kept store B. If yes, keep them in one entity. Create a new LLC for unrelated businesses. *(Older, 2022.)* [L1113.046]
- Run related stores in the same niche under one parent company, and open an unrelated niche under a separate business entity. Related stores can operate as DBAs under the parent company. *(2023.)* [L0835.113]
- Businesses that would be sold together should share one LLC, one EIN, one bank account and one resale certificate. Unrelated businesses should be set up as separate entities. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0963.120]
- An Amazon store and a Shopify store can share one LLC if they are related. Decide based on whether a future buyer would buy them as a bundle. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0963.122]
- Related stores that are cross-promoted to the same customers can be sold together as one listing. *(2024.)* [L1285.073]
- Group related stores under one LLC or S corp and form a separate entity for each unrelated category. This keeps stores easy to sell individually, since selling one store can compete with related ones. *(Older, 2021.)* [L1279.077]
- Operate a dropship business under a separate entity from other businesses, such as Etsy or Amazon, if you may sell that business later. Commingled finances make the parts harder to sell separately. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0665.098]
- Each store can be run under its own business entity so stores can later be sold separately. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0665.128]
- Mixing funds and expenses means the businesses are effectively one business, so decide whether that is what you want. *(Older, 2021.)* [L1090.124]
- Do not register an LLC under the name of a niche-specific website, because it ties the company to one niche and makes it hard to expand. Create a new entity for other stores and use DBAs for the store names. *(2023.)* [L0835.114]
- One LLC can run several businesses under different trade names. A single LLC can be kept as a long-term vehicle for future projects. Each business does not need its own LLC. *(Older, Source B 2021-22.)* [L1415.019]

#### Conflict: One Umbrella Entity or One Entity per Store

- Sources agree that related stores can share an entity. They differ on whether each store should have its own entity.
- *Shared entity:* related stores under one LLC when they may be sold as a network or cross-promoted. [L0471.059, L1285.073]
- *Separate entity per store:* each store under its own business entity so stores can be sold separately. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0665.128]
- Apply the shared entity when you plan to sell a network as one asset. Apply separate entities when you plan to sell stores individually. [L0471.059, L0665.128, L1279.077]

#### Single Entity Mechanics

- All stores can be run through one business entity with one EIN and one sales tax permit. Use one bank with sub-accounts for each store to see the money per store. *(Older, 2020.)* [L0852.094]
- Keep the company that holds the bank accounts and credit card, and that appears on supplier forms, as the company that operates the websites. *(Older, 2019.)* [L0960.059]
- An existing LLC can be used to run the stores as a business name, so a new company is not always needed. *(Older, 2019.)* [L0960.060]
- An EIN is tied to the business entity, not to one store. One entity can run many stores, so changing niche or rebranding does not require a new entity. *(Older, 2021.)* [L0968.025]
- Under one S corp, one entity can run several Shopify stores under different domain names, with each store linked to the corporation. A formal DBA filing for each domain was not needed in that setup. *(Older, 2018.)* [L1085.077]
- One S-Corp can own all of your stores. *(Older, 2018.)* [L1274.048]
- An existing registered company can sell online under a DBA. *(Older, 2021.)* [L1279.076]
- Keep the entity if possible instead of forming a new one and closing the old one, even if the business name no longer matches the niche. Choose a neutral name where a rename is needed. *(Older, 2022.)* [L1281.072]
- An older LLC can be reused for a new store only if it has a generic name. An LLC named after an unrelated former business should not be used. *(2024.)* [L1285.074]
- To open a new store name, buy a domain and create a Shopify store under the new name while the operating company remains the S corp. Check with a lawyer or accountant whether a DBA or separate entity is needed. *(Older, 2019. Not legal advice.)* [L0467.072]

### Naming the Entity

#### Generic Names

- Do not name the LLC after the store brand. Use a generic business name such as "AK Stores LLC" or "AK Shopping LLC". This allows you to own multiple stores under one LLC, keeps accounting clean and makes future sales easier. [L0118.002]
- Example: AK Stores LLC could own ShakaSurfboards.com initially, then add ShakaPaddleboards.com under the same LLC. [L0118.003]
- Form the LLC with a broad, general name rather than a store-specific name, such as "DD Stores LLC". *(Older, 2021.)* [L0270.012]
- Use a general, non-niche-specific LLC name so future related stores can be placed under the same entity. A narrow name makes it harder to add stores later. *(Older, 2022.)* [L1092.008]
- Name legal entities with broad, non-store names, because one entity can later run several stores. *(Older, 2020.)* [L1088.078]
- Name the parent company generically, for example with your initials plus "Stores, Inc.", rather than after one domain. This lets the same company own future stores. *(Older, 2019.)* [L1002.069]
- Keep the company name generic and let each store name carry the niche. Stores built in the same niche can sit under one company. The store name, not the company name, is the part tied to the niche and domain. *(2023.)* [L1114.067]
- The company name is mostly seen by banks and business contacts, while each store carries its own brand name. *(Older, 2018.)* [L1274.048]
- Give the LLC a generic name that is not the store name, for example a pattern like "X Stores LLC" or "X Enterprises LLC", so it can hold several stores over time. The exact wording matters less than the name being general. [L0695.003]
- Pick a vague LLC name such as a set of initials plus "Stores" or "Enterprises", so the same entity can own several stores. *(2023.)* [L0804.097]
- Use a neutral business name, such as initials with the word "Stores" or "Inc", before the niche is decided, because it appears on bank accounts and supplier applications. *(Older, 2022.)* [L1281.071]
- Do not name the company after the store. A separate company name, such as the owner's initials plus "Stores LLC", makes it easier to open additional stores later. *(Older, 2021.)* [L0834.080]
- Entity name examples: "ER Stores LLC" and "Online Stores LLC". *(Older, 2022.)* [L0249.073]
- Use the entity name for tax documents and bank accounts only. [L0249.073]
- The business name does not need to relate to your domain or niche. [L0249.073, L0429.010]
- Choose a different entity name rather than the store URL, so several related niche stores can be run under the same entity. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0429.010]
- Example: a business entity "AK Stores Inc" owns the stores "Antons-Paddleboards.com" and "Antons-Kayaks.com". *(Older, 2022.)* [L0272.043]
- The LLC name should differ from the store domain. This separates the legal entity from the brand. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0272.043]
- Business names used for an LLC or EIN do not have to match the store names. The LLC or S corp owns the websites, so the legal entity name can differ from the brand. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0854.086]
- The name on the LLC paperwork must match the name used for the tax ID and the submitted address. *(2024.)* [L0971.068]

#### DBAs (Doing Business As)

- A DBA is usually needed when the LLC name differs from the store name, although this varies by state. Consult local requirements. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0272.044]
- Register the DBA in the state where the LLC is registered. The existing sales tax certificate usually covers e-commerce sales in that state. *(Older, 2020.)* [L0468.160]
- Use a DBA when the store name is close to the LLC name or is general. Form a new LLC when the new store is unrelated. *(2023.)* [L0804.097]
- Check your original business setup with the person who helped create it before adding a DBA or reseller certificate to an existing LLC. Advice on legal or tax structures should come from an accountant or lawyer. *(Older, 2018.)* [L1085.079]
- Choose an LLC over a DBA if you are fully committed to the business and plan to form an entity eventually. Form it early. Both DBA and LLC are fine for supplier approval. *(2024.)* [L1285.072]
- Running an online store as a DBA under an existing LLC with the same EIN and bank account is possible if the businesses are related. *(Older, 2021.)* [L1090.124]

### Registered Agent, State and Address

#### Choosing a State

- Form the LLC in the state where you live. You need only one LLC per state. [L0118.004]
- *Alternative:* Choose the US state for your LLC. Texas is business-friendly. A virtual address through a UPS store or PO box can serve as the official business address, so you do not have to use your home address. *(Older, 2021.)* [L0834.078]
- *Alternative:* Choosing a state to form your company in is a personal decision. Texas is described as business friendly, and Delaware and Nevada are commonly used alternatives. *(Older, 2018.)* [L1274.049]
- Wyoming's tax benefits are mainly relevant to non-US residents forming a US company. A US resident should generally form the company in their own state, since Wyoming or Delaware give no clear advantage there. *(Source C.)* [L1512.023]
- An LLC can be formed in any state, but forming it in another state usually does not remove the obligation to pay tax in the state where you live. Check tax rules with a professional before relying on a different state. *(Older, 2019.)* [L0960.061]
- An LLC formed in Wyoming while physically operating in Florida: assume a nexus in the state of operation and confirm with a tax professional. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0969.028]

#### Registered Agents and Legal Address

- A registered agent is the person who receives legal paperwork for a business. A registered agent is needed when you set up a business in a state where you do not live. A legal service can provide one. *(2023.)* [L0835.115]
- A registered agent must be located in the state where the LLC is registered. An LLC outside your state needs a local registered agent who forwards mail. *(2024.)* [L0971.068]

### Before the First Sign-Up

- Settle the business structure before any supplier, platform or ad account sign-up, because those accounts use the registered business details. Get an EIN and a sales tax certificate only when they are needed for your situation (US context). [L0393.003]
- You do not need an LLC or business license before contacting suppliers. You can start as a sole proprietor, then hire an accountant to set up a corporation after revenue starts. *(Older, 2020.)* [L0268.045]
- A business structure is not required to get approved by suppliers. Starting as a sole proprietor is valid, and an LLC or S corporation can be added later. *(Older, 2018.)* [L1085.075]
- Entity formation is optional and is not needed before contacting suppliers. *(Older, 2020.)* [L1088.077]
- *Conflict:* Many US suppliers prefer to deal with a company structure such as an LLC or S corp rather than a sole proprietor. This is an additional reason to form one. *(Older, Source B 2021-22.)* [L1405.011]
- Australian suppliers generally do not care whether you trade as a sole trader or through a company, so the early choice does not limit supplier approval. *(Older, Source B 2021-22.)* [L1405.015]
- An EIN can be used for more than one business in the view presented, but confirm this with an accountant before relying on it. *(Older, 2021.)* [L1090.123]
- Get an EIN as part of the setup path. [L1277.104]
- Running a store under your own name typically requires being at least 18 years old. A younger founder would normally need an adult to sign the accounts and agreements. Confirm the rules for your country or state before opening business accounts. *(Older, 2022.)* [L0803.100]

### Structure by Country

#### United States

- The usual US business forms are a sole proprietorship (trading as yourself), an LLC and an S corp. The last two are legal entities. [L0698.015]
- *Conflict:* A sole proprietorship can be used at the start, and an LLC can be formed later. [L0698.006, L0962.122]
- *Conflict (older, 2021-22):* A sole proprietorship gives no separation between the business and the owner. The owner is personally liable, and personal assets such as a home can be seized to pay business debts. Do not run a US store as a sole proprietor. [L1405.008]
- Sole proprietorship, LLC and S corp differ mainly in liability and tax treatment. Use the LLC and S-corp options above for the relevant decision. [L0698.015, L0703.003, L0703.004]

#### Australia

- *Older advice (2021-22):* In Australia the two structures are the sole trader and the Proprietary Limited company (Pty Ltd). [L1405.012]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* A sole trader can be set up in minutes. A Pty Ltd costs about $800 to $2,000 to establish and requires an accountant. [L1405.012]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* In Australia, a sole trader is personally liable for business claims. A company director's personal assets are protected if the business operates legally. Liability protection and tax treatment are the two main differences. [L1405.013]
- *Older advice (2021-22). Recommended path for most Australian beginners:*
  1. Start as a sole trader to keep setup cheap and fast. [L1405.014]
  2. Once the business earns meaningful revenue and liability exposure grows, switch to a Pty Ltd company with an accountant's help. [L1405.014]
  3. If you already own a Pty Ltd, use it from the start. [L1405.014]

#### Sole Trader and Company Basics (Source B, Older)

- *Older advice (2021-22):* A sole trader has no legal separation from the owner. [L0702.003]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* The choice between sole trader and company has no universal right answer. It depends on liability exposure, tax situation, cash available and cost. Advice from an accountant is recommended when tax is complex. [L0702.008]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Starting as a sole trader is a valid low-cost choice, and it is easy to convert to a company later. Many owners do this when cash is limited and convert once the company's cost is financially justified. [L0702.011]

#### Other Countries

- Most other countries use different names but the same two-tier choice between trading as an individual and forming a company. [L0698.015]
- *Older advice (2021-22):* Outside the US and Australia, a local accountant or lawyer should confirm the structure choice. [L1405.007]

### Company Path for Beginners (Source C)

- Check whether Shopify Payments can be opened with personal documents. If so, a company may be unnecessary at first. [L1512.038]
- If a company is needed, form a basic company in your home country, not abroad. [L1512.038]
- After you have proof of concept, plan a better overseas setup for scaling. [L1512.038]
- At about 100,000 USD monthly revenue, the advice was to consider Dubai for tax savings. Hong Kong was recommended for payment stability if the seller lives at home. Both need a home-country basic company for tax reporting. [L1512.039]

#### Further details

- When structuring multiple stores, consult an accountant or lawyer about whether to use one LLC for all stores or separate entities; benefits of separate entities include asset protection from lawsuits and cleaner separation if selling a store later. [L0245.065]

## Forming the Entity and Registering the Business

### Order of registration

- Choose the legal form first: sole proprietor, LLC or S-Corp. The sales tax certificate is needed only where the seller must collect sales tax. [L0532.002, L0532.003]
- Form the LLC before you apply for the EIN. The EIN application uses the LLC name. [L0695.010, L0118.010]
- Register the business early, even before it earns money, because the LLC, EIN and any sales tax certificate will be needed anyway. This avoids later delays and makes the business look more professional to suppliers. [L0695.017]
- The business name and the store name can differ. A business name is often generic and not tied to one niche. [L0941.092, L0251.015]
- Do not set up ad accounts until supplier products are on the store. While waiting for the EIN or company paperwork, work on supplier research and niche understanding. [L0962.121]

#### Full procedure: US LLC with EIN and sales tax certificate (older sources marked)

1. Decide between sole proprietorship and LLC. Choose an LLC unless there is almost nothing to lose. [L0695.008]
2. Choose a generic LLC name that is not the store name. [L0695.008]
3. Form the LLC online in the state where you are located, using LegalZoom's $0 basic option plus the state filing fee (no more than $97). Decline all upsell offers. [L0695.008, L0695.006]
4. Confirm with LegalZoom, or the chosen service, that the LLC name is available and belongs to you. [L0695.008]
5. Apply for an EIN on the IRS website using the "Apply for an EIN" option. Answer the questions, which usually takes 5 to 10 minutes. You receive a 9-digit number. [L0695.008]
6. Only if your state has sales tax: search Google for "[your state] sales tax certificate", open the state's.gov sales and use tax registration page, choose to register online, and enter the LLC name. [L0695.008, L0695.013]
7. Download the PDF that shows your sales tax certificate number. If none is offered, the certificate is sent by mail. [L0695.008, L0695.015]
8. Use the LLC name, EIN and sales tax certificate when registering with suppliers. [L0695.008]

#### Other sequences in the sources

| Sequence | Source | Note |
|---|---|---|
| Form LLC → get EIN → register for sales tax certificate if required in your state | [L0118.010] | Canonical order used in the sources |
| Choose structure → apply for EIN and sales tax certificate if needed | [L0393.002, L0532.002] | Structure must come first |
| Form LLC → get EIN (usually during formation) → open bank accounts → apply for sales tax certificate once the niche is picked | [L0803.062] (2022, older) | Bank opens before sales tax |
| Form LLC → apply for EIN online in a few minutes, without calling the IRS → keep EIN and sales tax certificate under the LLC name | [L1115.030] (2024) | Keep all numbers under LLC name, not store name |
| Structure → free EIN from the federal government → seller's permit in home state → business bank account using LLC info and EIN → business PayPal linked to the bank, with auto sweep enabled by PayPal support | [L1415.005] (2021-22, older) | Includes PayPal step |
| Register company with state → EIN → sales tax permit → business checking and savings → business credit card | [L0966.106] (2025) | Pre-launch order for the store |

#### Step-by-step details (per step)

- The EIN can usually be requested at the same time as the LLC, or right after the state approves it, depending on the filing service. [L0468.149] (2020, older)
- The EIN is a unique tax number for the business and is needed for Stripe, Mercury and most platforms. [L1127.004]
- Use the EIN as the business tax number for Stripe, Mercury and other platforms. [L1127.002]
- Open the business bank accounts only after the LLC and EIN are in place. [L0693.001]
- Entity, tax ID and bank setup should be done before supplier applications. Suppliers ask for the business name, business structure, registration and tax numbers. Missing these can delay progress by about a week while government registrations are processed. [L1405.006] (2021-22, older)
- Keep the EIN and sales tax certificate under the LLC's name, not the store name. [L1115.030]

### Choosing the legal form

- Sole proprietor or LLC: choose an LLC unless there is almost nothing to lose. [L0695.008]
- Before the EIN and sales tax certificate, get an accountant's advice on the structure that fits you, and track every business expense from the start so all deductible costs can be written off. [L1344.023]
- Describe the business as "online retail" or similar. No physical location is required in the formation documents. [L0268.047] (2020, older)
- An S-corp can be set up with an accountant after the EIN and LLC are in place. Many owners set up the EIN, LLC and S-corp as a package. [L0468.164] (2020, older)
- Converting a sole proprietorship to an LLC (DBA, EIN, bank accounts): consult a CPA or the state where the DBA was registered. One call did not give the steps. [L0805.053] (2024)

### Forming an LLC: DIY, services and fees

- Forming an LLC means filing articles of organization with your state. This is a formal registration step separate from any federal number. It can usually be done without a paid service, although many online ads push paid helpers. [L1415.017] (2021-22, older)
- You can form a business entity yourself. Paying someone is optional, and many owners pay only after the business is making money. [L1112.061] (2021, older)
- An LLC and EIN can be set up directly online for little cost. Third-party formation services often charge about $99 and then upsell extra services. [L0855.123] (2023)
- An LLC filed online through LegalZoom costs about $99 and is optional. It keeps business and personal funds separated from the start. [L1275.061] (2019, older)
- Do not pay a service just to obtain a document you can file yourself. Compare quotes before paying. [L0468.151] (2020, older)
- A service that forms LLCs for a monthly fee charges only the state filing fee. Typical formation services add a markup of roughly $100 to $150 on top of it. [L0960.062] (2019, older)
- Forming a business entity directly through a local government website typically costs about $100 to $300 at most. [L0698.008]
- Paying third-party intermediaries to form a legal entity abroad can be far more expensive than the official government registration website. Use the government portal whenever it is available. [L0698.009]
- Use the official state website to apply for a business entity or the state's reseller certificate. A virtual address can be used where needed for business documents. [L0964.115] (2023)
- Formation services usually handle the state filing for you. Confirm with the service what is included. [L1113.048] (2022, older)

#### Fee and timeline reference

| Option | Cost | Time | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| LegalZoom basic LLC, plus state fee | $0 + state filing fee (usually under $100; no more than $97) | Not stated | [L0118.005, L0695.006] |
| LegalZoom, form-and-submit service (2022) | About $99 | "Usually fast" | [L0854.085] (2022, older) |
| Direct state filing | State fee only; example filed online for 85 USD and approved | Not stated | [L1092.013] (2022, older) |
| Government website, typical maximum | About $100 to $300 at most | Not stated | [L0698.008] |
| Filing service quotes, same filing (2020) | $250 vs $100 | Not stated | [L0468.193] (2020, older) |
| Wisconsin, self-filed with state (2016 example) | About $125 in filing fees | Not stated | [L1415.004] (2021-22, older) |
| Formation service, Europe-based example | About 150 EUR and about 350 EUR quoted by two providers | Not stated | [L0693.001] |
| FirstBase, US company | About 300 to 360 USD | About 1 to 2 weeks | [L1512.036] |
| Wyoming LLC through a formation service (case) | Around 300 to 400 USD | About 2 to 3 weeks | [L1512.022] |
| CPA filing (case) | About 200 USD at most | Under 3 to 4 days | [L1512.022] |

- Get at least two quotes before paying a filing service. [L0468.193] (2020, older)
- A US LLC or corporation typically takes about one day, sometimes one week, and in slow cases up to a couple of weeks. [L0698.007]
- Start the official business entity early rather than later if the business is serious, since the process takes time. [L0698.010]
- Opening a US LLC cost a couple of hundred dollars for one Source C speaker. [L0690.025]

#### Service-specific notes

- LegalZoom forms an LLC online for $0 plus the state filing fee. The basic option handles the paperwork. Form the LLC in the state where the owner is located. [L0695.006]
- After signing up with LegalZoom, an upsell flow offers website builders, accounting software and lawyer services. None are needed to form the LLC. Decline all of them. [L0695.007, L0118.005]
- LegalZoom registered agent: usually the only upsell worth buying as a recurring service. Expect upsells at checkout. [L0835.116] (2023)
- Better Legal, a bonus from the training provider, is available for setting up a business structure with no service fee. You pay only the state filing fee for your state. [L0551.065] (2020, older)
- Online formation services such as LegalZoom, or similar companies, can set up an LLC. The owner can also file directly with the state. [L1092.013] (2022, older)
- Source C formation procedure:
  1. Choose the state. Wyoming is named as a state with zero taxes, although other states are sometimes preferred. [L1127.002]
  2. Register the LLC on the service site. [L1127.002]
  3. Add the EIN option to the order. It carries an extra charge. [L1127.002, L1127.004]
  4. Use the EIN as the business tax number for Stripe, Mercury and other platforms. [L1127.002]
- Source C procedure (LLC formation and bank):
  1. Form the US LLC through a formation service. One provider quoted about 150 EUR and another about 350 EUR for the setup. [L0693.001]
  2. Order the EIN for the LLC. This is critical and must not be forgotten. [L0693.001]
  3. Open the business bank accounts only after the LLC and EIN are in place. [L0693.001]
  4. Start operating. [L0693.001]
- FirstBase needs a company name, a description of the business, address proof and an ID such as a passport. [L1512.036]

### Naming the entity

- Choose a general business name that does not tie you to a specific niche, for example "AM Stores LLC". [L0251.015] (2023)
- All domain names for different niches can be owned by the same LLC. [L0251.015] (2023)
- When contacting suppliers, reference the LLC name, not individual niches. [L0251.015] (2023)
- Confirm that the LLC name is available and belongs to you before you apply for the EIN. [L0695.008]

### Obtaining the EIN

- The EIN is free when requested directly from irs.gov during IRS business hours. Have it ready for suppliers. [L1344.023]
- Apply on the IRS website, not through paid third-party sites. [L0695.010]
- A formation service may charge extra for the EIN as an add-on. Add it when placing the LLC order if you use the service. [L1127.004]
- The EIN can usually be requested at the same time as the LLC or right after the state approves it. [L0468.149] (2020, older)

### Sales tax certificate (state level)

- A sales tax certificate is needed only in the state where the business is registered, and only if that state has sales tax. There is no single federal source. It is obtained through the state's government website. [L0695.012]
- States that charge sales tax require a state sales tax certificate. Get it early rather than late. [L0468.148] (2020, older)
- Depending on your state, you may need a sales tax certificate. It can be applied for online and usually arrives within a few days. Not every supplier asks for it at the start. [L0671.111, L0671.248] (2021, older)
- Search for the state name with "sales tax certificate" or "sales tax permit" to see whether your state requires one. [L1285.076, L1405.037]
- Use only the official.gov website. Commercial.com sites offer to file the same registration for a fee, although it can be done directly and quickly online. [L0695.013]
- After online registration, the state usually provides a downloadable PDF with the certificate number. If no PDF is provided, the state mails the certificate. [L0695.015]
- Example, North Carolina: the sales and use tax registration page has an online form that asks for the LLC name. There is no fee for the certificate of registration. [L0695.014]
- To get a certificate: search Google for your state's name with "sales tax certificate", open the state's official online form, and fill it out. It usually costs $0 and is often issued online within about a day. [L1285.076] (2024)
- In the US, sales tax permits are issued by each state. Many states let you apply online through the state government's website. [L1405.037] (2021-22, older)
- The sales tax certificate is only required in cases where the seller must collect sales tax. [L0532.003]
- Check your state at taxjar.com/states, click your home state, and read the registration FAQ to see whether you need a sales tax certificate there. [L1344.023]
- After getting a seller's permit in your home state, register separately for any sales and use tax obligations and for payroll if you will pay employees. These registrations are separate from the seller's permit itself. [L1415.018] (2021-22, older)
- Apply for a sales tax certificate once the niche is picked. It can often be done online in a short time, depending on the state. [L0803.062] (2022, older)

### Business licenses and state-specific rules

- Business license and permit requirements differ by state in the US. Look up the rules for the state where you operate instead of copying another state's steps. [L0801.183] (2021, older)
- A business license may be requested by suppliers. If the home state issues no license, ask the supplier whether an alternative form can be used instead. [L0855.121] (2023)
- Business license filing services can charge very different fees for the same filing, with one quote at $250 and another at $100 (2020 example). [L0468.193] (2020, older)

### California and out-of-state entities

- If you live in California but incorporate in another state (for example a Wyoming S-Corp), California requires you to register as a foreign business entity in California. [L0253.042, L0253.057] (2024)
- California has the strictest state tax requirements. Consult the Source A Discord community for specific steps, as this varies by situation. [L0253.042, L0253.057] (2024)

### S-corp election

- The S-corp election (IRS Form 2553) can be filed early. It has no effect until the business has revenue. When to file is a personal choice. [L0667.077] (2025)
- An S-corp can be set up with an accountant after the EIN and LLC are in place. [L0468.164] (2020, older)
- Converting a sole proprietorship to an S-corp: update the legal name and tax details with Shopify, update the legal details with each supplier account, update the financial accounts that hold the business's money, and keep a list of who has confirmed the change and who is still pending. Some accounts respond slowly. [L0834.124] (2021, older)

### Banking and payments timing

- Open bank accounts after the LLC and EIN are in place. [L0693.001]
- Identify which banking options you will use and open the business accounts early. [L0702.027] (2021-22, older)
- Before the store goes live, open a business checking account, a business savings account and a business credit card, in that order after registration and EIN. [L0966.106] (2025)
- Set up a business PayPal account linked to the business bank account, and ask PayPal support to enable auto sweep. [L1415.005] (2021-22, older)
- Use the EIN as the business tax number for Stripe and Mercury. [L1127.002, L1127.004]

### Non-US equivalents (Australia)

- In Australia, a sole trader needs an ABN and a company needs an ACN. Other countries have an equivalent business number that identifies the business for tax purposes. [L0702.021] (2021-22, older)
- Do business in the country where you live. If that is not possible, do it in the US. [L0702.027] (2021-22, older)
- Registering as a sole trader and getting an ABN online costs about 36 Australian dollars. [L0702.009] (2021-22, older)
- Forming a company with an accountant's help typically costs between 1,500 and 2,000 Australian dollars. [L0702.010] (2021-22, older)
- An Australian sole trader needs an ABN, obtained through a short online form and issued quickly. A company gets an ACN from its accountant when it is registered. Both numbers may be required on supplier documents. [L1405.016] (2021-22, older)
- A sole trader can begin trading as soon as the ABN is issued online. No further registration is needed before the first sales. Company formation usually takes longer and involves an accountant. [L0702.031] (2021-22, older)
- Government registrations can take one to two weeks to process. Start them early and have your business name, company name, company number and tax ID ready when you contact suppliers. [L0702.028] (2021-22, older)
- In Australia, a.com.au domain requires a registered Australian business, identified by an ABN. Buying an ABN is one route to obtain that domain. [L0549.039] (Source C)

#### Legal and banking setup checklist (Australia, in order)

1. Decide the country where you will do business. [L0702.027] (2021-22, older)
2. Start the process to form the business entity now. For a sole trader, use the government registration page for your country (in Australia, apply online for an ABN). For a company, work through the setup with an accountant. [L0702.027] (2021-22, older)
3. Obtain the tax ID required in your country (in Australia, an ABN or an ACN). [L0702.027] (2021-22, older)
4. Start looking for a business accountant now, even if you are not using one yet. Ask people you know for recommendations. [L0702.027] (2021-22, older)
5. Identify which banking options you will use and open the business accounts now. [L0702.027] (2021-22, older)

### Tax setup checklist (US e-commerce)

1. Decide which business structure fits you (sole proprietor, LLC or S-Corp), and get an accountant's advice on it. [L1344.023]
2. Track every business expense from the start so all deductible costs can be written off. [L1344.023]
3. Form the business type you chose. [L1344.023]
4. Get a free EIN directly from irs.gov during IRS business hours, and have it ready for suppliers. [L1344.023]
5. Check your home state at taxjar.com/states to see whether you need a sales tax certificate there. [L1344.023]

### Post-formation updates

- Update the legal name and tax details with Shopify after any change of legal form. [L0834.124] (2021, older)
- Update the legal details with each supplier account. [L0834.124] (2021, older)
- Update the financial accounts that hold the business's money. [L0834.124] (2021, older)
- Keep a list of who has confirmed the change and who is still pending. Some accounts respond slowly. [L0834.124] (2021, older)

### Case: US company (Wyoming LLC, reported)

- Formation cost around 300 to 400 USD, with formation time about 2 to 3 weeks. Filing by a CPA cost about 200 USD at most and took under 3 to 4 days. [L1512.022]
- Stripe payouts came within 1 to 2 days. The first year reached about 300,000 USD in revenue, with zero taxes reported as paid under that structure. [L1512.022]

| Formation time | One day to a couple of weeks | 1 to 2 weeks (FirstBase); 2 to 3 weeks (Wyoming case) | Depends on the service and state | [L0698.007] vs [L1512.036] vs [L1512.022] |

| Business license cost | $100 quote | $250 quote | Same filing; compare quotes | [L0468.193] |

#### Further details

- LLC business registration can be completed before the done-for-you store is built. This speeds up supplier conversations later. [L0278.038]

## EIN, Tax IDs and Supplier Paperwork

### Which tax numbers apply to you

| Your situation | What applies | Source |
|---|---|---|
| US business | An EIN is required and free from the IRS. A sales tax certificate applies where your state has sales tax and suppliers ask for one. | [L0278.037, L0118.008, L1095.002] |
| Non-US resident starting a US business | Apply for an EIN through the international route. An ITIN is used for tax filing without an SSN. | [L1090.128, L0804.095, L0963.126] |
| European business not selling into the US | A VAT number is enough. An EIN is not needed. | [L0360.070] |
| Canadian seller | Sources disagree (see Conflicts below). | [L1277.102, L0853.024, L1116.009] |
| Store that does not sell in the US | No EIN is needed for that store. | [L0360.070] |

### Decision order: what to get and when

1. **Get the EIN from the IRS first.** It is the minimum to have before contacting suppliers. [L0006.003, L0468.192, L0468.147, L1415.007]
2. **Get a sales tax certificate if your state has sales tax and suppliers ask for one.** [L0006.004, L0118.008, L0359.088]
3. **Keep a W-9 ready for suppliers that accept one.** [L0667.080, L0972.048]
4. **Apply before supplier outreach where possible.** Some suppliers require these documents and others do not. [L1116.008, L0808.045]
5. **If you are close to approval, have the documents ready within one to two weeks rather than re-contacting the supplier later.** [L0934.004]

### The core numbers and what they are

#### EIN (federal tax ID)

- An EIN (Employer Identification Number) is a 9-digit number the IRS issues to a business. It works like a Social Security number for the company and is given to suppliers instead of your personal SSN. [L0118.006, L0695.009, L1344.010]
- The format is two digits, a dash and seven digits. A tax ID in that format is the same kind of number as an EIN. [L0855.124]
- It is a federal tax ID. It is free from the IRS and is needed for tax reporting and for many business tasks in the US. [L0671.108, L1344.010]
- A US business typically needs an EIN for tax filings and for opening a business bank account. [L0675.004, L1095.002]
- It is needed to open a business bank account, apply for business licenses, file business taxes and fill in most supplier application forms. [L1415.007] (older advice, 2021)
- It keeps business tax and accounting records separate from personal records. [L0468.147] (older advice, 2020)
- Do not use your Social Security number in place of an EIN. Using the EIN keeps business and personal finances separate. [L1344.012]
- US business suppliers require an EIN. [L0278.037]
- Many suppliers ask for an EIN during approval. [L0247.102] (older advice, 2021)
- Suppliers prefer an EIN over a personal SSN. [L0272.047] (older advice, 2022)
- Suppliers use the EIN and the sales tax ID as interchangeable terms when they request a tax ID. [L0961.017] (older advice, 2020) See Conflicts below.

#### Sales tax certificate, resale certificate and related names

- A sales tax certificate is the same thing as a reseller permit. [L0671.112, L0671.249] (older advice, 2021)
- A sales tax certificate and a resale certificate are the same document, not two separate ones. They can be obtained online per state, and the process is typically fast and automated. [L0967.057] (older advice, 2020)
- A sales tax permit and a reseller certificate are the same thing. They are based on the EIN and cover collecting and remitting sales tax. Suppliers asking for either are requesting the same document. [L0969.029] (older advice, 2022)
- In the US, a reseller permit and a sales tax certificate are the same document. [L0934.006]
- In many states a sales tax license and a resale certificate are the same document, and suppliers may ask for either one. [L0855.120]
- A resale certificate and a sales tax ID refer to the same thing. Use them interchangeably when registering for sales tax. [L0963.125] (older advice, 2022)
- A reseller number is essentially a sales tax ID. Some suppliers ask for it, and depending on your state you may need one. [L1274.045] (older advice, 2018)
- A seller's permit is also called a sales tax permit, sales tax license or reseller's permit. [L1415.008] (older advice, 2021)
- A business license, sales tax certificate and sales tax registration number usually refer to the same thing, though the name varies by state. [L1285.075]
- A supplier's request for a business license number generally means the same thing as a reseller permit or sales tax ID. [L0971.015]
- Names differ by state. Search for the state name plus "sales tax certificate" or "resale certificate." [L0667.079, L0934.006]

#### W-9 (form, not a number)

- The W-9 is an IRS form that a US business uses to request the taxpayer identification number of a US-based supplier or contractor. It collects this information in a standard format and applies only when the business pays US-based payees. [L1008.005]
- A W-9 is usually not needed, but fill it out if a supplier asks for it. [L1085.078] (older advice, 2018)

#### ITIN

- An ITIN (Individual Taxpayer Identification Number) is a tax ID for individuals who are not US citizens or residents and need to file US taxes. Use it when you are a non-US resident starting a US business. [L1090.128] (older advice, 2021)
- An ITIN is used only for non-US citizens filing taxes without a Social Security number. [L0278.037]
- For non-US residents, an ITIN works similarly to an EIN, and suppliers may ask for one or the other. Ask each supplier what they require, and check forums and communities for answers. [L1277.103] (older advice, 2020)

#### VAT and corporate tax numbers

- VAT is specific to Europe and an EIN to the US. A VAT number is enough for European business. [L0360.070] (2024 advice)
- A corporate tax number is not the same as a VAT number. Suppliers may ask for a specific one before authorizing a store. Asking which number a supplier requires before submitting the application avoids delays. [L0360.072]

### Getting your EIN

#### Free application through the IRS

- An EIN is free on the official IRS website (irs.gov). Many third-party sites charge for the same EIN, so use only official sources. [L0667.078, L1274.044] (older advice, 2018)
- A third-party service is not needed. Some companies charge about $200 or more for the same free filing. [L0468.146] (older advice, 2020)
- Paid services that advertise EIN setup are unnecessary for US residents, though non-residents may need paid help to obtain one. [L1405.027] (older advice, 2021-22)
- Paid helpers are not needed for this simple process, since many online ads charge fees to do these steps for you. [L1415.016] (older advice, 2021-22)

**Procedure: free EIN in the US** [L1344.013]

1. Search for "EIN number" on Google.
2. Do not click the paid results marked as ads, because these companies charge a fee for setting up an EIN.
3. Click the first organic result, which is the IRS site at irs.gov.
4. Apply for the EIN online and answer the questions the IRS asks. No paid helper is needed.
5. Expect to receive the EIN typically within about 15 minutes. [L1344.013]

**Procedure: apply for an EIN** (older advice, 2022) [L0963.126]

1. Use only the IRS website, not paid third-party services.
2. If you live outside the US, use the international applicant route, which requires a phone call.
3. Check the IRS phone hours before calling. [L0963.126]

**Procedure: apply for an EIN** (older advice, 2020) [L0468.146]

1. Go to the IRS website and apply for an employer identification number (EIN) for free.
2. The application takes about five minutes.
3. Do not pay a third-party service for the EIN, since some companies charge about $200 or more for the same free filing.
4. Use the EIN instead of your social security number when applying for supplier accounts. [L0468.146]

#### How long it takes

| Time stated | Source |
|---|---|
| 5 to 10 minutes, number received immediately on completion | [L0118.007] |
| About 10 minutes online | [L0359.087] |
| About five minutes | [L0360.069] (2024), [L0468.146] (older, 2020) |
| 5 to 10 minutes, 9-digit EIN produced | [L0695.011] |
| 5 to 10 minutes | [L0671.108, L0671.245] (older, 2021) |
| About 15 minutes online | [L1274.044] (older, 2018) |
| About 15 minutes, received typically within this time | [L1344.013] |

- An online EIN application usually takes 5 to 10 minutes and produces a 9-digit EIN. [L0695.011]
- The IRS online EIN application only works during IRS business hours, even though it is an online form. If you try early in the morning or late at night, it may not be available, so plan the application for business hours. [L1344.014]
- Get the EIN as early as possible. It is free on the IRS website, is required by many suppliers, and takes about 10 minutes online. [L0359.087]

#### Timing relative to your LLC

- Apply for your EIN directly on the IRS website after your LLC is officially approved. [L0118.007]
- An EIN can be obtained without an LLC. When reaching out to suppliers without an LLC yet, get an EIN from the IRS website for free in about five minutes. [L0272.047] (older advice, 2022)
- Form an LLC first only if you want to give company details at the time of approval. [L0852.028] (older advice, 2020)
- Not having an LLC or S-Corp usually does not affect supplier approvals. The coach had never seen that happen. [L1277.107] (older advice, 2020)
- A business license and insurance are optional for an online store. However, get an EIN at a minimum if you do business in the US. [L1277.101] (older advice, 2020)

#### Sole proprietors

- You can contact suppliers as a sole proprietor once the store is built, and no legal entity is needed. [L0852.028] (older advice, 2020)
- A US business without a Social Security number can get an EIN from the IRS website. [L0801.140] (older advice, 2021)
- Get an EIN even as a sole proprietor. It is only needed for businesses in the US. [L1277.100] (older advice, 2020)
- A sole proprietor without employees does not strictly need an EIN. They can obtain one or use the owner's SSN instead. [L1415.006] (older advice, 2021-22)
- A sole proprietor can be approved by some US suppliers, according to reports from store owners, but this was not verified by the lesson. [L0359.090]

#### Reusing an existing EIN

- An existing EIN from a previous business may be reusable for a new store, although the business type on the EIN may need updating. Verify with a tax professional before reuse. [L1111.142] (older advice, 2020)
- Reusing an old EIN from a business that never started is probably allowed, but verify it with a lawyer or a legal forum before you do. [L1277.105] (older advice, 2020)

#### Who applies and how the form is filled

- An EIN can be applied for directly by the company. The title used on the form (president, CEO) is a matter of how you describe yourself. A partnership with several officers requires more care. [L0834.081] (older advice, 2021)

#### Why the EIN matters for pacing

- Tax documents, EIN and state registrations can be completed after you start, because many suppliers still approve new retailers without them. [L0934.004] (2023 advice)
- For a foreign founder, an EIN can take a long time, sometimes up to around 60 days. Keep building the store and contacting suppliers in the meantime, and hold back top-tier suppliers until your business documents are complete. [L0968.017] (older advice, 2021)

### Applying from outside the US

#### Non-US citizens and residents

- Non-US citizens must call the IRS to apply. US citizens can apply online at irs.gov. [L0270.026] (older advice, 2021)
- Non-US residents cannot get an EIN online and must call the IRS, which begins its process at IRS.gov. [L0469.050] (older advice, 2021)
- A non-US resident can get an EIN on the IRS website, though a phone call may be part of the process. [L0359.087]
- Non-US applicants can get an EIN by calling the IRS international applicant number listed on the IRS EIN page. US citizens can use the same online process as in the course. [L0804.095] (2023 advice)
- Applicants outside the US may be directed to call in instead of applying online, so check the foreign applicant route before applying. [L0968.018] (older advice, 2021)
- Non-US businesses can apply for the equivalent tax identification number on the same site. [L0801.140] (older advice, 2021)
- To obtain a US resale certificate as a non-US resident, form a US business entity first and apply through that entity. A virtual address is not enough, a real company address is required. [L0836.001] (2024 advice)
- Stripe Atlas was named as one common formation service, but a state-issued resale certificate, not Stripe Atlas, is what suppliers usually ask for. [L0836.001] (2024 advice)

#### Procedure for the international route

- See the international applicant procedure under Getting your EIN above. [L0963.126]

#### Canadian sellers

- A Canadian seller does not need an EIN, because it is an IRS number for US businesses. [L1277.102] (older advice, 2020)
- A Canadian business selling into the US probably needs a US tax ID, which is a FEIN that can be obtained online from the IRS. This is not certain, so confirm it with Canadians who already run US businesses or with a tax professional. [L0853.024] (older advice, 2021)
- Some US suppliers ask Canadian sellers for an EIN or federal tax ID, which requires a short call to the IRS. Requirements differ by brand, so check with other Canadian sellers in community forums. [L1279.080] (older advice, 2021)
- A business registered in Canada can get a US EIN from the IRS by phone and should not get a resale certificate first. When an application asks for one, leave that section blank or state that the business is based in Canada and has no nexus in any US state. A state tax ID and resale certificate follow later, once a state threshold is reached. [L1116.009] (2025 advice)

#### Foreign entities and sales tax

- A foreign entity without a nexus in any US state probably does not need a sales tax certificate, but this is unverified. Submit the supplier application and check whether approval requires it. [L0853.025] (older advice, 2021)
- Do not collect resale certificates for states where you have no obligation, just because a supplier asks for one. Only obtain them for states where you have physical presence or where you crossed the sales threshold in a year. [L0968.022] (older advice, 2021)

### Sales tax certificate and state resale documents

#### When you need one

- A sales tax certificate is required by most suppliers if your state has a sales tax. If your state has no sales tax, you do not need one. [L0118.008]
- A sales tax certificate is needed only if the supplier requires it and the business is in a state that has sales tax. In states without sales tax, no certificate is needed. In other states, the certificate can be applied for online in about 10 to 15 minutes. [L0359.088]
- Some suppliers ask for a state sales tax certificate and some do not. Requirements depend on the state where the business is based. Having the certificate does no harm and is required in some states. [L0664.026] (older advice, 2020)
- Whether you need a resale certificate depends on the supplier and on your state. Some suppliers ask for one and others do not, and some states do not issue them. Before a state's sales tax threshold is reached, you usually do not need a tax certificate in that state. [L0961.018] (older advice, 2020)
- Many suppliers ask for a seller's permit or sales tax certificate, but not all do. Requirements are state specific, and states without sales tax do not need a certificate there. [L1088.079] (older advice, 2020)
- Taxes and sales tax: for each state, look up the state's reseller certificate. The certificate allows collecting and remitting sales tax and is required by most suppliers. [L0964.123] (2023 advice)
- In a state where the business is based, a sales tax certificate is required, and suppliers may ask for a tax-exempt form. The tax-exempt certificate usually requires an existing sales tax certificate. [L0665.094] (older advice, 2022)
- Whether suppliers require an EIN and a sales tax ID before approval varies by supplier and by state. Ask each supplier directly rather than assuming the same requirement. [L0359.089]
- Some suppliers ask for a resale certificate and others do not, so ask each supplier before assuming you need one. [L0468.192] (older advice, 2020)
- Get a resale certificate early, since many suppliers ask for one even though not all do. Requirements depend on the state where you do business. [L0808.045] (2026 advice)
- Apply for a state resale certificate or sales tax permit early, as suppliers often ask for it when you are approved. [L0667.079] (2025 advice)
- Do not apply for resale permits in every state a supplier asks for if you have no sales nexus there. Offer a W-9 instead, along with your home-state resale certificate. [L0667.080] (2025 advice)
- Provide a resale certificate for the state where your business operates when a supplier asks for one. Do not treat the certificate as a general document for every state. [L0470.083] (2023 advice)
- Get a sales tax certificate and an EIN tax ID eventually. Obtaining them sooner rather than later avoids delays with suppliers that ask for them. [L1088.080] (older advice, 2020)
- A reseller number is not urgent in the early stage. [L1274.045] (older advice, 2018)
- Purchases from suppliers are not taxed when you resell, because you give the supplier a W-9 if requested or your sales tax certificate, also called a resale certificate. Sales tax is separate from income tax. [L0972.048] (2025 advice)

#### Getting a state certificate

**Procedure: sales tax certificate (general)** [L0118.009]

1. Search Google for "[Your State] sales tax certificate" and use only official.gov websites; do not pay third-party sites.
2. Complete online registration if available in your state (most allow this and often free).
3. You will receive a downloadable PDF or physical certificate by mail. [L0118.009]

**Procedure: resale or sales tax certificate** (2023 advice) [L0251.043]

1. Go to your state's.gov website.
2. Search for "[Your State] resale certificate" or "[Your State] sales tax certificate."
3. Complete the online form (typically 5 minutes).
4. Receive your certificate number.
5. Provide this number to suppliers to purchase at wholesale without sales tax. [L0251.043]

**Procedure: resale certificate** (2025 advice) [L1116.008]

1. Search Google for the registration state plus sales tax certificate or resale certificate.
2. Open the official.gov page of that state.
3. Complete the online form. Many states give a PDF immediately; some mail it, which can take about a week.
4. Get it before supplier outreach, because some suppliers require it and others do not. [L1116.008]

**Procedure: sales tax certificate online** (older advice, 2023) [L0934.006]

- In a state with sales tax, search for the state name plus sales tax certificate or reseller certificate and apply through the state's online form. [L0934.006]

**Procedure: sales tax ID after your EIN** (older advice, 2022) [L1113.050]

- Get a sales tax ID after your EIN, as early as practical, because many suppliers ask for it. The setup is usually done online, and states without sales tax do not need one. [L1113.050]

**Procedure: when a supplier asks for a resale number and the state has no certificate** (older advice, 2022) [L0854.088]

1. Search for an exemption form on the official state government website.
2. If none is listed, call or email the state's contact number and ask for a document stating you are exempt.
3. Send that document to the supplier. [L0854.088]

**Procedure: resale certificate in a state with no registration yet** (older advice, 2022) [L1092.006]

1. Ask the accountant for the exact name of the state sales and use tax form.
2. Search that form name online to check whether it can be filed online.
3. If it is available online, file it there instead of visiting a city or county office. [L1092.006]

#### Multi-state and specific states

- Do not apply for resale permits in every state a supplier asks for if you have no sales nexus there. Offer a W-9 instead, along with your home-state resale certificate. [L0667.080]
- A seller's permit is required in your own home state, and most suppliers will ask for it. You generally do not need a permit in every state, although some suppliers may request one per state. [L1415.009] (older advice, 2021-22)
- A supplier that does not receive a Florida annual resale certificate may offer to charge 7% Florida sales tax on orders shipped within Florida, or may refuse shipments to Florida. Send your home-state resale certificate first and state that you have no economic nexus in Florida. [L0835.136] (2023 advice)
- Resale certificate rules differ by state. A business formed in Delaware may not need a sales tax certificate because Delaware has no sales tax. A Delaware wholesaler license was mentioned as an option for businesses set up there. [L0836.002] (2024 advice)
- For a business in Delaware, the supplier example is handled with a W-9 instead. See the procedure under What to tell suppliers. [L0253.027]

### W-9 forms

#### When and how to use a W-9

- Fill out a W-9 when a supplier asks for one. [L1085.078] (older advice, 2018)
- Give a W-9 if requested, or your sales tax certificate, when purchasing for resale. [L0972.048] (2025 advice)
- Offer a W-9 instead of applying for resale permits in states where you have no nexus, together with your home-state resale certificate. [L0667.080] (2025 advice)
- A D&B (Dun & Bradstreet) number is not necessary for the business. [L1085.078] (older advice, 2018)

**Procedure: handling a W-9 request from a large business customer** (older advice, 2021-22) [L1396.066]

1. Keep a blank Form W-9 PDF in the PDF files section of the corporate information board.
2. When a customer asks for it, print the form.
3. Fill in the business name and the tax information.
4. Send the completed form to the customer.
5. Keep the file ready because large companies request it repeatedly. [L1396.066]

### What to tell suppliers

#### Before you contact suppliers

- Ask each supplier directly what they require before assuming a requirement. [L0359.089, L0468.192]
- Get the EIN before contacting suppliers. The EIN is the minimum to have before contacting suppliers. [L0468.192] (older advice, 2020)
- Get a resale certificate before supplier outreach, since some suppliers require it. [L1116.008]
- Store EIN numbers and resale certificate numbers are needed for supplier applications. D-U-N-S numbers are not required for this model. [L0835.112] (2023 advice)
- Ask which number a supplier requires, a corporate tax number or a VAT number, before submitting the application. [L0360.072]

#### What to give the supplier

- Provide the official LLC name and the business address where the LLC is registered, not a private mailing address. [L0801.148] (older advice, 2021)
- The company name and EIN are entered on supplier approval forms, which usually ask for company details in a one-page form. The company name is not shown to customers. [L0962.120] (older advice, 2021)
- One LLC can run several stores. When calling suppliers, use the store name, and on approval forms list the store names you will sell under alongside the corporation's legal name. [L0853.044] (older advice, 2021)
- Provide a resale certificate for the state where your business operates when a supplier asks for one. [L0470.083] (2023 advice)
- Store EIN numbers and resale certificate numbers are needed for supplier applications. [L0835.112] (2023 advice)

**Procedure: when a supplier asks for a state tax certificate and your state does not issue one** (2024 advice) [L0253.027]

1. Contact the supplier asking for their tax certificate requirement.
2. If they ask for a state tax certificate, start with your business registration state (e.g., Delaware).
3. If your state does not issue a sales tax certificate (e.g., Delaware), provide a W9 form from the IRS instead.
4. Tell the supplier: "I am a Delaware business; please see the attached W9."
5. Ask if the W9 is acceptable.
6. If not, follow up with any documents your state requires. [L0253.027]

### Credit, references and business details

- Suppliers typically request credit and banking information as part of the retailer approval process. Use a business credit card or debit card for authorization rather than bank account details to maintain privacy and control. [L0243.040] (older advice, 2019)
- Leave the business references blank if you are not applying for credit. Use cash or a credit card only, since no credit terms are needed in the dropshipping model. [L0274.046] (2023 advice)
- Drop shipping stores do not require credit for supplier approval. A low credit score caused by past insolvency or bankruptcy does not block working with suppliers. [L0801.141] (older advice, 2021)
- D&B numbers are not required for this model. [L0835.112, L1085.078]

### Supplier agreements and dropship policy

- For high-ticket items ($5,000+), you can request a supplier agreement. Search "dropshipping supplier agreement" on Google or check competitor sites for templates to adapt. The Source A material does not provide standard agreement forms. [L0268.046] (older advice, 2020)
- You do not need your own dropship policy. Suppliers that ask for one are rare, and a simple agreement from a template is enough. [L0666.048] (2024 advice)

*Conflict: [L0268.046] says high-ticket suppliers may ask for a supplier agreement, while [L0666.048] says a dropship policy is rarely requested. Use a template agreement for high-ticket suppliers, and a simple agreement otherwise.* [L0268.046, L0666.048]

### MAP, MSRP and coupons

#### Definitions

- On a supplier price sheet, MSRP is the manufacturer's suggested retail price, while MAP is the minimum advertised price you may list at. [L0470.073] (2023 advice)
- Check the case pack size, since the wholesale cost may cover a set of several units rather than one. [L0470.073] (2023 advice)
- Some markets have no legally enforceable minimum advertised price (MAP), but suppliers may follow unwritten rules, and discounting can reduce their supply. In the UK, undercutting a supplier's price can cause the supply to dry up because of damage to the supplier's reputation with other retailers. [L1441.034]

#### Coupons and MAP compliance

- MAP compliance is judged by the price displayed on the store, not by the price after a coupon. A coupon at checkout is usually allowed, but a displayed price below MAP is a violation unless the supplier agreement permits it. [L1112.057] (older advice, 2021)
- Check the supplier's MAP agreement before offering coupon codes. A 20 dollar coupon on a 200 dollar MAP product is allowed as long as the product page still shows 200 dollars. [L1112.058] (older advice, 2021)

### Conflicts between sources

| Topic | Position A | Position B | When each applies |
|---|---|---|---|
| EIN and sales tax ID | Interchangeable terms when a supplier requests a tax ID. [L0961.017] (older advice, 2020) | Separate: an EIN does not replace a resale certificate ID, and the EIN is free from the IRS while the reseller permit comes from the state. [L1086.055] (older advice, 2019), [L0249.072] (older advice, 2022) | Treat them as separate documents: the EIN is federal, the resale certificate is state-issued. [L0249.072, L1086.055] |
| Business license | Optional for an online store. [L1277.101] (older advice, 2020) | Generally the same as a reseller permit or sales tax ID when a supplier asks for it. [L0971.015] (2024 advice) | Optional for the store itself. If a supplier asks for a business license number, provide the reseller permit or sales tax ID. [L0971.015, L1277.101] |
| Sole proprietor EIN | Not strictly needed. [L1415.006] (older advice, 2021-22) | Get one even as a sole proprietor. [L1277.100] (older advice, 2020) | Get one as the lower-risk option, since suppliers prefer an EIN. [L1415.006, L1277.100, L0272.047] |
| Start point for EIN | Apply after the LLC is approved. [L0118.007] | Can be obtained without an LLC, and suppliers can be contacted before forming one. [L0272.047] (older advice, 2022), [L0852.028] (older advice, 2020) | Apply after the LLC if you have one. Otherwise apply without one, since the EIN is the minimum before supplier contact. [L0118.007, L0272.047, L0468.192] |
| Time for EIN | About 5 minutes. [L0360.069, L0468.146] | About 10 to 15 minutes. [L0359.087, L1274.044, L1344.013] | Plan for 5 to 15 minutes, and apply during IRS business hours. [L1344.014] |
| Canadian sellers | No EIN needed, since it is for US businesses. [L1277.102] (older advice, 2020) | US tax ID probably needed. [L0853.024] (older advice, 2021) EIN may be requested by some suppliers. [L1279.080] (older advice, 2021) | Check with the supplier. Give the EIN if asked, by phone, without a resale certificate first. [L1116.009] |
| High-ticket supplier agreements | Can request a supplier agreement. [L0268.046] (older advice, 2020) | Suppliers asking for a dropship policy are rare. [L0666.048] (2024 advice) | High-ticket suppliers: use a template agreement. Others: a simple agreement is enough. [L0268.046, L0666.048] |
| Non-US EIN route | Non-US residents must call. [L0469.050] (older advice, 2021) | Non-US residents may apply online on the IRS website, with a phone call possibly part of the process. [L0359.087] | Check the foreign applicant route on the IRS EIN page before applying. [L0968.018, L0804.095] |

#### Further details

- The IRS EIN Number page is listed as a resource for obtaining a US Employer Identification Number (EIN) for a US-based business. The lesson gives no further detail. [L0426.004]

- Applying for a seller's permit typically requires your SSN or EIN. It is the first administrative step before finding suppliers. [L1415.010]

## Operating Outside the US: Foreign Founders and Cross-Border Setup

### Decide first whether you need a foreign company

- A business does not need to reside in the country it targets to sell there. Stores have been run successfully by owners living abroad. [L0967.062] (2020, older)
- A seller outside the United States does not need a US company to run a store. Both options have pros and cons, so ask people in forums who have done it in their own country before deciding. [L0551.064] (2020, older)
- Sellers living outside the US can operate under their home-country business details or form a US company, for example through Stripe Atlas. Neither route is confirmed as required for every supplier, so check the choice with sellers who have done it in the same situation. [L0359.114]
- Being a non-US citizen does not block you from selling in the US. Legal obstacles are mostly about setup, not citizenship. [L1277.106] (2020, older)
- Non-residents who are US citizens can run US stores from abroad. One source ran stores from Southeast Asia and South America for years without problems, but check your own tax and legal situation. [L1090.125] (2021, older)
- A business with no US entity can still sell to US suppliers in some cases. The team advises confirming this in the community first and not relying on a general statement. [L0665.091] (2022, older)
- Sellers based outside the US without a US physical presence were reported to run US stores without a US business entity or sales permit. This is an informal view, not legal advice. [L1111.141] (2020, older)
- Do not form a new US business entity if the current foreign entity already sells successfully to US suppliers and customers. Register in the US only when there is a clear benefit. [L0806.087]
- A US entity is only worth forming when the current setup is not already winning supplier approvals and sales. [L0806.100] (2024)
- Selling in other countries does not require registering a company there at the start. Registration becomes relevant when sales in a country approach the local tax threshold (about 80,000 as of 2020) and you must start collecting and reporting tax. [L0664.028] (2020, older)
