- Expanding to a country where the company is not registered may require a local business partner or relocating the company. Check payment provider eligibility before planning the expansion. [L0492.030]
- Opening a US-based business is comparatively simple even for people who are not US citizens. It still has challenges that need separate research before you commit. [L0781.010] (2021-22, older)

### Routes to a US company for a non-US owner

**Route A: keep your home-country business details**

- Sellers living outside the US can operate under their home-country details. [L0359.114]
- A Canadian business selling to US customers can keep operating under its Canadian details. Both approaches are used in practice, so the choice depends on whether the owner wants a US entity. [L0429.084] (2022, older)

**Route B: form a US company through a formation service**

- Stripe Atlas (stripe.com/atlas) sets up a US company, a US bank account, a Stripe account and a legal entity together. It is one option and is not required. [L0359.092]
- Stripe Atlas enables non-US residents to establish US business formation and operations without being physically present in the United States. [L0044.001]
- Stripe Atlas lets non-U.S. residents set up a U.S. company and U.S. bank account without traveling to the United States. This was described for 2018, so check current pricing on the website before you commit. [L1274.050] (2018, older)
- For a store owner who is not based in the US, Stripe Atlas can set up the US company and related business bank setup. It is the recommended route for that situation. [L0360.064] (2024)
- A non-US seller can set up a US company and US bank account through Stripe Atlas. This gives a US business presence without living in the US. [L0852.092] (2020, older)
- Stripe Atlas can set up a US business entity for a founder living abroad and handles the setup steps. Use it as one of the options when you need a US business for suppliers and banking. [L0968.019] (2021, older)
- Stripe Atlas is a paid service that incorporates a company, commonly a Delaware entity, and helps set up its tax ID and banking. It suits founders who want a US company without handling the paperwork alone. [L0675.005] (2025)
- Stripe Atlas incorporates a business in Delaware, opens a bank account and obtains an LLC for a founder, including for those outside the US. It charges a fee for these services. [L0667.082] (2025)
- Stripe Atlas helps non-US residents form a US LLC and set up a Stripe payment account. It is a common route for international students or founders who cannot otherwise form a US company. [L0467.071] (2019, older)
- Stripe Atlas is a company-formation service for setting up a US company, which can then be used to open a Stripe payment account. It is useful for founders who need a US entity to accept card payments. [L0843.001] (2024)
- Stripe Atlas is a service for incorporating a US company and setting up its banking and Stripe payments account. Founders use it when they want a US entity without handling the paperwork alone. [L1095.003] (2024)
- Stripe Atlas can help with EIN, sales tax certificate and nexus setup. Confirm its included services directly with Stripe support. [L0969.032] (2022, older)
- Stripe Atlas was cited as an option for forming a company and opening a banking setup as a non-US founder, but requirements such as a social security number should be checked. [L0671.119, L0671.256] (2021, older)
- Stripe Atlas is an optional incorporation service that some companies offer during setup. You do not need it to form an LLC, get an EIN, open a bank account or connect Stripe. [L0808.059] (2026)
- Comparable company formation services exist for non-US residents. Several providers offer similar packages, so compare offers before paying if the flat fee is a concern, and check community forums for cheaper, trusted options. [L0698.002]
- Other countries may have comparable services, which should be checked before choosing. [L0469.046] (2021, older)
- Stripe Atlas for non-US residents costs a one-time payment of 500 dollars (2022 figure) and includes a Stripe account, a Delaware LLC, a US tax ID number and a business bank account. [L0665.092] (2022, older)
- The current flat fee is $500, per the time of recording. [L0698.001]

**Procedure: flat-fee US LLC through Stripe Atlas**

1. Go to stripe.com/atlas.
2. Pay the one-time flat fee of $500 (current pricing at time of recording). [L0698.001]
3. The service sets up a Delaware LLC as a legal US business entity. [L0698.001]
4. The service connects the entity to Stripe for accepting customer payments, which integrates with Shopify. [L0698.001]
5. The service also opens a US bank account for the business. [L0698.001]
6. Once set up, the business is ready to operate without further formation steps. [L0698.001]

**Entity type and state**

- Stripe Atlas is described as forming a Delaware corporation (2019 source) and as forming a Delaware LLC (2022 and later sources). Confirm which entity type you receive before paying. [L0357.049] (2019, older), [L0698.001, L0665.092]
- Wyoming and Delaware are the two most popular states for forming an LLC. Stripe Atlas can form a Delaware company. [L0806.086]
- Delaware was named as the default state in Stripe Atlas. [L0969.030] (2022, older)
- For non-US nationals forming a US LLC, Stripe Atlas is one option that sets up the entity. [L0969.030] (2022, older)
- A non-US founder can set up a US company (S corp or C corp), obtain an EIN, and get a US business address and phone number through a formation service. Choose the provider by checking reviews and experiences of people who have used it, not a single recommendation. Confirm that the entity type and state fit your plan before paying. [L1086.066] (2019, older)

**Choosing a provider and checking advice**

- Stripe is a trusted payment company that has been used for a decade. For an unanswered question, try the Stripe phone line and support forum. Stripe Atlas-specific advice should come from community members who have used it, not from a coach. [L0805.054] (2024)
- A Stripe Atlas or formation service may ask for a social security number. This is a common hurdle for sellers abroad who need a US bank account. [L1285.078] (2024)
- For sellers abroad needing a US bank account, Mercury bank and Stripe Atlas (which provides a US bank account, with a Delaware entity mentioned) are options. [L1285.078] (2024)

### Choosing the company location

**Step-by-step framework for any country**

1. Look up the business types your country offers. There is usually a choice between operating as yourself and operating as a legal entity. [L0698.011]
2. Search community forums and Facebook groups for your country name to see which entity others have used. [L0698.011]
3. Post a specific question in the community if no answer exists. [L0698.011]
4. Consider a free consultation with a local accountant about which structure fits your business and whether they can handle the books. [L0698.011]
5. Register the chosen entity directly through the official government website. [L0698.011]

**Principles**

- Business entity types differ by country and there is no universal recommendation. The same framework usually offers doing business as yourself or as a legal entity. The legal entity is generally preferred for tax and liability reasons. [L0698.013]
- International tax law is complex and region-dependent with no universal playbook. Hire a lawyer in each target region to provide written advice. This creates their professional liability and binding guidance. [L0041.035]
- Advice on tax for sellers located in another country, selling into the US, should come from an accountant with that situation in mind, not from general store rules. [L0855.126] (2023)
- Check current account approval rules and tax treatment with a local accountant before choosing a location. [L1512.049]

### Location comparison

| Location | Setup cost and time | Banking and payments | Limits and conditions | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| US LLC (general) | About $200 to $300 | Works with Mercury or Brex for a non-resident. [L1440.043] | Mercury holds Wise-linked funds; see the payment chain below. | [L1127.003] |
| US via Stripe Atlas (Delaware) | $500 flat fee (2022 figure); the service includes a Stripe account, US bank account and US tax ID. | Stripe account linked to the entity. [L0665.092] (2022, older) | Price may have changed. Compare with comparable services. | [L0698.001, L0665.092, L0698.002] |
| UK company (LTD) | Around 50 to 100, ready in 5 to 7 days. About $70 in another report. | Stripe UK account opened in 1 to 2 days with UK documents; Wise business account used for banking. | Not advisable above about 5,000 USD per month because of higher taxes and e-commerce taxes. Replace the setup above about 10,000 USD per month. A UK LTD opened with a USD setting and US bank details caused problems (see Common mistakes). | [L1512.020, L1127.003, L1512.021] |
| Hong Kong company | Around 400 to 500, formed in 5 to 7 days. | Strong PayPal and Stripe accounts; Airwallex banking ready in 2 to 3 days. | Tax filing not required for about 12 to 18 months; no maintenance fees reported in that period. First PayPal tax payment due after 18 months. | [L1512.032, L1347.008] |
| Singapore | Not stated in sources. | Considered strong for payment accounts; few online sellers use it compared with Hong Kong. | Check approval rules and tax treatment locally. Gave stronger PayPal processing with few holds. | [L1512.049, L1440.044] |
| Dubai | Investment of about 10,000 to 15,000 USD. | Not stated. | Needs a physical business place or workspace and in-person visits for setup. Worthwhile only for very high volume, roughly above 1 million USD profit per year. | [L1512.037] |
| India | Not stated. | PayPal holds of 21 to 60 days, sometimes 50,000 to 60,000 USD. | 18% tax withheld at source on foreign advertising payments: 1,000 USD of ads costs 180 USD extra. Unsuitable long-term. | [L1440.042, L1440.044] |
| Canada | See the Canada section. | Canadian banks may open accounts for the business. [L0667.083] (2025) | Depends on whether you sell into the US. | [L0667.083, L0429.084] |

### Hong Kong and UK as first steps

- A UK company was described as cheaper to start than a Hong Kong business, so a UK company is the suggested first step. A Hong Kong company with a PayPal account that accepts dropshipping is the fallback if UK restrictions continue. [L1347.007]
- A UK LTD was used as a cheap start, but the cheap option later caused account problems. Compare the total cost of the setup you will need before choosing. [L1127.003]
- Using a Singapore or Hong Kong company for PayPal gave stronger payment processing with few holds, at a dispute rate of about 4 to 5%. [L1440.044]

### Canada

**Options, by source date**

- Registering in Canada is often simpler for a Canadian seller who wants to sell into the US, since a US entity does not automatically unlock more supplier approvals. Shopify can show prices in the customer's currency. [L0671.124, L0671.260] (2021, older)
- A Canadian business selling to US customers can keep operating under its Canadian details or form a US entity. Both are used in practice. [L0429.084] (2022, older)
- Canadian sellers in the US often form a US LLC. A Canadian sole proprietor can register a DBA to show a business name to suppliers. Confirm the best Canadian entity setup with an accountant. [L0471.061] (2024)
- A Canadian founder can register the business in Canada, and Canadian banks may open accounts for it. The EIN is optional at the start, but the business still needs a US address and entity setup if it sells into the US. [L0667.083] (2025)
- You can operate a US-based dropshipping business from Canada selling to US customers if the business is registered in the US and suppliers ship from US warehouses. As of 2025, this should not be affected by current regulations, though tariff and trade laws may change. Confirm with legal or tax advice if uncertain. [L0255.075] (2025)
- A Canadian resident can form a US business, such as in Delaware, and operate it in the US. Whether the reverse arrangement works (operating a US company from Canada) should be checked with an accountant. [L1090.126] (2021, older)
- A Canadian sole proprietor selling only to US customers may be accepted by suppliers with an EIN. Start as a sole proprietor with a business name and an EIN, and check with an accountant before converting to a corporation. [L0667.084] (2025)
- Shopify can run Canada and US sales on one store, with currency conversion through Shopify Payments. A US entity is optional until the US business grows. [L0468.161] (2020, older)
- A business bank account and address for Canadians selling to US customers can be discussed in the Discord North America channel. [L0964.122] (2023)

### Selling in the US from outside it

**Payment and tax-ID requirements**

- For a foreign owner operating in the US, a US EIN (also called FEIN) is needed. Resale certificates are needed in some cases. Ask a US-based accountant or the Discord channel about setup rather than relying on general advice. [L0472.045] (2025)
- Non-US residents who are unsure about US paperwork, such as resale certificates, should get specialist advice. Forming a US LLC was suggested as a route that may simplify that paperwork. [L0466.068] (2018, older)
- A business can be run from Europe with a US LLC and Shopify, using a credit card or debit card linked to a European bank or a US bank account. A US credit card is not strictly required. [L1092.019] (2022, older)
- For a non-US founder who wants a US bank account, a service such as Stripe Atlas can set up the US company and bank account. This is 2020 information, so check current requirements. [L0468.162] (2020, older)
- A US PayPal account for a non-resident business can face long holds of 21 to 60 days, sometimes 50,000 to 60,000 USD. Obtaining an ITIN or SSN to remove these holds took about two to three months, so plan for that delay before scaling payments. [L1440.060]
- A traditional US bank account requires in-person opening at a US branch. That was impractical in 2021 because of travel restrictions. Mercury Bank was then the one option offering a virtual US bank account for non-residents. [L1405.024] (2021-22, older)
- A US company can get a bank account as a non-resident through banks or fintechs such as Mercury or Brex, which helps with payment processing. [L1440.043]

**US address and the card rate**

- The card rate can be lowered by running the store as a US LLC on the Shopify Basic plan, which the lesson gives as 2.9% per transaction. To get that rate you need a US address (a residential address of a contact or a US 3PL fulfillment center address) and a connection willing to lend it. [L1327.011] (2026)
- To establish a US LLC for banking, you need LLC registration, a physical US address, and potentially a representative (a real person) to appear in person at the bank if you are not physically present in the US. [L0324.030]

### Registration address and proof of address

- The Shopify account country and the business address must match the country where the business operates, not the country where the owner lives. For example, an owner living in Japan who runs a US store must enter a US business address and select United States as the country. [L1314.010] (2021-22, older)
- Mixing up the owner's residence country and the business country can cause problems with Shopify Payments, checkout functionality and payouts. Set the account country correctly from the start. [L1314.011] (2021-22, older)
- A formation service can provide a US business address and phone number for a non-US founder. [L1086.066] (2019, older)
- Canadian founders need a US address and entity setup if they sell into the US, even when the business is registered in Canada. [L0667.083] (2025)

### Banking, payment accounts and the owner's identity

**Multi-currency accounts**

- For payment setup, use multi-currency accounts such as Wise or Airwallex to get virtual cards and USD or EUR accounts. Personal bank cards may reveal your location to the ad platform. [L0363.061]
- Airwallex is an online business account used to pay advertising, send international transfers in about 1 day and convert currency at real-time rates. It can be used alongside a Hong Kong company. Its currency conversion was reported as more accurate than Wise. [L1512.033]
- Airwallex transfers from a Hong Kong-linked account were reported to reach a US account in about one day and an Indian account in about 2 to 3 hours. Conversion rates were reported as real-time with low transfer fees. These figures are seller-reported and should be verified. [L1512.051]

**Owner identity on supplier and platform accounts**

- A non-US citizen running a business as a sole proprietor gives suppliers personal details. A US LLC with a US bank account is generally easier for suppliers to approve. [L0671.116, L0671.253] (2021, older)
- A supplier's request for a Social Security number is a common hurdle. [L1285.078] (2024)

**Funding ads from a non-US card**

Procedure: funding Facebook ads from a non-US card with a USD account [L0690.024]

1. Open a US LLC. [L0690.024]
2. Open a Mercury account, which works with Facebook. [L0690.024]
3. Transfer money from the bank to Wise. [L0690.024]
4. Convert there before paying Facebook. [L0690.024]

### Payment processors and payout chain

**Merchant account**

- A merchant account is the payment-processing account a business needs to accept customer card payments. Stripe provides these accounts and connects to Shopify. A foreign owner can use it to take payments from US customers once a US entity is set up. [L0698.014]

**Payout chain for a non-US seller**

Procedure: receiving Stripe payouts as a non-US seller [L1127.001]

1. Open a Stripe account linked to a US LLC. [L1127.001]
2. Move the Stripe balance to a US business bank account such as Mercury or RelayFi, which requires a US LLC. [L1127.001]
3. Transfer the funds from that account to a WISE account. [L1127.001]
4. Convert USD to the local currency in WISE with minimal fees. [L1127.001]

**PayPal**

- Opening PayPal in one country while the company is registered in another, then switching to a different account type, led to repeated permanent bans in one reported case. Pick a single consistent company and country setup before opening PayPal. [L1347.006] (undated, Source C)
- PayPal from India can be held for 21 to 60 days, sometimes with holds of 50,000 to 60,000 USD. [L1440.044]
- A Hong Kong business account for PayPal requires paying taxes, and the first tax payment is due after 18 months. [L1347.008]

### Compliance: Corporate Transparency Act and annual filings

- Check whether Corporate Transparency Act beneficial ownership reporting applies to a US LLC before assuming any deadline. A reporting window of 30 to 90 days was mentioned for LLCs formed in 2024, but it was not confirmed. Verify the current rule with an accountant or an official source. [L0806.101] (2024)
- Each foreign company usually needs an annual filing, which an accountant or a CPA can handle. Plan for filing deadlines, which the coach noted fall in March in his case. [L0363.064]
- If a second company is set up in another country, confirm its tax obligations first. Check whether the local advisors understand the dropshipping model, since some do not. A US LLC is usually formed quickly. [L0693.002]
- Expanding into a country with a local company may need a local business partner or relocation. [L0492.030]
- The US resale-certificate and sales-tax-related setup can be checked with a US-based accountant. [L0472.045, L0969.032]

### Structure and tax cautions

- A coach's company setup presented dropshipping as an export of marketing services to avoid VAT. This applies only to the coach's own India and US setup. Confirm any such structure with a local tax advisor before use, especially in Germany, where invoices must show the company address. [L0363.063]
- In India, an 18% withholding applies to foreign advertising payments, so 1,000 USD of ads costs 180 USD extra. This made the setup unsuitable long-term, and a US company was set up instead. [L1440.042]

### Selling into a country without registering there

- Selling in other countries does not require registering a company there at the start. Registration becomes relevant at the local tax threshold (about 80,000 as of 2020). [L0664.028] (2020, older)
- A business does not need to live in the country it targets to sell there. [L0967.062] (2020, older)
- For selling into the US without a US entity, the source notes conflicting positions: some sellers operate that way (2020, informal), while others say it is possible only in some cases and should be confirmed in the community (2022). Consider both, and check the current position with a local advisor. [L1111.141, L0665.091] (2020 and 2022, older)

### Living abroad while running the store

Procedure: planning before moving abroad for long-term travel [L1441.035]

1. Find a tenant to rent your home. [L1441.035]
2. Research the visa requirements for each country. [L1441.035]
3. Clarify your tax situation with your home government, which may not be set up for long-term travelers. [L1441.035]
4. Book flights only after the above steps are complete. [L1441.035]

- Non-residents who are US citizens can run US stores from abroad, but check your own tax and legal situation. [L1090.125] (2021, older)

### Common mistakes

- A UK LTD opened with a USD currency setting and US bank details led Stripe to ask for tax residency documents before activating the account. Keep the company jurisdiction, currency and bank details consistent when opening a payment account. [L1127.005]
- Mixing up the owner's residence country and the business country can cause problems with Shopify Payments, checkout functionality and payouts. [L1314.011] (2021-22, older)
- Opening PayPal in one country while the company is registered in another led to repeated permanent bans in one reported case. [L1347.006]
- A cheap UK company later caused account problems, so compare total setup cost before choosing. [L1127.003]
- Presenting dropshipping as a marketing-services export to avoid VAT is only the coach's own setup, and it needs local tax advice before use. [L0363.063]

#### Further details

- Stripe Atlas: Service for non-US citizens to form a US LLC and set up a US bank account remotely. [L0270.003]

## Business Address, Phone and Mailing Setup

### Business Address Options

#### Address types compared

- A UPS Store mailbox gives a business address with a suite number (example: 3200 B Cave Road Suite 137) instead of a P.O. Box, which looks more professional to suppliers. Cost is about $320 USD per year (2019 advice, older). [L0243.021]
- Mailbox suites at a UPS Store cost about $100 to $150 per year and keep a home address off the public record (2019 advice, older). [L1069.070]
- A non-US seller who needs a US address can use a UPS Store location set up remotely. The cheapest option is about $20 per quarter, depending on the location (2019 advice, older). [L1086.054]
- A virtual business address is the recommended option over a P.O. box (2021 advice). [L0247.108]
- A virtual mailbox is a real address with a suite number. It receives mail and forwards it or holds it for pickup. A P.O. box can be used for business mail alongside a separate address on the website. [L1092.018]
- Business address options are a P.O. box, a UPS Store-style address with a suite number, or a virtual mailbox. Many LLC formation services also sell an address for a yearly fee. [L1092.017]
- A virtual address from some providers appears as a street address followed by a mailbox number, such as "#406". Use the full address line exactly as the provider lists it on forms and on the website. [L1434.015]
- Mailbox services such as PakMail can provide a business address when a UPS Store does not respond. Check their terms for business address use before signing up. [L0965.070]
- Earth Class Mail receives, scans and forwards a business's physical mail. It can support a US address for a business without a physical office. [L0984.003]
- PakMail receives parcels and letters at a US address and forwards them to the owner. [L0984.004]
- UPS Store addresses provide mail forwarding and a professional business location without maintaining physical premises. [L0270.028]
- A virtual office address in another city, a registered company address, or a correspondence address are all options for account verification. One seller reported about $20 for a virtual address and about $120 per year for a Swiss address service. Whether such addresses are accepted depends on the country and the platform's rules. [L1428.030]

#### Anytime Mailbox (US)

- Anytime Mailbox (anytimemailbox.com) lists US locations by state and city. Browse the state, then pick a city or address and select a plan. [L1434.005]
- Its basic US plan costs US$ 9.99 per month. The site advertises rates starting at US$ 5.99 per month across 1,127 locations. [L1434.005]
- It gives a real street address for a registered business address, with mail forwarding, check deposit, and open-and-scan mail. The lowest-cost plan is enough because the address is not used as a mail service. [L1434.006]

#### Gordon Executive Centre

- Gordon Executive Centre has a one-month notice period after the initial 1, 3 or 6 month term. [L1434.017]
- Renewals are automatic. You are billed again for the same number of months you signed up for. [L1434.017]
- A Gordon business address includes up to five names, such as a company name, a trading name and the names of two principals (four names in this example). [L1434.018]
- Each additional name costs 10 per month, or a block of five names costs 35 per month, plus GST. [L1434.018]

#### Australia

- Companies in Australia are registered federally, not per state. The virtual address can be in any city in the country. It is usually placed in a capital city, such as the Sydney CBD or Brisbane. [L1434.007]
- Australian virtual business addresses typically cost AUD 30 to 40 per month. National providers with offices in every city often cost around AUD 50 per month. These figures are in local currency context and must be verified before use. [L1434.008]
- To find a cheap Australian virtual address:
  1. Search for "virtual mailing address" plus the city name.
  2. Compare the local providers.
  3. Pick one with a location in your own state. This avoids time-zone confusion and customers asking whether you are in the wrong city. [L1434.009]

### Choosing Between Home and Virtual Address

- A home address can also be the business address. Running a business from a non-business premises is acceptable. The choice between home and virtual address is personal. [L1434.011]
- A home address on supplier agreements is acceptable if customers do not send mail to the business. Changing it is optional in that case. [L963.124]
- Do not use a home address as the business registration address. Registrations are published online and can be found by searching the business name. [L1092.016]
- A PO box or business address can be used as the LLC address to keep your home address off documents. [L1113.047]
- Use a virtual business address so your home address does not appear on the store. A mailbox at a UPS Store does this. [L0671.117, L0671.254]
- Use a business address (such as a UPS Store suite address) on your website, supplier forms and documents instead of your home address, for privacy and professionalism. Do not advertise the address as a physical retail location or invite customers to visit. (2019 advice, older.) [L0243.022]
- Digital nomads who need a US business address can choose a tax-friendly state such as Texas, Delaware, Nevada or Florida and use a UPS Store address in that state. (2020 advice, older.) [L967.063]
- In the UK, a company's registered office can be changed at Companies House when a new one is set up. The speaker recommended a dedicated office address rather than a personal address, to keep private information out of public records. [L0552.041]

### Matching Addresses Across Entity, Bank, Shopify and Merchant Center

- Use the virtual address in the business bank account as well if the LLC is linked to it. Keeping them synchronized avoids mismatches. [L963.123]
- Once you have a business address, update your business bank account with it, so the bank records match your business details. [L696.010]
- A UPS Store or virtual address can be the business address for an LLC and for Shopify, as long as the address is consistent across all business documents. A virtual address may cause problems with Google Business Profile. [L971.069]
- Do not use a registered address in a different state or city from where you actually operate. Customers and suppliers may ask questions, and calls may come in at inconvenient hours across time zones. Choose a provider in the same state. [L1434.010]
- In the US, the virtual address should be in the state where the company is registered. A Nevada LLC should use a Nevada address. This avoids supplier questions caused by mismatched registration and postal addresses. [L1434.003]
- If the business operates from outside the US but is based in the US, the company is often formed in a state such as Wyoming. The registered business address should be in that same state. [L1434.004]
- Address and tax settings: a PMB or mail-forwarding address may cause Merchant Center to identify the store as being in a different region. Contact Merchant Center support to correct country settings. (2024 advice.) [L856.082]
- Remove an old mailing address from business accounts once it is no longer used. Replace it with the current business address the store uses. [L857.065]
- An online formation service can form an LLC and provide a virtual address. A mailbox service at a UPS Store can serve as a business address. (2020 advice, older.) [L468.150]
- The address and phone number entered during Shopify sign-up are used only for the Shopify account and billing. Customers do not see them. The phone number can be a mobile or a business number. [L1314.012]
- Google account setup asks for two contact details. A support email with a business address is a common combination. [L1428.031]

### Verification Risks for Payments

- A virtual business address is often not accepted by Shopify Payments or Stripe for verification. Stripe may require a physical address that proves the company exists. Buying a virtual address from an unknown online provider can block payment setup. [L1316.044]
- Shopify may ask for proof of address during payment verification. Before choosing an address service, confirm the provider supplies documents that payment platforms accept. Check this before setting up Shopify Payments or Stripe. [L1316.045]
- Setting up a company in a country (for example the US or UK) and using its address to verify Shopify Payments, Stripe or PayPal is a common path. Check the address type and provider with a qualified professional, because payment verification rules can change. [L1316.046]
- If a supplier rejects a UPS or PO box address as a business address, ask whether the supplier accepts a PO box or a virtual forwarding address. Some suppliers and states may require a registered address. Confirm with a qualified advisor. (2022 advice, older.) [L854.089]

### Google Ads and Website Address Requirements

- A business address shown on the website is needed for suppliers and some advertising platforms. Google Ads requires a visible postal address on the site before it approves advertising. A virtual office address is acceptable if you do not want to use your home address. [L1434.001]
- A virtual address is used only as a registered business and contact address. Expected mail volume is near zero, so the provider's service quality matters little. The address should look professional and exist as a real street address. [L1434.002]
- Pick the most basic plan and sign up before moving on to the next setup step. [L1434.012]
- The full setup procedure:
  1. Decide whether to use the home address or a virtual office address.
  2. If using a virtual address in the US, pick the state where the company is registered and find a location there on anytimemailbox.com.
  3. If in Australia, search for a virtual address provider in your own state, preferably in the capital city.
  4. Select the most basic plan and sign up before moving on to the next setup step. [L1434.012]

### Business Phone Numbers

- Landline or mobile numbers can both be used as business contact numbers on Shopify. There is no preference or requirement. [L245.067]
- Phone numbers on the website build trust without overloading calls. A visible phone number in the header increases conversion rates and buyer confidence, especially for expensive products. [L268.078]
- Most incoming calls are customers checking that you are a legitimate business before ordering, not complaints or rejections. Do not expect to be on the phone all day. [L268.078]
- Phone number alternatives for business addresses:
  - Google Voice and Skype offer inexpensive local or toll-free numbers.
  - Mighty Call provides professional toll-free numbers with a 50 percent discount for the first three months (code provided with partnership deal). (2019 advice, older.) [L243.023]
- A virtual business phone number rings through an app on an existing phone. No second device is needed, and it supports outbound calls. [L1116.011]
- Use a local business address such as a UPS Store address and skip e-fax. A fax number is rarely needed for a dropshipping store. [L1116.010]

### Business Email

- Use a business email for corporate communications and for linking bank accounts and tools, not a personal email. This lets the business be sold cleanly later. [L1090.127]

### Google Workspace and Microsoft Subscriptions

- Enable two-step verification on your Google Workspace account to protect against unauthorized access. [L119.010]
- Google Workspace Business Starter Plan costs $7 per month. [L125.002]
- Google Workspace DNS setup: allow up to 72 hours for DNS propagation. If setup still fails after 72 hours, retry or check DNS settings. [L272.045]
- Microsoft Office subscription costs $19 to $29 per month. Do not buy yearly plans, because you may need to cancel the tool if requirements change. [L218.010]

#### Further details

- Enable two-step verification on your Google Workspace account to protect against unauthorized access. [L0119.010]

- Google Workspace Business Starter Plan costs $7 per month. [L0125.002]

- Microsoft Office subscription costs $19 to $29 per month. Do not purchase yearly plans as you may need to cancel the tool if requirements change. [L0218.010]

- Phone number alternatives for business addresses: Google Voice and Skype offer inexpensive local or toll-free numbers. Mighty Call provides professional toll-free numbers with 50 percent discount for first three months (code provided with partnership deal). [L0243.023]

- Landline or mobile phone numbers can both be used as business contact numbers on Shopify; there is no preference or requirement. [L0245.067]

- Phone number on website establishes trust without overloading calls: Adding a visible phone number in header increases conversion rates and buyer confidence, particularly for expensive products. Most incoming calls are customers verifying you're a legitimate business before placing orders, not complaints or rejections. Don't expect to be on phone all day. [L0268.078]

- Google Workspace DNS setup: allow up to 72 hours for DNS propagation. If setup still fails after 72 hours, retry or check DNS settings. [L0272.045]

- An online formation service can form an LLC and provide a virtual address, and a mailbox service at a UPS Store can serve as a business address. [L0468.150]

- Once you have a business address, update your business bank account with it, so that the bank records match your business details. [L0696.010]

- If a supplier rejects a UPS or PO box address as a business address, ask whether the supplier accepts a PO box or a virtual forwarding address. Some suppliers and states may require a registered address, so confirm with a qualified advisor. [L0854.089]

- Address and tax settings: a business address that is a PMB or mail forwarding may cause Merchant Center to identify the store as being in a different region; contact Merchant Center support to correct country settings. [L0856.082]

- Remove an old mailing address from business accounts once it is no longer used, and replace it with the current business address that the store uses. [L0857.065]

- If an LLC is linked to a virtual address, use that address for the business bank account as well. Keeping them synchronized avoids mismatches. [L0963.123]

- A home address on supplier agreements is acceptable if customers do not send mail to the business. Changing it is optional in that case. [L0963.124]

- Digital nomads who need a US business address can choose a tax-friendly state such as Texas, Delaware, Nevada or Florida and use a UPS Store address in that state. [L0967.063]

- A UPS store or virtual address can be used as the business address for an LLC and Shopify, as long as the address is consistent across all business documents; a virtual address may cause problems with Google Business Profile. [L0971.069]

## Sales Tax, Nexus and Indirect Taxes (US, VAT, GST)

### Nexus: Which States Require Sales Tax

- Sales tax nexus determines which states you must collect and remit sales tax in, based on your business operations and customer locations [L0006.005].
- Nexus is a connection to another state that obliges you to collect sales tax there. It is triggered by meeting that state's threshold, not by simply having a customer in that state [L1344.019].
- Nexus is created by too many local employees, office locations, or other significant business activities. Consult your 3PL and a local tax specialist to avoid unintended tax liability [L0041.034].
- A business with a physical presence in a state (office, employees, or ownership there) can have nexus in that state, even below sales thresholds [L0665.129].
- Sales tax obligations follow where your business entity has a physical presence. Orders shipped into that state then require tax collection. Competitors in no-sales-tax states may look cheaper to buyers in that state, but this alone is not a reason to stop advertising there [L0968.107] (older, 2021).
- Sales tax is based on the shipping address, not the customer's billing address. Collect the tax for the state where the order is delivered [L0972.047].
- Collect sales tax only where you are legally required to collect it, but pay it wherever it applies. Services that claim to offer free sales tax either pay it themselves or operate in states where collection is not required [L1274.051] (older, 2018).
- Collect sales tax only in states where you have crossed the nexus threshold. Sales tax you collect belongs to the state, so you cannot keep tax you were never required to collect [L0358.044] (older, 2020).
- Most states with no nexus require no collection, so competitors not collecting tax may simply be below the threshold [L1281.063] (older, 2022).
- Not charging sales tax in states where the store has no nexus is normal for small online stores. Customers in those states do not expect to pay it [L1114.046].
- Sales tax obligations start only when a store has nexus in a state [L1114.046].
- Sales tax is charged on orders in the state where the business is registered if that state has sales tax, for orders shipped to that state [L0970.139].
- Do not charge tax in a state where the business has no registration or nexus. If you hold a sales tax certificate for one state, only that state's orders need tax collection [L1281.066] (older, 2022).
- Do not assume your home state has no sales tax. Verify its current rules before concluding you owe nothing [L0358.041] (older, 2020).
- Check your business's tax obligations early, before the first sales. Sales tax or VAT rules depend on where the business is registered and where its customers are located [L1007.006].
- Operating in many states creates sales tax nexus obligations. With nexus in 16 states, a business may need to track sales tax across all of them. Review rules regularly, because they change [L0960.063] (older, 2019).
- A business in one state generally only needs to register for sales tax in another state if it has nexus there. Check nexus with an official resource before registering. This is general information, not tax advice [L0666.008] (2024).
- Sales tax is usually charged only in the state where the business is located, because thresholds in other states are high. Some states have no sales tax, and sellers below a state's threshold do not need to report or pay there [L0664.025] (older, 2020).
- Federal income tax is based on overall net profit and does not require state-by-state registration. Register for sales tax only in states where you have nexus [L0971.014].
- A non-US seller with third-party suppliers in other countries usually does not have US nexus and is unlikely to exceed the sales thresholds in the first years. Confirm this with a tax provider for your situation [L0852.093] (older, 2020).
- Whether a non-US company must charge sales tax depends on how the business is structured. Confirm the setup with an accountant before deciding [L0803.071] (older, 2022).
- Each state sets its own nexus rules, so check them before charging tax on orders shipped to that state. Advice from a tax professional is needed before acting [L0470.099] (2023).

### Economic Nexus Thresholds

**Core rule**

- Once sales into a state cross that state's economic nexus threshold, you must collect and remit sales tax there [L0961.019] (older, 2020).
- Thresholds are set per state and are usually based on revenue. Check the current figure for each state where you ship before acting [L0852.091] (older, 2020), [L0358.042] (older, 2020).
- Track revenue by state each year [L0972.049] (2025).
- Most early-stage stores do not reach nexus thresholds, but check the nexus levels as sales grow [L0970.141] (2023).
- In most states, a business under $100,000 in annual revenue has no nexus outside its home state, so it needs a sales tax certificate only in its home state [L0855.119] (2023).
- Thresholds are typically high, so most new sellers need a certificate only in their home state. They register elsewhere when sales there reach that state's thresholds, which usually happens after several years of growth [L1344.021].
- Thresholds differ by state. Only collect and report tax where you are obligated to do so [L0968.021] (older, 2021).

**Threshold figures by source**

| Figure | Source | Year | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| About $100,000 in annual sales, most states | [L0253.010] | 2024 | Most states |
| About $100,000 per state per year | [L0270.043] | 2021 | Typical figure |
| About $100,000 in yearly revenue | [L0276.011] | 2024 | Check each state where you ship substantially |
| About $100,000 per year, once in a single state | [L0972.049] | 2025 | Single-state revenue |
| About $100,000 in a calendar year | [L0852.091] | 2020 | Check each state where you ship |
| About $100,000 in a year | [L0961.019], [L0967.058] | 2020 | Most states |
| About $100,000 in a year | [L0968.021] | 2021 | Differs by state |
| About $100,000 in a year | [L1111.139] | 2020 | Check at taxjar.com |
| More than $100,000 in sales per year | [L1279.075] | 2021 | Common threshold |
| Over $100,000 per year into a state | [L1116.006] | 2025 | Example: New Jersey |
| About $100,000 or about 200 transactions per year | [L0665.093], [L0835.108] | 2022, 2023 | Transaction test |
| About $100,000 or 200 transactions, plus office, employees, or ownership in the state | [L0665.129] | 2022 | Physical presence also counts |
| $80,000 to $150,000, about $100,000 as middle figure | [L0358.042] | 2020 | Approximate |
| $80,000 to $200,000 in most states | [L0834.079] | 2021 | Range |
| $100,000 to $150,000 per state | [L0836.005], [L0970.140] | 2024, 2023 | Range |
| $50,000 to $100,000 in sales in that state | [L0971.034] | 2024 | Range |
| Lowest about $50,000; most states near $100,000 | [L1092.002] | 2022 | Yearly sales |
| About $100,000 in a year; California cited at $500,000 | [L0671.113], [L0671.250] | 2021 | Two sources |
| $300,000 gross revenue in the state AND more than 100 transactions in the last four quarters (New York) | [L1344.020] | Undated | New York only |

- The 200-transaction test appears in [L0665.093], [L0665.129], and [L0835.108]. New York uses a different transaction count (100) together with a revenue threshold of $300,000 [L1344.020].
- The thresholds are approximate and change. Check each state's current figure before acting [L0358.042] (older, 2020).
- Current thresholds and nexus criteria per state are listed in the resource sections of tax tools [L0852.091] (older, 2020), [L1092.003] (older, 2022), [L1111.139] (older, 2020).
- Sales-volume-based nexus in a destination state: the destination state's threshold is checked against sales made into that state, not only against the seller's home state. For example, a seller in one state with customers in New York compares its New York sales with New York's threshold [L1344.022].
- Out-of-state nexus is usually based on sales volume into the other state. Sales into other states are the key figure to track. A business address in the home state creates primary nexus there [L1111.140] (older, 2020).

**Nexus notification procedure**

1. Wait for the sales tax liability notice that Shopify sends for that state [L0972.050] (2025).
2. Obtain a sales tax certificate or permit for that state [L0972.050].
3. Configure the tax settings in Shopify so orders shipping to that state are charged sales tax [L0972.050].
4. Repeat these steps for each state where you reach the threshold [L0972.050].

### Home-State Collection

- Collect sales tax in the state where the business is located [L0247.100] (older, 2021).
- In the US, sales tax generally applies when a product is shipped to a customer in the state where your business is based. You collect this tax from the customer and pass it on to your state [L1344.015].
- The state where your business is registered is where sales tax registration is first needed [L0836.065] (2024).
- Charge sales tax only on orders shipping to a state where the business holds a sales tax certificate, and only to addresses in that state [L0836.003] (2024).
- Charge sales tax on orders shipped to the state where the business is based, and check the rules for each state where orders are delivered. Do not absorb the tax to compete with out-of-state stores [L0667.011] (2025).
- Below nexus thresholds in other states, sales tax is charged on orders shipped to the state where the business is registered [L0967.059] (older, 2020).
- If the home state has sales tax, sales tax applies to orders shipped there. If the home state has no sales tax, this obligation does not apply [L1092.001] (older, 2022).
- A business may owe sales tax in its home state for orders shipped there even if the customer was not charged. Do not skip it to offer discounts. This is general guidance, not tax advice [L0965.066] (2024).
- Collect sales tax only in the state where the business has a physical presence or nexus [L1281.063] (older, 2022).
- Sales tax is shown at checkout, not on the product page, because it depends on where the business is registered and where the customer is. It must not be hidden from the customer [L1281.064] (older, 2022).
- Sales tax charged at checkout is collected for the government and does not reduce your margin. Your wholesale cost stays the same and competitors pay the same tax [L0469.049] (older, 2021).
- Advertising "no sales tax" is allowed only if you are not collecting sales tax on that order. If you collect sales tax in some states, add a note such as an asterisk that explains the exception [L0835.109] (2023).

**Specific states**

- **Texas:** Texas is a state with no income tax, and that point alone is used as an example in [L0270.043] (2021). However, Texas has a state sales tax and requires a sales tax permit. A Texas-based store must collect Texas sales tax on Texas shipments [L0972.051] (2025). The two sources answer different questions: no income tax does not remove the Texas sales tax duty.
- **Nevada:** Nevada levies a statewide sales tax. A Nevada store should check with the Nevada Department of Taxation for a sales tax permit when a supplier asks for a resale ID [L1086.056] (older, 2019).
- **Delaware:** Delaware was reported to have no state sales tax, but this should be verified [L0469.048] (older, 2021). An LLC based in Delaware that sells online usually has no sales tax certificate to obtain there, though a form stating the exemption may exist [L0666.055] (2024). Ask your company formation provider about filing requirements, including any franchise tax [L0469.048] (older, 2021). Sales from Delaware into other states are taxed only where nexus exists there [L0360.073] (2024).
- **Some US states have no sales tax**, so check the rules of the registration state before applying for a certificate [L1116.007] (2025). In a state without sales tax, search the state name with "sales tax exemption" to see whether a form is needed. In most cases no certificate is required [L0963.127] (older, 2022).
- **Maryland:** Maryland requires a sales tax permit under its own rules. Check your state's rules before you register [L0468.163] (older, 2020).
- **Illinois:** Illinois sales tax registration requires a sales tax and use certificate, which you can request from the state revenue department. It is different from your individual 1099 tax form [L0804.094] (2023).
- **Income tax versus sales tax:** Federal income tax is based on overall net profit and does not require state-by-state registration [L0971.014].
- **Multi-state sellers from Delaware or no-sales-tax states:** The sales tax obligations that apply are those tied to nexus in each destination state [L0360.073] (2024), [L0666.055] (2024).

### Sales Tax Certificates, Permits and Resale Certificates

**Certificate basics**

- In the US, each state issues its own seller's license or sales tax certificate. Search your state's name with "seller's license" or "sales tax certificate" [L1275.063] (older, 2019).
- Sales tax permits are issued by each state, not nationally. Some states, such as California, add extra sales tax. Check whether your state requires a permit by searching your state name with "sales tax permit" on your state government's website [L1405.028] (older, 2021-22).
- A sales tax certificate is also called a reseller permit in some states, such as California. The name differs by state, so use a resource that lists the local term for each state [L1344.018].
- A sales tax certificate is required in the state where the business is located. It is advisable to obtain it as the business grows, and it can usually be obtained online as a PDF [L1111.138] (older, 2020).
- Sources differ on timing for the home-state certificate. L0836.065 (2024) says register at the start. L1111.138 (2020) says obtain it as the business grows. Both agree that you should not charge tax before you are registered [L1114.044] (2023).
- A sales tax certificate is required in the state where the business operates. Once you have economic nexus in another state, you need a certificate there as well [L0665.093] (older, 2022).
- The EIN is a federal identifier. You usually do not need a sales tax certificate for every state until your sales volume becomes very large. Check the tax training before registering for each state [L1090.122] (older, 2021).
- When starting a US store, register the business in the owner's home state and obtain that state's sales tax certificate [L0836.065] (2024).
- Do not set up nexus tax collection from day one. Wait until you actually reach nexus before enabling it [L1283.050] (2023).
- Sales tax should be collected only in a state once the store is registered there. This normally happens after nexus is created. Charging tax before registration can create legal risk [L1114.044] (2023).

**Process to check whether you need a certificate**

1. Go to taxjar.com/states [L1344.017].
2. Click the state where your business is located [L1344.017].
3. Read the information on the page about sales tax in that state [L1344.017].
4. Click the link that explains how to register for a sales tax permit, which is provided on every state page [L1344.017].
5. Follow the FAQs there to see whether you need a certificate and how to get one [L1344.017].

**Resale certificates**

- You do not need a resale certificate in every state you ship to. Nexus forms only when you exceed your state's yearly revenue threshold, which varies by state [L0276.010] (2024).
- Most states require a resale certificate once you generate approximately $100,000 in yearly revenue in that state. Check your specific state and any state where you ship substantially [L0276.011] (2024).
- A resale certificate is needed for a state outside the home state once sales into that state exceed about $100,000 per year. Example: a business registered in New York needs a New York certificate from the start and a New Jersey certificate once it sells over $100,000 per year into New Jersey [L1116.006] (2025).
- If a supplier charges you sales tax but you have not reached the sales threshold (nexus) in that state, provide your resale certificate. You should not owe sales tax [L0276.031] (2024).
- If a supplier charges sales tax on a purchase, identify the state charging it, look up that state's minimum nexus threshold, and send the supplier documentation showing the threshold and your current sales in that state [L0358.045] (older, 2020).

**Procedure for supplier-charged sales tax**

1. If a supplier charges you sales tax on a purchase, identify the state that is charging it [L0358.045].
2. Look up that state's minimum nexus threshold [L0358.045].
3. Send the supplier documentation showing the threshold and your current sales in that state [L0358.045].

### Shopify Tax Settings

**Where settings are**

- To set up taxes in Shopify, go to Settings in the bottom-left corner of the admin and open the Taxes section. Shopify's own documentation covers the full steps [L0965.064] (2024).
- In Shopify admin, go to Settings > Taxes and select only the regions where you must collect tax. Tax will not be added for other regions unless you enable it there [L1283.049] (2023).
- Shopify's tax settings let you choose the regions where tax is charged. If orders from a state carry no tax, check these regional tax settings, since the store may not be collecting tax there [L0968.023] (2021).
- Shopify charges sales tax based on two settings working together: the Charge taxes option on each product and the tax rules set up in the Shopify admin taxes settings. The product option alone does not decide when and how much tax is charged [L1114.045] (2023).
- Shopify's built-in sales tax does not usually get charged automatically. The operator must determine liability and register for tax [L0969.031] (older, 2022).
- Shopify collects sales tax automatically once you configure the tax settings, but it does not file the tax returns for you. Use a separate service if you want collection and filing handled [L1274.052] (older, 2018).
- Sources differ on whether Shopify collects automatically. L1274.052 (2018) says it collects once configured. L0969.031 (2022) says it usually does not charge automatically and the operator must determine liability. Work from the liability analysis first, then configure the settings.
- Tax collection can be set up directly in Shopify or with an app such as TaxJar, which can also file the taxes. Compare both options before choosing one [L0356.015] (older, 2019).
- With "Include or exclude tax based on your customer's country" enabled in Tax calculations, checkout uses the local tax rate of the buyer's country rather than the store's own rate [L0677.038] (older, 2021-22).

**Default tax values on products**

- Disable the default tax value on new products (such as an included VAT of 20% or 21%) if you do not charge taxes. A shown tax can cause visitors to leave checkout [L1260.044] (Source C, undated), [L1345.059] (Source C, undated).
- When creating a product in Shopify, check the tax setting and disable the default VAT or tax-included option if no tax is charged. Otherwise a default tax line such as 20% or 21% appears at checkout [L1260.044], [L1345.059].

**Stage-by-stage defaults**

- At the foundation stage no tax entity needs to be configured. Leave Settings > Taxes and duties at default, which uses Shopify tax services with the default taxes for the countries you sell to [L1327.028] (Source D 2026).
- Taxes and duties for shipping are not configured at the start and are handled in a later stage of the build [L1306.018].
- Duties and import taxes, and taxes for each market, are initially set to "Not collecting." Shopify recommends starting collection in every place you sell. Make sure taxes and duties are accounted for in all markets where you sell [L1167.029] (Source D 2026).
- Sources differ on defaults. L1327.028 says to leave the foundation stage at default. L1167.029 says Shopify recommends starting collection in every market. Apply the default at foundation, then configure each market before selling into it.
- On the Shopify setup page, ticking "This store is a registered business" is relevant for tax purposes. The address entered during setup can be changed later [L1314.013] (older, 2021-22).

**Regions and shipping profiles**

- Settings > Taxes lists its tax regions from your shipping profiles. A destination only appears there once it is part of a shipping zone. To add a missing region, add the destination in shipping settings first [L0677.036] (older, 2021-22).
- If you do not sell to other countries, you do not need to set up tax for the "Rest of world" region in Taxes. Only regions where you have a tax obligation need to be collected [L0677.031] (older, 2021-22).
- Shopify calculates "Charge tax on shipping rates" automatically for Canada, the European Union, and the United States. The option includes shipping in the tax calculation [L0677.030] (older, 2021-22).
- On a tax region page in Settings > Taxes, the GST box reads "You're not collecting GST" until you select Collect GST. Below it, Tax overrides lets you set custom tax rates per product collection (Add product override) or per shipping destination (Add shipping override) [L0677.035] (older, 2021-22).

**Checking collected tax**

- Shopify's tax report shows the tax your store has actually collected. Use it to check collected amounts instead of estimating them [L0677.039] (older, 2021-22).

### Remitting Collected Tax

- Sales tax that a store collects from customers is not the store's money. It must be remitted to the state where it was charged [L0965.065] (2024).
- Customers cannot be asked to pay a supplier's tax. The built-in system does not work that way [L0965.065] (2024).
- Sales tax must be collected only where you are legally required to collect it, but it must be paid wherever it applies [L1274.051] (older, 2018).
- Shopify does not file returns for you. A separate service handles filing and remittance if you want that done for you [L1274.052] (older, 2018).
- TaxJar calculates tax, can file it, and remits it to the state on your behalf. It tracks tax and provides a resource library on nexus [L1281.065] (older, 2022).
- TaxJar can automate quarterly sales tax reporting when it is set up through the account. It is treated as an industry standard for this [L0803.060] (older, 2022).
- As of January 2022, TaxJar calculated sales tax for the US, Canada, and VAT, but did not file or report those taxes for Canada or VAT. Check the current scope before relying on it for filing [L0803.059] (older, 2022).
- Once you have a nexus notification in a new state, apply the steps in the nexus notification procedure above [L0972.050] (2025).

**Australia: BAS and remittance**

1. Register for GST [L1405.031] (older, 2021-22).
2. Lodge a BAS (Business Activity Statement) regularly [L1405.031].
3. Remit to the ATO the difference between GST collected from customers and GST paid to suppliers [L1405.031].

### TaxJar and Other Tools

**TaxJar**

- TaxJar is a service with a Shopify app, and it is also usable with other e-commerce stores. It collects and pays sales tax on your behalf, but it is not required to get started [L1344.016] (Source A, undated).
- TaxJar (taxjar.com) is the go-to resource for sales tax management on Shopify, including collecting sales tax for different states. Check its documentation to see whether you need its app [L1110.044] (older, 2019).
- TaxJar is an app that calculates US sales tax for online orders and helps with tax reporting and filing. It is used by ecommerce sellers who need to collect sales tax across several states [L1095.004] (2024).
- TaxJar integrates with Shopify and calculates sales tax, including US sales tax and VAT. Its resources section offers guidance written by accountants [L0803.058] (older, 2022).
- TaxJar offers sales tax and nexus resources and a one-click integration with Shopify for tax collection and filing. Use its resource pages to check current state thresholds and obligations [L0358.043] (older, 2020).
- TaxJar connects to Shopify, calculates the right sales tax, can file it, and publishes state-by-state nexus thresholds [L0671.114], [L0671.251] (older, 2021).
- TaxJar offers a Shopify app that calculates and handles sales tax automatically. Its research section lists state-by-state rules and thresholds for free [L0852.118] (older, 2020).
- TaxJar is a Shopify app that automates sales tax collection, filing, and remittance across states. Its resource section lists the nexus criteria for each state. It is mainly useful once sales cross thresholds in several states [L1092.003] (older, 2022).
- TaxJar calculates and tracks sales tax across states, which is useful when you must collect tax in many states [L0835.111] (2023).
- Before using a sales tax tool, first check where you actually have to charge tax. If you need to collect tax in many states, TaxJar makes state-by-state calculation and tracking easier [L0835.110] (2023).
- Most new stores selling mainly in their home state do not need a multi-state tool. Multi-state tax handling becomes a growth-stage task once order volume spreads across many states [L1092.004] (older, 2022).
- A later statement placed the point where a multi-state tax tool is worth it at roughly 100,000 USD in monthly orders spread across many states that each need a separate certificate. Below that volume, the home-state certificate alone is usually enough [L1092.005] (older, 2022).
- For tax nexus and resale certificates in specific states, ask an accountant. TaxJar resources on nexus can help, but state rules vary [L0803.061] (older, 2022).
- Rules by state should be checked with TaxJar's sales tax resources rather than relying on informal advice [L0834.087] (older, 2021).
- The TaxJar blog has resources on sales tax nexus and how drop shipping affects it. Search the blog for nexus. This is a research source only, not legal or tax advice [L0357.047] (older, 2019).
- TaxJar calculates and collects sales tax, and its Canadian coverage should be checked rather than assuming a single rate applies across Canada [L0665.095] (older, 2022).
- Check the thresholds at taxjar.com before expanding into a new state [L1111.139] (older, 2020).
- Tax Jar can handle nexus when it becomes relevant, after you reach the thresholds in a state [L1283.048] (2023).

**TaxJar collection procedure (Shopify)**

1. Install the TaxJar Shopify app [L1281.065] (older, 2022).
2. Enter your state tax account number [L1281.065].
3. TaxJar calculates and charges the tax on orders shipped to that state [L1281.065].
4. TaxJar files and remits the tax to the state on your behalf. The app also tracks tax and provides a resource library on nexus [L1281.065].

**Advice sources**

- For country-specific tax rules, look for articles published by accountants or lawyers who work in that country, since many publish guidance to attract clients. Do not rely on message boards or rumours for tax advice [L0356.016] (older, 2019).
- The Source C course treats tax advice as outside a non-accountant's remit. Consult a tax accountant before acting [L0552.042] (Source C, undated).
- For tax advice on Europe, check with a local accountant before selling there [L0773.051] (Source C, undated), [L0774.049] (Source C, undated).
- Before you charge tax on orders shipped to a state, check that state's nexus rules or get tax advice [L0470.099] (2023).
- Sales tax settings and state rules should be confirmed with an accountant before relying on them. This applies to the home-state no-nexus experience in [L0934.007] and [L0934.141] (2023).

### Registration Timing and Build Sequence

- Most early-stage stores do not reach nexus thresholds. Check nexus levels as sales grow [L0970.141] (2023).
- Register for sales tax only in states where you have nexus [L0971.014] (2024).
- Register for sales tax permits only in states where sales exceed the economic threshold or where the business has employees. Registering in all 50 states is not necessary [L0247.101] (older, 2021).
- For a US store, in the first stage: register in the home state, obtain its certificate, and collect tax on home-state orders only [L0836.065] (2024), [L0967.059] (older, 2020), [L1281.066] (older, 2022).
- Multi-state tools become relevant as order volume spreads across states [L1092.004] (older, 2022), [L1092.005] (older, 2022).
- Competitors in no-sales-tax states may look cheaper. Tax obligations still follow the rules above, and that is not a reason to stop advertising in those states [L0968.107] (older, 2021).
- Sales tax obligations depend on the state where your business is based and on economic nexus in other states. Verify current rules before concluding that you owe nothing [L0358.041] (older, 2020).
- Sales-tax registration is tied to nexus. Register in the state where the business is based and in states where you have nexus. For orders shipped to states without nexus, no registration was needed in the lesson's experience. Verify nexus rules with an accountant before relying on this [L0934.007], [L0934.141] (2023).

### Canada

- For a Canadian seller, sales tax rates differ by province. Use the ecommerce business's own taxing rules, and check TaxJar's Canadian coverage rather than relying on general statements [L0665.095] (older, 2022).
- Canadian sellers must check sales tax per province, because rates differ. For example, Alberta's sales tax is 5 percent. Confirm province-level rules with TaxJar's Canadian coverage instead of assuming one rate applies across Canada [L0665.130] (older, 2022).
- A Canadian business shipping to US customers does not charge US sales tax [L0964.121] (2023).
- Canadian sellers who are not charging sales tax to US customers should set their tax rates to zero in Shopify and Merchant Center to match competitor prices in Google Shopping [L0964.118] (2023).
- TaxJar's Canadian calculation was reported as not filed as of January 2022 [L0803.059] (older, 2022).

### Australia: GST

**Registration threshold**

- In Australia, a new business does not have to register for GST until its revenue reaches $75,000 or more in a single financial year. Before that it does not have to charge GST on sales [L0677.006] (older, 2021-22).
- GST sales tax registration is not required for a new business until revenue reaches 75,000 Australian dollars in a single financial year. A business already GST-registered under an existing structure may continue as it is [L0702.023] (older, 2021-22).
- GST registration only becomes mandatory once revenue in a single financial year reaches $75,000. Revenue from several businesses under the same legal entity is added together toward this threshold [L1405.030] (older, 2021-22).
- A new business that has not made any sales does not need to register for GST yet. Registration can be done later, either directly or with an accountant, once it becomes relevant [L0702.024] (older, 2021-22).
- In Australia you need at least an ABN from the start. GST registration is not required straight away for a sole trader. Registering early makes you collect and remit GST on every sale [L1405.029] (older, 2021-22).

**Obligations once registered**

- In Australia, once a business is registered for GST it must collect GST on sales. Not collecting it when registered is illegal [L0677.027] (older, 2021-22).
- In Australia, retail prices must include tax. Shopify's setting "All prices include tax" must be ticked for this [L0677.029] (older, 2021-22).

**ABN and Shopify billing**

- A business registered for GST should add its Australian Business Number under General settings (Add ABN) so Shopify does not charge GST on its own subscription invoices. In the US the equivalent federal number is the EIN [L0677.005] (older, 2021-22).
- In Australia, Shopify charges 10% GST on invoices. Adding an Australian Business Number (ABN) under Settings > General exempts invoices if you are GST registered [L1145.020] (older, 2021-22).
- For a store based in Australia, Shopify may show a missing-tax notice. A GST-registered merchant can enter an ABN so that Shopify bills are exempt from GST [L1166.032] (older, 2021-22).
- For a store based in Australia, Shopify bills are exempt from GST if the business is GST registered and enters its ABN in the tax settings. Without the ABN, the store will see a notice that tax information is missing [L1300.030] (older, 2021-22).

**Shopify GST setup procedure**

1. Go to Settings > Taxes and open the Australia tax region [L0677.028] (older, 2021-22).
2. Click Set up [L0677.028].
3. Click Collect GST [L0677.028].
4. Enter your ABN [L0677.028].
5. Click GST; Shopify then collects 10% GST on each order [L0677.028].
6. Under Tax calculations, tick "All prices include tax" [L0677.028].
7. Click Save [L0677.028].

**Australia: Process once threshold is reached**

1. Register for GST [L1405.031] (older, 2021-22).
2. Lodge a BAS (Business Activity Statement) regularly [L1405.031].
3. Remit to the ATO the difference between GST collected from customers and GST paid to suppliers [L1405.031].

### UK and EU VAT

**Registration and thresholds**

| Figure | Source | Year | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| About £80,000 (local currency), registration only after crossing | [L0664.027] | 2020 | Current rule must be checked |
| £80,000 to £100,000 discussed | [L0962.118] | 2021 | Discussion figure |
| £50,000 (later follow-up) | [L0962.118] | 2021 | Follow-up figure |
| Some European authorities issue a VAT number only after a sales threshold of 30k | [L0360.071] | 2024 | Varies by country |
| UK threshold uncertain: roughly 50,000 or 80,000 | [L0247.104] | 2021 | Not verified |

- The UK VAT threshold was discussed in several figures. Confirm the current threshold with HMRC before relying on any of them [L0962.118] (older, 2021), [L0247.104] (older, 2021).
- For the EU and UK, registration is required only after crossing the threshold, so it is not needed from day one. The current rule must be checked [L0664.027] (older, 2020).
- The UK VAT registration is required once gross income reaches the VAT threshold, not based on a forecast of income. Confirm this with a UK tax advisor before relying on it [L0934.012], [L0934.138] (2023).
- UK VAT registration may be needed once annual sales cross the threshold. Confirm the current threshold with HMRC before relying on either figure [L0962.118] (older, 2021).
- Selling into the UK may require VAT registration even if you do not sell there. Check the rule for your country before selling, and be careful about back-dated VAT [L0950.071] (Source C, undated).
- Some European tax authorities only issue a VAT number after a sales threshold of 30k is reached. Suppliers that ask for a VAT number can be told the planned launch date as the reason it does not exist yet [L0360.071] (2024).
- If suppliers demand VAT registration before approval, register early, since registration takes only a few days. Tell suppliers the registration is on its way and send the VAT number once received [L0962.119] (older, 2021).

**International expansion**

- International expansion requires VAT numbers (EU/UK) and EORI numbers for importing products. The source writes "EORE", which refers to EORI. Consult a local tax specialist familiar with e-commerce in each target market [L0041.033] (Source E 2025-26).
- VAT and tax for selling in Europe depend on the country where the seller lives and operates. Get advice from a local accountant before selling in Europe [L0773.051] (Source C, undated).
- VAT and tax rules depend on the country you live in and the countries you sell to. Check with a local accountant before selling in Europe or any other region [L0774.049] (Source C, undated).
- Tax is based on the country you sell to, not always the country where you live. Use common sense and check locally when unsure [L0677.008] (older, 2021-22).
- Sales tax rules depend on the country and, in some countries, the state where you sell. The US charges sales tax state by state only where the business is obligated. Australia uses one federal GST, and the EU uses VAT. Check local rules with an accountant or a local tax expert [L0677.007] (older, 2021-22).

**Shopify VAT settings**

- Settings > Taxes > Tax calculations: the "Charge VAT on digital goods" option creates a collection. Digital products added to that collection have VAT applied at checkout for European customers [L0677.037] (older, 2021-22).
- Ads taxes in the UK and EU were flagged as a risk in the Source C lesson. Consult a tax accountant before acting [L0552.042] (Source C, undated).

### Tax on Ad Spend

**Singapore GST on ad spend**

- Singapore GST on ad spend equals 9% of the spend converted into SGD. The speaker's example of $100k USD gave S$9,000 in GST. At about S$1.30 per USD, $100k is about S$130k, and the GST would be about S$11,700. Verify the conversion before budgeting [L0351.028] (Source D 2026).
- When estimating Singapore GST on USD ad spend, convert the spend to SGD at the current exchange rate first, then apply 9%. Treating USD 100k as S$100k understates the taxable base [L0351.050] (Source D 2026).

**Other countries**

- Ad spend in some other countries carries VAT rates higher than Singapore's 9% GST. The total tax on scaling spend must be included in the budget [L0351.029] (Source D 2026).
- Ads taxes in the UK and EU were flagged as a risk in the Source C lesson [L0552.042] (Source C, undated).

**Sales tax on ad spend in the US**

- Sales tax obligations for ad spend are not separate from nexus. Ad spend in a state can be a factor, so check the nexus rules for your situation [L0357.046] (older, 2019).

### Tariffs and Duties

- Shopify has no native tariff option. A third-party app such as Magical Fees and Tariffs can add tariffs at checkout, or shipping profiles can carry tariff charges for a specific brand [L1116.034] (2025).
- Duties and import taxes are initially set to "Not collecting" in Shopify. Shopify recommends starting collection in every place you sell [L1167.029] (Source D 2026).
- Importing products into the EU or UK requires EORI numbers [L0041.033] (Source E 2025-26).

### Checklist for Operators

- Identify your home state, check its sales tax rules, and register there before charging tax [L0836.065] (2024), [L1092.001] (older, 2022).
- Track revenue by state each year and check each state's nexus threshold as sales grow [L0972.049] (2025), [L0970.141] (2023).
- Do not charge tax in states without registration or nexus [L1281.066] (older, 2022), [L1114.044] (2023).
- Enable tax collection only in the regions where you must collect it, in the Shopify Taxes settings [L1283.049] (2023).
- Check the default tax value on every new product [L1260.044], [L1345.059] (Source C, undated).
- Verify all thresholds, VAT rules, and GST rules with an official source or a local tax professional before acting [L0664.027] (older, 2020), [L0962.118] (older, 2021), [L0666.008] (2024).

#### Further details

- Sales tax nexus is typically triggered at around $100,000 in sales per year per state. Collect sales tax only where you have nexus, and avoid collecting or paying it in states where you have none. [L0469.047]

- Sales tax applies in the state where the business is registered if that state has sales tax, for orders shipped to addresses in that state. Not every US state has sales tax. [L0806.084]

- For most states, economic nexus (a tax obligation in another state) starts when sales in that state exceed 100,000 dollars in a year. Check each state's rules with an accountant. [L0806.085]

- Before any state reaches its economic nexus threshold, a store charges sales tax only on orders shipping to the state where it is registered and holds a sales tax certificate. Each other state is added only when the store reaches that state's threshold. [L0972.098]

## Income Tax, Salary vs Distributions and Tax Planning

### Structure choice and how profit is taxed

- Sole proprietorships, LLCs and S-Corps differ in their tax obligations, and each carries distinct business and personal tax implications. [L0006.002]
- Under a sole proprietorship, business profit is added to personal income such as a job or investment income and taxed at the personal rate. Several income streams can push the owner into a higher tax bracket. (Older advice, 2021-22 source.) [L0702.006]
- In a company, profit is taxed at company level before money is paid to the owner. Outside the US, company tax rates are usually much lower than personal income tax rates, which can make a company the better option for some owners. (Older advice, 2021-22 source.) [L0702.007]
- No fixed tax percentage exists. Your liability depends on business structure (sole proprietorship, LLC, S-corp), state location, business income, personal income and deductions. Consult the business-tax module or an accountant for your situation. [L0251.051]
- In the US you pay tax on profit, not on sales. The rate depends on the business entity, because a sole proprietor and an S-corp paying salary and distributions are taxed differently. [L0941.087]
- Taxable income for a sole proprietor is business profit minus deductions. Example: $100,000 profit minus deductions is counted as income and taxed at the owner's tax bracket. [L0941.088]
- Net profit from an online store is taxable like any business income, and the business structure affects how much tax is owed. Ask a tax professional about structuring options before relying on them. [L0759.032]
- Dropshipping income is taxed like any other income. Personal income tax depends on tax bracket and state, plus federal rates. Consult an accountant for the exact setup. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0803.070]
- Income tax depends on business structure and any other income, so there is no single answer for every seller. (Older advice, 2019.) In past experience a sole proprietor setup did not block supplier approvals, and choosing a legal entity for liability is the owner's decision. [L1110.045]

### Pass-through treatment (US)

- An LLC by itself does not reduce income tax in the United States. Profit passes through to the owner's personal return and is taxed at the owner's normal income tax rate, the same as a sole proprietorship. Only liability protection changes. [L0703.013]
