- An LLC or S-Corp is a pass-through entity. The business still files its own return, but profit is taxed on the owner's personal return and not at business level. The owner pays tax through the personal return either way. [L1344.002]
- Example of pass-through taxation: a sole proprietorship or plain pass-through LLC with $1,000,000 in sales and $200,000 net profit adds the full $200,000 to the owner's personal return, taxed at the owner's normal rate. Net profit is the money left after all expenses, such as advertising, before income taxes. [L0703.006]
- If the business is taxed as an S-corp, profit and losses flow through to your personal return, so pre-launch expenses can offset other income. Check the exact situation with an accountant. (Older advice, 2022.) [L1113.049]
- An LLC can elect to be taxed as an S-corporation through a tax election form. Under an S-corporation you pay yourself a salary and take the remaining profit as distributions. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0801.147]
- Once the business is established, consider forming it as an LLC and electing S-corp tax treatment, which can be a significant benefit. Discuss the specifics with an accountant. [L1283.052]

#### Procedure: form an LLC and elect S-corp treatment (US)

1. Form an LLC for the store. [L0703.005]
2. File the form that elects S-corporation tax treatment for that LLC, typically with help from an accountant. [L0703.005]
3. As the only working owner, set a reasonable salary for the work performed and run it through payroll, which is taxed at the owner's normal income tax rate. [L0703.005]
4. Take the remaining net profit as distributions to the owner rather than as salary. [L0703.005]

### S-corp salary and distributions (US)

#### How the split works

- The S-corp benefit applies only to profit paid out as distributions, not to the owner's salary. The advantage grows as net profit rises above the salary level. The benefit becomes meaningful once the business is consistently profitable, not in the first months. [L0703.010]
- S-corp distributions still flow onto the owner's personal return and remain subject to income tax. The estimated saving applies only to the Social Security and Medicare payroll tax that would otherwise be charged on the salary portion, not to income tax on the whole profit. [L0703.014]
- Distributions are not subject to Social Security or Medicare tax and can be taken at any frequency, such as monthly or quarterly. Profit is usually taken out as distributions rather than salary. [L0835.106]
- Once the S-corp is formed, pay yourself through payroll or automatic payments as an employee. Social Security and Medicare tax (about 15.3 percent on the year cited) applies to salary but not to distributions, which is why the structure can lower tax. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0803.069]
- Taxes for an S-corp separate salary and distributions. If the business is not taxed as an S-corp, consult an accountant to clarify how payouts are classified. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0801.149]
- The S-corp salary and distribution split can reduce the total tax burden, but the salary must be reasonable, so set it with an accountant. [L1344.003]

#### Reasonable salary and audit risk

- In the United States, the IRS expects an S-corp owner who runs the business to pay a reasonable salary for the work done, and an audit can check this. The salary must be a defensible amount for the actual workload, not an arbitrary minimum. [L0703.008]
- Setting the salary very low to push more profit into distributions is risky in the US. The IRS expects a reasonable salary for the actual work, so an audit could reclassify distributions as wages and cancel the payroll tax saving. [L0703.015]
- Only pay a salary if the business can still cover its expenses and keep cash accumulating. Take distributions when you want, throughout the year, and let the accountant reconcile them at year end. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0803.066]
- Do not pay yourself a salary from day one, because the business should first prove what it can earn. Distributions can be taken whenever you want, but keep money in the business when a big initiative needs investment. [L0835.107]
- Pay a salary only after the business has about three months of history showing consistent monthly profit. Salaries of around $500 or $1,000 per week are examples of what can be paid. [L0835.105]
- Taking small distributions regularly keeps cash flow consistent. Larger distributions can be taken quarterly or annually, based on monthly goals. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0803.068]

#### Worked examples

- **Example A, part-time owner.** An S-corp owner who works about 10 to 15 hours a week on the store might pay themselves a $50,000 salary, taxed at their normal income tax rate. The remaining $150,000 of a $200,000 profit is paid out as a distribution, which avoids the Social Security and Medicare tax that applies to salary. [L0703.007]
- **Example B, round figures (not tax advice).** With net profit of $200,000 for the year, a salary of $2,000 per week equals $104,000 over 52 weeks. The remaining $96,000 of profit is taken as distributions, which generally carry lower tax than salary. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0803.065]
- **Example C, $1 million profit.** A business earning $1 million profit and paying the owner a $100,000 salary leaves $900,000 that passes through as a K-1 distribution. The quoted saving on that portion is around 15.3% self-employment tax, compared with an LLC taxed on the full $1 million. Verify the figures with an accountant. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0671.110, L0671.247]

#### Procedure: pay the owner from an S-corp (US)

1. Pay yourself a small minimum salary, for example $500 to $2,000 a month, on a regular schedule. [L1281.074]
2. Take the remaining profit as a distribution, monthly or quarterly. [L1281.074]
3. Keep business and personal funds separate instead of mixing them. [L1281.074]

The salary is subject to payroll taxes, while distributions avoid the extra tax of about 15.7% on the salary. (Older advice, 2022.) [L1281.074]

#### Estimating the saving

- Estimated Social Security and Medicare savings (US only) = distribution amount × about 15%. Example: a $150,000 distribution gives an estimated saving of about $22,500 per year, and the saving grows as profit rises. [L0703.009]
- The rules in this section (LLC, S-corp, reasonable salary and the roughly 15% Social Security and Medicare saving) apply to the United States only. Other countries have different structures and rules. Before choosing a structure, confirm the decision with a licensed accountant or attorney who knows the owner's jurisdiction. [L0703.011]

#### Figures that differ between sources

| Figure | Value | Source | When it applies |
|---|---|---|---|
| Estimated saving on distributions (planning estimate) | about 15% of distribution | L0703.009 | Rough planning estimate for the Social Security and Medicare saving |
| Social Security and Medicare rate on salary | about 15.3% | L0803.069 (older, 2022); L0671.110, L0671.247 (older, 2021) | As quoted in those units for the year cited |
| Extra tax avoided on the salary | about 15.7% | L1281.074 (older, 2022) | As quoted in that procedure |

Use the rate your accountant confirms for your year and situation. [L0703.009, L0803.069, L0671.110, L0671.247, L1281.074]

### Tax reserves and cash discipline

*(All units in this group are older advice, 2021-22 source.)*

- Set aside a fixed tax percentage at every allocation, for example 10% of real revenue, so that a tax bill can be paid in full when it is due. This matters most after a high-revenue month, because that month's gains can be consumed by taxes. [L1200.019]
- Treating a record sales month as spendable cash without setting aside a tax share can leave nothing to pay the tax bill. Plan the tax reserve before spending any part of a strong month. [L1200.020]
- Case: in 2018 the owner had a long revenue decline, then one record sales month in March that felt like a turnaround. No tax had been planned for. When taxes were paid in April, the entire gain from that March sales month went to the government, and possibly more. Because the owner had not saved or planned for the tax bill, the one-off month left the business in a worse position. [L1200.021]

### Deductible expenses and income classification

- In the US, mentorship, mastermind and course costs for growing a business are often treated as deductible business expenses. Confirm the treatment with a CPA before relying on it. (Older advice, 2022.) [L1281.073]
- Run all real business expenses through the business account, have an accountant check that every deductible cost is written off, and reinvest extra profit where it can grow the business. Track every expense from the start so it can be deducted. [L1344.008]
- Reinvesting profit is a legitimate way to reduce taxable profit. Large companies often appear to pay less tax because they reinvest profit in growth, which tax rules allow and encourage. [L1344.004]
- Home-based operation: write-offs include a proportional share of rent or mortgage based on the area used for the business, plus product costs and advertising. Full rent or mortgage cannot be written off. A CPA should categorize the expenses. [L1283.051]
- Cash back from the Capital One card is described by the issuer as a rebate rather than income, so the issuer would not send a tax form reporting it. This is the issuer's position. Confirm it with a qualified accountant in the owner's country before relying on it. (Older advice, 2021-22 source.) [L0903.021, L0903.029]

### Tax-advantaged jurisdictions and where to register

- Register the business in the simplest place you already live while profits are small. Once profit creates a large tax bill (around $40,000 per year in one example), invest effort in moving the personal address or structuring the business. [L1515.017]
- Register the business where you live at first. Once it starts making profit, research countries or US states with lower tax and consider moving the business there, since the tax burden can be reduced significantly this way. [L0827.007]
- A Hong Kong company can be registered as an offshore, tax-free entity once net profit reaches about $100,000 to $200,000 USD. Specialist providers offer this for roughly $100 to $300 USD. The benefit depends on the structure and must be checked with an accountant. [L1512.031]
- Australian structure, as a procedure:
  1. Invest funds through a trust structure. [L0041.017]
  2. The trust invests into a holding company. [L0041.017]
  3. The holding company owns both a trading company (which handles all sales and bears operational liability) and an IP company (which holds intellectual property and remains saleable if the trading company fails). [L0041.017]
  4. The holding company receives dividends from the trading company, protecting cash reserves at a lower tax rate. [L0041.017]

  This structure separates liability, protects assets and optimizes tax. For the US, consult local tax specialists. [L0041.017]

### Residency planning and relocation

- Moving the personal residence from California (about 8% state income tax) to Texas (0% state income tax) eliminates state income tax, but federal taxes still apply. [L1515.014]
- Choosing a state with no state income tax, such as Texas in the lesson's example, can reduce tax separately from the foreign income exclusion. Plan residence changes with a tax professional. [L1126.025]
- One US business owner paid about 40% or more of income in combined federal and state income tax while living in California. After relocating the personal residence and working location-independently, the total tax burden dropped to about 5% of income. Results vary by residence, visa status and business structure, so check against your own situation. [L1515.045]
- Combined top personal income tax in California was given as around 52% (older advice, 2022). This applies to the owner's total income, not to dropshipping as a separate category, and lower rates are possible in other states. Confirm the exact rate and structure with an accountant. [L0803.101]
- Residency by investment: Ukraine offered a residency option requiring about $100,000 in 2021, while comparable programs in other European countries were often in the range of $300,000 to $500,000. These thresholds were recorded before 2022 (older), so check current requirements before relying on them. [L0863.013]
- Thailand: foreigners can legally buy a condo, but staying there still depends on a tourist visa or visa runs, so ownership does not provide residency or tax status. Furnished short-term rentals were cheap and easy to find in 2018 (older), so buying usually only makes sense where short-term rental is hard to get. [L1126.045]
- Citizenship by investment or a second passport used for tax planning is a large-scale tool mainly used by big companies with professional advisors. Individuals should not assume they can copy it. US citizens still owe US tax on worldwide income even with a second passport. [L1126.047]
- Offshore company setups, such as the Belize example where the owner works for their own foreign company, are complex and need professional setup. [L1126.026]

### Foreign Earned Income Exclusion (FEIE)

- US citizens living abroad may exclude earned income from federal income tax. Self-employment tax still applies, and the exclusion typically requires long physical presence outside the US. Confirm current qualification rules with a tax professional before relying on it. [L1515.015, L1126.024]
- The two sources give different exclusion limits. Use the limit confirmed for the current year.

| Item | Value | Source | When it applies |
|---|---|---|---|
| Exclusion limit | $104,000 | L1126.024 (also quoted in L1126.026) | As stated in that lesson |
| Exclusion limit | $105,000 | L1515.015 | As stated in that unit |
| Presence test (US citizens) | about 11 months, roughly 330 days, outside the US per year | L1126.024, L1126.046, L1515.015 | US citizens claiming the exclusion |
| Presence test (some other countries) | reportedly about six months of absence | L1126.046 | Check the exact test for your passport and residence |

[L1126.024, L1126.026, L1126.046, L1515.015]

- The tax benefits of living abroad apply to citizens of many countries, not only the US. Check the exact test for your passport and residence. [L1126.046]
- The lesson's claim that pay up to $104,000 is tax-free through an offshore company is not a reliable rule for US citizens. Get expert advice first. [L1126.026]

### Lawyers, accountants and specialists

- Hire a creative tax lawyer, not an accountant. Accountants check boxes in black and white, while lawyers help you think creatively within tax law to minimize your burden. [L0041.018]
- Set the S-corp salary with an accountant, and have an accountant reconcile distributions at year end and categorize expenses. [L1344.003, L0803.066, L1283.051]
- Non-US owners of a US LLC can learn the basics of US tax obligations from CPAs who specialize in working with foreign owners. Use their public educational content as a starting point, then confirm your own situation with a qualified professional. [L1127.039]

### Family and asset protection

- Tax planning must also consider family protection and asset protection. Trust structures vary if you have dependents, a partner you don't trust, or other personal liabilities. Consult a tax lawyer on your full situation. [L0041.019]
- In the Australian structure, the IP company holds intellectual property and remains saleable if the trading company fails, which separates liability and protects assets. [L0041.017]

### Cross-border tax obligations

- US citizens owe US federal income tax on worldwide income, no matter where they live or which company earns the money. Moving abroad or using a foreign company does not remove that obligation. [L1126.023]
- Citizenship by investment and second passports do not remove the US tax obligation on worldwide income. [L1126.047]
- Profit received abroad and spent at home is still taxable income in the home country. A basic company at home, such as a sole trader or sole proprietorship, lets business expenses be deducted, which can reduce income tax on that profit. [L1512.017]
- Money sent from an offshore company to a personal home bank account must be documented for the tax authority, so the origin of the funds has to be explainable. A basic company at home remains useful even when an offshore company handles payments. [L1512.018]
- Rules for LLCs, S-corps and the reasonable salary apply to the United States only. Confirm the decision in the owner's jurisdiction with a licensed accountant or attorney. [L0703.011]

## Accountants, Lawyers, Bookkeeping and Records

### Scope of Course Advice

- The course material is general and based on experience, not professional advice. Outside the US, personal circumstances such as other businesses, investments or unusual tax situations can change the answer, so an accountant or qualified adviser must be consulted. [L0702.001]
- Course guidance is not legal or tax advice. Tax and legal matters must be confirmed by a licensed accountant or attorney. [L0969.108]
- Do not treat course advice as legal or tax advice. Get guidance from a qualified professional, such as a local accountant, before setting up multiple business names (DBAs). [L1002.067]
- Use course material only as a starting point for understanding tax topics. [L0965.067]
- Tax questions go to a professional, not to a mentor. [L1469.040]
- Ask an accountant before deciding how to report unusual sales structures, such as gift card promotions, DBA setups or multiple businesses, because accounting decisions depend on the specific business and laws. [L1090.129]

### When to Hire an Accountant

- Hiring an accountant is optional. Many people do their own taxes at first and hire an accountant once revenue arrives. [L0671.121, L0671.258]
- Older (2024 advice, no hire needed at start): You do not need a bookkeeper, accountant, or insurance when starting a business. A resale certificate is required only upon reaching your state's nexus threshold. Delaware (where Stripe Atlas incorporates companies) has no minimum income tax requirement, though verify any small filing fees. [L0276.051]
- Hire an accountant as soon as your dropship store becomes profitable to guide you through tax strategy and compliance. [L0006.001]
- Hire an accountant as soon as the first store becomes profitable. Paying a professional for tax work is usually worth it at that stage. [L1344.009]
- Once a store has consistent sales, hiring an accountant is usually the best way to handle taxes and gain peace of mind. [L0965.067]
- Find an accountant once the business is earning money, and speak to one before choosing a business structure, because the best entity type depends on the country and the owner's situation. [L0853.120, L0698.012]
- Older (2019): Paid professional advice can wait until the business is earning revenue, while free initial consultations are used to compare options. [L0663.076]
- Older (2019): Most good professionals offer a free initial consultation, which is worth using before you have revenue. [L0663.059]
- Older (2019): Lawyers and accountants often offer a free initial consultation. [L0663.076]
- Keep a list of business expenses, including formation and legal fees, because these may be deductible business expenses. Confirm the treatment with an accountant. [L0855.125]
- A tax accountant gives personal tax advice; for general e-commerce tax and law information, TaxJar resources are a good starting point. [L0551.063]
- A fractional CFO or a better accountant can give tax advice, help you set up a different legal entity and provide professional reports. As a business grows, these experts become useful, but they cannot work with you without proper records. [L0632.015]
- For DIY tax filing, TurboTax Business is a common low-cost option for business owners. For higher volume, an accountant is useful for tax advice and for allocating money in the business. [L0801.144]
- Before tax decisions, consult a CPA or tax accountant. Sales tax obligations depend on the state, the nexus and the business entity. [L0806.088]
- For tax issues in cross-border sales, hire an accountant who specializes in e-commerce rather than a general accountant. [L1469.040]

### Finding a Local E-commerce Accountant

- Choose a tax advisor or accountant based in the state where your business operates, because tax rules differ by location. [L0663.059]
- For state sales tax questions, ask a CPA licensed in the state where the business is registered. Find accountants with strong Google reviews, contact several, and choose the one who gives a clear answer. [L0667.081]
- Accounting and tax advice for a dropshipping store should come from a certified accountant who specialises in e-commerce, for example someone who has experience with Amazon FBA or dropshipping, and who is in the same country as the business. Tax rules vary by country, so generic advice can be costly. [L1440.045]
- Engage a business accountant who specializes in e-commerce or high-ticket dropshipping rather than a general personal tax preparer, because business books differ from personal finances. A specialist can also set up spreadsheets for tracking numbers if you cannot afford full-time accounting help. [L1405.034]
- Older (2020): For questions about setting up a business in a specific country, search the Fast Business Forum for that country's name to find experiences from members who have already done it. Their firsthand steps are often more practical than general advice. [L0551.082]
- Older (2020): If you cannot find a specialized accountant for a company with a foreign partner, ask in your community groups or forum for a recommendation, because a general small business accountant may not know the specific case. [L0961.023]
- Older (2019): To find a small business accountant, search Google for 'small business accountant' plus your city, and check whether the accountant has e-commerce experience. [L1002.068]
- Older (2021): Accountants are best found locally by searching for small business accountants, reading reviews, and booking a free consultation; working with someone local is good in the beginning. [L0834.086]
- Older (2021): Tax rules vary by state and country, so no single accountant is the right choice for everyone. [L0853.120]

**Procedure: find an e-commerce accountant (2024)**

1. Search Google for 'small business accountant [your city]'.
2. Filter results by 5-star Google Maps reviews.
3. Schedule free consultation calls with 3-4 options.
4. Meet each accountant and explain your business model.
5. Select the accountant you trust and communicate with best.
6. Hire them for ongoing bookkeeping and tax filing. [L0276.039]

**Procedure: find a small business accountant (2021)**

1. Search Google for small business accountants in your area.
2. Meet the most suitable candidates in person for a face-to-face consultation.
3. Compare the options they offer and choose one.

Tax rules vary by state and country, so no single accountant is the right choice for everyone. [L0853.120]

**Procedure: find a small business accountant (2022)**

1. Search online for small business accountant plus your town name.
2. Pick a candidate with good reviews and contact them.
3. Book a free consultation, then let them set up the entity structure, such as an S corporation. [L1092.015]

- Older (2019): Search Google for 'small business accountant' plus your city, and check whether the accountant has e-commerce experience. [L1002.068]

### Working with Lawyers

- Get legal and tax advice from an attorney who specializes in e-commerce, taxes, and policies, and ask for referrals instead of relying on a general family-friend lawyer. [L0466.066]
- Legal advisory subscriptions with unlimited chat are not needed for a typical startup. A business owner may speak to a lawyer only about once every few years. [L0934.149]

**Lawyer communication process**

1. Outline your current situation and questions to the lawyer in bullet points.
2. The lawyer will reply via email listing risks and disclaimers, because they hedge via email to protect themselves.
3. If it is unclear after the email, demand clarification and push back on vague language.
4. The lawyer will typically call to give actual advice, because the phone call creates a record of their liability for accuracy.
5. Keep large email chains as documentation. [L0041.036]

- If a lawyer hedges their email advice and you remain unclear, push back and demand clarity. Vague risk language is not actionable guidance; get a phone call where they state their actual advice. [L0041.037]
- If you pursue a lawyer for malpractice, they carry professional liability insurance. The insurance claim is your remedy, which makes the written advice important documentation. [L0041.038]

### Accounting Platforms and Bookkeeping Setup

- Xero is accounting software for financial record-keeping, bookkeeping and easy coordination with an accountant. [L0230.020, L0853.092]
- Xero tracks expenses and reconciliation. Grant your accountant access so they manage your finances in real time. [L0276.038]
- Use Xero alongside a separate tool for project and day-to-day work. [L0853.092]
- Older (2021): Track bookkeeping through credit card statements that go directly to your accountant at first. Bookkeeping software like Xero is worth adding once sales reach roughly seven figures, not before. [L1112.059]
- Older (2021-2022): Xero or QuickBooks should be the first step of professional bookkeeping, instead of a home-made spreadsheet. This is the first thing to implement when the business moves beyond the starter stage. [L1516.006]
- Both sources are older. Where they differ, Xero is adopted at the seven-figure revenue threshold [L1112.059] or, in the course author's later view, at the move beyond the starter stage [L1516.006]; before either point, card statements go straight to the accountant [L1112.059].
- Older (2021-2022): Record the transactions of all your financial accounts, such as credit cards, checking accounts and PayPal, in the Transactions sheet. Payouts from payment providers and buy-now-pay-later processors arrive as deposits in your bank account, so enter the bank deposit. [L0763.015]
- Older (2021-2022): Shopify payouts usually reach the bank account about 3 days after the sale, and the payout amount is already net of Shopify's fees. Use the date the payout hits the bank when entering it in the P&L. [L0763.016]
- Older (2021-2022): Shopify payouts typically reach the bank about three days after the sale, which is why cash and profit timing differ. [L1046.022]

### Cash vs Accrual Accounting

- Cash-based accounting records income and expenses when money physically enters and leaves your accounts. Accrual-based accounting records income when earned and the corresponding expenses in the same period. [L0231.014]

### Separating Entities and Stores

- Older (2022): Keep store income separate from other businesses in a different entity. Mixed bookkeeping makes it hard to show clean numbers when you later sell a store. [L0854.087]
- Store owners who plan to sell their stores should keep the company and bookkeeping together if the stores are related. Separate accounting, such as one QuickBooks account per store, only makes sense for stores that would be sold independently. [L1114.047]
- Speaking with an accountant before choosing a business structure is advisable, because the best entity type depends on the country and the owner's situation. An accountant can also take over bookkeeping later. [L0698.012]

### Record-Keeping and Receipts

- Bookkeeping serves the owner first and the tax authority second, so clean records are needed from day one. Records should be kept in a way that makes profit and expenses clear at any time. [L1148.004]
- Poor bookkeeping from the first sale can lead to large unexpected tax bills later. Keep proper accounting records from the very first sale rather than fixing them later. [L1405.032]
- Keep the receipts for all business software, courses and tools, so an accountant can assess whether they are deductible in your country. Such costs are often tax-deductible business expenses in most countries. [L0458.057]

### Reading the Financial Statements

- Clearing accounts from payment providers such as Shopify, Klarna or PayPal hold money that has been earned but has not yet reached the bank. Show them as current assets and track them, because they should turn into cash within days. [L1046.021]
- Owner draws and profit distributions are financing activities, not operating expenses. They appear in the financing section of the cash flow statement. [L1046.023]
- Suppliers and advertising are often paid on credit cards, so card balances rise as orders come in and fall when the cards are paid off. The financing section shows this build-up and repayment, and a large positive financing figure means more borrowing than repayment. [L1046.024]
- A PPP loan is a COVID-era government stimulus loan in the US and appears as a liability on the balance sheet. Any government stimulus or grant funds received should be tracked as liabilities or equity until their treatment is settled. [L1046.025]
- Interest expense on loans and card balances appears in operating expenses in the example, and should be reviewed alongside the debt balances to understand what is being paid on borrowed money. [L1046.027]
- In the example store, merchant account (card) fees of 46,733 were about 2.6 percent of gross sales and about 3 percent of total income. Card fees are part of cost of goods sold, so they reduce gross profit alongside product cost and shipping. [L1046.057]

### Profit First Account Allocation

- Keep a separate bank account for each allocation bucket, so money set aside for suppliers, taxes, owner pay, staff and operating costs cannot be spent elsewhere. A single mixed account defeats the system. Some buckets, such as profit, can be savings accounts instead of checking accounts. [L1200.005]
- In this cash allocation system, the tax account holds money for the owner's personal income tax on the business's earnings, so the business sets that money aside before it is spent. Set the tax percentage at every allocation so the bill can be paid in full when it is due. [L1200.036]

**The seven-account Profit First setup**

| Account | Purpose | Money comes in from | Paid out from it |
|---|---|---|---|
| Income | Receives all sales payouts | Shopify, PayPal, Klarna deposits | Transfers to the other accounts only |
| Cost of goods (COGS) | Supplier costs | COGS percentage of income | Supplier invoices and supplier cards |
| Profit | Reward for owning the business | Profit percentage of real revenue | Distributed quarterly |
| Owner pay | Regular salary for the owner | Owner pay percentage of real revenue | Paid to the owner at every allocation |
| Taxes | Tax reserve | Tax percentage of real revenue | Tax payments when due |
| Staff | Payroll | Staff percentage of real revenue | Payroll |
| Operating expenses | All other business costs | Operating percentage of real revenue | Operating cards and bills | [L1200.006]

**Procedure: beginner setup**

1. Open three accounts: an income account, a COGS account, and a profit account.
2. Send all payouts from Shopify, PayPal, and other payment sources to the income account.
3. Start by moving 1% of income into the profit account, which should be easy to keep without missing it.
4. Move 80% to 90% of income into the COGS account as a starting estimate, and adjust it based on what you observe.
5. Add the owner pay, taxes, staff, and operating expenses accounts as you get comfortable with the system. [L1200.027]

- A starting profit allocation of 1% should be easy to keep. If it cannot be kept, the business likely has a serious underlying problem. The 80% to 90% COGS starting range is an experiment, not a fixed rule. [L1200.028]

### Monthly Finance Overview

**Procedure: monthly finance overview delegated to an assistant**

1. At the end of each month, prepare the total Shopify revenue.
2. Prepare the cost of fulfilling all orders.
3. Prepare the total fees paid to Shopify, Stripe and PayPal.
4. Use these figures to support monthly bookkeeping. [L1432.026]

### Outsourcing Accounting

- Accounting is a strong outsourcing candidate, especially for owners who dislike working with numbers or lack formal qualification in accounting. The owner can still review bank balances and financial reports. [L1247.017]
- Use real accounting software such as Xero, which the course author uses, or QuickBooks, rather than a home-made spreadsheet, as the first step of professional bookkeeping. [L1516.006]

#### Further details

- An accountant still files a multi-state business's main income and business tax returns. A multi-state sales tax service such as TaxJar handles state sales tax collection, filing and remittance, so the two roles complement each other. [L1092.107]

- To find an e-commerce accountant, search Google with location turned on for 'e-commerce accountant', then ask on the phone or by message whether they have experience with Amazon FBA or dropshipping. [L1440.046]

## Business Banking and Separating Finances

### Core principle: keep business and personal money apart

- Never co-mingle business and personal funds, and keep the business as a separate entity from you personally. [L0247.106]
- Use a business bank account separate from your personal banking. Suppliers accept either a personal or a business account, and the business account keeps business and personal finances apart. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0665.090]
- Keep business and personal finances separate for clearer tracking and for a future sale of the business. This is recommended but not legally required for a sole proprietor. (Older advice, 2020.) [L0268.051]
- Keep business and personal money strictly separate. Customer deposits from a job or other businesses, personal bills, and business costs such as ad spend and Shopify fees should never be mixed in one account, because commingled funds make it hard to see what the business earns and spends. [L0697.002]
- Keep every credit card, checking account and PayPal account used for the business completely separate from personal accounts. This applies whether you operate as an LLC or as a sole proprietor. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1148.005]
- Mixing personal spending such as groceries with business transactions in the same accounts creates a large mess that becomes a serious problem later. Separate the accounts at the beginning instead of cleaning them up afterwards. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1148.006]
- Keep all business finances in separate accounts from personal finances from the first day. Mixing them makes accounting messy, complicates tax reporting and creates problems as the business grows. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1405.017]
- Running a dropshipping business through a personal bank account mixes frequent sales and supplier payments with personal money, which makes accounting and tax filing messy. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0702.013]
- Using a personal bank account for business transactions can work for a few months, but the bank will likely later send a notice that it can no longer be used for business. Open a dedicated business bank account from the start. [L0360.065]
- Personal accounts can technically be used for a business, but this is not recommended. Banks may flag personal accounts used for business and ask the owner to stop. (Older advice, 2021-22 for the first point; 2023 for the second.) [L1415.012, L1094.085]
- Mixing personal and business money is allowed but raises personal risk, and it complicates the books when the business is later sold. (Older advice, 2019.) [L1069.066]
- Commingling the finances of unrelated businesses for two to three years makes it hard for a future buyer to separate them. Plan the entity structure before the business grows. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0963.121]
- Personal bank accounts do not work well at scale, because high transaction volume can lead to restrictions or suspension. Start with a personal account when launching, then move to a business account once sales volume is high. [L1384.039]
- Use a business bank account once daily deposits begin, because banks flag personal accounts used for business volume. A personal account can start the business, but it should be switched and kept separate early. (Older advice, 2022.) [L1281.068]
- When a business is sold, buyers verify transactions. Separate business accounts make due diligence much easier. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1415.011]

### Is a business account required, and when to open it

- A business bank account is not legally required to sell online, receive customer payments, pay suppliers or sign up for Shopify. Opening one early is strongly recommended, because running everything through a personal account works but makes bookkeeping messy. [L0697.001]
- Opening a business checking account is optional, but it is recommended once you are serious about the business. (Older advice, 2019.) [L1275.060]
- Once a store is running and earning sales, open a business entity and business bank accounts instead of using personal accounts. This keeps bookkeeping clean and reduces personal liability. Forming the entity can be done cheaply by yourself. (Older advice in a 2024 source: the advice is current.) [L0971.013]
- Open a business bank account as early as possible. (Older advice, 2023 source, current.) [L1094.085]
- A business bank account is needed early because a merchant account opened directly with a bank often requires an existing bank account there. Having one early keeps future options open. [L0697.003]
- Use a business credit or debit card for store expenses as early as possible. This makes expense and profit tracking cleaner and easier to audit later. (Older advice, 2019.) [L1069.065]
- A business bank account signals professionalism to larger B2B customers who order in bulk (for example $20,000 to $100,000) and prefer paying by bank wire or check instead of credit card. A personal account name looks far less professional. [L0697.005]
- A history of business activity with a bank can later unlock larger loans at lower interest rates and better credit card offers with higher limits and more rewards. [L0697.006]
- Business banking requires the business name on the account. A nickname can be anything. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0834.085]
- A sole proprietor can open a business bank account using their SSN and switch over once the business entity is formed. Forming a business entity separates business assets from personal assets. (Older advice, 2023 source, current.) [L1094.087]
- Apply for business bank accounts and business cards early rather than waiting until a supplier asks for them. Banking delays can hold up supplier onboarding. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1405.039]

### Account structure: one entity, one set of accounts

- Keep a separate business bank account and credit card for each unrelated business to avoid commingling funds. Related businesses that might be sold together can share one entity. (2023 source, current.) [L0804.096]
- Each LLC needs its own business bank account, its own Shopify Payments link and its own business credit card. Funds must never be commingled between entities. (Older advice, 2022.) [L1092.010]
- Multiple LLCs require separate bank accounts, because a shared account exposes each entity to the legal risks of the others. (Older advice, 2022.) [L1092.012]
- Open a separate business bank account for the business, using the LLC information and EIN, to isolate all transactions for bookkeeping. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1415.012]
- Use a separate company bank account for Shopify Payments and for payment providers such as Klarna. Keep a separate day-to-day account for ad payments. Some payment providers do not accept every neobank, so a traditional bank account linked to them is needed. [L1216.007]
- A DBA bank account can be used for the store if it is set up cleanly. Check the requirements in Shopify before relying on a Shopify Balance business account. [L1380.043]

### Account names and registration

- Open the business bank account in the company's name. Domain names do not need to match the bank account name. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0247.107]
- Open bank accounts in the name of the LLC entity, not the store name. (Older advice, 2022.) [L1113.045]
- Open the business checking and savings accounts in the registered business name, not the owner's personal name. Customers who pay by bank wire or check see the business name on the account. (Older advice, 2021-22 for the source of the account context; the rule itself is current.) [L0697.013]

| Question | Rule | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Whose name is on the account? | Registered company or LLC name. A store name or nickname is not enough. | [L0247.107, L1113.045, L0697.013, L0834.085] |
| Must the domain match? | No. | [L0247.107] |

### Proof of address, business history and country requirements

- Some online business banks rejected a non-US owner's application over proof of address for a US business. Ways past this were a US mailing address for the business or a setup through Stripe Atlas. An existing bank account opened without proof of address is another route worth checking first. (2024 source, current.) [L0360.092]
- A US business bank account opened directly in the US usually requires a US physical or virtual address. An account opened overseas in US dollars may not need one. (2024 source, current.) [L0360.063]
- In one case, a business checking account was opened online at an existing bank without any proof of address, while new banks may ask for proof of address. Check the existing bank route first. (2024 source, current.) [L0360.066]
- Opening a US bank account from abroad often requires being physically present. Some store owners reported a virtual US dollar account through OFX as an alternative. These are unverified reports and should be checked in community sources before use. [L0359.091]
- A business bank account may require proof of business history in some countries, so the business may need to operate before the account is granted. Ask the provider what it requires before you build the account. (Older advice, 2021.) [L1112.062]
- A company registered in Canada that sells in the US may open a Canadian bank account with a US dollar option, or a US business account. (2023 source, current.) [L1094.086]
- In Canada, opening a business account requires a business license, and registered businesses must handle business taxes. Set up a business account once sales justify it, because you need a business for tax purposes. [L1384.041]
- The bank account and card should be based in the country you sell to. PayPal, Stripe and Shopify Payments can hold money for long periods when payouts cross borders, which makes dropshipping cash flow very hard. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1405.023]
- It is ideal to have a bank account in the country you sell in, for example a US-based business bank account for US sales or an Australian bank account for Australian sales. This is strongly recommended for receiving funds. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1480.023]
- The business bank account must be based in the country where you sell. For Australia, any bank the owner is comfortable with is fine, because differences in business account fees are usually minor. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0702.014]
- If you cannot get a bank account in your target country, rethink which market you sell in or get advice on the options. No fully perfect banking setup exists. Plan the banking and business structure before you start selling. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1480.025]

### Choosing a bank

- Choose a bank where you already have a personal account and are satisfied with the service, and open the business accounts there. Business offerings across banks are broadly similar. [L0697.010]
- Open a business account with low or no fees in the country where you sell. Many banks offer a business version of your personal account with extra business features. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1405.018]
- Traditional local banks are not a barrier to multiple accounts, though some banks are inflexible. At the lesson's local bank, opening each account took two hours or more, and a 10 dollar monthly fee applied when minimum balances were not met. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1200.031]

#### Online and neobank options

| Bank | Status and conditions | Source ids |
|---|---|---|
| Mercury (US) | 2021 member reports: an option for non-US citizens. Check recent community discussions before opening. (Older advice, 2021.) 2024: Mercury rejected some non-US owners over proof of address. Route: a US mailing address or Stripe Atlas. Use the 2024 rule when you are a non-US owner without a US address. | [L0671.118, L0671.255, L0360.092] |
| Mercury as Relay alternative | A similar option to Relay for running multiple accounts. (Older advice, 2021-22.) | [L1200.030] |
| Mercury and Amex vs Brex | Mercury (US) is a business bank option. American Express is a card option that gives credit for online spending. Both are alternatives to Brex for US online businesses. (Source C, current.) | [L1512.027] |
| Relay | Online business bank that allows many checking and savings accounts in one place. At the time of the lesson it was invite-only. An account could be opened in about 10 minutes, and new accounts could be created in seconds, with easy transfers between them. (Older advice, 2021-22.) | [L1200.029] |
| Novo | Rejected some non-US owners over proof of address for a US business (2024). Route: a US mailing address or Stripe Atlas. | [L0360.092] |
| Revolut | Denied some applications from non-US residents (2024). Klarna may not accept Revolut accounts. | [L0276.032, L1216.008] |
| Wise | Banking option for non-US residents setting up US business bank accounts when traditional banks such as Mercury and Revolut deny applications (2024). Do not receive business payouts into a personal Wise account (see the risk below). | [L0276.032, L1127.006] |
| Local bank | Usually the option for payment providers such as Klarna. A local bank account in the country of the business is the usual option. | [L1216.008, L1200.031] |

- Mercury and Novo rejected non-US owners in 2024. Mercury was reported as an option for non-US citizens in 2021. Use the 2024 position when you are a non-US owner without a US address, and check current discussions before applying. [L0360.092, L0671.118, L0671.255]
- Wise was a route for non-US residents when traditional banks denied applications. Receiving business payouts into a personal WISE account risks an account ban. One seller had a WISE account banned earlier for a different transaction issue and used a friend's account in the meantime. [L0276.032, L1127.006]
- Members advised against using money transfer services such as TransferWise as a substitute for a business bank account. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0671.120, L0671.257]

### Bank fees and minimum balances

- Most major banks offer free business checking only if a minimum balance is maintained. Minimum balance requirements differ by bank, so check them before opening an account. The example given for Bank of America is a $16 monthly fee if the minimum is not met. (2024 source, current.) [L0697.009]
- Before opening, confirm the bank's minimum balance requirement for a free business account. [L0697.014]
- The local bank example charged a 10 dollar monthly fee when minimum balances were not met. [L1200.031]

### Opening the accounts

Procedure (from the course):

1. Choose the bank where you already hold a personal account, or compare the online options above. [L0697.010, L0697.014]
2. Confirm the minimum balance requirement for a free business account. [L0697.014, L0697.009]
3. Prepare the registered business name, EIN or entity documents, and proof of address if the bank asks for it. [L0247.107, L1415.012, L0360.063]
4. Open the business checking account by visiting a branch, calling the bank, or applying online. [L0697.014]
5. Most banks let you open a business account online in about 10 minutes, and cards for the account are then sent to you. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0702.015]
6. Some banks take about 20 minutes to open a business checking account online. (Older advice, 2019.) [L1275.060]
7. Open the savings account after the checking account. [L0697.007]

### Checking and savings structure

Procedure (from the course):

1. Open two business bank accounts. [L0697.007]
2. Checking account: the transactional account where all revenue arrives and all expenses are paid from. [L0697.007]
3. Savings account: profit is moved from checking into it so it stays clearly separated, for distributions or tax payments. [L0697.007]
4. The savings account is for separation, not long-term wealth building. [L0697.007]

Example:

- With a goal of $20,000 monthly net profit, the profit is transferred from the checking account to the savings account instead of staying in checking. The money earmarked for owner distributions or taxes is then visibly separate from daily transactions. [L0697.008]

### High-yield savings

- US figures from the lesson: traditional big-bank savings accounts paid about 0.0001% interest, while online banks such as Ally or Marcus paid about 1.9% to 2%. Choose an online account with no fees and low minimums that still pays a real rate. [L1126.008]
- Outside the US, the lesson names YouSaver and ING (Australia) and Marcus (UK) as high-yield online savings options. Check current rates, fees and eligibility for your country before opening one. [L1126.009]

### Payment routing: Shopify Payments, PayPal and customers

- The Shopify checkout belongs to Shopify. Shopify uses payment gateways to capture payments, then pays the money out to a PayPal account or a nominated business bank account. Payments should go to a business bank account and a business PayPal account, never a personal one. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0677.009]
- Route all business revenue into the business checking account: Shopify Payments (card, debit, Apple Pay, Google Pay), PayPal withdrawals transferred to the bank, and bank wire or check payments from customers. [L0697.011]
- Payment gateways deposit customer payments into the business account. Suppliers are typically paid from it or from a card linked to it. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0702.012]
- PayPal is an acceptable payment method if funds can be withdrawn to a business bank account. If a bank does not allow linking to PayPal, open a different business bank account with a bank that permits third-party access. (2025 source, current.) [L0278.032]
- PayPal balances must be withdrawn from PayPal to your bank. Shopify Payments transfers go directly to your bank. Plan cash flow with that difference in mind. (2023 source, current.) [L0934.017, L0934.137]
- Shopify Payments deposits sales funds within 48 hours. A Shopify Balance debit card gives faster access to funds, but compare it against rewards cards before use. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0963.128]
- Use a separate day-to-day account for ad payments, separate from Shopify Payments and payment providers. [L1216.007]
- Currency mismatch adds cost. When a store prices in one currency, such as USD, but pays out to a bank in another currency, such as GBP, each payout can carry conversion fees. A company and bank account in the store's own currency, or a bank with low real-time conversion rates, reduces this cost. (Source C, current.) [L1512.052]

### Supplier payments and card hygiene

- Never give a supplier your business bank account number when all purchases are paid by credit card. You can ask the supplier why it needs the number. (2025 source, current.) [L0667.056]
- Paying suppliers from a personal account can cause transfer problems. Keep store money separate from personal money by using a dedicated business bank account. [L1380.042]

### Travel and cash access

- A no-fee card, such as the Charles Schwab card in the example, lets you withdraw cash abroad from ATMs without ATM fees. Owners who work from abroad should carry one. [L1515.018]

### Shopify Balance

- Shopify Balance is Shopify's built-in banking option. It holds payments inside Shopify and lets you see your balance. It is a useful interim option when you cannot get a business account, but it has eligibility requirements to check. (Source C, current.) [L1384.040]
- Shopify Balance may offer a business bank account if the account qualifies. Check the requirements in Shopify before relying on it. [L1380.043]
- The Shopify Balance account is not used by the people who run stores in this model. Separate business bank accounts are preferred instead. (Older advice, 2022.) [L1281.069]
- Limits: a Shopify Balance debit card gives faster access to funds than Shopify Payments' 48-hour deposits, but it should be compared against rewards cards before use. [L0963.128]

### Backup and risk

- Payment processors and banks can change which options they allow at any time. An account used for cross-border selling may be banned without warning. Plan a backup banking route rather than relying on one provider. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1405.025]
- Opening a US bank account from abroad is not guaranteed. Verify each route before committing your store to it. [L0359.091, L0360.092]

### Older advice and source conflicts

- Older advice (2019 to 2022) in this section: [L1069.065, L1069.066, L1275.060, L0268.051, L0665.090, L0671.118, L0671.120, L0671.255, L0671.257, L0702.012, L0702.013, L0702.014, L0702.015, L0963.121, L0963.128, L1281.068, L1281.069, L1405.017, L1405.018, L1405.023, L1405.025, L1405.039, L1415.011, L1415.012, L1480.023, L1480.025, L1112.062, L1113.045, L1148.005, L1148.006, L1200.029, L1200.030, L1200.031, L0247.106, L0247.107, L0834.085, L1216.007, L0677.009, L0697.* with no year are current, not older].
- Mercury: 2021 member reports say it is an option for non-US citizens. 2024 reports say it rejected a non-US owner's application over proof of address. Both are kept above. Use the 2024 position when you lack a US address. [L0671.118, L0671.255, L0360.092]
- Minimum balance fees differ by bank. Bank of America charges $16 monthly if the minimum is not met, and the lesson's local bank charged $10 per month. Check each bank's rule before opening. [L0697.009, L1200.031]

## Business Credit Cards, Rewards and Supplier Payments

### Whether a business card is required

- A business credit card is not required to run a dropshipping business, and a debit card is enough to pay suppliers. [L0005.001, L0699.001]
- A card is still recommended because business spending accumulates points quickly, and cash back, travel rewards or other benefits are substantial enough to make getting and keeping one worthwhile. [L0005.001, L0255.072]
- Pay as many business expenses as possible with a business credit card, including advertising, Shopify subscription fees and cost of goods sold, because points, miles or cashback accumulate quickly in this model. [L0697.012]
- Many suppliers require payment by card and do not accept bank transfers for orders, so at least one business card, credit or debit, is needed before approaching suppliers. [L1405.021]
- Suppliers typically accept ACH transfers, checks and credit cards. [L0255.067]
- (2019, older) Suppliers generally do not care whether a personal or business credit card pays, as long as the payment is accepted. [L1069.067]
- (2020, older) Suppliers generally accept debit card payment, so orders can be paid with a debit card as well as a credit card. [L1111.144]
- (2022, older) Credit card terms are preferred with suppliers: the supplier keeps the card on file, charges it on each order, ships immediately, and the business earns points. [L0665.097]
- (2020, older) Credit card versus debit card for supplier payments: both work, but a credit card is preferred to build points or miles and to request limit increases based on daily payoff history. [L0268.050]
- (2019, older) An alternative to credit is to order from the supplier only after the customer's payment has been received. Points-earning credit cards are a useful option for supplier payments. [L1275.066]
- Prepaying suppliers by credit card earns card points on those purchases, which is an extra benefit of a dropshipping store, but this only applies when the card is in your name. [L0933.025]
- Use a personal credit card to buy inventory until the business is formed, then use a business credit card for purchases. [L0359.093]
- (2019, older) With good or decent credit, a rewards credit card can be used from the start to build points and miles for the business. [L1068.073]
- If you have no credit yet, give the supplier a debit card number to start with. Once you have built up credit, replace it with a credit card on file. [L0933.024]

### Starting with a checking account and debit card

- Open a business checking account and use its debit card for early business expenses before applying for a business credit card. This keeps business and personal funds separate and delays annual credit card fees until the business is profitable. [L0853.063]
- A business debit card should run on the Visa or Mastercard network. [L1405.020]
- (2019 to 2022 sources, older) Where a business credit card is not possible because of personal credit history or finances, a debit card linked to the business bank account can be used to buy products. [L1405.020, L0702.020]
- A debit card linked to the business bank account can serve as the card when credit is unavailable, and it is needed when a supplier requires a card or when a card must be linked to a Google Ads account. [L0702.020]
- Keep business cash flow positive rather than taking loans or funding programs. Pay ad spend and cost of goods on a business credit card and pay it off in full each month to earn rewards without carrying debt. [L0804.099]

### Choosing a card by the reward you want

| Card | Earn rate | Limits and terms | Fees | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| AmEx Business Gold | 4X points on advertising and social media spend. The cap is 100,000 USD per year in the 2020 Source A lesson, and 150,000 USD per year in Source B's 2021 to 2022 terms. Also 4X on shipping and other 4X categories. Points only, no cash back. | Charge card. Full balance due monthly. | 295 USD per year | [L0699.014, L0358.039, L0903.008, L0903.028] |
| AmEx Business Platinum | Upgraded travel benefits for business expenses and travel. | Charge card. No preset spending limit stated, but a practical ceiling exists. Full balance due monthly. | About 500 USD per year (2020 figure). | [L0005.003, L0699.007, L0468.156] |
| Capital One Spark Business | 2% cash back on every dollar spent. | Balance must be paid in full monthly, per the source. | No fee in year one, 95 USD per year from year two. | [L0903.009, L0903.030] |
| Brex (US) | 1.5x to 4x points on online spending; points can be used as cash. | Credit line that can build credit. | Offered a 250 USD bonus at account opening (verify current terms). | [L1512.024] |

- (2020, older) The AmEx Business Gold card reportedly gave 4x points on an initial block of ad spend, but the exact threshold was not confirmed. Check current card terms before applying. [L0468.195]
- (2020, older) In the 2020 lesson, one personal AmEx Gold card gave 4x points on the first 100,000 USD of ad spend per year. Card offers change over time, so verify current terms. [L0358.039]
- The Gold card suits people who want travel rewards, and it is the recommended starting card for most beginners. [L0699.014, L0941.089]
- The Platinum card is the current recommended business card in this source, with the Gold as the earlier recommendation. [L0699.006]
- Platinum is worth using for ad bills when charges are above 5,000 USD each, as the card then earns many points and travel benefits. [L0941.089]
- Platinum travel benefits include status upgrades with large hotel chains (sometimes free nights, better rooms, early check-in and late check-out), lounge access at airports worldwide, and travel credits. [L0699.012]
- (2020, older) Some American Express business cards have high or no preset spending limits, for example the Platinum card (annual fee about 500 USD). Check current terms before applying. [L0468.156]
- Choose a business card by the reward you actually want. Travel points suit owners who will use hotel and airport perks, and cash-back cards suit owners who want steady cash returns. A card with no reward you will use adds nothing to the business. [L0699.017]
- (2021 to 2022, older) Rewards schemes outside the US are usually points rather than cashback, and they are generally smaller than US schemes. A business card with rewards should still be based in the country where you sell. [L0702.016]
- (2021 to 2022, older) In Australia, an American Express card can earn 1.5 to 2 reward points per dollar spent, and the points can be used for things such as airfares. [L0702.017]
- (2021, older) Suppliers often accept a business credit card linked to a Canadian business, but this varies supplier by supplier. Ask each supplier directly which payment methods it accepts. [L1279.081]
- If you are a Canadian resident selling in the US and lose money on exchange rates or points with a Canadian card, check whether a US card avoids those fees. Ask the card issuer about all fees before switching. [L0941.090]
- Credit cards with free foreign-transaction (currency conversion) fees, including business cards, avoid conversion costs when paying Facebook ads in USD. Examples named in the source are Revolut, Starling, NatWest, American Express and Capital on Tap; the speaker uses Mercury. [L0690.022]
- (Source C, UK) Personal credit cards in the UK often offer 0% for 12 months, which is better for leverage if the business is unprofitable. [L0308.019]

### Scaling with multiple cards

- Use business credit cards for scaling, since AmEx offers a near-limitless limit. The balance must be paid off within the month or face high interest. [L0308.019]
- Build a business credit rating by using cards consistently and paying on time. [L0308.019]
- When ad spend outgrows one card's limit, combine several cards: personal credit cards at different banks, a business card and partner cards. [L0308.033]
- Example from the Black Friday run: personal cards at about 5,000 GBP, a business card at about 10,000 GBP and a partner's card at about 10,000 GBP. Total leverage was roughly 25,000 to 35,000 GBP combined. [L0308.033]
- (2021 to 2022, older) Ask American Express for additional business cards under the same company once the 4X category limit has been reached. Each card can earn 4X on its own first 150,000 USD of ad spend, and cards can be used for separate businesses to keep books apart. [L0903.019]
- (2021 to 2022, older) Rotating American Express cards over time, for example moving ad spend to a new card every few months, allows 4X earning to continue beyond the first 150,000 USD of spend under one company. The author reports no known limit on the number of cards. [L0903.020]
- Ask the card issuer for a credit limit increase while paying the balance off every day. Repeated increases over a few months can take a small starting limit to tens of thousands of dollars per card. [L0468.194]

### Building credit from a low limit

#### Credit path for a new store

1. Start with the debit card linked to the business bank account, and accept that the first orders wait for the 48-hour payout before they go to suppliers. [L0360.068]
2. Once profits build up in the account, open a secured credit card. [L0360.068]
3. Secure the card with a certificate of deposit (CD) equal to the wanted credit limit, for example a 5,000 USD CD for a 5,000 USD limit. [L0360.068, L0699.004]
4. Use the card and pay it off as orders come in. The credit score improves quickly. [L0360.068]
5. Once credit is strong, close the secured card and cash out the CD. [L0360.068]

#### Building business credit without a credit history

1. Open a business checking account, which comes with a business debit card. [L0699.003]
2. Pay suppliers with the business debit card while cash on hand is small. [L0699.003]
3. Build a cash reserve from sales over time. [L0699.003]
4. Once the reserve is large enough, open a secured credit card. [L0699.003]
5. Use the secured card and pay the full balance each cycle so credit builds. [L0699.003]
6. As credit improves, move to cards with better rewards, benefits and higher credit limits. [L0699.003]

- A secured credit card requires opening a certificate of deposit (CD) at the issuing bank. The bank holds the deposit as collateral against the credit limit, so a 5,000 USD limit needs a 5,000 USD CD, and the bank pays interest on the CD for its term. [L0699.004]
- Using a secured card and paying it off in full each cycle builds credit quickly. Strong credit can then qualify a business for cards with more rewards, benefits and higher limits. If the card is not paid, the bank can collect the balance from the CD. [L0699.005]
- (2022, older) Building credit with a new card: use a low-limit card, pay it in full each night, and request a limit increase every few weeks. A secured card can help if credit is poor. [L0963.129]
- (2021, older) Paying card balances down promptly as sales come in can raise credit limits quickly. With a low credit limit, suppliers can still be paid by debit card or wire transfer. [L0853.069]
- (2019, older) A card with a credit limit of about 2,500 USD allowed several orders a day when paid off regularly. Requesting credit limit increases every few months grew that capacity over time. [L1275.065]
- (2020, older) A store owner can build card credit by paying balances off promptly and asking the card issuer for periodic credit limit increases. This helps cover supplier orders before customer payments settle, which typically takes about 48 hours. [L1111.183]

#### Daily payoff and limit increases

- (2019, older) Build available credit limits by paying the balance every couple of days instead of waiting for the full billing cycle. This renews the credit line frequently and keeps credit available for continuous business purchases without waiting 30 days. [L0243.047]
- (2021, older) If the personal credit limit is low (300 to 1,000 USD), provide the credit card to suppliers. If the limit is insufficient for orders, offer a debit card instead or request multiple charges. Build credit over time, and eventually upgrade to a secured credit card with a limit of 5,000 USD or more. [L0270.048]

#### Daily payoff strategy (small limit)

1. Put business charges on the card throughout the day. [L0699.009]
2. Each morning, check that the day's store deposits have reached the business bank account. [L0699.009]
3. Pay off the full card balance. [L0699.009]
4. Repeat every day until the credit limit has grown. [L0699.009]

#### Requesting a credit limit increase

1. Set a recurring reminder to call the card company every week, every two weeks or every month, depending on sales volume. [L0699.010]
2. Call the card company. [L0699.010]
3. Explain that the card balance is paid off every night or morning. [L0699.010]
4. Ask whether the credit limit can be increased. [L0699.010]

#### Case: daily payoff and recurring calls

- **Starting point:** a small Chase credit card with a very low limit, paid off every morning after store deposits arrived. [L0699.011]
- **Actions:** full payoff every day, plus recurring calls to the card company asking for limit increases. [L0699.011]
- **Results:** the limit rose from about 5,000 USD to 10,000 USD, then 25,000 USD, then 100,000 USD, and later reached spending of up to 1,000,000 USD per month without problems. [L0699.011]
- (2020, older) Repeated payoffs and limit requests over a few months grow the limit. Ask the supplier to split charges, pay in full daily, and request increases monthly, as set out in the procedure below. [L0664.024, L0468.155]

#### Paying a supplier when the limit is below the order

1. Ask the supplier to split the charge into several smaller charges, for example 500 USD today and 500 USD tomorrow. [L0664.024]
2. Pay the card balance in full every day, not at the end of the month. [L0664.024]
3. Alternatively, use a debit card linked to your checking account. [L0664.024]
4. Call the card company every month and request a credit limit increase, pointing to the daily payments as proof. [L0664.024]

(2020, older) The same approach, in short:

1. Use the business credit card to pay suppliers. [L0468.155]
2. Pay the card balance off every day while the credit limit is small. [L0468.155]
3. Build the limit over a few months by repeating the daily payoff. [L0468.155]
4. Consider a rewards card once the limit is larger. [L0468.155]

### Rewards math

- Card reward points from ad spend equal the spend in the capped bracket multiplied by the earn rate. For example, 100,000 USD of annual ad spend at 4x on the first 100,000 USD earns 400,000 points. Check current terms, since reward rates and caps change. [L0358.058]
- (2021 to 2022, older) At 1 to 2 reward points per dollar, a card used for 100,000 USD in monthly supplier and ad spend would earn about 100,000 to 200,000 points per month. [L0702.019]
- (2021 to 2022, older) An AmEx Business Gold card used for 150,000 USD of ad spend earns 600,000 points, which transfer one-to-one to 600,000 Delta SkyMiles. [L0903.013, L0903.008]
- (2021 to 2022, older) At 100,000 USD monthly cost of goods sold on the Capital One Spark card, 2% cash back equals 2,000 USD per month. At 1,000,000 USD monthly spend across all cards, the reward equals 20,000 USD per month. [L0903.022]
- (2021 to 2022, older) Break-even on the Capital One Spark card's 95 USD annual fee from year two: 95 USD divided by 2% equals 4,750 USD of annual spend. Spending 4,750 USD on the card in a year covers the fee. [L0903.023]
- (2021 to 2022, older) Example: spending 5,000 USD in the first three months on a Capital One Spark Business card meets the 4,500 USD threshold for the 500 USD cash bonus and also earns 100 USD of 2% cash back. These figures assume the offer is still active. [L0903.038, L0903.009]
- (2021 to 2022, older) Choose cash-out or spending on rewards according to a tax adviser's advice, since the reward can be used as cash, to cover purchases or in other ways. Confirm the tax treatment with an accountant before choosing how to use it. [L0903.024]

### Card split and bookkeeping

#### Keeping business charges on one or two cards

- (2021 to 2022, older) Use a business credit card only for business expenses, and keep all business charges on one or two cards so month-end bookkeeping and reconciliation stay simple. Choosing a separate card for each expense category also helps avoid hunting for charges during reconciliation. [L0903.001]
- (2021 to 2022, older) Use the American Express card for advertising spend (Google, Bing, remarketing, Facebook lead ads, Snapchat, Pinterest, TikTok and similar) and for shipping costs, since these categories earn 4X points. Use the Capital One card for cost of goods sold and all other business expenses such as Shopify, email and virtual assistant tools. [L0903.010]

#### Two-card setup

1. Apply for an American Express Business Gold card and use it only for advertising spend and shipping costs. [L0903.011]
2. Apply for a Capital One Spark Business card and use it for cost of goods sold (supplier orders) and all other operating expenses such as Shopify, apps and virtual assistants. [L0903.011]
3. Keep spending mostly on each card as assigned, so that the monthly statements map directly to the books. [L0903.011]
4. Pay off both cards in full each month. [L0903.011]

#### Separate cards per allocation account

1. Get a separate credit card for each expense account, or a card with a different number if the issuer allows it. [L1200.032]
2. Pay supplier invoices with the supplier card. [L1200.032]
3. Pay the supplier card off from the COGS account. [L1200.032]
4. Pay operating expenses with a separate operating card. [L1200.032]
5. Pay the operating card off from the operating expenses account. [L1200.032]

#### Statements, payoff and balances

- A business credit card is usually backed personally, so the balance appears on your personal credit file even when the card is issued for a business. Never carry a balance month to month. Pay the statement in full every month to avoid interest and protect your credit. [L0903.004]
- Pay credit card balances off in full every month. Any balance carried reduces the cash needed for other business costs and creates interest. As sales grow, the balance rises faster, so repayment must keep pace with spending. [L0903.005]
- Use the business credit card only for product purchases and marketing spend, never for personal purchases. Carrying personal debt on a business card is bad practice. [L1405.022]
- Use a credit card for the business as a whole, but only when the owner is personally comfortable with the credit arrangement. [L0702.018]
- (2021 to 2022, older) Paying a supplier with a credit card covers the gap between a customer's payment and the payout from the store's payment processor. The card balance then gives roughly 30 days or more to repay, so inventory can be ordered before sales money reaches the bank account. [L0903.002]

### Billing cycles and statement timing

- Some credit cards have a 30-day billing cycle, while others, such as Amex in the example, bill in about 16 days. A card with a longer billing window gives more time before ad spend has to be paid. [L0351.027]
- (2021 to 2022, older) The source describes Amex billing as roughly 30 days or more to repay. Check the actual cycle on your statement, since the Source D example gives about 16 days for Amex. [L0903.002, L0351.027]
- (2021 to 2022, older) Capital One Spark limits are based mainly on the owner's income and what the issuer will extend. Very high monthly sales will not automatically unlock a matching limit. Plan payments so the balance is cleared before the next statement closes. [L0903.007]
- Use the daily payoff method to make the balance clear before the statement closes. [L0699.009]

### Limits, estimators and charge card rules

- (2021 to 2022, older) American Express business card limits are not a fixed number. The issuer sets them by purchase estimator and risk, so a large monthly spend can still be declined. Test a large purchase with the issuer's estimator before relying on it. [L0903.006]
- American Express charge cards must be paid in full each month. In exchange, the issuer gives very high credit limits and no preset spending limit is stated, though a practical ceiling still exists. [L0699.007, L0934.013]
- (2023) The Gold and Platinum charge cards were described as having no preset spending limit, but they must be paid in full every month. Apply early for one as a second card when the business credit line is too small. [L0934.139]
- (2023) Qualifying for a US business card generally requires a US business with its information on file. [L0934.013]
- (2021 to 2022, older) The 4X Amex reward on the first 150,000 USD of ad spend is stated without a time period in the source, so it may not reset each year as an earlier summary assumed. Confirm the period with the issuer before planning spend around the cap. [L0903.031]
- (2021 to 2022, older) The terms cited in the source, including the Amex and Capital One annual fees, the 4X and 2% earn rates, and the Capital One sign-up bonus, reflect offers as of September 2021. Check current issuer terms before applying, since fees, earn rates and bonuses change. [L0903.028]
- (2021 to 2022, older) The source states that the newer Capital One Spark Business card requires the balance to be paid in full each month. Verify this with the issuer before assuming a revolving balance is allowed. [L0903.030]
- (2021 to 2022, older) Apply for a business credit card with a rewards program such as cash back or travel points, since ad and product spending can earn meaningful rewards. The credit limit does not need to be high, and approval is what matters. [L1405.019]

### Redeeming points

#### Options and value

- Points can be redeemed for travel, gift cards, Amazon purchases, merchandise, PayPal payments and airline miles. Airline transfers give the best value per point. The other options are simpler but lower value. [L0903.033]
- Redeeming points for Amazon purchases at checkout is a lower-value option than airline transfers, but it is a simple way to use points for ordinary purchases. [L0903.017]
- (2021 to 2022, older) Amazon checkout accepts American Express points when the Amex card is linked to the Amazon account. At checkout, the buyer can choose to pay with points instead of dollars. [L0903.018]
- American Express points can be used at checkout with many merchants. For example, a ride-hailing app such as Uber may offer to pay with points when it recognises the American Express card. [L0699.013]
- (2021 to 2022, older) Only transfer American Express points to an airline partner. Transferring to a hotel chain gives poor value per point. Compare the points needed on the airline's own search before deciding where to move points. [L0903.012]
- (2021 to 2022, older) American Express points transfer one-to-one to airline loyalty programmes such as Delta SkyMiles. [L0903.013]
- (2021 to 2022, older) On Delta, a first-class one-way flight from the Minneapolis hub to many US destinations cost roughly 25,000 to 70,000 SkyMiles, depending on destination and how close the booking date is. Shorter routes such as Minneapolis to Denver sometimes cost under 20,000 miles, while long routes and smaller airports cost more. [L0903.014]
- (2021 to 2022, older) Using 100,000 Amex points for a single hotel night gives far less value than using the same points on a first-class domestic flight that would cost well over 1,000 USD in cash. This comparison is the reason hotel transfers are avoided. [L0903.035]

#### Portal or transfer

- (2021 to 2022, older) The American Express travel portal lets cardholders search flights, hotels and rental cars and pay with Amex points. Compare its price with the airline's own points price before choosing between portal booking and a transfer. [L0903.032]
- (2021 to 2022, older) When an airline award price is close to the portal price for the same flight, booking through the portal can be preferable because you keep the airline's loyalty earning and status progress. If the portal price is clearly higher, transfer the points to the airline instead. [L0903.015]
- (2021 to 2022, older) One flight cost 56,000 Amex points in the Amex portal, while the same booking on Delta cost 46,000 Delta SkyMiles. Transferring to Delta costs a small fee, but booking on Delta earns more value for the airline loyalty account than the portal booking. [L0903.016]
- (2021 to 2022, older) If the preferred airline is not on the American Express transfer list, transfer points to one of its partner airlines, such as a European partner, and book the flight through that partner. Check the partner's award availability before transferring. [L0903.034]
- (2021 to 2022, older) Flying roughly once a month on one airline and booking through the card portal when its price is close to the airline award price can build elite status without paying for flights. The source reports reaching Delta Platinum Medallion this way. [L0903.037]
- (2021 to 2022, older) A charge billed to a hotel room that posts to an American Express card as a travel charge can be covered with Amex points. This works for purchases such as shopping inside a resort hotel that are billed to the guest room. [L0903.036]

### Funding supplier orders and payment methods

#### Timing and float
