- A business credit card can float supplier orders, with the balance paid off every day or two so no interest accrues. [L0247.098]
- (2020, older) Early on, with little credit available, supplier orders can be charged to a credit card and paid off each day as payment settles. Customer payments to the bank take about 48 hours. [L1111.145]
- If no credit line exists, a debit card can pay suppliers, with customer funds arriving about 48 hours later. [L0247.098]
- Paying a supplier immediately by ACH can speed up processing of the purchase order. Use this when suppliers are slow to fulfil orders. [L1472.033]
- Place supplier orders with a credit card where possible, because it makes payments simple and the card can earn points or cash back. As order volume and advertising spend grow on the same card, the rewards accumulate. [L1480.022]
- (2021, older) Free domestic ACH transfers (Brex) meant payments to suppliers arrived within about one day, and recipients were notified within seconds. This makes it practical to pay suppliers from a US company account (verify current terms). [L1512.050]
- (2024) Freight booking payment: in the Payment section, click the "Stored Debit/Credit Card" button, which charges the company account automatically. Leave the card dropdown on its preset card, tick the confirmation box, then click "Pay Now". [L1291.013]

#### Choosing the payment method

- Suppliers' payment options commonly include ACH transfers, checks and credit cards. [L0255.067]
- Bank transfers keep payment order-by-order like credit terms and eliminate the processing fee that some suppliers charge on cards. [L0274.045]
- If a supplier requests a credit card processing fee (for example 4 percent), ask whether it accepts ACH or bank transfers instead. [L0274.045]
- (Source D, 2026) Wise and bank transfer are the two payment methods used to pay private suppliers per order. [L1156.012]
- Paying suppliers or CJ Dropshipping through a wallet makes little difference to the cost. A wallet is useful only when you want a fixed budget for fulfilling orders. Otherwise, paying directly by card or PayPal works the same way. [L1216.016]
- (2021, older) Some suppliers add extra fees when paid with American Express, which can cancel out the rewards. In Australia, avoid Amex for supplier payments, while it is believed the same surcharge does not apply in the US. [L1405.026]

#### Prepaying and card on file

- Prepaying suppliers by card earns points on those purchases. This only applies when you have a credit card in your name. [L0933.025]
- Replace a debit card on file with a credit card once credit has been built. [L0933.024]
- The supplier keeps the card on file, so the card can be updated with the supplier later by email. [L0358.038]

### Personal liability, guarantees and co-signing

- (2020, older) Suppliers generally do not care which credit card you use, as long as it processes orders. A new business is usually personally liable for its card until it builds its own credit history, so put the business name on the card when possible. You can update the card on file with a supplier by email later. [L0358.038]
- AmEx personally guarantees you for any loans against the card. If you take Shopify Capital (which uses AmEx), the business is liable, but you personally guarantee it. Review the terms and conditions carefully before using business credit for company loans. [L0308.020]
- (2023) For first-time business credit card applications, expect to co-sign personally even though the card is in the business name. Once business credit is established, you can remove yourself as co-signer. [L0251.016]
- (2021, older) A new business credit card usually still requires a personal co-signature and relies on personal credit in the early years. A UPS Store mailbox address is a convenient option, and a standard forwarding address is an acceptable alternative. [L0968.026]
- (2019, older) A sole proprietor can still open a separate card for business expenses only. For an LLC or S-corp, a new business has no credit history, so the owner will usually have to co-sign the card. [L1069.068]
- (2021, older) If a business card application is denied while a credit freeze is active, lift the freeze and reapply. Many banks offer business credit cards, and American Express and Chase were cited as popular options. [L0671.115, L0671.252]

#### Further details

- AMEX Business Gold is used to maximize rewards on paid advertising, earning 4X points per dollar spent on ads, making it suitable for businesses with significant ad budgets. [L0005.002]

- Separate virtual cards can be created per advertising or software platform. A virtual card can be closed immediately to stop recurring charges, which helps with free trial sign-ups. [L1512.026]

## Funding, Cash Flow and Financing the Business

### Debt principles

- Avoid debt where possible and prefer funding through margins. Shopify Capital and similar financing take a share of sales and can be expensive. *(older advice, 2021)* [L0247.097]
- Credit can be a useful tool when used carefully, but a business should not be run on borrowed money. If a loan's purpose is unclear and the business does not need it, treat the repayment as a risk to address. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1046.026]
- You do not need large upfront capital to pay for inventory. Paying suppliers with a credit card and paying the card off quickly needs only about the wholesale cost of one order in credit. *(older advice, 2019)* [L1275.064]

### Shopify payout timing

| Payment route | Stated timing | Condition | Units |
|---|---|---|---|
| Shopify Payments payout | Within 48 hours | Most sources | L0468.154, L0664.023, L0835.119, L0853.060, L0941.091, L1283.110 |
| Shopify Payments payout | About two business days | Older source; also the later figure for a new account | L0967.060, L0699.002 |
| New account, first deposit | Within 48 hours | Applies to the first deposits of a new account | L0699.002 |
| Payment processing, general | 2 to 3 days | Can be faster (next day or same day) depending on gateway and bank | L1480.021 |

- Shopify Payments deposits funds into the bank account within 48 hours, which helps cover supplier payments. *(older advice, 2020)* [L0468.154]
- Shopify Payments pays out customer payments within 48 hours. Use this timing to plan supplier payments made after customer orders. *(older advice, 2020)* [L0664.023]
- Shopify typically deposits sales revenue into the bank account of a new account within 48 hours. Later in the same lesson, Shopify payouts are described as arriving within two business days, so store cash covers card payments without a long wait. [L0699.002]
- Shopify typically deposits sales into the bank about 48 hours after the sale. Plan supplier payments around that timing, especially when starting with limited cash. *(older advice, 2021)* [L0853.060]
- Shopify Payments typically pays out within 48 hours. Sales can be used to buy products before payment clears if you have cash reserves. [L0941.091]
- Shopify Payments deposits funds within about two business days. Deposits are fast enough that a retailer should not need to pre-fund supplier orders. *(older advice, 2020)* [L0967.060]
- Shopify Payments deposits are usually made within 48 hours. If you want to send the order to the supplier immediately, you can do so before payment arrives, since the supplier charges your card on file. [L0835.119]
- Payment processing from customer to your bank account typically takes 2 to 3 days, but can be faster, sometimes next day or same day, depending on the payment gateway and your bank. If you pay a supplier before those funds arrive, the money is still coming because the customer has already paid. *(older advice, 2021-22)* [L1480.021]

### Limits on the Shopify balance

- Do not rely on the Shopify Payments balance for working capital. Withdrawals are limited to 5,000 dollars per payout, which can block funds needed for orders. [L1469.041]
- Shopify Balance can be used as a short-term source of funds for scaling ad spend, but only for stores on Shopify Payments and only for US residents. Treat it as a stopgap and focus on getting a business credit card as the longer-term funding source. [L1124.019]

### Matching supplier and ad payments to cash inflows

- Scaling requires cash flow to cover the MOQ purchase up front, because Shopify payouts may take longer than supplier and ad payments. Suppliers with net terms, such as about 14 to 20 days, and credit cards with a 30-day billing window can help match outflows. *(older advice, 2020)* [L0351.026]
- When stock must be paid before sales come in, some established sellers cover the gap with business lending or other financing rather than relying only on cash. Plan for month-end inventory bills so that a slow month does not force a sudden sale of assets. [L0690.043]
- When a store sells, the customer's money goes into your own account, and you pay the supplier separately for each order using the payment method you agree on with them. *(older advice, 2024)* [L0965.068]
- Plan cash flow before peak sales: ad spend on credit cards and fast budget increases quickly exhaust cash, so arrange extra funding in advance. [L1363.042]

### Funding stock with credit cards (routine)

Use this routine when the business credit card limit is too low to fund orders. Sources: L0934.014 and L0934.140 are the same routine, merged here. *(older advice, 2023)*

1. Max out the business credit card every day to pay supplier orders as they come in.
2. Pay the card off in full every night.
3. Call the card issuer every day and request a higher limit, repeating until the limit rises gradually.
4. Apply early for an American Express charge card as a second card.
5. Optionally open a second credit card from another issuer and rotate between both cards while continuing the nightly payoff and limit requests.
6. When no credit is left, wait for Shopify payouts (about 48 hours) before paying the supplier, or use a debit card as a backup even though it earns no points.
7. Spend the first one to two months building credit limits and cash reserves this way.

[L0934.014, L0934.140]

### Short-term loans and lines of credit

| Product | Term or condition | Cost | Units |
|---|---|---|---|
| American Express working capital | Repaid within about 30 days | Near 0 to about 1% | L0247.099 |
| American Express working capital | Repaid over 60 or 90 days | Rates rise | L0247.099 |
| Amex short-term loan | 30, 60 or 90 day term, repaid within window | Cheap; interest accrues after the period ends | L0358.040 |
| Line of credit | Only after ad spend is proven | Not needed at the start | L0804.100 |

- PayPal offers business loans to eligible accounts. *(older advice, 2021)* [L0247.099]
- Short-term business loans such as Amex short-term loans with 30, 60, or 90 day terms can be cheap if repaid within the window, but interest begins to accrue once the period ends. Compare the fees of each lender and confirm you can repay within the allotted period before borrowing. *(older advice, 2020)* [L0358.040]
- A line of credit is not needed at the start. It only makes sense once ad spend is proven to turn clicks into customers within two to three days and is profitable. *(older advice, 2023)* [L0804.100]

### Checkout financing

- When a customer pays through a financing provider at checkout, the store receives the full order amount right away, so the merchant does not finance the purchase. If cash is short before the first payout arrives, a credit card can cover the supplier order and be paid off later, while Shopify Payments payouts typically arrive within about 48 hours. *(older advice, 2023)* [L1283.110]

### Peak season and reserves

- Around the end of the year, banks and suppliers can slow down because many people are on holiday, while order volume peaks. Keep reserve cash or a backup payment source to cover fulfillment during the peak period so that payout delays do not stall orders. [L1469.046]
- Treat cash as profit only after the money is in the bank, all expenses are paid, and the return window has closed. Keep a cash reserve for returns and unexpected costs. *(older advice, 2022)* [L0803.067]

### Currency conversion

- Revenue earned in US dollars and converted into another currency is valued at the exchange rate on the day the money is transferred. A stronger US dollar adds value only if the transfer happens at a favorable rate, so compare the transfer-day rate with the sale-day rate before counting any gain. [L0667.092]

## Setting Up Shopify Payments and Payment Gateways

### Shopify Payments: What It Is and What It Covers

- Shopify Payments accepts credit cards, debit cards, Apple Pay and Google Pay. [L0096.007]
- It runs on Stripe, a separate company that handles card processing and can request its own checks. [L0677.040, L0272.046]
- It accepts American Express, Discover and Mastercard, plus Apple Pay and Google Pay, without extra apps. [L0470.043]
- Shop Pay Installments is built in and is the equivalent of Klarna. [L0272.046]
- The Shopify admin includes Shop Pay as a checkout option, payout and cashflow tracking, and chargeback management. [L1167.026]
- It automatically shows options such as Apple Pay, Shop Pay and Mastercard at checkout. [L1377.046]
- Customer payments are deposited into the linked bank account. Customers can also use other payment methods alongside it. [L0934.135, L0934.015]
- It is managed under Payments in the Shopify admin. Analytics and finance reports are kept in the same Shopify business admin. [L0934.015]
- Shopify Payments is the preferred processor. Linking a third-party merchant account is possible but not recommended. [L0934.016, L1111.181]
- Use Shopify Payments whenever possible. Third-party processors add Shopify transaction fees on top of their own fees. [L0470.043, L0805.034]
- Setup needs only bank account details and takes about five minutes. [L1111.181]
- Setup is configured under the Shopify settings area and takes only a few minutes. [L0836.004]
- The bank details needed are the bank account number, routing number and checking account number. After setup, the processor accepts credit cards from customers worldwide and deposits the sales into the bank account. [L0759.010]

### Country Availability and Eligibility

- Shopify Payments is available in specific countries only. [L0272.046]
- *Older list, 2019:* Merchants in Australia, Canada, Denmark, Germany, Hong Kong, Ireland, Japan, the Netherlands, New Zealand, Singapore, Spain, the UK and the US could use Shopify Payments. Businesses elsewhere were told to use Stripe. [L0357.048]
- *Listed in another lesson:* Shopify Payments is available in the USA, Canada, Australia, Germany and the UK. In countries where it is not available, Shopify offers other payment options. [L0759.009]
- Shopify Payments works in dozens of countries, so banking limitations do not automatically block the store. Check the country's availability before choosing another solution. [L0967.064]
- The Netherlands is an available country. A Dutch company with a Dutch bank account can use Shopify Payments to accept payments from customers anywhere in the world. [L1088.082]
- Availability may depend on the account's country of residence. If it is not available, PayPal can be added as a second payment option. [L0855.127]
- Shopify Payments is not always available to every seller, so check access before planning around it. [L1158.051]
- Where Shopify Payments is not available, such as Mexico at the time of the lesson, use a payment gateway from the country's provider list. Switch to Shopify Payments once it is available there. [L0469.044]
- If Shopify Payments is not available and 2Checkout cannot be used, connect the main local payment processor to Shopify, because Shopify integrates with almost any processor. Check the local processor's Shopify integration before signing up. [L0359.113]
- Shopify integrates with over 100 payment processors. [L1088.084]
- Stripe acceptance can differ by country. Stripe India showed lower acceptance and some banks blocked transactions, which led one seller to move to a company abroad. [L1512.019]
- Before launching in a new country, enter the shipping details for that country in checkout and confirm that the payment providers work there. A checkout that fails for one target country loses sales before any ads are paid for. [L0661.054]

### Choosing a Payment Option

- A payment gateway (payment processor) accepts card payments on an online store and forwards the funds to the seller's bank account. Reliable access to one is the basic requirement for receiving store revenue. [L1512.002]
- Procedure to choose payment options for a store:
  1. Check which payment options are available in your country at shopify.com/payment-gateways.
  2. If Shopify Payments is available, start with it.
  3. If not, check Stripe as the alternative.
  4. Add PayPal (it needs a business account once business transactions begin, with company details and fulfillment method).
  5. If no suitable gateway is available at home, form a company in a country that offers them, such as the US or Hong Kong.

  [L1512.003]
- For beginners, use Shopify Payments if it is available, because it avoids most chargebacks and saves 1 to 2 percent in fees. [L1158.036]
- With Shopify Payments in a country where it is available, a store can often test products and scale to about 1,000 to 15,000 USD in monthly sales without a large company setup. Starting out, the US owner's social security number is usually enough to open it. [L1512.008]
- Enable Shopify Payments where it is available. Stripe and PayPal are the other common and trusted processors, and Shopify displays the methods supported by the processor you connect. [L1377.046]
- Alternatives when Shopify Payments is unavailable include Skrill, which is common in some Asian and African countries, plus Amazon Pay and Airwallex. Amazon Pay and Airwallex require creating an account on their websites and connecting their API to Shopify. [L1512.015]

### Activating Shopify Payments

- Under Settings > Payments, click the button labeled Activate Shopify Payments. [L0096.007, L1306.010]
- Procedure to prepare the store before activation:
  1. Open Settings (gear icon, bottom-left corner).
  2. Open Store details and review the fields (store name, store email, store phone) so you know where to edit them later, without changing email or phone yet.
  3. Go to Settings > Payments and click Activate Shopify Payments.
  4. Choose Shopify Balance as the deposit destination for the initial payouts.
  5. Leave all other settings at their defaults.

  [L0096.009]
- Procedure for activating Shopify Payments through Shopify Balance:
  1. Open Settings with the gear icon at the bottom left.
  2. Click Payments.
  3. Click Activate Shopify Payments.
  4. Answer the setup questions about where the money should go.
  5. Select a Shopify Balance account as the destination so payments land there instantly.
  6. Withdraw funds from the Shopify Balance account to your bank account.

  [L1306.011]
- Shopify Payments is set up inside Shopify so customers can pay by credit card, debit card or Apple Pay, and the money is paid out directly to the seller's bank account. [L1304.004]
- Of all the settings covered, activating Shopify Payments is the one that must be completed before the store is ready to take orders. [L1306.024]
- The billing address in Store details must match the address linked to the card on file with Shopify, since it is the account's billing address. [L1306.004]
- Create a separate Shopify Payments account for each store through the payment integration, rather than linking one Stripe account directly. Shopify Payments is powered by Stripe. [L0852.095]
- Each Shopify store needs its own Shopify account. Several accounts can share one login email using single sign-on, but one merchant account number cannot be used across several websites. [L0934.136]

#### Example: Activation in Australia (older, 2021-22)

- Shopify Payments in Australia costs 1.75% plus $0.30 per card transaction. Other third-party payment gateways typically charge 2% per transaction. [L0677.010]
- Shopify Payments in Australia accepts major credit cards, Shop Pay, Apple Pay and Google Pay. After activation, the Payments screen shows the last two digits of the connected payout bank account. [L0677.012]
- Procedure to activate Shopify Payments in Australia:
  1. Go to Settings > Payments.
  2. Click Activate Shopify Payments.
  3. Enter your bank account details (account numbers differ by country).
  4. Enter information about the products you will sell.
  5. Enter personal details and legal business name.
  6. Enter your business number (ABN in Australia, a federal number such as the EIN in the US).
  7. Submit and wait for Shopify to review the account.

  [L0677.011]
- Bank account fields for Shopify Payments and PayPal differ by country because banking systems are structured differently. Fill in the fields the local form asks for, using the business bank account. [L0677.044]

### Setup Order and First-Day Readiness

- Set up your payment processors as part of the initial store build, so orders can be taken from the first day the store opens. *Older advice, 2021.* [L0801.142]
- Open the business bank account before connecting checkout payments during the store build, because the gateway setup needs it. Payment gateways that take customer checkout payments must be linked to a business bank account, which receives the sales money. *Older advice, 2021-22.* [L0702.029]
- Checkout is not usable until at least one payment gateway is connected. The recommended pair is Shopify Payments and PayPal. *Older advice, 2021-22.* [L1145.004]
- Payment setup is the first gate to launch. A merchant account is the intermediary that accepts card payments and forwards the money to the seller's bank account. Shopify Payments works with any bank and can be used without opening a separate merchant account at first. [L0697.004]

### Identity, Address and Account Verification

- Shopify Payments can be applied for in the payment section of the Shopify admin and is usually approved right away. Shopify can check credit, and if volume grows it may ask for identity verification such as a driver license or passport photo. Account reserves are uncommon. *Older advice, 2021.* [L0968.027]
- Shopify or Stripe may ask for further identification at any time after activation, even months later. Provide the requested documents, and the account will be reviewed and activated. *Older advice, 2021-22.* [L0677.013]
- Both companies review new accounts because anyone can open a Shopify store. The review aims to confirm the owner is a legitimate business that will deliver orders. *Older advice, 2021-22.* [L0677.040]
- If Shopify Payments places the account on standby for identity checks, make sure the name on the account matches the name on the ID, including any middle name. Update the account and reply to the verification request. *Older advice, 2020.* [L1111.143]
- Processors ask for company documents mainly because a business that receives large amounts of money is treated as higher risk. Shopify Payments usually does not ask for a company until volumes become high. [L1512.010]
- Without proof of address, a seller cannot open Shopify Payments, so Stripe may be the only processor available. In one case, the seller had PayPal restrict the account and Stripe was used instead. [L1127.007]
- Stripe needs a tax number for the business owner or company during account approval, and every platform asks for the same tax number. Have the EIN and tax residency documents ready before applying. [L1127.008]

### Payout Destination and Schedule

- The deposit destination can be set to the Shopify Balance account or to a bank account. [L0096.008]
- Shopify Balance is recommended for the first payouts because funds are instantly accessible and can be transferred to a bank later. [L0096.008, L1306.012]
- Shopify Balance receives customer payments instantly, and money is then withdrawn to a bank account. For beginners this payout route is recommended over a direct bank payout. [L1306.012]
- The payout destination can be changed at any time. [L0096.008, L1306.013]
- Shopify Payments can be set to pay out directly to your own bank account, instead of through a third-party processor. The lesson's store uses Shopify Payments only and no third-party gateway for payouts. [L1327.015]
- Shopify Payments deposits into the bank account linked in Shopify. You then pay each supplier yourself from that money. *Older advice, 2020.* [L1277.108]

#### Payout timing by source

Sources give different timings. Each applies to the period it describes:

| Source | Timing stated |
|---|---|
| 2019 lesson | Usually about 48 hours; can take longer over holidays [L1110.041] |
| 2020 lesson | 48 hours, shorter with more established accounts; eventually 24-hour deposits [L0268.048] |
| 2021 lesson | About 48 hours after the order [L0247.112] |
| 2022 lesson | Within 2 business days of order placement [L0249.071] |
| 2023 lesson | Typically within 48 hours; expected timing shown when the account is opened [L1283.053] |
| 2024 lessons | Within 48 to 72 hours [L0253.013]; typically within 48 hours of an order [L0360.067] |
| Source D 2026 | New accounts start at 21 days after the sale [L1327.016] |

- *Older advice, 2021-22:* A payout schedule of 2 business days from order placement means no upfront capital is required. [L0249.071]
- Plan cash flow around the 48-hour delay. [L0247.112]
- PayPal payouts typically arrive about one day after withdrawal. [L1283.053]
- A longer hold, such as a seven-day hold, typically signals previous chargebacks or a high-risk account. *Older advice, 2019.* [L1110.041]
- Payout progression for a new account: payouts start at 21 days after the sale, then move to 14 days, 7 days, 5 days, 3 days and finally daily payouts as the store fulfills orders consistently. A new Shopify account is at the 21-day stage first. [L1327.016]

| Payout stage | Days after the sale |
|---|---|
| Start (new account) | 21 |
| Step 2 | 14 |
| Step 3 | 7 |
| Step 4 | 5 |
| Step 5 | 3 |
| Final | Daily [L1327.016] |

- Withdrawal limits on Shopify can be raised by asking Shopify support to increase the daily payout limit. At a larger scale, get a proper business bank account to avoid cash flow problems. [L1469.042]
- Amazon payouts go to the seller's bank account every two weeks, and they can go to any bank account in a supported country. Plan cash flow around this two-week cycle when reinvesting in inventory. [L1153.076]
- Payouts from the store are converted to the payout account currency. Example: the payout account shows in SGD while the store currency is USD. [L0351.031]

### Fees and Plan Comparison

#### Shopify Payments rates by plan

Credit card rates by plan, as shown in the lesson:

| Plan | Monthly price | Credit card rate |
|---|---|---|
| Basic | not stated | 2.9% plus 30 cents per transaction |
| Mid plan | 105 USD | 2.6% |
| Advanced | 400 USD | 2.4% |

The rate depends on the plan, not on order size. [L0470.041]

- To decide whether a higher Shopify plan pays off, compare the rate saving on your card sales with the monthly plan cost. For example, saving 0.5% on sales must exceed the extra monthly plan fee. [L0470.042]
- The Shopify Payments processing rate can be improved by upgrading the Shopify plan. [L0351.030]

#### Regional rates

- Singapore-based store, Shopify Basic plan, as shown in the lesson: 3.2% plus S$0.50 per online transaction and 3% plus S$0.50 per in-person transaction. The Basic plan was shown at S$348 per year, with a monthly option. These are the starting baseline for a Singapore setup. [L1327.010]
- Shopify Payments in Australia: 1.75% plus $0.30 per card transaction; other third-party gateways typically 2%. *Older advice, 2021-22.* [L0677.010]
- Typical payment processor fees for new sellers are 2.9% plus $0.30 per transaction. Processors such as Stripe, Shopify Pay and PayPal charge these fees on card payments. *Older advice, 2021-22.* [L1148.013]
- Stripe has no account fee but takes about 2% of each transaction, so a 40 dollar sale can lose roughly 2 to 3 dollars before payout. [L0678.041]
- High-volume and high-risk rates are covered in the section on higher-risk processors below. [L0363.065]

#### Fees when Shopify Payments is not used

- *Older advice, 2021:* Shopify charges a 2 percent transaction fee on the Basic plan when Shopify Payments is not used. [L0469.055]
- Source C: if Shopify Payments is not used, a fee of about 1 percent, 1.5 percent or 2 percent applies on the basic Shopify plan, on top of the processor's own fees. [L1158.038]

#### Calculator and checks

- Check the gateway formula in the calculator against the payment rate your Shopify Payments account shows, which the example sets at 5.5% of the order price plus $0.30. Change the formula if your rate differs, because the gateway fee is the largest variable cost after COGS on a low-priced product. [L0986.017]
- Use the net payout in the finance sheet, not the gross order value. [L0678.041]
- Worked example (older advice, 2021-22): a PayPal fee on a 500 order at 2.9% plus a 0.30 static fee: 500 × 2.9% = 14.50, plus 0.30 gives a processor fee of 14.80. Use this same method to check any processor fee before entering it. [L1201.025]
- For a store that sells in one market only, the Shopify Markets fee input should stay at zero. A store that uses Markets must weight the Markets fee by the share of total revenue that comes from Markets orders, not apply the full fee rate to all orders. [L0433.017]

### Plan Costs and Billing

- Shopify Settings > Billing shows the upcoming bill, which is issued at the threshold (example: S$260) or at the end of the billing cycle, whichever comes first. [L0351.030]
- Settings > Plan: check the plan, the card rates and the billing cycle. Shopify bills the store in cycles and charges for apps and usage on the same account, so app costs must be counted with the plan. [L1327.012]
- Shopify bills app charges and usage-based fees such as transaction fees on the store account at the end of the billing cycle, or earlier once a usage threshold is reached. The Billing page shows the next bill date and the amount due, so check it before installing paid apps. [L1327.066]

### Payment Capture

- Procedure to activate payments and test the checkout, in this order:
  1. Go to Settings > Payments and click Activate Shopify Payments.
  2. Choose the payment capture method. Payments were authorized when an order is placed and captured Automatically at checkout in the example.
  3. Open the preview of your checkout and check that products, prices and countries display correctly.
  4. Once Shopify Payments is active, run a test checkout from the test option that appears.

  [L1167.025]

### Stripe: Terms, Scaling and Account Rules

- Stripe allows fast-shipping dropshipping (orders arrive in 1-3 weeks) but does not allow slow-shipping dropshipping from overseas (orders take weeks, high chargeback rates). Dropshipping from US suppliers with fast delivery fits Stripe's terms. *Older advice, 2022.* [L0272.060]
- Stripe may close accounts it classes as high risk, and drop-shipping from China is one reason. Check the provider's risk policy before building a store around a payment processor. *Older advice, 2021.* [L0469.045]
- A Stripe account connected to a business bank account does not require a store URL linked to the LLC or a company web page. An LLC does not need its own website to set up Stripe. [L0808.044]
- You can only use one payment processor at a time. Shopify Payments is built on Stripe, so you cannot enable Shopify Payments and a separate Stripe account together. [L1158.035]
- Shopify Payments can cause problems once monthly revenue reaches about 20,000 to 30,000 dollars, or up to 50,000 to 60,000 per month, due to chargebacks. Stripe is preferred for scaling stores because it offers more payment features. [L1158.037]
- Once a store scales at high volume, consider Stripe or another processor for better features. [L1158.036]
- Stripe runs on the same infrastructure as Shopify Payments. If a processor stops supporting a store, check with the bank whether it is due to the industry. *Older advice, 2020.* [L1088.084]

### Merchant Accounts and Gateways

- A merchant account is the intermediary that accepts customer card payments (credit or debit) and forwards the money to the seller's bank account. [L0697.004]
- Authorize.net is only a gateway, so it needs a separate merchant account at a bank or processor to deposit your money. Start with Shopify Payments. *Older advice, 2021.* [L0671.075, L0671.212]
- When applying for a merchant account, state a realistic expected monthly volume, for example between $5,000 and $10,000. Very high numbers look like more risk to the processor, and the volume can grow over time. *Older advice, 2021.* [L0671.076, L0671.213]
- If Shopify Payments is not available, open a merchant account directly with your own bank and connect it to Shopify, either through a direct integration or a gateway such as authorized.net. Third-party processors such as Checkout.com that integrate with Shopify are a faster alternative. *Older advice, 2021.* [L0968.028]
- Linking a third-party merchant account to Shopify is possible but not recommended. Shopify Payments is the preferred processor. [L0934.016]
- Shopify Payments has strict terms for supplements, so sellers of such products may need a third-party merchant account instead. *Older advice, 2022.* [L1113.044]
- *Older advice, 2019:* Shopify can work with other payment processors, but PayPal Payments Pro is not recommended. You can instead open a merchant account through your bank and compare terms and rates; keeping Shopify Payments is simpler. [L1110.043]

### Other Payment Methods and Processors

- Settings > Payments has a Manual payment methods section for payments taken outside the online store. Orders paid this way must be approved before they are fulfilled. *Older advice, 2021-22.* [L0677.042]
- Settings > Payments also lists Third-party providers, which charge the provider's own rate, and Alternative payment methods, which can be added on top of Shopify Payments or a third-party provider. *Older advice, 2021-22.* [L0677.043]
- Settings > Payments shows a "See all other providers" option for using a payment provider other than Shopify Payments. Shopify Payments is still required to manage each market's base currency and to offer local currency checkout, so a different provider means giving those up. [L1167.041]
- PayPal needs a business account once business transactions begin, with company details and fulfillment method. [L1512.003]

### Higher-Risk Processors and Restricted Products

- High-volume payment processing needs a higher-risk payment provider when sales spike suddenly, since PayPal and Stripe may freeze funds. Authorized.net was cited as a high-risk processor, charging about 5-6%. [L0363.065]
- Payment processors may place a store under a standby review. Keep identity documents ready, because the review can occur at any time. [L0677.013]
- Stripe may close accounts it classes as high risk, and drop-shipping from China is one reason. Check the provider's risk policy before building a store around a payment processor. *Older advice, 2021.* [L0469.045]
- Shopify Payments has strict terms for supplements, so sellers of such products may need a third-party merchant account instead. *Older advice, 2022.* [L1113.044]

### Checkout Trust Signals

- Anxiety-reducing payment and checkout signals: a 'secure checkout' label on the button, secure payment logos, and a note that transactions are encrypted and protected. These reassure the buyer that they will not be scammed. [L0919.027]

### Shopify Markets, Currency and Local Pricing

- Shopify Markets requires Shopify Payments, so a store that only uses Stripe cannot set up markets and must price everything in one currency, such as USD. Check the payment setup before planning multi-market selling. [L0694.025]
- Local pricing shows customers prices in their local currency and requires Shopify Payments to be active. Without Shopify Payments, you cannot manage the base currency for a market. [L1167.028]
- Set the store currency in Settings to match the target market. Without Shopify Payments (for example, when using Stripe), customers see only the store currency, such as USD. With Shopify Payments, customers can check out in local currencies. Use USD only for USD-targeted markets. [L1260.047]
- Set the store currency inside Shopify to your local currency, such as pounds for a UK seller. Shopify Payments converts currency automatically for worldwide sales. *Older advice, 2020.* [L0852.096]
- If Shopify Payments is not available and another gateway such as Stripe is used, checkout may show only the store currency, for example USD. Shopify Payments lets customers pay in their local currency. [L1345.066]
- Use the local currency of each target market. Use USD only when the store targets USD markets. [L1345.067]

#### Procedure: check markets before sending traffic

1. Go to Settings > Markets.
2. Check which countries are active, for example United States, Canada, Australia, and United Kingdom.
3. Activate every target country that is inactive.
4. Place a test order with a random address in each target country to confirm checkout works.

[L1345.064]

#### Case: inactive markets blocked sales

- Starting point: a newly created store had traffic and a high add-to-cart rate but almost no sales. Cause: only the primary market was active, and other countries were inactive, so buyers from them could not complete checkout. Result: roughly 100 to 200 sales were lost within one hour before the market was activated. [L1345.065]

### Test Mode and Test Checkouts

- If Shopify Payments is already set up, put Shopify Payments into test mode using Shopify's own guide before placing a test order, so the test runs through the payment system without charging a real customer. [L1302.003]
- Settings > Payments > Shopify Payments > Test mode: switch it on to place a test order and see how payments and orders flow through the store before launch. [L1327.065]
- To test payment providers cheaply, create a 95% discount code, then complete a real checkout using PayPal, Shopify Payments and Stripe (or the other providers in use). [L0773.008]
- Procedure after changing the store currency or payment setup:
  1. Make a test purchase yourself.
  2. Check that no error message appears at checkout.
  3. Repeat the test after provider updates, because payment providers sometimes need updates that break checkout.

  [L1185.021]

### Fixing a Payment Error That Blocks Checkout

- Procedure for a payment error that blocks checkout:
  1. Check the Shopify orders to confirm customers add their details but fail to pay.
  2. Deactivate the payment provider in Shopify.
  3. Reactivate the payment provider.
  4. Create a 95% or 99% discount code and place a test order yourself to confirm checkout works.

  [L1216.010]
- Uninstalling and reinstalling a broken payment provider app can fix setup errors. [L0773.008]

## Payment Methods, PayPal, Financing and Local Options

### Payment setup and checkout options

- Offer every common payment method, not card payment alone, because more options mean fewer drop-offs at checkout. [L1098.016, L0268.049, L0894.038]
- In Shopify Payments, turn on Apple Pay, Google Pay, PayPal, PayPal Credit and Amazon Pay so shoppers can pay with the method they prefer. [L1098.015]
- Show the full range of accepted methods: credit card, debit card, and express checkout with Shop Pay, Google Pay or PayPal. [L0894.038]
- Express wallets such as Apple Pay and Google Pay are especially useful on phones, where one tap can complete the order. [L1098.016]
- Amazon Pay and Shopify Payments add options for customers. They do not produce many sales on their own, but they are worth turning on. [L0856.080]
- A checkout guarantee listed these accepted payment methods: Visa, Mastercard, Amex, Discover and PayPal. [L0577.068]
- "Accept Multiple Payment Options" was named as a resource in a December 2020 coaching call. The lesson gave no setup steps. [L0426.008]
- Additional providers such as PayPal are added under Settings > Payments > Payment configuration. [L0351.032]
- Fee examples differ by store:
  - In one example, PayPal charged no transaction fee, but PayPal processing fees still applied. [L0351.032]
  - In another example store, PayPal was added with a 2% transaction fee, and its account setup had to be completed before PayPal payouts could be received. [L1167.027]
- Installment providers can be added at the bottom of the checkout area, with a link to the provider's terms. Showing the option in the cart warns customers in advance that they can pay in installments at checkout. [L1231.051]
- Confirm that the local payment methods buyers expect are enabled before launching ads in a country. A missing payment option can lose sales even when traffic and ad results are good. [L1185.044]
- One store with a single payment processor saw conversion fall to about half of its previous level. Adding PayPal raised conversion again. [L1345.071]
- A missing PayPal option once halved conversion for one seller. [L1260.048]
- Shopify Payments and Stripe mainly process credit and debit cards. PayPal is a separate system that customers widely recognise and trust. [L1512.004]
- PayPal and Shop Pay redirect customers away from the site during checkout, as Bread and Klarna do. Sales then appear as referral traffic to the payment provider instead of being attributed to the traffic source that brought the customer. [L0256.018]

### Apple Pay

- Apple Pay usually comes with Shopify Payments once payments are set up in the store. [L0352.059]
- Shopify Payments or Stripe bring Apple Pay as a checkout option. If it is missing, check the payment provider settings and test a payment to confirm it works. [L0774.050]
- Apple Pay can still fail to appear on a TikTok link even when it is enabled, so check the link on a phone after setup. [L0352.059]
- Apple Pay must appear on the link itself to work for TikTok buyers. If it does not show, follow these steps (older advice, Source C):
  1. Open Shopify Settings > Payments.
  2. Switch Apple Pay off and on again.
  3. Return to the TikTok link, search the link again if needed, and place it once more. [L0352.014]

### PayPal

#### Role and timing

Advice on PayPal conflicts across sources, so the conditions for each view are given.

- Most buyers pay with credit cards or Apple Pay, so PayPal is optional for a new store. [L0352.016]
- Avoid PayPal as a payment or payout method at the foundation stage. The lesson's reasoning is that PayPal approves transactions slowly and can limit the account after returns or chargebacks, while Shopify Payments is the stable in-house option. [L1327.018]
- Offer PayPal Express Checkout alongside Shopify Payments from the start, because some shoppers will not buy from a store without PayPal. If the business is registered in one country and sells into another, PayPal may refuse to work, so this option may not be available. [L0677.041]
- Offer PayPal as an additional payment option, because a share of online buyers prefer it and it adds trust. [L0667.057]
- PayPal can raise trust and conversion, but payouts can take weeks, funds can be held, and buyers can request chargebacks more easily than with credit cards. Weigh these risks against the conversion gain before enabling it. [L0352.015]
- One seller disabled PayPal because payouts were slow and chargebacks were harder to contest than card chargebacks. A few PayPal sales may be lost when deciding whether to offer it. [L1381.045]
- Credit card chargebacks are easier to contest than PayPal disputes. [L0352.016]
- Some buyers will only pay through PayPal. PayPal may hold a new seller's funds for several days, and the length of that hold is not known (2025). [L0472.047]
- Germany requires a PayPal account for payments, since most German customers pay with PayPal. [L1184.051]

#### Account structure

- PayPal should never be the only merchant account. [L0970.142]
- Relying on PayPal alone is risky because accounts can be problematic. PayPal can be used as an additional payment option alongside another processor. (2020, older) [L0967.061]
- PayPal should not be the primary payment method. Withdraw PayPal funds to the bank every one to two days so money is not held up. (2020, older) [L1088.083]
- A single PayPal business account can handle payments for several Shopify stores, and Stripe allows separate profiles for different stores. [L1382.062]
- One PayPal account under a company name can handle payments for several Shopify stores. Stripe can use separate profiles for different stores. [L1436.073]
- A business PayPal account is required because customers commonly pay through PayPal, even if the owner dislikes the platform. (Source B, 2021-22, older) [L1415.015]
- Getting a PayPal account is harder without a registered company. PayPal asks business accounts for business details once sales start. This is a common reason why beginners need a company before they can use PayPal fully. [L1512.005]
- Non-US owners of a US company generally struggle to get a US PayPal account, because it requires a US tax ID (EIN) that can take about 2 to 3 months. A PayPal account from the owner's home country is the more practical choice. [L1512.035]
- PayPal states on a dedicated Hong Kong page that it supports dropshipping for businesses there. Check this policy directly on PayPal before relying on it, as rules can change. [L1512.029]
- PayPal financing should only be used in countries where it is available. For split payments that combine PayPal with other cards, ask support for custom payment links. [L1115.032]

#### Setup

- Activating PayPal Express Checkout creates a PayPal account automatically, using the email address from the Shopify signup. PayPal emails instructions for setting up a PayPal business account and claiming funds after the first sale. (Source B, 2021-22, older) [L0677.014]
- Complete PayPal business account setup (Source B, 2021-22, older):
  1. In Settings > Payments, click Complete account setup under PayPal Express Checkout.
  2. Enter the primary business email, country (Australia) and create a password.
  3. Select the business type.
  4. Enter business details: the business type or trust, the primary authorised user and the people associated with the business. Have this information ready; it takes about 5-10 minutes.
  5. Confirm the email address and any identity documents PayPal requests, such as a driver's licence or passport photo.
  6. Log in to the PayPal business back end.
  7. Go to Pay & get paid and link your business bank account (Banks > Link a new bank account). [L0677.016]
- To withdraw money from PayPal, you need to link a business bank account. A card only needs to be linked if you want PayPal to charge it when paying on other websites, and it cannot receive payouts. [L0677.017]
- If PayPal is missing in the payment settings, check Settings > Payments, where it should appear as an additional payment option. If it does not, use the live chat on the Shopify help page and ask to speak to a human, who can walk you through adding it. [L1287.065]

#### Payout sweeps and balance risk

- Set up a business PayPal account under the LLC or EIN, connect it to the business bank account, then contact PayPal support via live chat and ask them to enable auto sweep. Auto sweep transfers incoming funds to the bank account daily, automatically. (Source B, 2021-22, older) [L1415.013]
- Limiting PayPal balance risk (2023):
  1. When an order ships, mark it as shipped in PayPal and enter the tracking number.
  2. Do this for every order as soon as tracking numbers are available.
  3. Every night, withdraw the PayPal balance to the bank account. [L0970.144]

#### Reference table: PayPal share of orders (Source C)

Share of orders paid via PayPal in two stores of one operator. The advice was that PayPal is good to have but not required.

| Store | Share of transactions via PayPal |
|---|---|
| Store A | 30-40% |
| Store B | 20-30% |

[L1512.006]

### Financing: how it pays the merchant

#### Mechanics

- The merchant receives the full payment immediately as a standard credit card transaction. Shopify handles all customer financing, installment scheduling and payment collection. [L0153.003]
- With third-party financing, the merchant gets paid in full right away, while the customer repays the financing provider. [L0894.031, L0971.070, L1283.047]
- Payments through financing options such as PayPal Pay Later or Affirm pay the store in full right away. The customer repays the financing provider. (2024) [L0971.070]
- Even when a customer pays in installments, the merchant receives the full order amount immediately, the same as with a card or Apple Pay. The customer repays the financing to Shopify, so the store does not collect or manage the installment payments. [L1296.002]
- With Shop Pay Installments, the merchant receives the full order amount immediately, just like a normal sale. Repayment schedule and billing are handled between the customer and Shopify, so the merchant does not chase payments or manage loans. [L1295.004]
- With third-party financing, the financing company approves the customer and pays the store from day one, as with a credit card. If the customer does not pay the financier in time, the financier handles collection, not the store. (2020, older) [L0551.067]
- Affirm, Klarna and PayPal Credit are free to set up, with no setup fee. They pay the merchant upfront and take on the customer's debt, so the merchant has no collection work. (2019, older) [L1069.060]
- Financing options such as Shop Pay Installments, Afterpay or Klarna let customers spread payments, while the store is paid right away. This helps during inflation or a recession. (2022, older) [L1092.020]
- Customers can pay in installments through Shop Pay Installments, Klarna or Affirm. The merchant receives the full amount right away, as if the customer paid in full, while the customer pays the financing company. (2023) [L1283.047]

#### When to offer financing

- Financing can be offered on high-ticket products. [L0894.031, L0247.110]
- Financing is not needed for low-priced products such as t-shirts. Offer it only where order values justify it. [L0894.032]
- Offering installment payments on expensive products is a good idea because it gives customers more options to pay, even though financing is not a large share of revenue. (2019, older) [L1069.061]
- Accepting financing through a credit option such as PayPal Credit can be offered on higher-priced items. It does not require a separate medical or insurance billing setup. (2020, older) [L0664.029]
- Installment and credit terms can increase sales, but they are most effective when offered to people whose good standing has been vouched for by a trusted person. Open accounts without investigation only for referred customers. [L1261.126]
- Apply for a financing app as part of store setup so customers have payment-over-time options at checkout. [L0532.014]
- A buy-now-pay-later provider application, such as Klarna or Affirm, can be slow. Follow up by email or live chat with the business name you applied under, check the application status in your provider control panel, and apply to a second provider as a backup. (2021, older) [L0801.143]
- Shop Pay is approved instantly through Shopify Payments, and it can be turned off in Settings > Payments in the Shopify admin if you do not want it. Buy-now-pay-later options such as Klarna, Afterpay or Affirm usually take about two weeks up to one to three months for approval, but can be swapped in later for higher credit limits. (2022, older) [L1281.067]
- Payment expectations by price (Source C): Klarna or Afterpay works well for products above 60 dollars. [L1260.048, L1345.070]
- Show a monthly payment message on product pages with Shop Pay enabled. In the demo, orders over $50.00 can be paid in 4 interest-free installments. A sale price of $22.50 with a compare-at price of $25.00 showed this message below the price. Shoppable video that leads to a native checkout therefore also gets these Shop Pay installment options. [L0959.015]

#### Financing and fee economics

- Most PayPal Credit approvals are around $2,000 or less, so financing decisions should be based on the average order value. (2019, older) [L1069.063]
- Affirm and Klarna perform better when the average order value is about $2,000 or more, so test them for expensive products. (2019, older) [L1069.064]
- Klarna fee on a 3,000 order at 3.5% plus a 1.00 static fee: 3,000 × 3.5% = 105, plus 1.00 gives 106.00. With a 2,000 supplier total and 30 fraud insurance, switching that order from Shopify Payments (87.30 fee) to Klarna lowers net profit from 882.70 to 864.00. (Source B, 2021-22, older) [L1201.026]

#### Insurance as payment

- Accepting insurance as payment is only worth exploring if many competitors do it, because it requires an approval process. Selling without insurance acceptance can still perform well. (2020, older) [L0664.030]
- Insurance payments are optional for some product categories. (2020, older) [L0664.029]

#### Promotion and content

- Financing and installments are promoted with a how-to infographic. A prompt for building one follows. Use it only as written.

```
Please create an info graphic showing how to use [payment option, e.g. Shop Pay Installments] when checking out on a Shopify store. Before creating the infographic, check the current steps for using this payment option online, and show each step in order.
```

[L1075.021]

- Checkout steps to show in the infographic:
  1. After adding items to the cart, proceed to checkout.
  2. Select Shop Pay at checkout.
  3. Complete the purchase using the Shop Pay installment plan. [L1075.022]
- A how-to infographic for a payment option such as Shop Pay Installments can be placed on the financing page, embedded in the standard email that answers customer questions about financing, and used in customer communication. Adding the store's logo and colours makes it the store's own asset. (2020s, Source A) [L1075.023]
- Infographics about payment steps are built from steps ChatGPT finds online, so verify each step against the store's actual checkout before publishing the infographic. [L1075.027]

### Shop Pay Installments

#### Basics and requirements

- Shop Pay Installments is a native Shopify payment option that lets shoppers split a purchase into interest-free installments, usually two to four payments. It requires Shopify Payments and does not need a separate app. [L1296.001]
- Shop Pay Installments is a Shopify payment option that lets customers split a purchase into several payments at checkout. Check its eligibility rules for your country and order value before enabling it. [L1105.013]
- Shop Pay Installments lets customers split a checkout total into several payments. It is available in supported regions and can help sell higher-priced products. [L0675.006]
- Availability depends on Shopify Payments. The installment limits have been increased over the last few years, so check the current limits in the Shopify settings. [L0856.075]
- Shopify Payments must already be active before Shop Pay Installments can be turned on. Without it, the payment method cannot be configured. [L1296.006]
- Shop Pay Installments requires Shopify Payments as the payment processor. If a store does not use Shopify Payments, the feature cannot be used. The alternative is applying directly with Klarna or Affirm, which offer similar financing. [L1295.005]
- Shop Pay Installments is powered by Klarna for the financing part, while the integration is built into Shopify. Once enabled, the option appears on the store and in checkout without custom code. [L1295.006]
- Shop Pay Installments is the recommended installment option instead of Klarna, which received many negative reviews. Choose it for financing options in the store. (2025) [L1287.066]
- Use Shop Pay Installments for financing when Shopify Payments is active, instead of adding Klarna. Without Shopify Payments, contact Klarna support directly if an application is denied for a functioning URL. (2024) [L1115.031]
- Shop Pay Installments can replace Klarna for buy-now-pay-later. (2025) [L0667.057]

#### Limits: sources differ

| Source and date | Stated limit or condition | When it applies |
|---|---|---|
| Source A, 2021 | Up to $3,000 per order | Older guidance from 2021. Verify in current settings. [L0469.054] |
| Source A, about three years before the lesson | Maximum around $300, only for low-priced items | Launch-era limit. Older guides listing $300 are outdated. [L1295.018] |
| Source A (later lesson) | Cap raised to $30,000 | Current cap as stated in that lesson. [L1295.018] |
| Source E 2025-26 demo | Message shown for orders over $50.00 | Demo threshold for showing the message, not a limit. [L0959.015] |

- Shop Pay Installments was available only with a store-age and traffic-based invitation for Shopify Premium Installments, which show a monthly payment estimate on product pages. Merchants who want access can ask Shopify live support to raise the issue, and a supervisor said enough complaints could change the policy. (2025) [L1287.067]

#### Interaction with Klarna and direct applications

- Applying directly with Klarna can give a higher customer financing limit from the start, compared with Shop Pay Installments, which starts lower and grows with store history. For high average order values such as around $5,000, direct Klarna may be worth considering. (2022, older) [L0429.066]
- Shop Pay Installments is the native option when Shopify Payments is active. Direct Klarna applications are the alternative when it is not (see the conflicting guidance above). [L1295.005, L0429.066, L1115.031]

#### Setup

- Settings path: Shopify Admin > Settings (bottom-left corner) > Payments > Shopify Payments > Manage > Manage payment methods > Shop Pay Installments: toggle on. [L1296.007]
- Apple Pay and Google Pay should be enabled alongside Shop Pay Installments, so that shoppers have all the payment options available at checkout. [L1296.009]
- Setup procedure:
  1. Open the Shopify admin.
  2. Click Settings in the bottom-left corner.
  3. Click Payments.
  4. In the Shopify Payments section at the top, click Manage.
  5. Click Manage payment methods at the top of the page.
  6. Find Shop Pay Installments in the list of enabled payment methods (it was the second entry in the demo).
  7. If its toggle is off, switch it on.
  8. Confirm that Apple Pay and Google Pay are also turned on.
  9. No separate save click is needed, because the page saves automatically.
  10. Go back to a product page to confirm the installment messages appear. [L1296.008]

### Klarna

- Klarna is a financing option suited to expensive products, with approval depending on the customer's credit. Klarna's average merchant fee was estimated at about 4.5 percent (2021). This is unverified and should be checked on the current pricing page. [L0469.053]
- The 3.5% plus 1.00 static fee used in the Klarna example is a different figure from the 4.5% estimate. Use the rate on the current pricing page, and see the fee example under "Financing and fee economics." [L0469.053, L1201.026]
- A Canadian business selling to US customers on Klarna financing should most likely apply with the Klarna US entity, because the customers applying for credit are in the US. This is an informed guess rather than confirmed guidance (2022). [L0429.031]
- Klarna's Shopify setup, if the option does not show:
  1. Confirm that your Klarna approval is complete and the Klarna app is linked to your store.
  2. Follow Klarna's Shopify setup documentation step by step.
  3. If it still does not show in the store, contact Klarna support first.
  4. If the problem remains, use Shopify live chat and ask their team to check the Klarna setup on your store. (2020, older) [L0551.068]
- Klarna and Afterpay applications were not approved for one seller, so Affirm was recommended. (2020, older) [L0852.098]
- Klarna is expected in Germany, so the store should offer it. [L0361.040]
- Klarna is not available to a Swiss-based company for German use. A company based in Switzerland could install Swiss providers, but Klarna in Germany was not available from Switzerland. [L0492.029]
- Klarna's dispute risk by market is covered in the next section.

### Afterpay

- Afterpay no longer offers financing for more than 12 months (as of this 2022 call). No other financing option above 12 months was known at that time. (2022, older) [L0429.065]
- Adding Afterpay to a Shopify store (Source C):
  1. Apply as a merchant on afterpay.com.
  2. Wait about one to two days for approval.
  3. Once approved, add Afterpay as an additional payment provider in your Shopify payment settings. [L0335.054]

### Affirm

- Affirm is an alternative to Klarna and Afterpay for buy-now-pay-later options. Klarna and Afterpay applications were not approved for one seller, so Affirm was recommended. (2020, older) [L0852.098]
- Affirm, Klarna and PayPal Credit are free to set up, with no setup fee (2019, older). [L1069.060]
- Affirm and Klarna perform better when the average order value is about $2,000 or more (2019, older). [L1069.064]
- PayPal Credit is a steady choice in the US, while Affirm and Klarna are alternatives with deeper Shopify integrations (2019, older). [L1069.062]

### PayPal Credit

- Offer PayPal Credit on big-ticket items, for example above about 1,000 USD: qualifying buyers pay no interest for 6 months, and the merchant receives the money at once. Setup takes about 5 minutes from the PayPal account, and the option should be shown clearly on product pages (2019, older). [L0356.037]
- When PayPal is accepted, PayPal Credit can be offered as a payment option: customers can pay over six months without interest or payments (2021, older). [L0469.052]
- PayPal Credit was described as a US-only financing option, so confirm its availability in your country before offering it at checkout (2019, older). [L1069.090]
- PayPal Credit was believed to work only in the US and UK, so check whether any financing provider works in your country before promising installments (2021, older). [L0671.126, L0671.261]
- Most PayPal Credit approvals are around $2,000 or less, so financing decisions should be based on the average order value (2019, older). [L1069.063]
- In 2019 the stores offered only PayPal Credit as a buy-on-credit option. Klarna was being evaluated but was not yet in use. (2019, older) [L0960.070]
- PayPal Credit is a steady choice in the US, and keeping payment volume through PayPal consistent reduces the risk of account issues caused by revenue spikes (2019, older). [L1069.062]

### Shopify Installments (announced, not live)

- Shopify Installments was announced as a built-in payment plan at checkout, where Shopify finances the plan and the merchant gets paid right away. It was not yet live at the time of the call and required a waitlist (2020, older). [L0852.097]
- Shopify Installments, a financing option announced for Shopify Payments, was not yet live at the time of this call. It would let customers finance purchases directly (2021, older). [L0671.125]
- Later units describe Shop Pay Installments as the native option. [L0856.075, L1296.001]

### Klarna dispute risk by market

- Klarna disputes can lead to full refunds even when tracking shows delivery, especially if delivery takes around 10 days. Klarna can also hold merchant funds, which creates cash flow problems. [L0660.037]
- Klarna is the most used payment method in Sweden, but it is risky for beginners. Only use Klarna in Sweden if you can ship within about 4 to 5 days, and ideally one day. [L0660.035]
- A beginner should avoid Sweden and start in the Netherlands or Germany instead. The risk of Klarna disputes is high in Sweden. [L0660.036]
- The UK was described as the worst market for Klarna disputes, so Klarna should be avoided there. Stores that do not offer Klarna at all avoid this risk. [L0660.038]
- Offering only credit card and PayPal at checkout lets a store avoid Klarna exposure. Some stores have operated this way since 2017 without issues. [L0660.039]

| Market | Klarna guidance | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Sweden | Use only with shipping in about 4-5 days, ideally 1 day; beginners avoid | [L0660.035, L0660.036] |
| UK | Worst market for Klarna disputes; avoid | [L0660.038] |
| Netherlands or Germany | Recommended starting markets for beginners | [L0660.036] |

### Cryptocurrency

- Use Coinbase Commerce to accept cryptocurrency payments such as Bitcoin. About 4.5% of customers typically adopt this option, and chargebacks are impossible via crypto, which eliminates chargeback disputes. [L0324.031]
- Cryptocurrency payments remove chargeback risk entirely. Customers can pay in crypto, and you can receive in crypto or convert to fiat (dollars, pounds). This is best for high-value orders or dispute-prone products. [L0324.032]
- Accepting Bitcoin is possible through a wallet, but it is unlikely to add meaningful sales. Bitcoin buyers often hold it rather than spend it. (2021, older) [L0247.113]
- Cryptocurrency was not a built-in checkout option on these stores in 2019. It could be enabled manually if needed. Only a couple of crypto transactions occurred during a peak period, so it is not recommended as a payment method (2019, older). [L0960.071]
- The 4.5% adoption figure (Source C) and the low-volume findings (2019 and 2021) conflict. Apply the Coinbase Commerce route for high-value or dispute-prone sales, and expect low volume for general stores. [L0324.031, L0960.071, L0247.113]

### Local payment methods by market

#### General principles

- Know the main payment methods of each target market. For the top markets, offer PayPal and credit cards through Shopify Payments or Stripe, and add Klarna or Afterpay when the product price is more than $60. [L1345.070, L1260.048]
- Install payment providers that make sense in each specific target country. Local payment options build trust and conversion. [L1108.040]
- Buyers expect the payment methods they know. Payment methods matter for expanding to a new country. [L0361.040]
- Before expanding into a European country, check which local payment methods buyers use, because conversion depends on them. In Germany, Klarna and PayPal are widely used, and in the Netherlands, Bancontact is a major method alongside credit cards. [L0645.050]
- Targeting European countries can produce some sales in English but usually lower conversion, and Facebook's cheaper CPMs there attract low-intent traffic. Check the local payment methods first. [L0645.037]
- Target countries where card payments through Stripe or PayPal are available, since the lack of these options in some markets limits sales. [L1212.031]
- Offer a local payment provider in the target market. Buyers trust a store more when they can pay with a method they know, and this was the most important local advantage in the example. [L0549.040]
- Trust can be built when the business is visibly local. A registered local company, local payment methods and visible local products in the content all reduce buying hesitation. [L0492.031]

#### Switzerland

- Install a local payment provider that Swiss customers recognise, such as one that accepts QR-code payments and is registered only in Switzerland. This builds trust and raised sales. [L0492.028]
- Local payment providers require a company registered in the country with a local bank account. [L0492.029]
- Adding TWINT in Switzerland raised the conversion rate of ads targeting Switzerland. It builds trust because buyers recognise the provider and use it daily, and payment is quick by scanning a QR code. [L0502.029]
- Show the local payment method logo (TWINT in Switzerland) on the store to build trust with local customers who already use it daily. [L0642.046]
- Twint can only be offered on a store run by a Swiss company, since it is a payment provider used only in Switzerland. A Swiss merchant account is required before the logo can be shown. [L1439.042]
- Swiss customers who pay in euros instead of Swiss francs face a conversion fee of about 3 percent. This matters when pricing a Swiss-facing store. [L0660.040]

#### Germany

- Offer PayPal in Germany because buyers value its security, especially if a supplier problem leads to a refund request. [L0519.041]
- Germany requires a PayPal account for payments, since most German customers pay with PayPal. [L1184.051]
- Klarna is expected in Germany, so the store should offer it. [L0361.040]
- Germany and the Netherlands are the recommended starting markets for beginners. [L0660.036]

#### Netherlands

- Bancontact is a major method in the Netherlands alongside credit cards. [L0645.050]

#### Spain

- In Spain, PayPal, Klarna and Scalapay are used alongside standard Shopify Stripe payments. [L0361.040]

#### Australia

- In Australia, a credit card provider such as Stripe was necessary, since about 90 percent of sales came through Stripe in this experience. [L0519.041]
- Beam and We Pay were named as popular payment providers in Australia. Check which local providers your store can integrate with before relying on them. [L0549.041]

#### Gulf Cooperation Council (GCC)

- Cash on delivery (COD) is typically the only payment option in GCC countries, while card payments via Stripe or PayPal are the other option. Running ads in GCC countries may be weak without COD, even though CPM can be low, around 1 USD per 1,000 impressions. [L1212.030]

#### Hong Kong

- PayPal states on a dedicated Hong Kong page that it supports dropshipping for businesses there. Check this policy directly on PayPal before relying on it. [L1512.029]

#### Multi-country European providers

- Mollie is a provider that bundles many European payment options in one service. It suits sellers who scale or run ads in several European countries. [L1512.016]

### Phone, bank transfer and card data

- Never record a customer's credit card details in any form unless the business owner gives express permission. If card details must be written down, destroy them immediately after the order is completed. [L0404.006]
- A PayPal payment cannot be processed on a customer's behalf over the phone, so phone orders must be paid by credit card or bank deposit. [L0404.007]
- For bank transfer payments, the customer must send payment confirmation to the business email address before the order is shipped. [L0404.008]
- For tax-exempt organisation buyers, one option is a Shopify draft order for the order. Another option is sending an invoice payable through PayPal. [L0551.069]

### B2B payment terms

#### Terms and third-party financing

- Do not offer net 30 payment terms to customers, because you carry the collection risk and many businesses struggle to collect. Instead, offer a third-party financing option, such as Klarna. (2020, older) [L0551.066]
- With third-party financing, the financing company approves the customer and pays the store from day one, as with a credit card. If the customer does not pay the financier in time, the financier handles collection, not the store. (2020, older) [L0551.067]

#### Wires, deposits and checks

- A B2B buyer placed an order of about 66,000 USD and asked to pay by check after delivery. The seller refused this and accepted a deposit of 30 percent, roughly 20,000 USD plus, wired before shipping. The remaining balance was wired on delivery the same day, and the seller never accepts payment by check after receipt. (2020, older) [L1277.109]

## Authorization, Capture and Out-of-Stock Handling

### Authorize-only versus automatic capture

- Authorize-only confirms the order and authorizes the customer's payment method, but charges nothing. You capture the payment manually to complete the charge once the product is confirmed in stock (older advice, 2019) [L0243.026]
- After checkout, the money is authorised (reserved) on the customer's card or PayPal balance but not yet captured. Capturing transfers it to you and sends it to your bank account (older advice, 2021-22) [L0677.018]
- Manual capture authorizes the card without charging it. A new store that is still learning which supplier stock is reliable should use manual capture. Automatic capture is acceptable once stock availability is consistent (older advice, 2020) [L1111.182]

### Setting up manual capture in Shopify

1. Go to Settings > Payments (older advice, 2021-22) [L0677.019]
2. Scroll to Payment capture (older advice, 2021-22) [L0677.019]
3. Select Manually capture payment for orders (older advice, 2021-22) [L0677.019]
4. Keep the box Send a warning email when authorizations are close to expiring checked (older advice, 2021-22) [L0677.019]
5. Click Save (older advice, 2021-22) [L0677.019]

### Capture only after stock is confirmed

- Use manual capture, especially in high-ticket dropshipping where products may be out of stock (older advice, 2021-22) [L0677.020]
- Capture only after you have confirmed stock with the supplier and placed the official order. Refunding a captured payment to the customer can take several days (older advice, 2021-22) [L0677.020]
- Capture the customer's payment only after you have confirmed that the item will ship. This avoids charging customers for orders you cannot fulfill (older advice, 2021-22) [L0949.011]
- Do not capture payment in Shopify until stock is confirmed and the order has been checked for risk warnings. Medium- and high-risk orders must be escalated to the business owner first [L1119.012]
- Authorize the customer's card at checkout but charge it only after the supplier confirms stock, so the customer is not billed for an item that cannot be delivered. The VA then captures the charge in Shopify [L1473.014]

### Core out-of-stock workflows

**Protecting against out-of-stock orders**

1. Set your payment settings to authorize the customer's card without capturing the charge (older advice, 2021) [L0671.200]
2. When an order comes in, confirm with the supplier that the item is in stock (older advice, 2021) [L0671.200]
3. Capture the payment in the Shopify admin (older advice, 2021) [L0671.200]
4. Ship the order, which is when the customer is charged (older advice, 2021) [L0671.200]

**Payment capture when stock is uncertain (Shopify payment settings)**

1. In the Shopify payment settings, set the payment mode to authorize, so the card is checked for the order total without taking the money (older advice, 2021) [L0469.043]
2. When the customer orders, the order goes through and you receive the usual notification (older advice, 2021) [L0469.043]
3. Contact the supplier and confirm the product is in stock and can ship right away (older advice, 2021) [L0469.043]
4. If it is in stock, open the order in the Shopify admin and capture the payment, which sends the money to you (older advice, 2021) [L0469.043]
5. If it is not in stock, email the customer and ask whether they want to wait or cancel (older advice, 2021) [L0469.043]

### Pre-authorization workflow

**Using pre-authorization instead of charging immediately**

1. When an order is placed, pre-authorize the card so the funds are confirmed as available without charging the customer (older advice, 2021) [L0968.029]
2. Check with the supplier that the product is in stock (older advice, 2021) [L0968.029]
3. If it is in stock, capture the payment, which charges the customer, and process the order (older advice, 2021) [L0968.029]
4. If it is out of stock, cancel the order so the customer is never charged and no refund is needed (older advice, 2021) [L0968.029]

**Order confirmation and pre-authorization rule**

- When an order is placed, the customer gets an order confirmation email and the card is only pre-authorised, not charged (older advice, 2021-22) [L1264.019]
- The store checks stock and charges the card only when the item is available for immediate shipment, within 5 business days (older advice, 2021-22) [L1264.019]
- If the item is on back-order, the pre-authorisation is voided and the customer is contacted by email about alternatives (older advice, 2021-22) [L1264.019]

**What to do when stock is not available**

The sources give different actions for an out-of-stock or back-ordered item. Choose by the situation:

| Situation | Action | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Out of stock within the pre-authorization workflow | Cancel the order. The customer is never charged and no refund is needed (older advice, 2021) | [L0968.029] |
| Stock not confirmed, item not in stock | Email the customer and ask whether they want to wait or cancel (older advice, 2021) | [L0469.043] |
| Item on back-order under the 5-business-day rule | Void the pre-authorisation and email the customer about alternatives (older advice, 2021-22) | [L1264.019] |

### Seven-day authorization window

- A payment authorisation is valid for 7 days. If it is not captured within 7 days, it is cancelled automatically and the customer must re-enter their payment details (older advice, 2021-22) [L0677.021]
- Some credit cards authorize a payment for only about seven days, so the authorization may not last through a long delay (no date given) [L0359.086]

| Situation | Consequence | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Not captured within 7 days | Authorization cancelled automatically; customer must re-enter payment details (older advice, 2021-22) | [L0677.021] |
| Some credit cards | Authorize a payment for only about seven days | [L0359.086] |

### Backorders

- For a backordered product whose supplier has a reliable ETA of about two to three weeks, confirm the backorder with the customer. Capture the payment right away once they confirm they are willing to wait [L0359.085]
- Do not charge the customer's card for a backorder that could last months with no reliable date [L0359.086]

| Supplier situation | Payment action | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Reliable ETA of about two to three weeks | Confirm with the customer, then capture once they agree to wait | [L0359.085] |
| No reliable date, could last months | Do not charge the card | [L0359.086] |
| Item on back-order under the 5-business-day rule | Void the pre-authorisation (see pre-authorization section) (older advice, 2021-22) | [L1264.019] |

### Split payment for backordered items

**Splitting a payment through PayPal**

1. Confirm the customer's email address (older advice, 2021) [L0247.111]
2. Send a PayPal invoice to the customer's email requesting the partial amount (older advice, 2021) [L0247.111]

- A basic 29-per-month Shopify plan did not allow partial payments natively, so the PayPal invoice route was needed on that plan (older advice, 2021) [L0247.111]

## Chargebacks, Fraud Prevention and Processor Holds

### Chargeback Rates and Shopify Reserves

- Shopify Payments places a reserve that holds 10% of payouts when the chargeback rate is above 1%. Keeping the rate under 1% avoids this hold. [L0351.001]
- Shopify Settings > Payments > Chargeback monitoring shows the current chargeback rate (example: 3.81%) and whether payouts are held in reserve (example: 10% held due to a high number of chargebacks). Check this section to see whether you are over the threshold. [L0351.004]
- Two different holds exist and must not be confused: the chargeback-rate reserve (10% of payouts), which is tied to disputes, and the reserve payout for slow fulfillment (25% of payouts), which is tied to delivery speed. [L0351.005]
