- A reserve payout can hold 25% of the payout balance when Shopify judges that orders are not fulfilled fast enough, for example after about six weeks of slow fulfillment. The hold can last 1, 2, 3 or 4 months depending on the case. [L0351.006]
- Shopify Finance > Payouts shows the payout balance and the reserve. The reserve is the portion of the payout balance set aside to cover disputes, refunds and chargeback risk. [L0351.007]
- Case: a store selling a niche product had a four-month reserve hold on payouts from roughly $30k in revenue, because orders were not fulfilled quickly enough. The owner expected a payout of about $32k to $33k once the won chargeback disputes were recovered. Lesson: slow fulfillment led directly to a long cash freeze. [L0351.008]
- Case: a seller with unfulfilled orders had their Shopify Payments account banned after about two months, and the whole payout was lost. A store with slow fulfillment can lose its payment processing completely, not just a portion of payouts. [L0351.025]
- A public accusation by a rights holder that a seller is a fraud can push loyal buyers to dispute charges with their bank. Each dispute adds to the chargeback rate and its fees. [L0351.049]
- Threshold figures differ by source. Shopify Payments uses 1% as the reserve trigger [L0351.001]. Another source advises keeping the chargeback rate below about 1.5% of transactions, because higher rates lead to fines and damage the payment processor profile. Use 1% as the working target on Shopify Payments, and treat 1.5% as the outer limit cited elsewhere. [L0639.039]
- Each chargeback can incur an extra processor fee of roughly $15 to $30, depending on the processor. [L0639.039]
- *Older, 2021-22:* A payment processor can hold back money as a reserve, for example an amount such as 60,000 USD, when refunds and chargebacks are high. If the high level continues, the processor can close the account, and you can no longer take sales. [L0632.014]
- *Older, 2019:* A chargeback happens when a customer tells their bank that an order was fraud and the bank reverses the payment. Chargebacks are the main reason payouts get held. [L1110.042]

### Cost of a Dispute

- Shopify charges a fee of $15 per chargeback, and the full order amount is withdrawn from the seller and returned to the customer. Budget for both the fee and the lost revenue when calculating the cost of a dispute. [L0352.017]
- The fight window for a chargeback is a few weeks, possibly up to about a month. Respond within this window to contest the dispute. [L0352.018]
- Chargeback insurance is only worth paying when a single event could ruin the business financially. An occasional chargeback does not meet that condition. The operator in the lesson chose to keep that money instead of buying the insurance. [L0910.038]

### Chargeback Types and Dispute Response

- Chargebacks come in two types: fraud with a stolen card, and customer dissatisfaction caused by a store error. Each needs a different response. [L0963.130]
- *Older, 2022:* Avoid chargebacks caused by store errors through good customer support, honest communication and quick responses when problems occur. [L0963.133]
- *Older, 2022:* Chargebacks in favor of the customer after proof of fulfillment (tracking, delivery, five-star review, ID, FaceTime verification) are hard to reverse. A chargeback filed for "item not as described" or "fraud" is a different reason code and may require an attorney or small claims action. [L0969.035]
- *Older, 2022:* If a customer who received an item files a fraudulent chargeback, consult an attorney. A letter from an attorney or a small claims court can recover the money. [L0969.036]
- If a customer files a chargeback after a shipment, first stop the shipment with the carrier and submit evidence to Shopify. Then contact the customer to check whether the chargeback was intentional. Returning stock to the supplier is a cost, but it is better than delivering to a fraudulent customer. [L0856.078]

**Chargeback response procedure**

1. Stop the shipment with the shipping company. [L0856.079]
2. Submit evidence to Shopify support. [L0856.079]
3. Contact the customer to confirm whether the charge was intentional or a misunderstanding. [L0856.079]
4. If it was a misunderstanding, the customer can reverse the chargeback with their bank, and the shipment continues. [L0856.079]
5. If the customer does not respond, or it was fraud, arrange the return of the stock to the supplier. [L0856.079]

### Fraud Analysis and Risk Levels

- Shopify Payments includes built-in fraud analysis at no extra cost. It flags suspicious orders as "high risk", meaning Shopify suspects the order may be fraudulent. The information needed to review the order is on the same order page. [L0960.007, L1302.007]
- *Older, 2020:* Shopify includes built-in fraud detection and risk protection, so a separate fraud protection service is optional and not required at launch. Any order that does not pass Shopify's checks should be verified with the customer before shipping. [L0961.086]
- Sources describe the rating differently. One describes three levels: low, medium and high [L0856.076]. Another describes the default tool as rating each order as high or low risk, and it is the primary tool for fraud prevention and chargeback control [L0965.069]. Read the rating shown on the order and apply the handling below.
- Handling by risk level (2024):
  - **Low:** usually safe. [L0856.076]
  - **Medium:** verify with research on the customer's email or phone and the shipping address. [L0856.076]
  - **High:** almost always fraud, best canceled without verification. [L0856.076]
- *Older, 2022:* High-risk orders are usually fraud, so check them before shipping. [L0963.131]
- Sources differ on high-risk orders. The 2024 guidance cancels them without verification [L0856.076]. The 2022 advice checks them before shipping [L0963.131]. Both agree that an order that cannot be verified should not ship. [L0934.147]
- *Older, 2019:* A third-party screening tool... (see the screening section below).
- Medium-risk order check:
  1. Read the fraud analysis reason shown in Shopify. [L0856.077]
  2. Contact the customer to verify why the shipping address differs from their location. [L0856.077]
  3. Optionally research the customer's email and phone number online to confirm they match. [L0856.077]
  4. Cancel the order if verification fails. [L0856.077]
- Do not automatically decline orders that fail an AVS (Address Verification System) postal code check. Treat the mismatch as one signal, review the Shopify Payments fraud analysis first, and reserve automatic cancellation for orders rated high risk. [L0856.122]
- *Older, 2019:* A billing address that differs from the shipping address is not proof of fraud on its own, since many legitimate buyers bill a business address and ship elsewhere. Treat the mismatch as one signal to combine with others, not as a reason to cancel alone. [L0960.013]
- *Older, 2021:* Shopify Payments can hold a first high-value order for up to 120 days when the billing and delivery addresses differ. In one case the hold applied to a 4,700 dollar order. Contact the customer for an alternative payment before fulfilling. [L0962.115]
- *Older, 2021:* Handling a payment hold on an order with mismatched addresses:
  1. Contact the customer and confirm the order details. [L0962.116]
  2. Ask the customer for an alternative payment method, such as PayPal. [L0962.116]
  3. If the customer cannot pay another way, refund and cancel. [L0962.116]
  4. Ask the payment provider for the reason and whether there is a threshold for address mismatch holds. [L0962.116]
- *Older, 2021:* When PayPal holds a payment, ask PayPal for the reason. Check whether the account is new and whether a tracking number is required. [L0962.117]

### Verifying Suspicious Orders

- *Older, 2019:* Fraud check for a suspicious order:
  1. Look for red flags such as fake-looking names, email addresses or phone numbers, an order far above your average order value, or an IP location that does not match the billing or shipping address. [L0960.014]
  2. If you are still unsure, call the customer using the phone number on the order. [L0960.014]
  3. Ask about the mismatch, for example the shipping state, billing state and IP location, and listen to whether the answers are consistent. [L0960.014]
  4. Cancel the order if it is clearly fraudulent or the verification is not convincing. [L0960.014]
- *Older, 2021:* If an order triggers a fraud warning, verify it before shipping: find the buyer online, check the phone number and IP address, and call the buyer. Cancel any order that cannot be verified, and never accept wires or payments outside Shopify. [L0834.083]
- *Older, 2022:* Verify a suspicious order:
  1. Check the risk ranking that Shopify Payments assigns. [L0963.132]
  2. Google the shipping address and check whether the customer's name is linked to it. [L0963.132]
  3. Call the number given on the order. Fraudsters usually do not answer, or the number is fake. [L0963.132]
- *2023:* Handling a flagged high-risk order:
  1. Call the customer to find out why the order was flagged. [L0934.147]
  2. Verify identity and order details. [L0934.147]
  3. Process the order only once the risk is resolved. [L0934.147]
  4. Decline the order if the customer cannot be verified. [L0934.147]
- *Older, 2019:* An order far above your average order value is a fraud warning sign. Example: a $12,000 order when the average sale is about $500. Check such an order before it is fulfilled. [L0960.011]
- *Older, 2019:* An order with a fake-looking name and an IP address from a country known as a common origin of online scams should be canceled if the suspicion is clear. Cancel when the red flags are obvious and verification cannot resolve them. [L0960.012]

### Scam Patterns and Red Flags

- Check and wire payment requests carry a high fraud risk. Verify payments with the customer before goods are released. [L0984.007]
- Wire transfers are legitimate for B2B orders, government entities and institutional purchases such as schools. The scam pattern is an unsolicited buyer who makes an unusual request: shipping to an unexpected country, using a freight forwarder, purchasing on behalf of someone else, or mentioning an atypical payment method upfront. [L0294.001]
- Decline international wire transfers from unverified buyers. They carry over 99% probability of fraud. Genuine customer inquiries focus on stock availability and delivery timeline, not payment methods. If any hesitation arises about a wire request from an unknown buyer, refuse the order. [L0294.001]
- Overpayment scam example: a buyer requests a $20,000 wire transfer but sends $40,000, then claims it was an accident and asks for the overage back. After you refund, the original wire is reversed within 2-3 days because it came from a fraudulent account, leaving your account short by the full amount. Always assume this pattern is fraudulent and refuse such requests. [L0294.002]
- *Older, 2021:* Large overseas orders that offer to pay shipping at double the product price are likely scams. Verify the buyer's identity and organization before accepting such an order. [L1279.079]
- *Older, 2020:* Large orders from new buyers, such as a university purchase on 30-day terms, should be verified before they are accepted. A safer approach is to have the supplier take the risk and pay a finder's fee or commission of about 10% to 15%. [L1111.146]
- *Older, 2021:* Accepting orders by email from unknown customers who want to avoid Shopify checkout is a scam pattern. Delete such requests. [L0834.084]
- *Older, 2021:* Do not ship to addresses in Dubai, and do not allow sales outside your store's sales region. Emails from buyers asking for Dubai delivery are often scams designed to cause losses. [L0962.123]
- *Older, 2019:* Red flags for a suspicious order include fake-looking names, email addresses or phone numbers; an order far above your average order value; and an IP location that does not match the billing or shipping address. [L0960.014]
- *Older, 2019:* When red flags appear together with a fake-looking name and an IP address from a high-scam country, cancel if verification fails. [L0960.012]

### Fraud Screening Tools and Fees

- Shopify Payments fraud protection costs 1.2% of order value, for example $12 on a $1,000 order. It reimburses chargebacks from fraud on covered orders. [L0960.007]
- *Older, 2018:* Without paying extra, Shopify flags high-risk orders, which you can review and decide to process or cancel yourself. Paying for fraud protection means fraudulent orders are refunded. [L1085.080]
- Fraud insurance services such as Signifyd or ClearSale charge a percentage on each order, for example around 1%. Include this fee in the net profit calculation if you use the service. [L1148.014]
- *Older, 2022:* Fraud screening apps such as ClearSale typically charge a percentage of the sale. If an approved order turns out to be fraud, the provider refunds the money, which makes it a guarantee against chargebacks. [L1281.077]
- *Older, 2021-22:* ClearSale is a fraud-screening tool that reviews each order before you act on it. Early in a business it can be used on every order, and you follow its decision before fulfilling or charging anything. [L0949.012]
- Policy for stores that already screen every order: run every order through an outside fraud-screening service such as ClearSale before forwarding it to a supplier. Orders are only sent to suppliers after they pass the check. [L1473.015]
- *Older, 2019:* Use the built-in fraud protection by default, because keeping payment handling close to the payment source makes it simpler and easier to manage. Use a third-party screening tool only when fraud is a real, recurring pattern in your orders. [L0960.008]
- Sources differ on screening every order. Use the built-in tool by default and a third-party tool only when fraud is a recurring pattern [L0960.008]. Use screening on every order, as in [L1473.015], for stores whose supplier process depends on passing the check. In both cases the choice follows order volume and fraud pattern.
- *Older, 2019:* A third-party fraud screening tool is worth considering when you see a pattern of fake names, fake email addresses, fake phone numbers, or messy shipping and billing details. Compare rates, since the tools charge different percentages, and choose based on your order volume. [L0960.009]
- *Older, 2019:* The built-in fraud protection was sufficient for the stores described, so a separate fraud app was not used. [L1002.031]
- *Older, 2020:* The Fraud Prevention App is listed as a resource in the December 2020 coaching call for screening orders against fraud. The lesson names it without describing how it is configured. [L0426.009]
- *Older, 2020:* Fraud protection tools are the right answer to fears about large fraudulent orders, such as a 1,000-dollar order that turns out to be fraud. With these tools in place, the risk of expensive products is manageable. [L0551.074]
- Settings > Payments > Fraud prevention: keep CVV verification on to reduce credit and debit card fraud. The lesson also shows Payment capture method and Payment method customizations as options that stay at default at the foundation stage. [L1327.019]

### PayPal Holds, Freezes and Rolling Reserves

- PayPal can hold funds for up to 180 days for compliance checks. Exceeding 180 days requires proof of suspicious activity, which is very difficult to trigger. [L0324.025]
- Case: a seller accumulated $324,000 on a PayPal account held for exactly 180 days due to a compliance review. The full amount was released after the hold period, but without capital during that time, operations were strained. [L0324.026]
- If PayPal freezes funds for compliance and you need working capital for fulfillment, your only options are taking a credit loan, finding a personal loan from a friend, or refunding customers. There is no other mechanism to access frozen funds. [L0324.027]
- PayPal accounts can have $2,000 automatically deducted if the processor detects misinformation or policy violations claimed online. This is a recently introduced rule. [L0324.028]
- PayPal processes 30-35% of typical ecommerce store transactions. Reducing this dependency is strategic due to recent policy tightening. [L0324.029]
- *Older, 2018:* PayPal often freezes an account when sales suddenly grow and asks for business documentation. Expect this as a normal event once volume rises. [L0466.062]
- *Older, 2018:* After a PayPal account is unlocked, PayPal may apply a rolling reserve of 10 percent to new orders, held for 90 days before release. Example: on a 1,000 dollar order, 100 dollars are held back for 90 days before that money is released. [L0466.064, L0466.065]
- *Older, 2022:* PayPal can hold funds for long periods on new accounts with high sales, and may attach a rolling reserve to established accounts. Keep PayPal as one payment option rather than the only method, because a share of buyers prefer it and it increases total revenue. [L0854.090]
- PayPal can hold payments at the start of a store's sales and apply a second hold, called a delivery hold, until the product is delivered. Money is only released after delivery, so plan cash flow with these holds in mind. [L1173.034]
- PayPal can hold funds from an order for up to 21 days when the account has processed few transactions. Expect delayed payouts from a new PayPal account with little history. [L0835.137]
- PayPal can hold funds for up to 21 days... Shopify fraud protection: see above.
- PayPal may lock accounts and hold funds, sometimes for two weeks or more, even after a large sale. Plan for this risk, because it is not a question of whether it happens but when. [L0960.065]
- PayPal can hold funds in an account when orders look suspicious. In one case the amount held reached $75,000 for up to a year before release. Enabling auto sweep on a business PayPal account reduces the risk of money getting stuck, though it does not eliminate holds. [L1415.014]
- PayPal can impose a withdrawal limit. In the lesson, the threshold was 18,000 EUR or USD: more than that could not be withdrawn in one go, and the cap applied per month. To lift it, register a tracking app with PayPal to show the business is legitimate. PayPal may withhold funds from stores without tracking numbers. [L1363.041]
- *Older, 2021-22:* The PayPal account should be connected to the primary business email, not a personal one. If details such as name, address or email differ between the store, PayPal and other platforms, PayPal may freeze the account. [L0677.015]
- Logging into Shopify through a VPN made the platform detect a login from two distant locations in a short time, which triggered a fraud check. The first payout was withheld for three months, which is very damaging to a business with high-ticket orders. Avoid logging into Shopify through a VPN and use a separate browser for admin work instead. [L1441.023]
- When a payment processor withholds a payout, you may need to prove the item was delivered to get the funds released. Escalating with support may not speed this up, so keep delivery proof for every order. [L1441.024]
- *Older, 2021-22:* Processing payments across borders, such as a US store paying out to a bank account in another country, goes against the terms of payment gateways like PayPal and Shopify Payments. They can withhold your funds for up to 90 to 120 days, which is a serious problem for dropshipping. [L1480.024]

**Hold durations at a glance**

| Hold | Duration or amount | Trigger | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Compliance review, PayPal | Up to 180 days; beyond that requires proof of suspicious activity | Compliance check | [L0324.025] |
| New PayPal account with few transactions | Up to 21 days | Little transaction history | [L0835.137] |
| Volume jump, PayPal | 7 or 30 days of payout delay | Sudden volume increase | [L0468.157] |
| Rolling reserve after unlock, PayPal | 10% of new orders held 90 days | Account unlocked | [L0466.064] |
| Rolling hold, PayPal | 21-day rolling hold | Monthly volume about $30k-40k | [L1512.028] |
| Hold after sudden growth, PayPal | Release 10-20 days after accepted proof | Growth without proof of shipment | [L1512.041] |
| Suspicious-order hold, PayPal | Up to $75,000 held for up to a year | Orders look suspicious | [L1415.014] |
| Large-sale hold, PayPal | Two weeks or more | Large sale | [L0960.065] |
| First high-value order, Shopify Payments | Up to 120 days | Billing and delivery addresses differ | [L0962.115] |
| Cross-border payout, processors | 90 to 120 days | Payout to a bank in another country | [L1480.024] |
| Stripe hold | About 30 days, extendable by 30 days | Dispute rate keeps rising | [L1512.044] |
| Account with high volume, new | 180 days | Sudden high volume after adding PayPal | [L0970.143] |

- Sales growth from about 200 to 300 USD per day to 5,000 USD per day is a typical trigger for holds. Processors hold funds to protect themselves from unfulfilled orders, so growth should be matched with proof of delivery. [L1512.042]
- A PayPal rolling hold released funds up to about 15,000 USD to the bank, and held the amount above that for a set period. The release limit rose from about 15,000 USD to 20,000 USD, then 30,000 USD, with consistent volume and a good dispute history. [L1512.043]
- A seller at about 30,000 to 40,000 USD in monthly PayPal volume had about 40,000 USD held on a 21-day rolling hold. See the Hong Kong case in the processor section. [L1512.028]
- Case: a new store with traditional merchant accounts later added PayPal, and within a short time it processed about $50,000 in sales. PayPal flagged the account as high risk and held the balance for 180 days. [L0970.143]
- *Older, 2018, PayPal:* PayPal account holds can start at a few thousand dollars, and the volume at which holds appear varies by platform. [L0468.158], (see the scaling section for the volume guidance.)

### Resolving PayPal Holds and Restrictions

- *Older, 2018:* To resolve a PayPal account hold, call repeatedly until you reach a useful representative, and explain that you run an e-commerce business. Emails and live chat were not effective. [L0466.063]
- *Older, 2019:* Recovering a locked PayPal account:
  1. Send PayPal the proof of delivery and any other documents they request. [L0960.064]
  2. Call PayPal directly, since email responses are slow and impersonal. [L0960.064]
  3. Keep calling and speaking with different representatives, because a later call can reverse an earlier decision. [L0960.064]
  4. Provide whatever documents are requested until the account is reopened. [L0960.064]
- *Older, 2021:* When PayPal holds a payment, ask PayPal for the reason. Check whether the account is new and whether a tracking number is required. [L0962.117]
- *Older, 2022:* If a PayPal account is suspended, keep the account under follow-up and contact PayPal regularly, because some suspended accounts are restored after reaching the right person. Meanwhile keep other payment methods running, such as Shop Pay, Apple Pay, Google Pay and Klarna. [L1092.021]
- *Hold procedure, Source C:* Procedure when PayPal places a hold on sales:
  1. Connect a tracking app (TrackShow was named) to PayPal and the store, so tracking numbers are sent automatically when orders ship. [L1512.041]
  2. When a hold appears, provide proof of shipment for the sample transactions PayPal requests, such as a supplier invoice, tracking information or a shipping label. [L1512.041]
  3. Expect the release after about 10 to 20 days once proof is accepted. [L1512.041]
  4. Check the PayPal account regularly and resolve disputes early to protect the account. [L1512.041]
- PayPal can permanently restrict a dropshipping account and ask for fulfillment proof and delivery proof, which sellers usually get from their supplier. Sending these documents does not guarantee the account is restored. [L1347.001]
- Recovering a restricted PayPal account:
  1. Send the fulfillment proof and delivery proof from the supplier when PayPal asks for them. [L1347.004]
  2. Verify the PayPal account with the registered company details. [L1347.004]
  3. Keep syncing tracking numbers for every order. [L1347.004]
  4. Stay in contact with PayPal and ask for the specific reason for the restriction, because some reviews are made by automated systems that a conversation can override. [L1347.004]
  5. At the same time, open a second PayPal account with a different email address and verify it. [L1347.004]
- Step 5 conflicts with the advice on replacement accounts below. Both apply to different situations: the second account in step 5 is a parallel option during a restriction, while the replacement after a ban should wait until the cause is fixed. [L1347.004, L1128.026]
- Around 300 orders produced only one PayPal dispute, which was answered with a tracking number and documents. [L1347.009]
- Missing a registered company was given as a cause of restrictions. PayPal accounts were verified with the registered company details; in the example, a UK Ltd. [L1347.005]
- Not registering a company and not verifying the PayPal account led to about 15,000 USD being blocked. Registering the company and verifying the account released the funds. [L1431.043]

### Tracking Sync

- Not updating tracking numbers on PayPal for each order is a likely trigger for restrictions, because PayPal needs evidence that orders are being fulfilled. [L1347.002]
- Apps such as tracky.pay sync tracking numbers from a supplier account like CJ Dropshipping to PayPal automatically, so every new order gets its tracking number without manual entry. [L1347.003]
- Connect a tracking app (TrackShow was named) to PayPal and the store, so tracking numbers are sent automatically when orders ship. [L1512.041]
- To lift a PayPal withdrawal limit, register a tracking app with PayPal to show the business is legitimate. [L1363.041]
- Keep delivery proof for every order. Processors may require it before releasing withheld payouts. [L1441.024]

### Withdrawing Funds Regularly

- Prevent PayPal from holding funds by withdrawing regularly (ideally daily), keeping only a small balance in PayPal, and transferring bulk funds to your business bank account. [L0278.042]
- PayPal may lock large balances for extended periods, six months or longer. [L0278.042]
- Plan cash flow around holds. A hold on incoming payments at the start of sales, and a delivery hold until delivery, both delay access to money. [L1173.034]
- If frozen funds leave you short of working capital, the only options are a credit loan, a personal loan from a friend, or refunding customers. [L0324.027]

### Diversifying Processors and Backup Payments

- Use PayPal as an additional payment method next to Shopify Payments, not as your main merchant account. PayPal can apply holds, such as seven days or 30 days of payout delay, when volume jumps suddenly. [L0468.157]
- Keep PayPal as one payment option rather than the only method, because a share of buyers prefer it and it increases total revenue. [L0854.090]
- Keeping all sales and working balances on a single payment account leaves the business exposed: a ban can freeze funds for around 180 days. Keep a backup payment option ready before problems start. [L1128.003]
- While a PayPal account is suspended, keep other payment methods running, such as Shop Pay, Apple Pay, Google Pay and Klarna. [L1092.021]
- Shopify Payments runs on Stripe in the background but has been reported to hold funds earlier than Stripe, around 20,000 to 30,000 USD in volume. Stripe itself was reported to show holds around 35,000 to 40,000 USD. Every processor can hold funds at scale. [L1512.045]
- Stripe holds usually last about 30 days, and a hold can be extended by another 30 days if the dispute rate keeps rising. Resolving holds quickly and lowering disputes can speed up the release and remove the hold. [L1512.044]
- Case: a Hong Kong company after PayPal holds. At about 30,000 to 40,000 USD in monthly PayPal volume, about 40,000 USD was held on a 21-day rolling hold. The seller moved to a Hong Kong company with Hong Kong PayPal and Stripe accounts and later reported much higher volume without holds. [L1512.028]
- Opening a company in Hong Kong to obtain a PayPal account is a high-risk shortcut. In one reported case the setup cost about 700 USD and the account was banned within a week, with about 5,000 USD held for 180 days. Use a payment account that matches the country where the owner lives instead. [L1128.001]
- The two Hong Kong reports differ in outcome. The shortcut in [L1128.001] ended in a ban, while the relocated seller in [L1512.028] had accounts matching their new residence. Use a payment account in the country where the owner actually lives. [L1128.001, L1512.028]

### Account Setup, Identity and Bans

- Register a PayPal account in the country where the owner lives. When you move, open a new account in the new country. A mismatch between residence and account location can contribute to bans and frozen funds. [L1128.002]
- Sales outside your store's region, and payouts across borders, expose the account to holds. See [L1480.024] and [L0962.123] in the sections above. [L1480.024, L0962.123]
- Connect the PayPal account to the primary business email, and keep name, address and email the same across the store, PayPal and other platforms. [L0677.015]
- After a payment account is banned, opening a replacement account in another country can be closed within days, as happened with a US account opened two days after a ban. Find out the cause of the ban and fix it before opening any new account for the same business. [L1128.026]
- Shopify Payments banned an account with unfulfilled orders after about two months, and the whole payout was lost. [L0351.025]

### Scaling Volume Gradually

- Scale the store gradually. A sudden jump from a few thousand dollars to very large monthly volume can trigger a payment processor's risk review. [L0468.158]
- *Older, 2021:* A new PayPal account went from zero to about $50,000 in sales within a few weeks, and the rapid growth triggered a reserve on the account. A merchant account may place a reserve, which holds part of your funds, when chargebacks are high or revenue rises very quickly on a new account. [L0671.073, L0671.074, L0671.210, L0671.211]
- Holds are likely when sales volume jumps sharply, for example from about 200 to 300 USD per day to 5,000 USD per day. Match growth with proof of delivery. [L1512.042]
- At $100k-500k per month, chargebacks, payment processing holds and monthly processing limits become likely. A payment processing agent can monitor chargebacks, monthly volume and processing statistics, at about $8-12 per hour. [L1129.016]
- A payment processor can hold a first high-value order for up to 120 days when addresses differ. Scaling into high-ticket products is safer with fraud tools in place. [L0962.115, L0551.074]
- *Older, 2019:* Shopify fraud protection costs 1.2% per order, so include it in the cost of scaling when you use it. [L0960.007]
- *2023:* A new store with traditional merchant accounts that later added PayPal processed about $50,000 in sales in a short time. PayPal flagged the account as high risk and held the balance for 180 days. Sudden high volume on a new account can trigger holds. [L0970.143]

## Store Policies and Legal Pages

### Store Policies and Legal Pages

### Which pages are required, by source

The required set differs by source and by platform. Each source's list is kept below, so you can see what it asks for.

| Source | Required set | Context | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Google Merchant Center approval | Four: privacy policy, terms of service, shipping policy, refunds/returns/exchanges policy | Approval requirement for ecommerce stores | L0014.001 |
| Five pages before opening accounts | Privacy policy, terms and conditions, contact page, shipping policy, refund/return policy | Before Google Merchant Center, Google Ads, Microsoft Advertising, Facebook Ads, or the Facebook Shop channel | L0377.001, L0377.003 |
| Footer list before traffic | Privacy policy, terms and conditions, contact us, shipping policy, refunds and exchanges | Create and link in the footer before sending traffic | L0162.001 |
| Four footer-linked policies | Return/refund policy, shipping policy, privacy policy, terms of service | All four must exist and be linked in the footer | L0167.007 |
| Google Ads core pages | Privacy policy, terms of service, returns and refunds | Add before running paid traffic | L0421.001 |
| Supplier-ready store | About Us, Contact Us, Privacy Policy, Terms of Service | Before supplier outreach; privacy and terms are also required for a store that runs ads | L0509.002 |
| Source D launch gate | Shipping policy, refund policy, privacy policy | Missing any of these should block the launch | L1324.005 |
| Source D pre-launch | Privacy policy, terms of service, contact information, refund or return policy | The pages shoppers look for and that Shopify links at checkout | L1250.001 |
| Source D written policies | Return and refund policy, privacy policy, terms of service, shipping policy, contact information | Each shows "No policy set" until added | L1167.031 |
| Source D full legal section | Terms and conditions, shipping and delivery, returns and exchanges, customer service and complaint handling, data privacy and security | Every page must describe what the shop really does | L1250.002 |
| Source C cross-border store | Privacy, terms of service, returns and refunds, shipping, payment terms, contact details | Builds trust, shows compliance, helps resolve disputes with customers and payment providers | L1233.001 |
| Source C checkout minimum | Refund and shipping pages with company name and at least an email address; privacy and terms are secondary but good to have | Pages shown in checkout | L1260.051 |
| Source C Shopify list | Return policy, privacy policy, terms of service, shipping policy, contact information | Shown under Settings > Policies | L1233.009 |

- Practical rule: build the five pages from the 5-page set (privacy, terms, contact, shipping, refund/return), which covers every list above, and add payment terms, which the cross-border list requires [L0377.001, L0377.003, L1167.031, L1233.001]. Add About Us if you will approach suppliers [L0509.002].
- Launch with all required pages live: a missing privacy policy can lead to Google Merchant Center account suspension, and repeated violations can lead to a ban [L0014.006].
- Merchant Center flags an account automatically when required pages are missing, and you must then request a manual human review, which slows the process [L0377.002].
- Create the pages before opening any ad or merchant account; this helps avoid suspensions and closures [L0377.001].
- Google Merchant Center requires the merchant to confirm they have read the policies and that the account complies with them [L0377.002].
- Completing these pages does not make the store fully compliant; other compliance topics are handled in each platform's own setup [L0377.024].
- Policies can be refined after supplier relationships are finalised. The launch goal is supplier-ready functionality, not perfection [L0090.012].
- Policies must be published on the site and must match what is entered in Google Merchant Center [L0966.091].
- Policies must be easy to find. Some traffic platforms require easy access before approving ads and may refuse to run ads otherwise [L1303.022].
- Privacy policy and terms of service must match what Google Ads requires [L0965.090].

### Work order in Shopify

#### Compliance setup order (complete sequence)

1. Generate the privacy policy with Shopify's privacy policy generator. Answer the company questions, then request the document by email [L0377.004].
2. Add the required extra privacy text to the generated policy, if it is missing [L0377.004]:
   - a remarketing sentence at the end of the introduction or closing;
   - a Google Analytics sentence as the last line of the third-party services section;
   - a Google "pref" cookie line as the last line of the cookies section.
3. Generate the terms and conditions with Shopify's terms generator. Skip the trial, enter company and website information, and receive the document by email [L0377.004].
4. Review the generated terms, modify anything needed, then publish them on the store [L0377.004].
5. Create or confirm a contact page with at least two contact methods, for example phone number and business email, optionally Shopify's contact form [L0377.004].
6. Create a shipping policy stating when orders ship, average transit time, shipping cost, and any free or upgraded shipping options [L0377.004].
7. Create a refund and return policy stating the refund rules, how to request a refund, how returns work, and how a return is started [L0377.004].
8. Link all five pages in the footer menu [L0377.004].
9. Add two contact methods to the footer [L0377.004].
10. Confirm accepted payment methods are displayed in the footer [L0377.004].

#### Publish order (Source D method)

1. Go to Settings > Policies (the legal settings page) [L1167.030].
2. For each policy (return and refund, privacy, terms of service, shipping, contact information), either turn on the automated policy option or click Insert template [L1167.030].
3. Replace every placeholder with your real business details: trading name, business address, business phone number and contact information [L1167.030].
4. Click Save, then Publish for each policy [L1167.030].

#### Generate with Shopify's free tools

- Shopify has free privacy policy and terms generators. They ask for company information and send the generated document by email, so you can paste it onto the policy pages [L0377.022].
- Shopify Policy Generator (shopify.com/tools/policy-generator): enter company name, email address, business address and website link. Shopify emails a ready-made privacy policy or terms text that can be pasted without changes. Other generators on the same page create refund and terms texts [L1233.017].
- Privacy generator, skip-trial step: at the bottom of the page, click the link labelled "I'll skip the Shopify trial now, just create my policy". Enter the company information and click "send me my privacy policy" to receive the generated document by email [L0377.025].
- Generated text changes over time. Regenerate privacy and terms pages from the current generator and replace older text when the generator updates [L0965.090].
- Check the generated privacy policy regularly against the current store setup [L0855.130].
- Older (2021): a privacy policy from Shopify's generator, used with only a few modifications, was roughly 95% standard Shopify text [L0247.109].

#### Automated policy or template

| Option | How to use it | Behaviour | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Use automated policy (toggle) | Settings > Policies, open the policy, turn on "Use automated policy", click Save | Text stays in sync with your store settings and Shopify's latest templates and updates on its own. Still check that business details are correct | L0090.007, L1048.022, L1167.042 |
| Insert template | Click Insert template (Terms of Service), or Create from template (privacy, terms) | Static text. You replace every placeholder and edit the text yourself | L0090.008, L0509.010, L0509.013, L1048.023 |

- The automated option exists on each policy page under Settings > Policies [L1167.042].
- Creating a page from a template means you confirm you have read the template disclaimer [L0677.049].

### Privacy policy

#### Creating the page

- Shopify admin steps (template route):
  1. Click Settings in the bottom left corner of the Shopify Admin.
  2. Scroll to the bottom of the Settings page and click Policies.
  3. In the Privacy policy section, click Create from template.
  4. Read the highlighted instructions at the top of the generated text.
  5. Delete the highlighted instructions after reading them.
  6. Enter today's date as the last updated date.
  7. Replace the generic example domain with your store's actual URL.
  8. Edit any other template parts that refer to your business [L0509.010].
- Automated route: go to Settings > Policies, click Privacy policy, turn on the Use automated policy toggle at the top, then click Save [L1048.022]. The same toggle is described as Turn on the automated policy option [L0090.007].

#### Editing the content

- Delete the "notes to merchant" section for now. It applies only once you run ads. Update the privacy policy with ad-specific details before ads go live [L0509.011].
- In the contact section, delete any phone number or physical location you do not yet have. Keep an email address there: the default is the email used at Shopify sign-up, and it can be changed in the same place at any time [L0509.012].
- Add the extra privacy text if missing: the remarketing sentence, the Google Analytics sentence and the Google "pref" cookie line described in the compliance order [L0377.004].
- The privacy policy should cover data privacy and security as part of the full legal section [L1250.002].

#### Business name in the policy

- The trade name (DBA) is used as the store name in terms and privacy policies. The speaker gives no legal advice on whether the LLC name must be included. Older (2023) [L0964.114, L0964.119].
- Conflicting values, both kept:
  - Practice: use the DBA as the store name and do not add the LLC name. Applies where the speaker's practice is followed [L0964.119].
  - Caution: the privacy policy may need to name both the store name and the legal LLC name, since they often differ. Unverified. A lawyer should confirm the exact wording required in your jurisdiction. Older (2021) [L0853.121].

#### Missing pieces

- The ready-made policy set (terms, shipping, returns, customer support and data protection) contains no privacy policy text. Shopify's written policies list still expects a Privacy policy page, so write and publish it separately [L1250.040].

### Terms of service

#### Creating the page

- Shopify admin steps (Insert template route):
  1. Go to Settings > Policies.
  2. Click Insert template. Shopify generates the full policy.
  3. Replace any bracketed placeholders with your business name and support email.
  4. Optionally leave address and phone number blank.
  5. Click Save [L0090.008].
- Full template procedure:
  1. Go to Settings > Policies.
  2. In the Terms of service section, click Create from template.
  3. Replace the sentence that names the operator of the website with your store name.
  4. Read the whole text and replace every generic item with your own information.
  5. Remove references to policies you have not created yet, such as the refund policy.
  6. Replace the link to the privacy policy with a real link to the Privacy Policy you just created.
  7. Fill in the contact information section with your details, or remove lines that do not apply.
  8. Click Save [L0509.013].
- Shorter variant:
  1. Go to Settings > Policies and click Terms of service.
  2. Click Insert template at the top to have Shopify generate the template.
  3. Replace the link to privacy policy placeholder with a link to the Privacy Policy page you created.
  4. Replace the trading name placeholder with the store name.
  5. Replace the contact email placeholder with the support email address.
  6. Leave the business address and phone number fields empty if only an email address is provided.
  7. Click Save [L1048.023].

Both variants above describe the same Shopify route. Their wording differs, and both are kept.

#### What the terms must cover

- Terms set the rules for using the website and buying products. They should cover payment, shipping, returns and warranties [L1233.003].
- The terms should state that the store may update them at any time and that changes are posted on the page. They should also cover correct use of the site, prohibited activities and age restrictions [L1250.027].
- Standard template text (Source D). Use it as a starting point and edit it to fit your business:

```
Terms and Conditions

By visiting our website and buying products from us, you agree to follow these terms and conditions. This includes correct use of the site, activities that are not allowed, and age restrictions. We may change these terms at any time, and any changes will be posted on this page.
```
[L1250.034]

#### Publishing and reading

- The generated terms are the store's real legal terms, not placeholder text. Read them, adjust anything that does not fit the business, and only then publish [L0377.007].
- In one store the generated terms were published unchanged. The owner must still read them before going live [L0377.008].
- Templates are a starting point. Edit each one to match how your business handles orders, refunds and customer data before publishing [L1124.020].
- Remove any reference to a policy you have not built yet, and link the privacy policy to the real page [L0509.013, L1048.023].

### Shipping policy

#### Creating the policy (Shopify route)

1. In Shopify Admin, go to Settings and select Policies. Open Shipping Policy [L0090.005].
2. Get the Shipping Policy AI Prompt that starts with "You are a professional e-commerce operations copywriter" and copy it completely. The full text of this prompt is not included in these source units, so take it verbatim from the course prompt file and do not rewrite it [L0090.005].
3. Fill in the Student Inputs: store name, support email address (use support@yourdomain.com format), shipping region, handling time, transit time, delivery timeframe, and shipping method details [L0090.005].
4. Use the standard timeframes below unless different circumstances apply [L0090.005].
5. Create the policy using the prompt. Insert the generated policy into Shopify and save [L0090.005].

| Stage | Standard timeframe | Ids |
|---|---|---|
| Handling | 3-5 business days | L0090.005 |
| Transit | 3-7 business days | L0090.005 |
| Total delivery | 7-12 business days | L0090.005 |

#### Dropshipping timeframes

| Item | Value | Ids |
|---|---|---|
| Processing time | 1-3 business days | L1233.014 |
| USA delivery | 7-12 business days | L1233.014 |
| UK delivery | 5-10 business days | L1233.014 |
| Australia and New Zealand delivery | 7-15 business days | L1233.014 |
| European countries delivery | 6-10 business days | L1233.014 |

- These figures are rough estimates in business days. Before publishing, replace them with the delivery times your supplier and carrier actually achieve, because promises the store cannot keep lead to customer complaints [L1233.025].
- Which figure applies: the standard table applies for the usual case, and the dropshipping table applies to supplier-fulfilled stores, as the source describes them. Both values are given here so you can choose the one that matches your supply chain [L0090.005, L1233.014].
- Example values from a 2026 course: orders leave in 2-3 working days and reach customers in 6-10 days [L1250.043].

#### What the shipping policy must contain

- Shipping methods, estimated delivery times, shipping costs and international shipping details, and which customs duties or taxes the customer must pay [L1233.005].
- Processing time, delivery times per country, shipping fees charged, and the domestic shipping time for the store's home country [L1233.015].
- When orders ship, average transit time, shipping cost, and any free or upgraded shipping options [L0377.004].
- Confirmation and tracking email, how shipping rates are calculated at checkout, which countries are served and how international shipping times can differ, and that customs or weather can delay delivery [L1250.004, L1250.043].
- If the offer includes free shipping, state it clearly, and make the shipping cost terms transparent [L0167.011].
- Google Shopping requires the customer to know the exact shipping cost before checkout begins. A free-shipping header must be matched by an explicit free-shipping statement in the shipping policy. Vague wording about shipping costs is a common reason for Merchant Center flags [L0724.027].
- If products are built for one market, say so on the shipping page, for example that they need a 110V electrical system and are certified only to US standards. This protects the store from electrical-compatibility complaints from buyers elsewhere. It is a condition-specific notice, not a general legal disclaimer. Older (2021-22) [L1264.020].
- Shipping and refund policies must be shown in checkout with the company name and at least an email address [L1260.051].
- Text for the shipping policy prompt, with placeholders, from the 2026 course:

```
Write a shipping policy for my Shopify store called [Your Store Name]. Orders are shipped within [X-X] working days and usually arrive within [X-X] working days. Cover the processing time, the confirmation and tracking email, how shipping rates are calculated at checkout, which countries are served and how international shipping times can differ, and that delivery can be delayed by customs or weather. Use [placeholders] for any values I have not given you.
```
[L1250.004]

- Second shipping prompt, for a store with known figures, from the 2026 course:

```
Draft a shipping policy for my Shopify store, [Your Store Name]. Orders leave our hands in 2-3 working days and reach customers in 6-10 days. The text must cover processing time, how checkout calculates shipping rates, international shipping, and the tracking email sent when an order ships. State that customs or weather can delay delivery. Put [placeholders] wherever I haven't supplied a figure.
```
[L1250.043]

### Refund, return and exchange policy

#### Creating the policy (Shopify route)

1. In Shopify Admin, go to Settings > Policies [L0090.006].
2. Click Return and Refund Policy [L0090.006].
3. Use the Return & Refund Policy AI Prompt. Its full text is not included in these source units, so take it verbatim from the course prompt file [L0090.006].
4. Fill in the Student Inputs: store name, support email, return window (recommended: 30 days), condition requirements, restocking fee (recommended: none), return shipping responsibility, cancellation policy, damage reporting timeframe [L0090.006].
5. Leave most other defaults as-is for now [L0090.006].
6. Generate the policy [L0090.006].
7. Paste into Shopify [L0090.006].
8. Click Save [L0090.006].

#### Return window: values by context

| Context | Window | Applies when | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Standard recommendation | 30 days, no restocking fee | Default for a new store | L0090.006 |
| Dropshipping, defect cases | 14 or 30 days money-back guarantee | Defective, damaged or not-as-described items. Chinese suppliers usually do not accept returns | L1233.011, L1233.012 |
| 30-day guarantee in sales | 30-day money-back guarantee | Removes purchase risk; shown near the purchase decision | L1209.025 |
| EU shipping | 14-day right to cancel or return for any reason | Applies whenever the store ships into the EU, even if the normal window is shorter | L1327.052 |

- Make the 30-day guarantee visible near the purchase decision [L1209.025].
- If the store ships into the EU, keep the 14-day right to cancel or return for any reason in the returns policy, even when the normal return window is shorter [L1327.052].
- Chinese suppliers usually do not accept returns but do refund or replace items that arrive damaged, defective or not as described. The return policy should therefore offer a 14-day or 30-day money-back guarantee for these cases only [L1233.011].
- Dropshipping clause to adapt. Replace the bracketed placeholder with your support email and choose 14 or 30 days:

```
We provide a 14-day (or 30-day) money-back guarantee if you receive a defective, damaged product or if the product is not as described. Please contact us at [support email] and we will do our best to help. Please send us a photo or video as proof so our team can investigate. If our team approves your request, we will issue a full refund or a replacement, as agreed.
```
[L1233.012]

#### Return address and return conditions

- For a new store without a return address, the owner's own address or a virtual address can be used as the return address. Customers pay the return shipping label [L1233.013].
- A returned item is accepted only if it is in good condition with intact, unopened packaging. Add these conditions to the return policy [L1233.013].
- If you have no return address, remove or change that line in the generated policy [L1233.010].

#### Content of the refund policy

- Rules for refunds, exchanges and returns, the conditions for returns, time limits and any restocking fees [L1233.004].
- Eligibility, return window in days from purchase, proof of purchase, non-returnable item types, how refunds are processed and how long they take, when partial refunds apply, when exchanges are allowed, who pays return shipping and whether it is refundable, sale-item rules, and how customers contact you [L1250.003].
- Refund rules, how to request a refund, how returns work, and how a return is started [L0377.004].
- Return prompt from the 2026 course:

```
Write a return and refund policy for my Shopify store called [Your Store Name]. Cover eligibility, the return window in days from the date of purchase, the proof of purchase requirement, non-returnable item types, how refunds are processed and how long they take, when partial refunds apply, when exchanges are allowed, who pays return shipping and whether it is refundable, the rules for sale items, and how customers can contact us. Use [placeholders] for any values I have not given you, and keep the wording plain and customer-friendly.
```
[L1250.003]

#### Specific product risks

- Fashion products have high return rates, so return and shipping policies must be clear before launch. Suppliers should also agree to refund defective or wrong-size items [L0335.039].
- Do not copy another store's policy. Write the refund, return and shipping text from your actual return window, conditions and exceptions, and check it against consumer rules in each country you sell to [L1188.035].
- Before writing your own return and shipping policies, review established dropshipping stores' policies as a reference. Adapt the structure to your own supplier terms rather than copying their wording [L1233.024].
- The policy should protect the business while being fair to customers [L1124.020].

#### Dropshipping return procedure

- Open Shopify Admin > Settings > Policies [L1233.010].
- Click the policy you need (for example Return and refund policy) to generate the default template [L1233.010].
- Replace the store's contact email with your support email [L1233.010].
- Insert a return address if you have one; otherwise remove or change that line [L1233.010].
- Adjust any other line to your needs [L1233.010].
- Copy the finished text into a page, or save it in the policies section [L1233.010].

### Contact page and contact details

#### Contact page

- Shopify provides a default contact page for every store. Add its link to the footer [L1233.007].
- The default page named Contact is already present under Online Store > Pages > Add page. If the store is password protected, remove the password before viewing it [L1233.018].
- Create or confirm a contact page with at least two contact methods, for example a phone number and a business email, optionally Shopify's contact form [L0377.004].
- Use a business email address, not a personal email, on the contact page and in the footer [L0377.005].
- Use a support mailbox on the store's own domain, for example support@yourstorename.com. Use the same address in the return policy, shipping policy and contact page, and make sure someone reads it [L1233.022].

#### Minimum contact details: sources compared

| Source | Minimum required | Notes | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Google (contact information rule) | At least two methods, recommended email plus phone, with business hours | Address not required for this model. Email and phone must be real and working | L0167.010 |
| Google (general expectation) | At least two of: physical address, phone number, email address | Phone plus email meets the requirement without a physical address | L0724.008 |
| Cross-border minimum | Email is essential; business address and phone are optional | Use the email in every policy | L1233.016 |
| Source B, 2021-22 (older) | Three details: phone number, email address, postal address | Phone and address should also appear in the footer. Google may check these on the site | L1145.012 |

- Google's contact information rule and the general expectation both accept a phone plus email without an address [L0167.010, L0724.008].
- The 2021-22 source asks for all three details. Older, so treat it as the stricter option, not as the current rule [L1145.012].

#### Filling the contact information section

1. Open the policy in Shopify Settings > Policies and scroll to the contact information section [L0724.009].
2. Click the template option, which prefills the store information [L0724.009].
3. Confirm the email address, which can be a support or sales address, uses the real domain [L0724.009].
4. Add a phone number labelled as a call option [L0724.009].
5. Add hours of operation, for example 9am through 5pm EST [L0724.009].
6. Confirm there is no placeholder text, then click publish [L0724.009].
- A physical address is not needed in this section [L0724.009].
- A valid hours entry for a US-based store can read 9am through 5pm EST, with the time zone stated. Only the contact section of the policies needs this. Customers rarely see it, but Google's crawler reads it [L0724.010].

#### Real, working details

- Every email address and phone number in policies and contact pages must be real and current [L0724.007].
- In every policy page, use the correct real email address and real phone number, remove any "555" placeholder phone number, and delete all bracketed or template filler text [L0167.009].

### Footer, menus and visibility

#### Footer content

- Create and link these pages in the footer menu before launching traffic: privacy policy (use a Privacy Policy Generator), terms and conditions (use a Terms and Conditions Generator), contact us, shipping policy, refunds and exchanges [L0162.001].
- Add two contact methods to the footer [L0162.001, L0377.004].
- Display accepted payment methods in the footer [L0162.001, L0377.004].
- Link the refund, privacy, terms of service and shipping policy pages in the footer before submitting to Google [L0724.005].
- Refund, shipping, privacy and terms pages should appear in the footer Quick Links menu, not hidden. Make them easy to find before running paid ads, since these pages are required storefront elements [L1319.013].
- Put a footer link to the policy pages so customers can find them easily [L1326.015].
- The footer phone number and address should match the Contact Us page details [L1145.012, older 2021-22].

#### Creating the footer menu (Shopify)

- Create the menu under Online Store > Navigation > Add menu. Name it, for example, "Our policies". Add a menu item for each policy page (Privacy policy, Terms of service, Shipping policy, Return and refund policy, optionally Contact). Then Save menu [L1233.019].
- In the theme footer:
  1. Go to Online Store > Themes > Customize.
  2. Open the footer section in the theme editor.
  3. Click Add block, choose Menu and select the policy menu.
  4. Rename the heading from "Quick links" to "Our policies".
  5. Check that all policy links appear in the footer and save [L1233.020].
- Main menu dropdown (Source B, 2021-22, older):
  1. In the main menu, click Add menu item and name it Our Company.
  2. Link it to the About Us page.
  3. Drag the item under Our Company so it becomes a dropdown.
  4. Add the child items Contact Us, Shipping, Easy Returns, Price Match, Privacy and Terms of Service, linking each to its matching page.
  5. Click Save menu [L1303.021].

#### Easy to find

- Make the refund and return policy clear and easy to find; the website footer is a recommended placement location [L0014.002].
- Terms and privacy pages must be easy to find for customers, because some traffic platforms refuse to run ads without easy access [L1303.022].
- Policies saved under Settings are also linked in the checkout footer. Pages and footer links are added afterwards so visitors can find them [L1233.021].

### Checkout legal settings

#### How the checkout gets its policies

- The Shopify checkout is hosted separately from the main site. Footer policy links do not appear there, so legal policies must be entered separately, even though the checkout appears to be on your domain. Older (2021-22) [L0677.032].
- Policies entered in Shopify's policy settings are the texts linked at checkout, so fill them in there first to meet the checkout requirement [L1233.021].
- Policies in the legal settings are linked in the checkout footer. They do not appear automatically in the storefront footer, so add them to your online store menus [L0677.051].
- Legal pages must be visible in the checkout, because customers enter personal and card details there [L1233.008].
- Publish each policy after saving. Each policy shows "No policy set" until it is added [L1167.031, L1167.030].

#### Menu name: sources compared

| Source | Menu name in admin | Date | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Source B | Settings > Legal, which lists refund, privacy, terms of service and shipping policies, each with a Create from template button | 2021-22 (older) | L0677.050, L1145.013 |
| Source C | Settings > Policies: return, privacy, terms of service, shipping and contact information | 2026 | L1233.009 |
| Source D | Settings > Policies (the legal settings page) | 2026 | L1167.030 |

- Use the menu name your admin shows. The policy text must be the same in both places [L0677.034].

#### Copy the policy text into the checkout (older method, 2021-22)

1. Go to Online Store > Pages and open the Refund policy page [L0677.033].
2. Select all text and copy it [L0677.033].
3. Go to Settings > Legal and paste it into the Refund policy box [L0677.033].
4. Repeat for the Privacy policy, Terms of service and Shipping policy pages [L0677.033].
5. Make sure each policy in Legal matches the text on your site exactly [L0677.033].
6. Click Save [L0677.033].
- Do not keep two different versions of the same policy on the site and in the checkout. Pages and the Legal section must contain identical text [L0677.034].
- Older (2021-22): enter the return and shipping policy, privacy policy and terms of service in Settings > Legal. These feed the checkout page, and their text must match the corresponding site pages exactly [L1145.013].

### Placeholders, sample values and accuracy

#### Placeholders to remove

- Do not publish a template with unfilled placeholders such as "(INSERT TRADING NAME)", "[INSERT BUSINESS ADDRESS]" or "[INSERT BUSINESS PHONE NUMBER]". Replace them with your real business details before publishing [L1167.032].
- Templates contain bracketed placeholders, and each one must be replaced with real store information before publishing. Leaving a placeholder such as the trading name or the privacy policy link in the terms makes the page incomplete [L1048.024].
- Shopify's generated policy texts contain bracketed placeholders and instruction notes. Replace every bracket with your store name, contact email and address, and delete the notes before publishing [L1233.023].
- Sample values left in a prompt's input fields, such as a template's example brand name, carry into the generated policy or About Us text. Replace or delete every sample value before pasting the output into Shopify [L1048.027].
- Placeholder text, bracketed template fields and fake phone numbers such as 555-555-5555 are flagged by Google as signs of an unprofessional or incomplete store [L0724.007].
- Shopify's policy generators insert placeholder text, so each generated policy must be checked and edited [L0724.005, L0167.007].
- Confirm there is no placeholder text before you publish [L0724.009].
- Suppliers may click through your policies during review. A policy with placeholder links, missing sections or references to pages that do not exist makes the store look incomplete. Remove or fix every such item before submitting the store for approval [L0509.014].
- Templates contain placeholders that must be replaced before submission. Review every generated policy under Shopify Admin > Settings > Policies [L0167.008].

#### Revising with real figures

- After a generator (such as ChatGPT) writes a policy, reply in the same chat with your real figures and ask for a revised version. Include the exact processing days, delivery days, return window and contact email in each revision so the sample numbers are replaced [L1250.044].
- In every policy page, the placeholders for the store name, the privacy policy link and the contact email must be replaced in turn [L1048.023].

#### Accuracy and reality

- Policies must describe what the store actually does, not just exist as placeholders. In most US states, the core legal pages (privacy policy, terms of service, return and refund policy) are binding legal documents [L0421.002].
- Every page has to describe what the shop really does in practice [L1250.002].
- Promises the store cannot keep lead to customer complaints. Replace rough delivery estimates with actual figures before publishing [L1233.025].
- Conditional statements, such as financing availability, exclusions, sales tax by state or a shipping restriction to certain states, must link to a page that explains the details. Without that, the visitor becomes confused and worried. Older (2021-22) [L0409.032].
- Statements such as "Some exclusions apply" or "No shipping to" a state, with no explanation or link, make a store look unreliable. State the restriction plainly and explain it, even when there is a good reason for it. Older (2021-22) [L0409.033].
- The price match guarantee can be stated clearly as applying to the listed price before coupon codes. This helps customers understand it before ordering. Older (2023) [L1283.057].

### Google review requirements

- Contact information read by Google's crawlers should list at least two contact methods, recommended as email plus phone, along with business hours. An address is not required for this business model, but the email and phone must be real and working [L0167.010].
- Adding an at-a-glance summary, such as a table or a short summary section, to policy pages helps Google's crawlers parse the key details such as return and shipping terms [L0167.012].
- Google Merchant Center flags accounts automatically when required pages are missing, and a manual review must be requested [L0377.002].
- Policy pages must match the policies entered in Google Merchant Center [L0966.091].
- Google Ads requires privacy policy and terms of service pages that match its requirements. Check them whenever the generator updates [L0965.090].
- Missing a privacy policy can result in Google Merchant Center account suspension. Repeated violations can lead to account banning [L0014.006].

### AI review of policy pages

#### Review prompt

Paste the policy text directly into the chat. The tool may crawl the live site, which may still show old policies, and results based on the live site can point to errors that have already been fixed [L0724.013].

```
Hello. I own [your domain] and I am about to submit my website to Google Merchant Center and Google Ads for approval. Please review the policy pages I paste below, not the live version of the website, and tell me whether anything is not compliant with Google Ads and Google Merchant Center policies. If you find anything non-compliant or questionable, reply with the exact text in question, why it is a problem and your recommended update.

[paste the text of the policy page here]
```
[L0724.012]

#### Revision prompt

```
Thank you for your feedback. I have revised our [policy name] and pasted the new version below. Please check this revised text against Google Merchant Center and Google Ads policies and tell me whether it is compliant. If anything is still questionable, reply with the exact sentence, the reason it is a problem and your recommended wording.

[paste the revised policy text here]
```
[L0724.014]

#### Review procedure, one page at a time

1. Send one policy page to Google Gemini and ask for a compliance review [L0724.015].
2. Read every issue it lists, including the exact sentence and the recommended update [L0724.015].
3. Apply every recommended change to that policy in Shopify [L0724.015].
4. Paste the revised policy back to Gemini and ask for a second check [L0724.015].
5. Repeat until Gemini reports the policy as fully compliant, then move to the next policy page [L0724.015].
- Repeat this process for the refund policy, shipping policy, privacy policy and terms of service [L0724.015].

### Country-specific content

- For the four main markets (US, Canada, Australia and the UK), add the required privacy policy and terms text to the legal pages. Other countries, especially in Europe, have separate rules that must be added too [L1213.067].
- If the store ships into the European Union, keep the 14-day right to cancel or return for any reason in the returns policy [L1327.052].
- Check consumer rules in each country you sell to when writing refund, return and shipping text [L1188.035].
- Customs duties and taxes that the customer must pay belong in the shipping policy [L1233.005].
- In the US, the core legal pages are binding in most states. State-specific wording, such as for California, must be checked with a legal source rather than assumed. Older (2022) [L0421.002, L1092.022].
- A product built for one market needs an explicit notice on the shipping page, for example for 110V electrical systems and US-only certification. Older (2021-22) [L1264.020].

### Legal notice and disclaimers

- Legal pages are not legal advice. Have a lawyer review the privacy policy, terms of service and refund policy, especially for stores with custom situations [L0421.003].
- Shopify's legal page templates are not legal advice and must be customised for your store [L0677.049].
- Shopify policy templates are not legal advice. You are responsible for their accuracy and for compliance with the laws that apply to your business [L1167.033].
- Create the privacy policy and terms of service in Shopify admin under Settings > Policies, not as ordinary pages. This makes Shopify treat them as legal pages, and the links in Google Merchant Center should point to those pages. Older (2023) [L0470.052].
- Legal ownership details: use the trade name in the policy text, and check the LLC question with a lawyer [L0964.114, L0853.121].

### Payment and billing terms

- The terms or a separate payment page should describe how payments are processed, which payment methods are accepted, and any recurring billing if subscriptions are offered [L1233.006].
- Payment and billing belong in the terms of service, which should cover payment [L1233.003].
- Display accepted payment methods in the footer [L0162.001, L0377.004].
- Customers enter personal and card details at checkout, so the legal pages must be visible there [L1233.008].
- Legal pages help resolve disputes with customers and payment providers [L1233.001].

### Pre-launch checklist

| Check | Ids |
|---|---|
| Privacy, terms, contact, shipping and refund pages exist and are published | L0377.001, L0377.003, L1250.001 |
| Pages are linked in the footer, with two contact methods and accepted payment methods | L0162.001, L0377.004 |
| Pages are created before opening any ad or merchant account | L0377.001 |
| Pages and checkout legal settings contain identical text | L0677.034 |
| No placeholder, bracket, sample value or 555 number remains | L0167.009, L1048.024, L1048.027, L1233.023 |
| Shipping and refund text states real timeframes, the free-shipping terms and the return conditions | L0167.011, L0724.027, L1233.025 |
| Each policy has been reviewed with AI on pasted text, and then with a lawyer if the case is custom | L0724.015, L0421.003 |
| The contact email is a real business mailbox on your domain and someone reads it | L0377.005, L1233.022 |

### Required pages

| Page | What it must contain | Units |
|---|---|---|
| Privacy Policy | Template text, see "Privacy Policy template" below | L1327.058 |
| Terms of Service | Template text, see "Terms of Service template" below | L1327.059 |
| Refund policy | Must be in place before traffic starts, because it appears in checkout | L1331.033, L1345.072 |
| Shipping policy | Must be in place before traffic starts, because it appears in checkout. Must be visible to buyers, for example in tabs on the product page | L1345.072, L1346.024 |
| Guarantee (for example 30-day) | Must be visible to buyers if offered | L1346.024 |
| Contact details | Company name and at least one email address on every legal page | L1345.072 |
| Legal notice (Impressum) | Required in Germany, with business information on the website | L1428.029 |

### Setting up policies in Shopify

1. Go to Settings > Policies. [L1331.033]
2. Open the Shopify policy templates, for example the refund policy. [L1331.033]
3. Draft the policy text, for example with ChatGPT. [L1331.033]
4. Read every promise and make sure you can keep it. [L1331.033]
5. Have a lawyer check the policies if you are unsure they are fully compliant. [L1331.033]
6. Save each policy and link it in the footer menu. [L1331.033, L1446.009]

### Placeholders to replace in the templates

- `{Store name}`: replace with the name of your store. It appears throughout the Privacy Policy and Terms of Service. [L1327.058, L1327.059]
- `{Business email}`: replace with your business email address. It appears in the Privacy Policy contact section and in Terms of Service Section 20. [L1327.058, L1327.059]
- `{Month Year}`: replace with the month and year of the last update. [L1327.058]
- `[Replace with the governing law of your country or state.]`: the template's Section 18 names the laws of the United States by default. Replace this with the governing law of your own country or state. [L1327.059]

### Privacy Policy template

```
Last updated: {Month Year}

{Store name} runs this store and website, including all related information, content, features, tools, products and services, to give you, the customer, a curated shopping experience (the “Services”). {Store name} is powered by Shopify, which allows us to deliver the Services to you.

This Privacy Policy explains how we collect, use and disclose your personal information when you visit or use the Services, make a purchase or other transaction through them, or contact us. If our Terms of Service and this Privacy Policy conflict, this Privacy Policy takes precedence regarding the collection, processing and disclosure of your personal information.

Please read this Privacy Policy carefully. By using or accessing any of the Services, you confirm that you have read it and understand how your information is collected, used and disclosed as described here.

Personal Information We Collect or Process
“Personal information” means information that identifies you or another person, or can reasonably be linked to you or them. It does not include information collected anonymously or de-identified information. Depending on how you interact with the Services, where you live, and what applicable law permits or requires, we may collect or process these categories:
- Contact details: your name, address, billing address, shipping address, phone number and email address.
- Financial information: credit card, debit card and financial account numbers, payment card information, transaction details, form of payment and other payment details.
- Account information: your username, password, security questions, preferences and settings.
- Transaction information: items you view, add to your cart or wishlist, purchase, return, exchange or cancel, and your past transactions.
- Communications: information you include when you send a customer support inquiry.
- Device information: details about your device, browser or network connection, your IP address and other unique identifiers.
- Usage information: how and when you interact with or move through the Services.

Personal Information Sources
We may collect personal information from these sources:
- You directly, when you create an account, visit or use the Services, communicate with us, or otherwise give us your information.
- Automatically through the Services, including from your device when you use our products or services or visit our websites, and through cookies and similar technologies.
- Our service providers, when we engage them to enable certain technology and when they collect or process information for us.
- Our partners or other third parties.

How We Use Your Personal Information
Depending on how you interact with us and which Services you use, we may use personal information for these purposes:
- Providing, tailoring and improving the Services. We use your information to process payments, fulfill orders, remember your preferences, send account notifications, manage returns and exchanges, arrange shipping, enable product reviews, and create a personalized shopping experience.
- Marketing and advertising. We use your information to send marketing, advertising and promotional messages by email, text message or postal mail, and to show you relevant online ads based on your activity on the Services.
- Security and fraud prevention. We use your information to authenticate your account, provide a secure payment experience, detect and investigate fraudulent or malicious activity, and protect public safety.
- Communicating with you. We use your information to provide customer support, answer your inquiries and maintain our relationship with you.
- Legal reasons. We use your information to comply with applicable law, respond to valid legal process, and enforce or investigate possible violations of our terms or policies.

How We Disclose Personal Information
In certain situations, we may disclose your personal information to third parties for legitimate purposes, subject to this Privacy Policy:
- To Shopify, vendors and other third parties that perform services for us (for example IT management, payment processing, data analytics, customer support, cloud storage, fulfillment and shipping).
- To business and marketing partners, to provide marketing services and advertise to you. These partners use your information under their own privacy notices.
- When you direct, request or otherwise consent to a disclosure, such as shipping you products, or using social media widgets or login integrations.
- To our affiliates or others within our corporate group.
- In connection with a business transaction such as a merger or bankruptcy, to comply with legal obligations, to enforce applicable terms of service or policies, and to protect or defend the Services and our users.

Relationship with Shopify
The Services are hosted by Shopify, which collects and processes information about your access to and use of the Services in order to provide and improve them for you. Information you submit through the Services is transmitted to and shared with Shopify and with third parties that may be located in countries other than where you live.
To help protect, grow and improve our business, we use certain Shopify enhanced features that draw on data from your interactions with our store, other merchants and Shopify. In those cases, Shopify is responsible for processing your personal information, including responding to requests to exercise your rights.
```
