To learn how Shopify uses your personal information and what rights you may have, see the Shopify Consumer Privacy Policy (https://www.shopify.com/legal/privacy/app-users). Depending on where you live, you may exercise certain rights through the Shopify Privacy Portal (https://privacy.shopify.com/en).

Third Party Websites and Links
The Services may link to websites or online platforms run by third parties. If you follow such links to sites we do not control or are not affiliated with, review their privacy, security policies and terms. We do not guarantee, and are not responsible for, the privacy or security of those sites.
Including a link does not by itself mean we endorse the content of those platforms or their owners or operators, unless we say so on the Services.

Children’s Data
The Services are not intended for children, and we do not knowingly collect personal information from anyone under the age of majority in your jurisdiction. If you are a parent or guardian and believe a child has given us personal information, contact us using the details below to request its deletion.
As of this Privacy Policy’s effective date, we have no actual knowledge that we “share” or “sell” (as defined by applicable law) personal information of anyone under 16.

Security and Retention of Your Information
No security measures are perfect or impenetrable, so we cannot guarantee “perfect security.” Information you send us may also be insecure while in transit. Please do not use unsecured channels to send sensitive or confidential information.
How long we keep your personal information depends on factors such as whether we need it to maintain your account, provide Services, comply with legal obligations, resolve disputes, or enforce other applicable contracts and policies.

Your Rights and Choices
Depending on where you live, you may have some or all of the rights below. These rights are not absolute, may apply only in certain circumstances, and in some cases we may decline a request as permitted by law.
- Right to access or know. You may request access to the personal information we hold about you.
- Right to delete. You may ask us to delete personal information we maintain about you.
- Right to correct. You may ask us to correct inaccurate personal information we maintain about you.
- Right of portability. You may receive a copy of the personal information we hold about you and ask us to transfer it to a third party, in certain circumstances and subject to certain exceptions.
- Right to opt out of sale or sharing for targeted advertising. Depending on where you live, you may opt out of the “sale” or “sharing” of your personal information, or out of its processing for targeted advertising.
- Managing communication preferences. We may send promotional emails, and you can opt out at any time using the unsubscribe option in those emails. If you opt out, we may still send non-promotional emails, such as ones about your account or orders.
You may exercise these rights where indicated on the Services or by contacting us using the details below. To learn more about how Shopify uses your personal information and your rights, visit https://privacy.shopify.com/en.
We will not discriminate against you for exercising these rights. We may need to verify your identity before processing a request. Where applicable law allows, you may appoint an authorized agent to make requests for you. We will respond to your request in a timely manner as required by applicable law.

Complaints
If you have a complaint about how we process your personal information, contact us using the details below. Depending on where you live, you may have the right to appeal our decision or file a complaint with your local data protection authority.

International Transfers
We may transfer, store and process your personal information outside the country where you live.
If we transfer your personal information out of the European Economic Area or the United Kingdom, we will rely on recognized transfer mechanisms such as the European Commission’s Standard Contractual Clauses, or equivalent contracts issued by the relevant UK authority, unless the transfer is to a country determined to provide an adequate level of protection.

Changes to This Privacy Policy
We may update this Privacy Policy from time to time, for example to reflect changes in our practices or for operational, legal or regulatory reasons. We will post the revised policy on this website, update the “Last updated” date, and give notice where applicable law requires it.

Contact
If you have questions about our privacy practices or this Privacy Policy, or want to exercise any of your rights, email us at {Business email}.
```
[L1327.058]

### Terms of Service template

```
This website is operated by {Store name}. Throughout the site, “we”, “us” and “our” refer to {Store name}. We provide this website, including all information, tools and Services available from it, to you, the user, on condition that you accept all the terms, conditions, policies and notices stated here.

By visiting our site or buying something from us, you use our “Service” and agree to be bound by these terms and conditions (“Terms of Service”, “Terms”), including the additional terms, conditions and policies referenced here or available through a hyperlink. These Terms of Service apply to all users of the site, including, without limitation, browsers, vendors, customers, merchants and contributors of content.

Please read these Terms of Service carefully before accessing or using our website. By accessing or using any part of the site, you agree to be bound by them. If you do not agree to all of them, you may not access the website or use any Services. If these Terms of Service are considered an offer, acceptance is expressly limited to these Terms of Service.

Any new features or tools added to the current store are also subject to these Terms of Service. You can view the latest version at any time on this page. We reserve the right to update, change or replace any part of these Terms by posting updates or changes on our website. It is your responsibility to check this page regularly. Your continued use of the website after changes are posted means you accept them.

Our store is hosted on Shopify Inc., which provides the e-commerce platform that lets us sell our products and Services to you.

Section 1, Online Store Terms
By agreeing to these Terms of Service, you confirm that you are at least the age of majority in your state or province of residence, or that you are at that age and have given us consent to let any minor dependents use this site. You may not use our products for any illegal or unauthorized purpose, nor may you violate any laws in your jurisdiction (including but not limited to copyright laws) while using the Service. You must not transmit any worms, viruses or code of a destructive nature. Any breach or violation of the Terms will result in immediate termination of your Services.

Section 2, General Conditions
We reserve the right to refuse Service to anyone, for any reason, at any time. You understand that your content (excluding credit card information) may be transferred unencrypted and involve (a) transmissions over various networks, and (b) changes to conform and adapt to the technical requirements of connecting networks or devices. Credit card information is always encrypted during transfer over networks.
You agree not to reproduce, duplicate, copy, sell, resell or exploit any part of the Service, its use, or access to it, or any contact on the website through which the Service is provided, without our express written permission. The headings in this agreement are for convenience only and do not limit or affect these Terms.

Section 3, Accuracy, Completeness and Timeliness of Information
We are not responsible if information on this site is inaccurate, incomplete or out of date. The material here is provided for general information only and should not be relied on, or used as the sole basis for decisions, without checking primary, more accurate, more complete or more timely sources. Any reliance on it is at your own risk.
This site may contain historical information. By nature it is not current and is provided only for your reference. We may change the contents of this site at any time, but we have no obligation to update any information. You agree that it is your responsibility to monitor changes to our site.

Section 4, Modifications to the Service and Prices
Prices for our products are subject to change without notice. We may at any time modify or discontinue the Service, or any part or content of it, without notice. We will not be liable to you or any third party for any modification, price change, suspension or discontinuation of the Service.

Section 5, Products or Services
Some products or Services may be available only online through the website. They may be limited in quantity and may be returned or exchanged only according to our Refund Policy. We have made every effort to display the colors and images of our products as accurately as possible, but we cannot guarantee that your monitor will display any color accurately.
We may, but are not required to, limit sales of our products or Services to any person, geographic region or jurisdiction, and we may do so case by case. We may limit the quantities of any products or Services we offer. All product descriptions and prices are subject to change at any time without notice, at our sole discretion. We may discontinue any product at any time. Any offer for a product or Service made on this site is void where prohibited.
We do not warrant that the quality of any products, Services, information or other material you buy or obtain will meet your expectations, or that any errors in the Service will be corrected.

Section 6, Accuracy of Billing and Account Information
We may refuse any order you place with us. At our sole discretion, we may limit or cancel quantities purchased per person, per household or per order. These limits may apply to orders placed under the same customer account, the same credit card, or the same billing and/or shipping address. If we change or cancel an order, we may try to notify you using the email and/or billing address or phone number you provided when ordering. We may limit or prohibit orders that, in our sole judgment, appear to come from dealers, resellers or distributors.
You agree to provide current, complete and accurate purchase and account information for every purchase. You agree to promptly update your account and other information, including your email address and credit card numbers and expiration dates, so we can complete transactions and contact you when needed.

Section 7, Optional Tools
We may give you access to third-party tools that we neither monitor nor control, and we have no input into them. You acknowledge and agree that we provide such tools “as is” and “as available,” without warranties, representations or conditions of any kind and without endorsement. We have no liability whatsoever arising from or relating to your use of optional third-party tools.
Your use of optional tools offered through the site is entirely at your own risk and discretion. You should make sure you are familiar with, and accept, the terms set by the relevant third-party provider(s). Some content, products and Services available through our Service may include materials from third parties. Third-party links on this site may direct you to websites not affiliated with us. We are not responsible for examining or evaluating their content or accuracy, and we do not warrant and accept no liability for any third-party materials, websites, or other materials, products or Services of third parties.

We have no obligation to update, amend or clarify information in the Service or on any related website, including pricing information, except as required by law. No specified update or refresh date on the Service or any related website should be taken to mean that all information there has been modified or updated.

[L1327.059]

### Company identification and contact details on legal pages

- Each legal page should include the company name. [L1345.072]
- Each legal page should include at least an email address so customers can contact you. [L1345.072]
- The Terms of Service and Privacy Policy templates use `{Store name}` and `{Business email}` for these details. [L1327.058, L1327.059]

### Checkout and pre-traffic readiness

- Refund policy and shipping policy appear in the checkout. [L1345.072]
- Both must be in place before traffic starts. [L1345.072]

### Visibility of shipping policy and guarantees

- Publish the shipping policy and any guarantee, such as a 30-day guarantee, on the store where buyers can see it. [L1346.024]
- Examples of placement: tabs on the product page. [L1346.024]
- Visible policies signal that the business is legitimate. [L1346.024]

### Footer links and Google ads compliance

- Link the policy pages in the store footer. [L1446.009, L1331.033]
- Do this before running any ads, for Google compliance. [L1446.009]
- Do not start ads until the policy pages are linked in the footer. [L1446.009]

### Country-specific content: legal notice (Impressum)

- In Germany, an online store must show a legally required legal notice (Impressum) with the business information on the website. [L1428.029]
- Missing information can lead to warning letters from lawyers and complaints from customers. [L1428.029]
- The Impressum must be complete before the store goes live. [L1428.029]
- Check the legal requirements of the country you sell from and the countries you sell to. [L1428.029]

#### Further details

- TermsFeed is an all-in-one wizard tool recommended by Google for generating privacy policy agreements without legal expertise. [L0014.004]

- Google Adwords provides sample privacy policy templates to use as a starting point. [L0014.005]

- A store should have a privacy policy and terms of service live on the site before launch. These pages are part of the basic legal setup, separate from marketing copy. [L1264.010]

## Privacy, Cookies, GDPR and Marketing Consent

### Start With the Regions You Sell To

- If you sell to customers in the EU, cookie consent and GDPR apply. Check the requirements for each target market before you decide anything else. [L0775.060, L1309.060, L0421.004]
- Regional compliance varies widely. For subscriptions and data collection, check US state by state, the EU, Australia and other markets, because lawsuits target this area. [L0041.023]
- For each country you sell to, check the legal requirement with a qualified professional. The lessons are not legal advice. [L0358.056, L0775.060]
- Apps such as Recart, SMSBump and Klaviyo give country-specific guidance for legal text. One account with a common privacy policy section that includes each country's requirements is usually enough, rather than separate accounts per country. [L1213.068]
- GDPR is a real requirement for stores that handle personal data of EU visitors. Shopify and Google Ads terms cover part of this, but the store must still meet the remaining obligations in its own policies. [L0421.004]
- GDPR covers stores that serve European customers. [L0358.056]
- CCPA applies to California customers. Check whether it applies to your business. [L1145.023]

### Shopify Customer Privacy Settings

- Shopify's Preferences page has a Customer privacy section with two options. [L0726.015]
  - **Limit tracking for customers in Europe:** requires consent under GDPR before tracking. [L0726.015]
  - **Limit the third-party sale of California customers' data:** applies under CCPA. [L0726.015]
- Enable the setup like this (source from 2021-22, marked older):
  1. Go to Online Store > Preferences and find Customer privacy. [L1145.023]
  2. Enable the EU option (Limit tracking for customers in Europe) if you sell to Europe. GDPR requires consent before tracking. [L1145.023] *(older: 2021-22 source)*
  3. Enable the California option (Limit the third-party sale of your California customers' data) if you sell to California. CCPA requires an opt-out. [L1145.023] *(older: 2021-22 source)*
- Under Settings > Customer privacy, the current Shopify settings include:

| Setting | What it does | Example | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Cookie banner | Published on the online store, can be shown by region | Austria, Belgium and 14 other regions | L1250.012 |
| Data sales opt-out page | Active for certain states | California, Colorado and 6 other states | L1250.012 |
| Data storage hosting location | Set here | United States | L1250.012 |
| Marketing settings | E-mail and SMS marketing in checkout, asks for marketing preferences, double opt-in | Customers confirm their contact details | L1250.013 |

- Shopify calls its default privacy settings recommended. These settings are a starting point, not legal advice. The store owner stays responsible for complying with the privacy and marketing laws that apply to customers' countries. [L1250.013, L1250.014]

### Cookie Consent Banners

- A cookie consent banner is a pop-up at the bottom of the page where visitors accept cookie tracking. It matters most when you sell into the EU, where cookie opt-in is required. For other markets, check the requirement with a lawyer. [L0775.060]
- Banners are mainly required under EU law. [L0934.145]
- Cookie consent pop-ups and tracking disclosures come from privacy regulations such as GDPR. [L0358.056]
- Enable cookie consent in the Shopify theme settings if you sell outside the US, especially in the EU. It is optional for a US-only demo store. [L0102.009]
- The cookie consent toggle is in the footer settings. It switches the consent banner on or off. Switch it on when the business operates out of the United States. [L1018.004]
- A cookie consent app was not used for a US-based store. Cookie consent tools are usually required by laws in other countries, so check your jurisdiction before you skip them. [L0835.118]
- Do not switch off the banner before you check the requirement for each target market. [L1309.060]
- For an older theme that has no banner option, ask Shopify support how to add one. Do not build it yourself. [L0934.145]

**When each rule applies:**

| Situation | What to do | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Selling into the EU | Enable cookie consent. Opt-in is required. | L0775.060, L1309.060, L0102.009 |
| Selling outside the US, not only the EU | Enable cookie consent. | L0102.009 |
| US-only demo store | Optional. | L0102.009 |
| Business operates out of the US | Switch the footer toggle on. | L1018.004 |
| US-based store without a consent app (2023 case) | Check your jurisdiction before you skip the tool. | L0835.118 |

### Privacy Policy Content

- The privacy policy must explain:
  - what customer data is collected,
  - how it is used and stored,
  - cookies and third-party services,
  - how customers can request access to or deletion of their data. [L1233.002]
- The privacy policy must be fully compliant. [L0041.022]
- Monitor Robert Frude, a lawyer on Twitter, for subscription lawsuit patterns. This shows which compliance failures lead to litigation. [L0041.022]
- Do not publish the generated Shopify privacy policy as it is. [L0377.006]
  1. Compare the generated text with the store's real tools: remarketing, Google Analytics and cookies. [L0377.006]
  2. Add any missing sentences that describe these tools. [L0377.006]
- If you run remarketing campaigns, the privacy policy must disclose that you:
  - use cookies,
  - work with third-party vendors that gather data from cookies,
  - use cookies to personalize ad experiences. [L0014.003]
- If the store runs remarketing ads, the personal data paragraph must end with a sentence saying that personal data may be used for remarketing. The default template may not include it, so check the generated text before you publish. [L0377.026]
- Place privacy text about cookies or consent under the section that describes information collected or how it is used. Check the template Shopify provides, because the wording changes over time. [L0835.117]
- The privacy policy must include the behavioral advertising section when Google requires it, or when the compliance lesson in module six says so. [L0964.117]
- Use this security statement as a template. Insert it as written. [L1250.037]

```
Our systems apply industry-standard encryption and safeguards so personal data stays away from unauthorized access, changes or disclosure. Card and payment details are kept safe and can be reached only by approved staff.
```
[L1250.037]

### Meta, Facebook Pixel and Data Sharing

- Customers can stop their website activity from being shared with Meta. They do this in the Off-Facebook Activity settings of their own Facebook account. The store cannot switch this off for them, so pixel data is limited for those customers. [L1027.015]
- Choose the dataset category that matches your business most accurately. The category decides what data may be shared with Meta. Depending on the category and location, data sharing can be limited or blocked. Never send sensitive user data in events. [L1027.021]
- Older advice (2022): use the customer privacy setting that allows the highest level of data sharing, so that Facebook tracking captures the most data. The Shopify Facebook pixel must be enabled under Online Store preferences. [L1281.075] *(older: 2022 source)*
- **Conflict:** the 2022 advice maximizes data capture. The 2026 advice limits sharing by category and location and forbids sensitive data in events. Follow the 2026 advice where the two conflict. The 2022 setting is only the older route to the pixel. [L1281.075, L1027.021, L1027.015]

### Email and SMS Consent

**SMS**

- SMS sales messages need consent. Show the consent in two places:
  - in checkout, next to the phone number field, with a short note on how the number will be used,
  - in the privacy policy and terms. [L1114.042]
- Keep SMS abandoned cart messages disabled until SMS consent is set up correctly. Sending SMS without consent can lead to fines. [L1114.043]
- A pre-ticked SMS consent checkbox is not valid consent under GDPR. Leave the phone marketing opt-in unticked so the customer must actively agree. [L1213.064]

**Email**

- The pre-chat form can include a checkbox that opts visitors in to marketing emails. Checking it adds them as email subscribers. In one 2023 setup, the default setting for this opt-in was left unchanged. Review that default before you go live. [L1311.013]
- Never add purchased or rented email lists to a store's marketing. They hurt email deliverability and sender reputation, and they make it harder for legitimate opt-in subscribers to receive messages. [L0836.006]

### Anti-Spam Requirements

- Automated marketing emails must show a valid business address. Anti-spam law in the US, Australia and the EU requires it. Drip or Klaviyo insert the address from the Shopify account. [L1298.032] *(older: 2021-22 source)*
- Removing the tawk.to app triggers a request that erases customers' personal data held by tawk.to after 48 hours. [L1298.033] *(older: 2021-22 source)*

### Subscription Compliance

- Subscription compliance is critical. If subscriptions are a substantial part of revenue and non-compliant (unclear billing terms, difficult cancellation, poor disclosure), one lawsuit can destroy the business. [L0041.021]
- Check your billing terms, cancellation path and disclosures against the rules of each region you sell to. [L0041.021, L0041.023]

### User-Generated Content

- Add a user-generated content clause to the terms and conditions. [L1012.023] *(older: 2021-22 source)*
- The clause states that any content a customer provides becomes the business's property once they buy. This covers:
  - reviews,
  - images,
  - video,
  - survey responses,
  - social comments. [L1012.023] *(older: 2021-22 source)*
- The clause also protects the business if a customer later disputes the use of their content. [L1012.023] *(older: 2021-22 source)*

### AI Tools and Customer Data

- Assume that any data you put into an LLM carries some risk, because AI data handling is ambiguous. Claude is currently among the safest options because of Anthropic's commitment to data protection. Understand and accept this risk before you proceed. [L0218.012]
- Before you paste any data into an AI tool:
  1. Anonymise the brand name if needed. [L0876.026]
  2. Remove confidential information. [L0876.026]
  3. Know your own data handling rules first. Data sent to an AI tool leaves your account. [L0876.026]

### Call Recording

- Recording customer calls without informing the caller can be illegal in many places. Check local law before you record calls. [L0910.055]
- The lesson describes a recording service with unreliable quality and does not recommend it. [L0910.055]

### ADA Accessibility

- ADA lawsuits target websites that cannot be read. Companies may receive compliance letters, which force expensive responses, or they may be sued directly. [L0041.020]
- Invest in website accessibility and in terms of service that people can read. [L0041.020]

## Ad Platform Compliance and Account Setup

### Compliance checklist before traffic

- Review the compliance checklist before launching any paid traffic, including Google, Bing and Facebook campaigns. Traffic campaigns must not start before this review is done. [L0393.011, L0532.015]
- Running Google, Bing or Facebook campaigns before the compliance checklist has been reviewed risks account problems on the ad platforms. [L0393.025]
- A compliance lesson covers the legal and platform rules a dropshipping store must follow before launch. Check these rules before running ads or taking payments. [L1089.009]
- Review the Google Ads compliance policies before launch, because policy issues are the main cause of problems at first launch. Fix any issue before turning on ads. [L0972.053]
- The checklist covers the most common reasons Google suspends Merchant Center or Google Ads accounts, especially misrepresentation. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L1145.050]
- Setting up a parent LLC and a separate brand name (DBA) before starting is generally acceptable and does not by itself cause Google account problems. [L0808.046]

### Store requirements

- Put a privacy policy on the website, because Meta's terms of service expect one. [L0649.054]
- Include a complete privacy policy on the site before setting up Google Merchant Center. (Older: Source A, 2021.) [L0671.069]
- The store website must show a phone number, a contact us page and a privacy page in customer-visible places. Missing any of these is a common cause of Merchant Center suspension. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L0727.006]
- Use the same contact information on the store as is registered in Google Merchant Center. Phone, email and contact page options together can be enough even if no virtual address is displayed. (2023.) [L1283.056]
- Footer social links that point to Shopify's social pages or platform home pages, instead of the store's own profiles, were linked in one case to a Merchant Center suspension. Replace them with your own profiles or remove them, then resubmit for review. (2023.) [L0934.146]
- A financing or payment-option tab is typically not a reason for suspension, because financing options are common on stores without issues. (2023.) [L1283.055]
- Each store's ad campaigns should match the store's selling region and currency. Keeping region, currency and traffic aligned avoids confusion about prices and shipping. (Older: Source A, 2021.) [L0853.103]

### Google Merchant Center requirements

- Merchant Center first performs an automatic bot-based review of the store and flags problems for manual review by a human. [L0377.023]
- Tax setup is mandatory for the United States. See the tax section below. [L0731.005]

### Google Ads billing setup

**Setup sequence**

1. Select the billing country, time zone and currency. [L0184.004]
2. Check the currency carefully before submitting the billing information, because it cannot be changed later. [L0716.005]
3. Customer info: choose Individual or Business. For a business, enter the business name and the business address. [L0716.006]
4. Create a payments profile by selecting Individual or Organization and adding a valid credit card. A temporary $50 authorization may appear. [L0184.004]
5. Payment method: choose automatic payments and enter a credit card. Automatic billing means you do not have to pay invoices manually each cycle, similar to a phone bill. [L0716.007]
6. Decline Google Ads strategist support, because the strategists are sales representatives, not strategic advisors. [L0184.004]
7. Multiple payment methods can be added later. The billing page shows the account information and payment methods in one place. [L0716.008]

**Alternative steps (Source B, 2021-22, older)**

1. Open Tools and settings (cogwheel, top right), then go to Billing > Billing info. [L1145.046]
2. Enter the account name, the business address and the credit or debit card that will pay for the ads. [L1145.046]
3. Campaigns can be built before billing is added, but ads cannot run until billing details exist. [L1145.046]

**Rules and states**

- The Google Ads billing country must match the country where the business operates, not the country where the owner lives. For a US business, select United States. For a UK business, select United Kingdom. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L0726.020]
- A new ad account shows no payment methods, no recent spending and an outstanding balance of $0.00 until a payment method is added in Billing & payments. [L0651.017]
- Keep a valid payment card on file in Google Ads. A declined bill can lock the account, and reopening it may require contacting Google directly. (2025.) [L0972.052]
- A credit such as a 500 dollar Google credit usually appears on its own after the account processes it. If it does not, contact Google Ads support. (2024.) [L0856.081]
- To restart a business in a niche with suspended Google accounts, call the Google Ads policy support number shown in your account to request a live review. (2023.) [L1283.054]

### Google Merchant Center tax settings (US)

- Tax setup in Merchant Center is mandatory for the United States. For sellers without a US business address, Google proposes a default tax setup that can be saved without entering US sales tax rates. Tax can alternatively be submitted through the product feed. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L0731.005]
- Tax details in Google Merchant Center must match the tax rates set in Shopify settings exactly. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L1145.016]
- For a US-based dropshipping store, the tax setting in Google Merchant Center should copy the tax configuration used in Shopify. The state of business registration determines which tax nexus applies. In the setup described, no nexus is configured for all states. (2023 or undated, Source A.) [L0728.004]

**Sales tax steps (Source B, 2021-22, older)**

1. Go to Merchant Center > Tax settings > Sales tax. [L0731.006]
2. Turn on custom sales tax only for the states where the business must collect tax. [L0731.006]
3. Enter each state's rate as a percentage (example: 7 for a 7% rate). [L0731.006]
4. Turn off all other states, then click Save. [L0731.006]
5. Check the rates with a tax advisor before saving. [L0731.006]

**Which setup applies**

| Situation | What to set | Ids |
|---|---|---|
| No US business address | Google's default tax setup can be saved without US rates, or tax can be submitted through the product feed. | L0731.005 |
| Business must collect tax in some states | Custom sales tax on only for those states, rate as a percentage, all other states off, then Save. | L0731.006 |
| Shopify-matched setup, business registered in a state | Copy the Shopify tax configuration; the registration state determines the nexus. | L0728.004 |

- US sales tax for Shopping ads applies in all states. Buy on Google requires collecting and remitting tax in Florida, Kansas and Missouri. Whether a seller must collect tax depends on the state where the business is registered. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L0731.007]
- The sample US setup shows the question "Is shipping and handling taxable?" with the answer No. The link "Need more tax options? Switch to advanced settings" opens the advanced tax configuration. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L0731.035]

### Google suspension appeals

**Inventory mistake (older: Source A, 2021)**

1. Correct the inventory by restocking the item or removing products that will not be in stock from the product feed. [L0853.005]
2. Make sure the feed no longer lists items customers cannot buy. [L0853.005]
3. Request a review from inside Google Merchant Center. [L0853.005]
4. If the review is denied, contact Google Ads support by phone or live chat. You must be logged in to reach that support. [L0853.005]

**Misrepresentation (2023)**

- A misrepresentation suspension can be appealed by showing corrected changes. The appeal should list the changes made, explain what was wrong, and cite Google's policy documentation. [L1114.048]

1. Identify what was not compliant in the store. [L1114.049]
2. Make the required changes. [L1114.049]
3. Review Google's full policy documentation and confirm the store complies. [L1114.049]
4. Submit the changes with a clear explanation and links to the policy pages. [L1114.049]

**Timing and resubmission (2023)**

- Google appeals about a Merchant Center suspension typically receive a response in one to two weeks. A failed appeal can be submitted again. Fix the cause before appealing so the first appeal succeeds. [L0934.148]

### Meta Business Manager verification

**Documents and profile**

- Verify the business manager with proper documentation: company registration certificate, certificate of incorporation, business address and contact details. Fill out all required fields in the business profile. [L0067.037]
- Add the business documents, such as a certificate of incorporation and business address, and complete all contact details on the business page. An unverified or incomplete business profile can look like a scam to Meta. [L0640.056]
- Documents that support verification include LLC or Ltd registration papers. A business registered in one country can still run ads targeting other countries, because verification only checks that a legitimate business is advertising. [L0637.008]
- Verification needs a registered business with complete business details. Verifying it significantly reduces the chance that the ad account is banned or restricted. [L1024.009]
- Entering registered business details builds trust with Meta and lowers the chance of bans. A beginner without a registered business may use personal information at first, but a proper business setup is recommended. [L1024.003]

**One company, several accounts**

- Register one company and use it to verify all accounts, including Facebook and PayPal. The company name does not need to match the product or the store domain. [L1431.042]
- One company can be used to verify several connected accounts, such as PayPal, a Facebook account and Shopify Payments. The company name does not have to match the store names. For every new store, the domain must be verified again in the ad account. [L1428.028]

**Location and country of registration (sources differ)**

- The business location does not matter for verification. Verification needs to show that the business is registered and legitimate. [L1431.044]
- For verification of a US business, the ad account asks for the business document matching the country where the business is registered. Examples: a Corporation document from Companies House or HMRC for the UK, or a GST certificate in Australia. Have the registration certificate ready, or Facebook will ask why it is missing. [L1440.041]
- Apply both rules: the business location and the ads' target countries do not limit verification [L1431.044, L0637.008], but the document you submit must match the country where the business is registered [L1440.041].

**Account access**

- Use an email address you check regularly for the Business Manager, because verification codes and one-time passwords are sent there. Losing access to that inbox can lock you out of the account. [L1024.007]
- Facebook accounts can be banned for reasons outside the seller's control, including ownership verification issues with business documents. One seller lost an account a week after launching a winning product, after conflicting document requirements from Facebook. [L0690.027]

### Meta ad policy and rejections

**Policy training**

- To find the free Facebook Advertising Policy Blueprint course, go to the Policy, Data Privacy and Ads section of Meta's advertising resources. The beginner course takes about 15 to 20 minutes and issues a certificate on completion. [L0637.071]
- Keep the certificate to show in appeals that the advertiser knows Meta's policies. [L0637.071]

**Rejected ad appeal**

1. Read the rejection reason and the review points Facebook provides. [L0871.076]
2. Remove or rephrase any flagged claim, skin exposure or comparison visual. [L0871.076]
3. Submit an appeal if the ad did not break a rule. Appeals often succeed for ads with no real issue. [L0871.076]
4. Relaunch the adjusted version after the appeal. [L0871.076]

**Other policy rules**

- Ad platforms can detect a creative that was previously used in a suspended account. Reusing it may get it taken down. Resolve the policy issue at the root rather than relying on re-uploading creatives. [L0638.050]
- Meta allows fictional characters on Facebook pages only if the character is clearly a brand persona and does not impersonate a real, identifiable person. Standard ad policy still applies to every ad run from the page. (Source D, 2026.) [L1476.003]

### Meta billing and payment setup

- Add payment info to the new ad account: select the country first, then choose a payment method among debit card, credit card, PayPal or online banking, and enter the details. The account is ready to run ads once payment info is added. [L1024.018]
- Keep a valid credit card on file with Meta and pay all invoices, since unpaid balances are another reason ad accounts get restricted. [L0649.055]
- Wise is flagged and declined by Facebook because it is not a bank. Avoid using Wise to pay Facebook ads. [L0690.023]
- Paying Facebook in a local currency such as CAD is allowed, but it makes results harder to track because the local currency is weaker. Many marketers track metrics in USD for comparison. [L0690.026]

### Late payments and restricted accounts

- Late or unpaid ad account bills are a common reason for ad account disabling. Repeated overdue payments, around 3-4 times, can make Meta treat the account as suspicious, so pay due amounts on time. [L0637.023]
- Pay Facebook ad account bills on time, because late payment can trigger spend limits such as a daily cap of 50 USD. [L1212.034]
- Logging into a Facebook ad account from a different personal account, or switching between accounts, can cause Facebook to restrict the ad account's automatic payments. This produces failed payments, which can cap the daily budget and freeze ad delivery. [L1432.027]

**Recovering a restricted account after a failed payment**

1. Pay the open balance through Facebook support chat, where a support agent opens the payment for you. [L1432.028]
2. Make small advance payments to rebuild payment history. [L1432.028]
3. Confirm that the spending limit and daily budget have been restored. [L1432.028]

- After a payment failure, a Facebook account's spending limit can be cut to a low amount. One seller's limit went from 300 to 50 USD. Support may only promise an increase without applying it, so check the limit yourself after the payment is cleared. [L1432.029]

### Microsoft Ads

- If a new account is automatically suspended for "egregious violations," file an appeal as instructed by Microsoft support. [L0253.052]
- Do not create duplicate accounts or try different credentials. Duplicate accounts are flagged as a violation of Microsoft's policy. [L0253.052]
- Wait for the appeal to be approved and the account reinstated. [L0253.052]

### TikTok payments

**Account and tax requirements**

- TikTok payments may not ask for a billing address. If one is requested, a US address is required. US virtual cards or PayPal can be used as payment methods in that case. [L0363.062]
- Owning an LLC alone may not be enough for a non-US seller to open a TikTok Shop, because a tax identification number is often required. Check the exact tax ID requirement for your country with a local tax advisor before applying. [L1215.019]
- A verified TikTok Business Center account usually requires a business account plus legitimate business documents, and often an EIN. Receiving proposals requires no verification, but initiating proposals does. [L1379.044]
- You need a bank account or a payment method such as PayPal to pay creators. Confirm first whether TikTok takes a commission on Business Center deals, since this was not verified. [L1379.045]

**Automatic or manual payment**

| Mode | How it works | When the source recommends it | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| Automatic | Charges the saved credit or debit card automatically once a set threshold is reached. | Recommended option in the general setup. | L1373.004 |
| Manual | Adds budget to the TikTok wallet in advance. Automatic payment shows spend that updates a minute or two late and typically understates real spend by 10 to 30 percent. | Recommended for aggressive scaling with higher budgets, and for scaling campaigns. | L1382.045, L1436.057 |

- The sources differ on the default: automatic is recommended in one lesson, while manual is recommended for scaling. Use automatic for ordinary spend, and manual when budgets are high or campaigns are scaling. [L1373.004, L1382.045, L1436.057]

**Linking accounts**

- Linking an account may prompt a switch from a personal to a business account. One speaker reported that copyrighted sounds stop being usable after such a switch. This was described as a risk and was not verified, so check the current platform rules before linking. [L1383.015]

### New ad account payment setup

- Make the first four payments with a debit card on a new ad account, because early credit card use on a new account was linked to flagged behavior. Credit cards are better used later for their billing window. [L0351.051]
- Use a new bank account and payment method dedicated to each business. Credit cards tied to a business address in a different country than the business operates in, or whose time zone and country differ from the Google account settings, can be flagged as potentially fraudulent. Cards already flagged elsewhere or with past failed payments can also trigger suspension. (Older: Source B, 2021-22.) [L0727.010]
- The customer-facing billing descriptor (the statement name on the card) must be entered together with a number field. Do not use a personal phone number there, because customers may call that number heavily if the product goes viral. Use a separate business or alternate number. [L0655.007]
- For Ads > Billing, enter the business details and click Save, then add a credit card, debit card or virtual card. PayPal is not accepted as a payment method. [L1135.024]

### Agency accounts

- Setting up an agency account costs $400+/month. It is named as one recovery option for a banned business manager. [L0067.037]
- With an agency ad account you do not connect your own payment card. You top up the account by bank transfer or crypto instead. [L1021.017]
- ROAS Agency accepts funding by international bank transfer or crypto only. Top-ups are arranged through WhatsApp with the agency contact. The account is ready for ads only after the first top-up has been made. (Source D, 2026.) [L1253.010]
- Agency accounts are listed as a workaround for bans, but the same source advises against circumventing platform bans with agency accounts, new accounts or VPN workarounds. [L0067.037, L0577.067]

### VPN and account safety

- A VPN is only needed when researching search results from a different country. It is not required for normal business operation. (Older: Source A, 2020.) [L1111.147]
- In one case, a TikTok ad account suspension was linked to using a VPN. Check your connection and login setup before managing ads. Once the cause is found and removed, the ads account can be run again without the same problem. [L0638.057]
- Circumventing platform bans (Google, Facebook, TikTok) using agency accounts, new accounts or VPN workarounds is not recommended. The lesson advised against it in general. [L0577.067]
- Keep one consistent login and contact setup for each account, and do not switch between personal accounts when logging into an ad account. [L1432.027, L1283.056]

### Bans and recovery

**Facebook business manager**

- If the business is banned and ID-verified, account recovery is difficult. The options named in the source are using a friend's account, setting up an agency account ($400+/month), or using third-party unlock services (Fiverr). The same source advises against circumventing bans in general. [L0067.037, L0577.067]
- When a Facebook business manager account is permanently banned, the source describes this procedure:
  1. Contact a Fiverr seller specializing in business manager rental. [L0209.040]
  2. Request new account access. [L0209.040]
  3. Set up ads in the new account. [L0209.040]
- Following a correct process for launching ads and setting up the business manager reduces the ban risk. A banned account can be recovered by following a specific appeal process. Several students reported few bans when the process was followed. [L1382.068]

**Other causes**

- Reusing a creative from a suspended account can get it taken down. Resolve the root policy issue instead. [L0638.050]
- Suspensions for circumventing systems are often linked to an earlier account. Call the Google Ads policy support number to request a live review. (2023.) [L1283.054]

## Product, Claims and Advertising Legality

### Restricted and prohibited products

- Start every product decision with this filter. Products that raise legal concerns face obstacles: payment processors are reluctant to approve them, and major traffic and advertising platforms restrict promotion, which limits how far you can scale. [L0031.004]
- Do not sell or list products that require a doctor's prescription. Customers may submit fake prescriptions, and you could be held legally responsible if the wrong person obtains a prescription-required item. Avoid these products entirely. [L0255.071]
- Do not sell firearms, other weapons, knives, swords, adult products, vehicles, drug-use equipment, tobacco products or gambling products. Google, Shopify and Facebook prohibit promoting these categories, which blocks most of your marketing. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0994.026]
- Fringe products such as vaporizers, e-cigarettes and many supplements make it hard to keep a payment processor. PayPal and Shopify Payments can cut off your account when you sell them. [L0769.013]
- The source does not claim to know whether these products are legal in every market. Check the legal status yourself before choosing a product. [L0769.014]
- Paintball guns, bowhunting equipment, archery equipment, scuba diving tanks and hyperbaric chambers can fall under weapon, pressure-vessel or medical-device rules in some countries and on some marketplaces. Check the legal status and platform policy in the target market before building a store around them. [L0546.019]
- Self-defense products, such as a keychain, can be treated as weapons in the UK and US. Check the weapons laws of every target country before listing. A product that is illegal to sell in a market cannot be marketed there, so verify this before building ads. [L0995.047, L0999.071]

### Certifications, licensing and regulated categories

Compliance sequence for a regulated product:

1. Confirm the required certifications for your country and category. [L0041.016]
2. Work with your supplier, or hire a China-based testing agency to handle compliance testing. [L0041.016]
3. Check licensing or extra requirements on official government sources in your country. Do not rely on forum guesses for licensing questions. (Older advice, 2021.) [L1279.082]
4. Verify legal compliance for every target country before launch. [L0280.033]

Additional rules:

- When moving from dropshipping to manufacturing branded products, secure FDA approvals for regulated product categories, create instruction booklets, and verify legal compliance for all target countries. [L0280.033]
- Child-related products, such as car seats, cribs, bassinets, playpens, high chairs, toddler beds and baby rockers, are usually subject to child safety standards, for example federal crib and car seat rules in the US. Check the required certificates and labeling for the target country before listing them. [L0546.018]
- Product claims for DIY, building or safety-related projects must be checked against local building codes and safety standards before publication. Add a disclaimer that projects must be confirmed with local regulations before work starts. [L0794.035]
- Google and Meta restricted-category policies show which products need extra steps, such as disclaimers or policy agreements. Many restricted products can still be sold after those steps. [L0855.129]
- California Proposition 65 warnings may be required on product pages. Ask your suppliers which warning wording they recommend. A warning can be shown as plain text on the site, or through an app that offered a single free warning. (Older advice, 2025 source; no age mark applies.) [L0837.044]

### Health, medical and supplement claims

Claim rules:

- Health or pain-relief claims, such as 'remove pain', 'make leg straight again' or other medical promises, will eventually trigger account suspension, even if sales occur at first. These claims violate platform policies. [L0209.039]
- Avoid bold health claims in ad creative, captions and voiceover, such as saying a product will make you lose weight or cure a condition. Use softer phrasing that describes relief or benefit instead. [L0871.077]
- Ad copy must not claim results such as 'clinically proven' unless there is evidence to back them. Unsupported medical or health claims in ads can get the ad rejected or create legal risk. [L1423.013]
- Verify health or science claims with laboratory tests or qualified review before using them. Unverified claims can be refused by respected media or challenged. Keep the test records on file. [L1261.125]
- Health-related product claims may need legal disclaimers. Ask the manufacturer for its compliance documents rather than adding claims without legal backing. [L0855.128]
- Copying health or supplement claims from competitor pages creates regulatory and advertising risk, especially when the claims cannot be proven. Publish only health claims you can substantiate, and remove any claim you cannot back. [L0600.034]
- For health and finance products, avoid health or financial result claims in headlines and page copy. Legal rules on what can be promised differ by country, and regulators can act against non-compliant claims. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0416.014]
- Products in the health space allow fewer claims in copy, so the wording of benefits must be checked against those limits. Mapping still works for unfamiliar, technical or expensive products, but health-related benefits must be phrased with restraint. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1160.013]

Safer wording:

| Avoid | Safer direction | Ids |
|---|---|---|
| Detoxifying the body, releasing toxins, healing tissue | Neutral wording such as 'a relaxing experience'; or have evidence ready and check each country's ad rules (older advice, 2021-22) | L0465.022 |
| Losing weight, curing a condition, removing pain | Softer phrasing that describes relief or benefit | L0871.077, L0209.039 |
| 'Clinically proven' without evidence | Use only with real evidence on file | L1423.013 |

Supplements:

- Verify each product's ingredients with third-party identity testing to confirm the active ingredients are present. This is harder for botanicals, but it is stronger proof than a label alone. [L0334.047]
- For US supplement ads and product pages, add a disclaimer that the statements have not been evaluated by the FDA and that the product is not meant to diagnose, treat, cure or prevent any disease. Place the same wording on ad creatives and landing pages before launch. [L1164.054]
- Health-related ebooks and supplement gifts should carry a note that the content is educational and does not replace advice from a qualified healthcare professional. Keep product claims soft and framed as support, not as a fix. [L0862.031]
- Do not build character pages with fake medical authority, such as an invented doctor, for medical treatments, supplements or health claims. This can imply endorsement of unapproved treatments, may be illegal and may breach platform terms. Non-medical authority, such as a parent or an experienced hobbyist, is much safer. [L1476.004]

### Income, finance and results claims

- Income claims cannot be guaranteed, and promising returns to customers or prospects is not allowed. Describe only results you can measure. (Older advice, 2020.) [L0852.099]
- For Meta ads promoting financial products to prospects aged 55+, fabricated testimonials, unverified star ratings, review counts and unattributed social proof create FTC liability.
  - Use only real, verified social proof with documentation, or leave social proof empty.
  - Fabricated evidence is an FTC violation, not a creative weakness. [L0221.014]

### Reviews, testimonials and endorsements

- Never copy reviews from a competitor's website, a competitor's supplier site, Amazon or any other online store. This is not allowed and can lead to legal trouble. [L0908.006]
- Reviews imported from a supplier's marketplace and shown as your customers' reviews can breach consumer-protection rules against fake reviews, such as the US FTC rule in force since October 2024, and platform policies. Show only genuine reviews, or clearly label imported reviews as coming from another source. [L0476.036]
- Attribute quotes, reviews or expert bylines only to real people who agreed to them, and keep every claim consistent with what the product actually does. Invented doctors or fake testimonials count as misleading advertising in most markets. The same applies to fabricated statistics or news-style sources. [L0578.038]
- Do not publish invented testimonials, made-up customer counts or fabricated guarantees, such as '2,000,000+ happy customers'. These claims can mislead buyers and create legal risk under consumer-protection and advertising rules. Use only reviews and counts you can verify. [L1188.034]
- Every specific claim must be true and provable. Readers take specific facts at face value, and the advertiser is accountable for them. Publish only figures that come from real tests or measurements. [L1261.124]

### Comparative claims and patent language

- Comparative claims, such as 'produces more than the closest competitor', need test results or documented evidence behind them before they go into product copy or ads. Keep the test report on file. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0465.021]
- Use the label Patent Pending only while a patent application for that feature is filed and not yet granted. Do not describe a feature as patented until the patent has actually been granted. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L0465.031]

### Fake news sites, advertorials and disguised mailings

- Use legitimate articles on legitimate websites. Never create fake news sites or fake blogs to disguise advertising. Regulators such as the FTC have acted against this practice, and it damages business credibility. Ethics and honesty protect the long-term business. [L0523.071]
- Tricked or disguised mailings annoy some readers, especially in small markets where people talk to each other. Limit disguised mailings to targeted prospects or customers who will be interested once they open the mail. This reduces annoyance and raises response. [L1370.039]
- Before writing any legal consequence into a letter, check the debt collection, consumer protection and fraud laws in your own country. Historical letters threatened criminal charges that may not fit current rules. Describe only actions you are legally allowed to take. (Older advice, 2019-2022 letters.) [L1369.103]

### Giveaways, discounts and subscriptions

Giveaways:

1. Write giveaway terms and conditions that satisfy the legal requirements of each country where entries are accepted. Adapt the terms per country. [L0797.009]
2. Award the advertised quantity of product. The landing page and the ads must state that about nine out of ten entrants will lose, and the same disclosure must appear in the terms and conditions. [L0797.010]

Discounts:

- Before launching any discount, check that it complies with the consumer-law rules of each market you sell in. For Australia, this means the ACCC requirements. Treat this check as part of launch preparation, not an afterthought. [L1404.033]

Subscriptions:

- Undisclosed subscription terms on a product page lead to unexpected recurring charges, one-star reviews and refund requests. State the subscription, billing frequency and cancellation rules clearly before checkout. [L1164.018]

### Shipping promises

- Mention fast, insured shipping in product or search copy. In many countries, including Australia and the US, the seller must replace a shipment that goes missing even if the customer did not pay for insurance. The seller therefore carries that insurance cost anyway. [L0575.015]

### Country-specific consumer-law rules

Key checks by market:

| Market | Check | Ids |
|---|---|---|
| US | FTC fake-review rule, in force since October 2024 | L0476.036 |
| US | FDA disclaimer for supplement ads and pages | L1164.054 |
| US | Federal crib and car seat rules for child products | L0546.018 |
| US | California Proposition 65 warnings | L0837.044 |
| UK and US | Weapons laws for self-defense items | L0995.047, L0999.071 |
| Australia | ACCC requirements for discounts | L1404.033 |
| Australia and US | Replacement of missing shipments, even without paid insurance | L0575.015 |
| Canada | Import, licensing and tax rules. A coach reported (2022, shared as experience, not legal advice) that a US supplier with stock already warehoused in the US needed no Canadian license to sell into Canada. Verify for your product and country before selling. (Older advice, 2022.) | L0963.157 |

General rule for every market:

- Verify the import, licensing and tax rules for your specific product and country before selling. [L0963.157]
- Check the legal status of each product in each target market before building ads or a store around it. [L0769.014, L0546.019]

## Intellectual Property, Trademarks and Copyright

### Trademark, patent and licence checks before naming or selecting products

- Before selecting a product, check it for trademark or patent infringement. Generic products such as garlic presses and resistance bands rarely have trademark issues, while unique design patents require a trademark search. [L0228.045]
- If trademark infringement occurs, switch products and recover the inventory investment by selling the remaining stock at wholesale price, so the financial loss is avoidable. [L0228.045]
- Do not sell products that carry trademarked or licensed characters, such as Game of Thrones merchandise, unless you hold the rights to sell them. Check every product in a store for such content and remove the ones you cannot sell. [L0355.005]
- Confirm you have rights to a product before scaling it. Selling copyrighted products without authorized-reseller status raises the risk of DMCA complaints through Meta and Shopify, which can take down a store and its ads. [L0351.048]
- Avoid selling merchandise that uses a singer's or public figure's likeness or name without checking copyright, because such products can violate copyright. [L1131.041]
- Before selling branded merchandise, such as a card game with a licensed game theme, confirm you have the right to sell that brand. A marketplace or supplier listing the product does not prove that permission exists. [L1216.035]
- Products that use famous brand IP, such as Nintendo characters, carry copyright risk. If you test one, use a separate Shopify store and a separate Facebook ad account, never your main ad account, because stores can be shut down for copyright violations. [L1185.009]
- Before production, hire a patent attorney to check that your product does not infringe existing patents. Patent infringement is a bigger risk for large products, but small products are also exposed, so budget for this check. [L1154.013] (Source B 2021-22, older advice)
- A patent offers a limited period of protection, and the name tied to it may lose exclusivity when the patent expires. Check name protection and trademark status separately from patent status. [L1261.127]

### Checking a store name and buying a domain

- Before picking a store name, search the US trademark registry for conflicts. Do not plan to rely on being able to absorb a lawsuit, since avoiding infringement is the safer approach. [L0468.159] (Source A 2020, older advice)
- Quick name check procedure [L0989.017, L0989.018]:
  1. Search the chosen name on Trademarkia, a free trademark search covering more than 12 million trademarks.
  2. Search the name on Google and check that it has no slang, swear word or unwanted meaning, for example on Urban Dictionary.
  3. If both searches return nothing problematic, the name is acceptable for the store.
- The trademark check described here is a quick screen, not a formal clearance. A brand that will be built seriously should still get proper trademark advice before major investment. [L0989.018]
- When buying a resold domain from a third-party marketplace, prefer a platform that holds payment in escrow and offers a guaranteed domain transfer. Marketplace pages of this kind also show a best-price guarantee, but compare the price yourself on other sites before paying. [L0989.031]

### Registering trademarks, designs and Amazon Brand Registry

- Register your brand on Amazon Brand Registry even if you do not sell on Amazon yet. A lawyer can handle this, and it protects your trademark rights. [L0041.039]
- Get as many trademarks as possible and make sure they are fully registered, not pending. Google, Alibaba and AliExpress only process takedown requests when you provide fully registered trademark numbers. [L0041.040]
- Get registered designs, where available in your country, for product design, packaging and paint or colour if possible. These create takedown grounds beyond trademark. [L0041.041]
- Trademark registrations list Nice classes. Record the class of each registration per country. For an apparel and textile brand, check class 24 (textiles, including blankets) and class 25 (clothing), and confirm the registrations cover the products being copied. [L0991.033]
- Use EUIPO (European Union Intellectual Property Office) for EU trademarks. Its registry entries give the registration number and the direct link used in infringement reports. [L0991.009]
- Keep a Google Sheets trademark register that lists each country, trademark name, word mark, class, status, registration number and registry link, so all trademark evidence is ready for reports. [L0991.011]
- Attach proof of ownership to trademark reports, such as registry certificates or registry search pages. Two registrations give extra verification. Include the registration number and a direct registry link when available. [L0991.008]

**Timing of trademark filing (sources differ):**

| Source | Advice | Applies when |
|---|---|---|
| Source E 2025-26 | Register as many trademarks as possible, fully registered [L0041.040] | You need takedown grounds on Google, Alibaba and AliExpress |
| Source A 2018 (older) | Not needed at the start of a store. Relevant when someone copies the business or once it is established. Filing fee is about 200 dollars [L0466.067] | The store is new and nobody is copying it |
| Source A 2019 (older) | Trademarking the business name is optional for most businesses and not necessary when starting out. It can be done later once the business is established [L0357.045] | The store is new and nobody is copying it |

Use the older advice while the store is unproven and no one is copying it. Use the Source E advice once you need takedown power on those platforms. [L0466.067, L0357.045, L0041.040]

### Reporting infringement on Meta and Instagram

- Brand infringement reports on Meta can only be filed for a trademark registered in the jurisdiction where the infringing party targets customers. A trademark registered in the EU supports a report against an ad aimed at the European market. [L0991.001]
- Infringers may use a different jurisdiction than their actual location. Check the advertiser details and page transparency information, and use the claimed location when it is the only information available. Record any location in the log. [L0991.013]
- The Meta Ad Library shows active ads and advertiser details, including the country and EU transparency data. Use it to find infringing ads and to identify the jurisdiction where the advertiser targets customers. [L0991.010]
- The Ad Library's EU transparency section for an ad shows the advertiser details, the beneficiary and the payer. Capture this section in the screenshot attached to the report. [L0991.034]
- Report two kinds of infringement separately: copyright (stolen product photos or images) and trademark (unauthorized use of the brand name or logo). Each needs its own form and its own argument. [L0991.002]
- Meta's trademark form defines a trademark as a word, slogan, symbol or design used to tell a company's products apart from others. It also offers a separate option for content believed to offer counterfeit goods. Choose the path that matches whether the content copies a product or only makes unauthorized use of the brand name or logo. [L0991.028]
- Use the trademark form only for alleged trademark infringements. Other claim types are not handled through it, and the form warns that misuse can lead to the reporter's account being terminated. Keep other complaints on their own forms. [L0991.029]
- On the Facebook trademark form, the question *Why do you believe this content infringes rights owner's trademark rights?* offers these options: *This photo, video, post or story uses rights owner's trademark.*, *This ad is using rights owner's trademark.*, *Rights owner's trademark is used in the username.* and *Other*. Pick the option that matches the type of content reported. [L0991.032]

**Facebook trademark report procedure** [L0991.003]:
1. Find the infringing ad (e.g. in the Meta Ad Library) and copy its link.
2. Open the platform's 'report a violation' form (found by searching the platform name plus the words report form).
3. Choose the trademark option and confirm you are reporting a trademark infringement.
4. Select 'I am the rights owner' (or the matching relationship if you act for an organisation or client).
5. Enter your full name, mailing address, and a dedicated reporting email address on the brand's domain.
6. Enter the rights owner's name and a link to the brand's official online presence (website or official page).
7. Enter the trademark name, the jurisdiction, the registration number and a direct link to the registry entry for each trademark (one trademark at a time, then add more at the end).
8. Attach a scan or screenshot of each registration certificate. Accepted formats: JPG, GIF, PNG, TIFF, PDF. For registry pages, the first page is usually enough.
9. Select the reason, e.g. 'This ad is using rights owner's trademark'.
10. Paste the direct link to the ad and the link to the page that posted it.
11. Describe where the ad appears (e.g. the Meta Ad Library, with the library ID).
12. Attach a screenshot of the ad, including the advertiser details section if visible.
13. Add any extra information that supports the claim, e.g. that no authorization was given and a request to remove the content.
14. Tick the declaration that the information is accurate and that you are authorized to act.
15. Submit, enter the security code sent to the reporting email, and complete the final check.
16. Wait for the automatic confirmation email, which contains the complaint ID. Record the complaint ID in your log.

**Instagram copyright report procedure** [L0991.004]:
1. Find the Instagram copyright report form (search the platform name plus copyright report form).
2. Select 'I am the rights owner' (or the matching relationship if you act for an organisation, client or someone else).
3. Enter your full name, mailing address and a dedicated reporting email address on the brand's domain.
4. Enter the rights owner's name and the email address.
5. Select the type of copyrighted work (here: photo) and provide public links to the original images on your official website or official Facebook page, or describe the work in the box.
6. Select the type of content you are reporting (photo, video or post) and paste the direct links to the infringing content.
7. Describe why the content is infringing and add any extra information that supports the claim.
8. Tick the declaration, submit, enter the security code sent to the reporting email, and complete the additional check if asked.
9. Record the complaint ID once it arrives.

**Who files, which email, which links:**
- Use a dedicated reporting email address on the brand's own domain (e.g. name@brand-domain), used only for infringement reports. Platforms check that the sender is an authorized rights owner, so a generic or personal address increases the risk of rejection. [L0991.005]
- Platforms regularly give the rights owner's name, the reporter's email address and the nature of the report to the account that posted the content, and that account may use these details to contact the reporter. Use a generic business or professional email address in reports. [L0991.030]
- The rights owner name in a report must be the person or company that actually holds the trademark or copyright. If a founder personally holds the rights, enter that person's name as rights owner and have them authorize the staff who file reports. [L0991.031]
- Authorize staff to file legal reports on your behalf only after training them properly and trusting them, because the declarations in the form are legally binding. [L0991.007]
- The complainant must confirm that the report is made in good faith and that the information is accurate. The platform sends a security code to the reporting email, and the report is only submitted after that code is entered. [L0991.012]
- Every link submitted in a copyright report is sent to the accused party. Only provide publicly accessible links, such as live product pages or official social posts. Never share private cloud folders with unedited, high-resolution or unreleased images or videos, because this gives the infringer access to your assets. [L0991.006]

### Managing copycats and takedowns

- Manage counterfeit products and copycats with a VA team, not AI software. The team monitors each platform, reports listings and tracks which takedowns succeeded. This is a manual process but necessary. [L0041.042]
- Use trademark numbers in takedown requests. When a competitor uses your brand name in a Google Ad, lodge a takedown form and provide your registered trademark numbers. [L0041.043]
- For large-scale counterfeiting, you have the option to attempt factory raids and seize counterfeit products. Provide trademark numbers in all takedown requests to Google, Alibaba and AliExpress. [L0041.044]
- Plagiarism is unavoidable once a store becomes a major player in a niche, so it must be dealt with quickly. Copycat stores often pull your watermarked images, copy your customer reviews, use your PDFs and copy your policy pages. [L1357.043]
- Account risks on the backend include Meta bans, DMCA takedowns and Shopify account removals due to DMCA claims. Ignoring these while focusing only on growth leads to repeated losses. Avoid them by reviewing your brand, content and listings for copyright issues before scaling. [L0906.022]
- Check your store for plagiarism of your content by reviewing these areas: 1) watermarked images, 2) customer reviews, 3) documents, 4) return policy, shipping policy and FAQ pages. [L0920.032]

**Shopify DMCA procedure** [L0920.033]:
1. Collect all instances of the copied content.
2. Submit a single notice listing all instances at https://www.shopify.com/legal/dmca, since Shopify prefers one submission and multiple instances can weaken the case against the copycat store.
3. If you must submit more than once, space submissions 1 to 2 weeks apart.

**Checking competitor stores for copied content** [L1357.044]:
1. Check for your watermarked product images.
2. Check for your real customer reviews.
3. Check your unique PDFs for your metadata or logo.
4. Compare their return policy, shipping policy and FAQ pages with yours.
5. Submit a DMCA takedown through the Shopify DMCA page, found by searching Shopify DMCA.
6. Submit each instance of plagiarism. Shopify generally prefers one submission listing all instances, but submitting multiple times spaced one to two weeks apart was reported to hurt copycat stores more.
7. Shopify reportedly requires the copied page to be taken down within 24 to 48 hours or shuts the store down.

Note on submission count: one source says multiple submissions weaken the case [L0920.033], while another reports that submissions spaced one to two weeks apart hurt copycat stores more [L1357.044]. Both agree that one consolidated submission is the default. [L0920.033, L1357.044]

- Rights holders can take action through DMCA notices from competitors or rights holders. Shopify provides the DMCA notice process for store-level reports. [L1218.031]

### Responding to claims against your store

- If you receive a copyright claim over a product image, act in this order [L1113.051] (Source A 2022, older advice):
  1. Remove the disputed image and the page it appears on right away.
  2. Ask the supplier where the image came from and whether they can show proof of rights or permission.
  3. Keep the claimant informed of progress.
  4. Consider a free lawyer question platform for advice, since this is not legal advice.
- If you receive a trademark complaint about your store name, ask for a free answer on a legal Q&A platform before changing the name, and do not decide based only on general advice. [L0854.092] (Source A 2022, older advice)
- If you get an email from a company warning of trademark infringement or a legal violation, search the sender's domain together with the words scam and trademark. Emails can be spoofed, so verify before responding. [L1285.079]

### Books and copyrighted written content

- Running a big-name author's book at scale can trigger DMCA takedown notices against your Shopify store and your Meta ad accounts, including ad agency accounts and personal accounts. Books by large, well-known authors carry the highest risk. [L0560.006]
- Case: a famous author's book was run as a main product with a proven concept and was running for an extended period. The store received a DMCA notice in Shopify and a DMCA notice in Meta. Lesson: running an unchanged big-author book at scale exposes the store and ad accounts to takedowns, so customisation must come first. [L0560.007]
- Customise a book only after it is a proven concept with a consistently profitable P&L, typically starting around day 6 or 7 of running it. Customisation is the lesson's safeguard against DMCA and plagiarism flags. [L0560.008]
- A DMCA claim from the main author of a book can lead to a Meta ad ban or a Shopify DMCA action against the store. Before launching a book that its author actively sells, plan a customised cover or internal edition. [L1175.025]
- Running a competitor's cover photo next to their ad raises the risk of a DMCA claim, which can get the Meta ad account or the Shopify store banned. The risk applies to both platforms. [L1030.017]
- Custom covers and custom UGC keep an ad from matching competitors' ads. This reduces DMCA exposure and makes it less likely that competitors can report the ads. [L1030.018]
- Rewording a copyrighted book, changing the author name or altering its images does not make the content your own. If the structure and meaning are still copied, it is infringement. A rights holder can send a cease-and-desist letter requiring you to stop selling, then pursue a claim. Use only book content you own or are licensed to use. [L0794.034]
- Customising a book to reduce DMCA and plagiarism risk [L0560.009]:
  1. Get the soft copy (PDF) of the book from your agent.
  2. Rewrite the wording with Claude while keeping the same meaning.
  3. Change the title completely.
  4. Change the formatting of the internal pages and the images.
  5. Change the cover page.
  6. Customise the UGC and AI ad creatives used to sell the book.
- Never copy or resell other people's ebooks or content. Use royalty-free PLR material or write original content yourself. [L0513.037]
- Reselling ebooks or digital products without rights can cause copyright issues. Ask the copyright owner for permission or create a very similar product. [L1184.052]
- Copying competitor text, testimonials and photos word for word can infringe copyright and creates a trust problem if the claims do not match your product. Rewrite copy in your own words and replace photos and testimonials with ones you have the rights to use. [L0600.033]
- Copying competitor images or product text led to copyright reports from another company, and the store was shut down. Create your own content and product text instead of reusing competitor material. [L1444.051]

### Video, music and sound

- Reusing footage from other creators or ads carries rights risk. Weigh that risk consciously before publishing an adapted ad. [L0341.036]
- Use only royalty-free music, sound effects and footage you are licensed to use, because copyrighted audio taken from video platforms can get an ad into trouble. Check the licence before publishing any clip or track you downloaded. [L0344.015]
- Before using a trending sound in a store's product video, verify that the sound is licensed for commercial use in the platform's own commercial sound library. A sound can be trending widely and still not appear on that list. [L0655.030]
- Sounds that list a real artist or named creator are usually copyrighted and will be blocked for ads. Bootleg sounds without a real artist name, including sped-up or slowed versions, are generally usable. [L1383.018]
- Music must be checked for copyright before it goes into an ad, because copyright strikes can stop the ad from running. CapCut has a copyright check in the Audio tab. A licensed library such as Envato, with a subscription, is an alternative for music cleared for use. [L1423.011]
- Downloading sound effects from YouTube through MP3 converters, or using free icons without checking the licence, can create copyright problems. Free icons often require attribution, so confirm the licence before publishing an ad. [L1423.012]
- Reusing another creator's footage carries copyright risk. Large channels are more likely to request takedown or payment, and reused footage can be flagged even when you recut it. [L1427.061]
- Speeding up, trimming or re-cutting another creator's footage does not reliably hide it from the ad platform, and the original creator can still request a takedown or demand payment.
