Reviews or customer videos that are verified content carry extra risk. Use footage you own, licensed stock, or supplier footage you have the right to use. [L1427.075]
- Test a concept with existing footage as a proof of concept before ordering stock or shooting your own footage. Once the concept shows results, invest in original footage. [L1427.062]
- Do not run another brand's video in ads beyond short tests, because copyright takedowns can disable your store or content. Treat copied videos as a short-term test, not a foundation for the campaign. [L1377.047]
- Running another seller's video unchanged can breach copyright or the rules of the ad platform. Check the rights and the platform policies before launching ripped creatives. [L1252.022]
- Never take a real creator's UGC video, screenshot the person and build a character page that copies them. This counts as impersonation and is fraudulent. [L1476.029]

### Fonts, stock assets and images

- Using fonts without a proper commercial licence exposes your business to lawsuits. Font foundries actively scan the internet for unauthorised font use. One company using the wrong font in the wrong places was sued for significant damages. Always use system fonts available in Shopify or legally licensed sources such as Google Fonts. [L0317.016]
- Stock images, audio and videos included in a Canva subscription were understood to be licensed for use in designs. Verify the licence terms in the Canva licence before using them in paid ads or products. [L0754.025]
- Supplier photos are commonly used with written permission, and a written price list alone is not a contract. Ask suppliers to confirm permission in writing before using their images. [L1283.105]
- A free or demo image may be used on a test site, but a live store that sells products needs commercially licensed images. Without a commercial licence, the creator can ask for the image to be removed for copyright reasons. [L1310.010] (Source B 2021-22, older advice)
- An image licensed through Adobe Stock can be used on a live selling website as many times as needed. Use only images you have bought a licence for on any store that sells products. [L1310.011] (Source B 2021-22, older advice)
- For product images on demo stores, use Google Image Search filtered for 'labeled for commercial reuse'. Do not purchase expensive commercial licences or copy images from supplier websites. [L0245.066] (Source A 2020, older advice)

### Creator content and usage rights

- Usage rights define how long the brand may use the video, in which territory, on which channels and formats, and what extra costs apply to keep using it. Include all of these in the brief before filming. [L0579.021]
- Define usage rights in the offer: duration, start date (for example six months from the date the first post goes live on the creator's account), territory, and every format where the brand may use the content. Formats should include websites and e-commerce product pages, email marketing, digital ads, and both computer and mobile platforms. [L0783.015]
- In many contracts a set usage period, such as 30 days, is included in the agreed rate. Continued use as paid media beyond that period can be charged an additional fee per month. [L0579.022]
- Example usage terms from a keyboard brand's brief: 6 months of use, the term ending six months after the first social post goes live on the creator's account, worldwide, across brand-controlled social accounts, brand and third-party websites, e-commerce product pages, email marketing, digital ads, and on computer and mobile platforms. Fee: $20,000 for this package. [L0579.023]
- Before reusing creator-made video, get written usage rights from the creator covering ownership, how long you may use it, and where it may appear. Posting creator content without approval can cause legal trouble. [L0784.001]
- If creator content will run as paid media, the agreement needs a clause stating the usage duration. Such clauses usually carry a fee for the usage period. [L0784.002]
- Example: if a brand uses creator content for 60 days and wants to renew for another month, the creator might be paid about 250 USD per extra month of renewal. [L0784.003]
- Some brands negotiate perpetual usage rights, but this is less favourable for the creator, so the request often meets resistance. Agree on a finite term and a renewal option instead. [L0784.004]
- Define the usage terms clearly before agreeing on a fee. State which exclusivity and which usage rights you need. Exclusive usage rights, or the right to repurpose content across channels and paid ads, usually require a higher fee. [L0785.006]
- Negotiate usage terms openly with the creator so both sides are satisfied. Document the rights granted to each party in writing. [L0785.007]

## Liability, Insurance and Risk Protection

### Insurance types for e-commerce

- The main e-commerce insurance types are Product Liability Insurance and DNO (Direct Officer) Insurance [L0041.015].
- Recall insurance exists but is very difficult to obtain [L0041.015].
- Product liability insurance is optional. If you want it, compare quotes from several insurers [L0671.123].

### Where lawsuits come from

- As a successful business you will be sued, possibly unfairly, through an accident, competitor action or totally unfounded claims. Expect it and prepare by keeping legal reserves [L0041.045].
- Most litigation risk in e-commerce comes from the business itself: terms of service, compliance, intellectual property and subscriptions. It does not mainly come from uncontrollable external events. Identify and fix the controllable risks [L0041.049].
- Lawsuits against a small e-commerce business are rare, but the financial consequences can be severe. This is a low-probability, high-impact risk, and choosing the right business structure is a protective measure against it [L1405.040] (older advice, 2021-22).
- Customers in the US can sue almost anyone, so general liability cover is a matter of your risk tolerance [L0961.021] (older advice, 2020).
- If a product could cause injury, a lawsuit may name every party a lawyer can find, including the seller and the supplier [L0961.022] (older advice, 2020).
- Plaintiffs often sue every party in the chain, including the store [L0247.105] (older advice, 2021).

### Supplier protection

- Suppliers do not protect you from product liability claims [L0247.105] (older advice, 2021).
- If you white label through a manufacturer, it will often cover your product liability under its umbrella insurance policy [L1154.012] (older advice, 2021-22).
- If the manufacturer does not cover you, you need your own product liability insurance, which is usually not very expensive [L1154.012] (older advice, 2021-22).

### Product liability and product safety

- Assess product liability by considering every way the product could injure someone, including misuse, and every person who might use it, not only the intended buyer [L1154.010] (older advice, 2021-22).
- Address all required warnings on the product to protect your company [L1154.010] (older advice, 2021-22).
- Products sold in plastic bag packaging need a suffocation hazard warning printed on the packaging [L1154.011] (older advice, 2021-22).
- Confirm the exact legally required wording rather than relying on memory, and budget money to get all required warnings right [L1154.011] (older advice, 2021-22).
- Avoid products that could be dangerous. If that risk concerns you, consider a general liability policy [L0961.022] (older advice, 2020).

### Business structure and asset separation

- Business liability insurance depends on the business entity, the expected liability and the owner's risk tolerance. Consult a professional before buying policies, because brokers are salespeople [L1111.135] (older advice, 2020).
- Choosing a business entity is similar to choosing insurance: the choice is up to the owner [L0472.046].
- Run the business through an LLC or S corporation and keep business expenses separate from personal ones [L0853.064] (older advice, 2021).
- Consider general liability insurance once the business holds meaningful assets, and confirm the approach with a professional [L0853.064] (older advice, 2021).
- An LLC or S-corp limits personal liability only when assets are kept separate. Consult an accountant once the business earns money [L0247.105] (older advice, 2021).

### General liability insurance: is it required?

- General liability insurance is optional for a US e-commerce business. Some suppliers require it before they will work with you [L0472.046].
- It is optional and depends on supplier requirements [L0468.152] (older advice, 2020).
- Most suppliers do not require business insurance [L0855.122].
- If a gold supplier requires it, request general liability quotes from the insurer you already use for home or auto cover [L0855.122].
- It is optional and depends on risk versus reward, meaning how much you have to lose [L0934.011].
- It is a personal decision. The business can run without it, though it may be useful depending on the risk [L1112.060] (older advice, 2021).
- It is optional and depends on where you operate [L1279.078] (older advice, 2021).
- It is recommended for peace of mind [L0249.075] (older advice, 2022).
- It is useful as the business grows [L0665.096] (older advice, 2022).
- Business insurance is not required for dropshipping in the US. For the UK, the speaker did not know of a requirement, so a local answer should be checked [L0964.113].

### Costs and coverage size

| Figure | Context | Source year | Ids |
|---|---|---|---|
| About $30 per month | Small store, example | 2020 (older) | L0468.153 |
| $100 per month or less | Some providers, large sales volumes | 2022 (older) | L0249.075 |

- The $30 figure applies to a small store. The $100 figure applies to providers offering coverage for large sales volumes. Both are older figures [L0468.153, L0249.075].
- Coverage size should be set according to revenue, which the insurer will ask about [L0468.153] (older advice, 2020).

### Buying without brokers

Sources differ on which channel to use. They agree on avoiding small or unvetted brokers and on comparing several quotes [L0249.076, L1094.084, L1279.078, L0671.122, L0671.259, L0961.021].

- Use an independent quote-comparison website and choose the lowest price for suitable coverage. A higher price does not mean better protection [L1088.081, L1088.095] (older advice, 2020).
- Buy from a large direct insurer rather than through a broker, since commissions make broker policies expensive [L0468.152] (older advice, 2020).
- Get general liability quotes through the same insurer that holds your personal insurance, such as auto or home. This usually gives better rates and less risk of being taken advantage of than online small business brokers [L1281.070] (older advice, 2022).
- Ask the insurer you already use for home, auto or life policies for a business policy, and compare it with other quotes [L0934.011, L0934.142].
- Start with a large insurer you already use for other insurance [L0804.098].
- Shop through major agencies such as Geico for Business, or ask connections in insurance [L0249.075] (older advice, 2022).
- Avoid small brokerages and use major agencies [L0249.076] (older advice, 2022).
- Be careful with brokers who charge high premiums, and compare multiple quotes before buying [L0671.122, L0671.259] (older advice, 2021).
- Brokers can overcharge heavily for weak coverage [L1279.078] (older advice, 2021).
- Agents may overcharge for policies you do not need [L0961.021] (older advice, 2020).
- Buy business insurance without overpaying [L1069.069] (older advice, 2019):
  1. Get a general, catch-all business policy first, since it covers the basics [L1069.069].
  2. Ask several insurance brokers for quotes, not only one [L1069.069].
  3. Be careful with agents who offer every policy they can sell, because many coverages may be unnecessary [L1069.069].
  4. Review the coverage again as revenue grows, since insurers may audit the business and premiums can rise [L1069.069].
- Start with a small general liability policy. It is a cheaper starting point than a bundle of policies, and more coverage can be added later. Pick coverage based on actual risk [L1111.137] (older advice, 2020).

### Premium audits and renewals

- Insurance audits can happen retroactively based on revenue growth. They can result in bills above $10,000 for past premiums, even if no claims occurred [L0249.076] (older advice, 2022).
- A revenue audit on a broker-sold policy can lead to tens of thousands in extra premiums [L1088.081] (older advice, 2020).
- Do not buy general liability through a broker, because a revenue audit after the policy starts can produce large extra premiums [L1088.095] (older advice, 2020).
- A carrier or broker audit after the first year can produce a large retroactive premium bill when sales grow. Check how audits and premiums are calculated before signing [L1111.136] (older advice, 2020).
- Before buying, ask the provider how year-end premium audits adjust the price [L0804.098].
- Check the premium once revenue grows. Low-price quotes can produce large bills at audit [L0665.096] (older advice, 2022).
- A cheap first-year rate can increase sharply later [L1094.084].
- Quotes can rise sharply at renewal, so compare quotes from several insurers [L0934.142, L0934.011].

### Shipping insurance

- Shipping insurance is important for expensive products, since it covers damage claims and lets you resolve them without a full return [L0970.145].
- Carrier shipping insurance policies determine whether a damaged item in transit is refunded along with shipping costs. Check the carrier's policy before deciding who pays [L1094.088].

### Licensing for food and special categories

- Before selling food or beverage products, check the licenses and regulations that apply. They add steps beyond normal drop shipping [L0854.091] (older advice, 2022).

## Contracts, Partners, Contractors and Agencies

### Written contracts and agency agreements

- Use a written contract even when the relationship feels casual or informal. It protects both parties legally, clarifies expectations, and gives you recourse if problems arise. [L0217.019]
- Every contract should specify:
  - payment structure
  - timeline of work
  - cancellation terms
  - performance expectations
  - communication protocols
  - penalties for unlawful behavior, such as deleting accounts without consent [L0217.020]
- Get a written contract even if the agency offers performance guarantees, refund guarantees, or payment-only-if-successful terms. Legal protection matters regardless of how confident either party feels. [L0217.035]
- Written contracts with offshore freelancers and staff are often seen as unnecessary for simple engagements, because cross-border legal issues are complicated. The main value of a contract is making each party's position and obligations clear. [L1359.039]

### Contractors, freelancers and employee classification

#### United States: 1099 contractor or employee

- In the US, pay independent contractors, including family members who work as freelancers, as 1099 contractors. Agree on this and on the expectations in writing before the work starts. [L0832.053]
- The team in the Source A example was hired as 1099 independent contractors, not employees. If the owner sets fixed hours and full-time control over the work, the contractor may need to be converted to an employee. That brings insurance and benefits obligations. Check local labor and tax laws before deciding. [L0337.026]
- Where the two rules meet: a 1099 setup assumes the contractor controls their own working pattern. If you set fixed hours and control the work full-time, the arrangement may need to become employment, as described above. [L0337.026, L0832.053, L1000.038, L1473.021]

#### Paying hourly contractors

Pay hourly contractors every two weeks:

1. Each contractor logs their hours on their personal board in Trello.
2. Every other Thursday the owner checks the logged hours.
3. Pay US contractors through QuickBooks.
4. Pay Philippines-based contractors through Wise with direct deposit.

[L0337.027]

#### Virtual assistants

- Sign a VA contract before training starts. Include:
  - pay
  - weekly work hours (for example Monday to Friday, 9:00 to 17:00 Eastern Standard Time)
  - the holidays observed (here US holidays)
  - vacation time and how much notice is required for it
  - how much notice is required to quit
  - the training obligations [L1473.021]
- Sign a short agreement letter with the assistant that repeats the job post terms in writing. Include an exclusivity clause stating the assistant will not work for other businesses. [L1000.037]
- Classify the virtual assistant as an independent contractor rather than an employee in the signed agreement. Confirm the tax reporting rules for your country with a qualified adviser before paying. [L1000.038]

#### Non-disclosure agreements

- For an NDA template, search Google for an NDA template in Google Docs, then adapt it to your needs before sharing it with a contractor. [L1285.077]

### Data and account ownership

- Clarify data ownership in the contract. Verify that ad accounts, creative files, and account configurations remain your property, and that the agency cannot delete or withhold access if the relationship ends. [L0217.024]
- Store all supplier, shipping and app logins in one restricted accounts sheet, so the assistant has what they need without searching. Keep the owner's social media logins there too when the assistant manages those accounts. [L1000.055]
- Do not give Fiverr freelancers access to Google Ads or Google Merchant Center accounts. If you need help unsuspending an account, use a reputable PPC agency with a public reputation, since logins from unfamiliar IP addresses can flag an account. (Older advice, 2022.) [L0803.049]

### Payment providers and bank access

- Give a team member access to your payment providers only after you have built enough trust with them, and handle this step with great care. They can install the payment providers in the store so it goes live. [L1358.013, L1360.014]
- If an assistant pays for orders, give them a dedicated payment method, such as a PayPal account or a credit card, rather than the owner's main account. [L1173.035]
- While delegating everything else, the owner kept control of the bank accounts and of supplier payments that needed transfers. This kept financial control in one place during a seven-week absence. [L1437.034]

### Business partnerships

- Two partners can own a store through a new corporation split 50/50 or 51/49, or through their existing LLCs owning the corporation. Whichever structure is chosen, a clear written partnership agreement is required. (Older advice, 2021.) [L0834.123]
- Any business partnership needs a written agreement covering:
  - the responsibilities of each partner
  - the money split
  - every foreseeable event
- Typical clauses cover one partner wanting to buy out the other, the death of a partner, and a partner failing to meet their obligations. [L1097.007]
- AI tools can produce a useful first draft, but a lawyer should review the final agreement before anyone signs. [L1097.007]
- Prompt for drafting a first partnership agreement:

```
I run a dropshipping store called [STORE NAME] on Shopify with my business partner [PARTNER NAME]. We have no written partnership agreement yet. Please help me draft a one-page partnership agreement. First, list everything a standard business partnership agreement should contain. Then include clauses for: what happens if one partner wants to buy the other out; what happens if one partner dies; what happens if one partner does not meet their obligations. Add a responsibilities section that splits tasks between us, for example: [PARTNER A] handles website design and maintenance, product uploads and supplier relationships; [PARTNER B] handles customer service and marketing. Also add a section on how profits, costs and finances are split and paid out. Ask me for any details you need before drafting, and mark which clauses should be reviewed by a lawyer before signing.
```

[L1097.008]

### Influencers and ambassadors

- A simple one-page, three-paragraph agreement with each influencer formalizes the arrangement and confirms the posting obligation. It acts as a digital handshake and does not create liability, but it helps set expectations. [L0790.072]
- A simple written influencer agreement with the creator's name and key terms creates a record, even if it is not a strict legal contract. Enforceability across countries varies, so get local legal advice before relying on agreements abroad. [L1213.054]
- Keep ambassadors unpaid and in the ambassador role. According to general counsel's advice, unpaid ambassadors who sell or refer products but are not paid can be treated as non-employees, which helps avoid employment liability. [L0790.073]

### Community rules

- Members must not post or offer paid services to other members of the program, and must not use the course community or direct messages to sell anything to other members. Anyone who wants to sell a service must build a separate business rather than use the program's audience. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1498.014]
- Members may not copy or share program content, such as lesson videos, attachments, and spreadsheets, with anyone who is not a member, unless the program owners give express permission. The content is copyrighted and owned by the company behind the program, and sharing it outside the membership can lead to legal action. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1498.015]
- Permitted sharing with a non-member, such as a virtual assistant helping build the business:
  1. Message the program owners before sharing.
  2. Name the specific videos or materials you want to share and explain why.
  3. Share only after receiving written approval.

  Sharing without approval is not allowed. (Older advice, 2021-22.) [L1498.016]

### Scam avoidance

#### Unsolicited offers and messages

- Shopify and Google never send unsolicited emails to a store asking for money to optimize the store. Emails from agencies offering store optimization are usually cold outreach and should be ignored. [L0472.048]
- Legitimate mentors, coaches, and staff of an online community never send unsolicited direct messages first. Any direct message that arrives right after joining, claiming to be from the community's team, is a scam until proven otherwise. [L1442.001]
- Anyone asking for money in a direct message, including for store setup help, course access, or a service fee, is a scammer. Real staff do not request payment through private messages. [L1442.002]
- Requests for payment in cryptocurrency made by someone posing as a community coach or support person are a clear fraud signal. Refuse and do not send any funds. [L1442.003]
- New members often trust the first private message they receive after joining a server, which is exactly what impersonators count on. Treat every unsolicited message from a community member as untrusted until it is verified in an official channel. [L1442.005]

#### Spotting impersonators

- Impersonation scams copy a real person's display name, and sometimes profile picture, then add a small change such as an extra word, a letter, or trailing punctuation. Check the exact spelling of every name against the official member list before trusting a message. Example pattern: a real handle `Alex_Store` becomes `Alex_Store vs` or `Alex_Store.` in a fake account. [L1442.004]
- Official announcements, pinned messages, and public channels are the reliable source for who the real staff and coaches are. Compare any suspicious account against these before replying. If a fake account is confirmed, block it and do not engage further. [L1442.006]

## Selling the Business: Legal, Tax and Transaction Matters

### Preparing the Store for Sale

- Keep accurate, complete books from the start, because brokerages and marketplaces typically require several years of complete financial records (the example given was four years). Older guidance from 2021 to 2022 gives the same requirement. [L0449.009]
- Store all supplier invoices in Google Drive so they are easy to show during buyer due diligence and verification. [L0231.022]
- Turn off WHOIS privacy in the last month before listing, so your own name shows as domain owner. Buyers look up WHOIS to confirm ownership. [L1468.042]
- Make sure you are verifiable as a seller: real social profiles with real friends, and a LinkedIn profile with genuine connections. Sellers without an online presence may be asked for ID, a passport or a social security card, which makes the sale harder. [L1468.043]

### Asset Sale or Stock Sale

- Decide before listing whether you will do an asset sale (specific assets transfer) or a stock sale (transfer of ownership of the entire company). [L0231.023]
- Asset sales are simpler. Stock sales require deeper buyer due diligence. [L0231.023]
- On this marketplace, more than nine out of ten sales are asset sales, where websites, files and domains are transferred but the LLC stays with the seller. [L0554.043]
- A stock sale of an LLC requires much deeper due diligence, because the buyer must consider lawsuits arising from earlier actions. [L0554.043]
- The usual way to sell a store is an asset sale in which the LLC sells the Shopify store, ad account access, supplier contacts and email lists. The LLC stays with the owner and can be dissolved or reused later. This is older advice from 2022. [L0803.064]

### LLC Structure and Transfer

- You typically do not have to sell your LLC structure. Buyers conduct more extensive due diligence when purchasing an LLC than when purchasing specific assets. [L0231.024]
- Large portfolio buyers usually set up a separate LLC for each store, which makes record keeping straightforward. This setup is common among portfolio buyers. [L0554.044]

### Trademark Risk in Listings

- A business with a partially trademarked name can be listed for sale. Some buyers may avoid it because of perceived legal risk. [L0231.028]
- A partially trademarked name can be listed if the seller has not received a warning or cease and desist. [L0554.045]
- Well-known brands, such as Marvel, should be avoided in listings. [L0554.045]
- Some buyers may still be uncomfortable with the risk, even when the listing is allowed. [L0554.045]

### Brokerage or Private Sale

| Factor | Brokerage | Private sale |
|---|---|---|
| Sale price | Typically higher, through professional buyer matching. [L0231.016] | You keep the full sales price. [L0231.015] |
| Negotiation | Broker guides you through negotiations and earn-out scenarios. [L0231.016] | You handle negotiation yourself. [L0231.015] |
| Buyer qualification | Broker qualifies buyers to protect your time. [L0231.016] | Harder to qualify serious buyers. [L0231.015] |
| Competitors | Broker screens competitors using deposit systems. [L0231.016] | Financial records are exposed to potential competitors. [L0231.015] |
| Migration | Broker handles business migration. [L0231.016] | You are responsible for business migration. [L0231.015] |
| Buyer list and protections | Broker's buyer list and protections apply. [L0554.046] | No broker buyer list or protections. [L0554.046] |
| Fee | 15% of the sales price. [L0231.017, L0554.046] | No broker fee. [L0554.046] |

### Broker Fees

- Brokerage commission on dropshipping store sales under $1 million is 15% of the final sale price. [L0231.017]
- The broker charges a fee of 15% of the sales price, which the source describes as standard in the industry. [L0554.046]
- Private sales avoid this fee, but they lack the broker's buyer list and protections. [L0554.046]

### Escrow and Payment Protection

- Escrow protects both sides by holding the buyer's money until the site has been fully transferred and checked. The seller is paid only after the new owner confirms that everything works. [L1468.040]
- Third-party escrow services such as escrow.com move money safely but do not handle the migration. Moving the store, its accounts and its domain still needs someone to manage the details. [L1468.041]
- An escrow service can protect a private sale when everything goes smoothly. Escrow services are not specialised in online businesses, so arbitration can be difficult if a problem arises. [L0554.047]
- A broker that holds the buyer's funds until the transfer is complete may not be allowed to call itself an escrow company under US banking rules. Before paying, confirm how the money is held and whether the arrangement is legally valid in your jurisdiction. [L1468.053]

### Due Diligence

- Buyers must complete due diligence themselves. The sale platform vets listings before publication, but the responsibility to check suppliers, relationships and whether the business suits them stays with the buyer. [L0553.032]
- Prepare records that answer a buyer's checks on suppliers, relationships and the store's history, since the buyer will run these checks regardless of the platform's vetting. [L0553.032, L0231.022]

### Addbacks in Valuation

- Addbacks allow you to reintroduce expenses from your tax return into net profit for valuation when they differ from what a buyer would actually incur. [L0231.013]
- Prepare to justify and negotiate these adjustments. [L0231.013]

### Seller Financing and Earn-Outs

- Seller financing, such as an earn-out or a share of profits paid over several months, becomes more common as deal sizes grow. It bridges the gap between the buyer's ability to pay upfront and the seller's asking price. [L0554.058]
- Seller financing can take the form of an earn-out or a share of profits over several months, for example $5,000 per month for six months. [L0554.048]
- In this structure, the broker holds the domain until the buyer makes the final payment. The broker can transfer it back or use it as leverage if the buyer breaks the contract. [L0554.048]

### Non-Competes

- When buying or selling a business, the buyer should require a non-compete agreement. Without one, the seller can open the same niche again soon after the sale. This is older advice from 2020. [L0852.008]
- Observed non-compete terms ranged from 12 months to 5 years. This is older advice from 2020. [L0852.008]

### Cross-Border Transfers

- Transferring accounts to a buyer in another country can take months. [L0553.034]
- One American buyer who had to set up an EU company for an EU migration was three months into a migration that was not going smoothly. Check cross-border handovers early. [L0553.034]

### Deal Process on a Broker Platform

1. The seller submits the store for vetting, and a team reviews it. [L0553.033]
2. Once approved, the store is listed. [L0553.033]
3. The sales team screens buyer interest. [L0553.033]
4. A prep call with the seller takes place. [L0553.033]
5. A buyer-seller call led by the broker takes place. [L0553.033]
6. A post-call follow-up with the seller takes place. [L0553.033]
7. The broker helps negotiate. [L0553.033]
8. After the asset purchase agreement is signed, the broker's team migrates the business and hands it over. [L0553.033]

## Other details

### Tracking and analytics

- Add campaign URL options and tracking templates only if you rely on third-party affiliate tracking, UTM builders or custom tracking software. If you use Google Analytics integrated with Shopify's native tracking, you already have sufficient performance data and do not need tracking templates. Most ecommerce stores get complete tracking through these platforms alone. [L0225.036]
- Google sets the default deadline for GA4 setup. Universal Analytics stopped working in July 2023. [L0964.120]

### Company, tax and compliance

- Treat regulatory and platform-policy compliance as a standing check, not an afterthought. Compliance was the single lesson topic raised in a January 2026 coaching call. [L0817.001]
- Business tax and personal tax guidance are in course lesson 0.5. The resale and state registration steps are in module zero. [L0964.116]
- Payment, banking and company rules depend heavily on the country and on local tax law. Check any country-specific decision on company formation or tax with a local accountant or lawyer. [L1512.001]
- General information only: this is not legal, tax, investment or financial advice. Each owner must evaluate the risks and consult a professional for their own situation. [L0903.025]
- Before selling into the EU, check the shipping policies of EU stores that sell the same products. Confirm whether they ship to every EU country and whether shipping and taxes differ across countries before you commit. Older advice (2021). [L0801.145]
- A supplier's name on the packaging is normal in drop shipping. You do not need to hide it. Older advice (2021). [L0834.082]
- You may buy a supplier's product for yourself or a family member directly from the supplier at wholesale price. This does not need to go through your Shopify store, and it is not unethical. [L0966.092]

### Payments and banking

- **Shopify Payments address verification:** Provide a utility bill or any mail that proves you own the address. If you are outside the US but have a US address, provide a contract with the address company or mail from a supplier. Older advice (2022). [L0249.077]
- **Payment details at onboarding:** These can include a bank account for transfers through TransferWise or a PayPal account email. Keep them in the standardized questionnaire responses so they are easy to find later. Older advice (2021-22). [L1054.012]
- **Online-only banks such as Mercury:** Shopify Payments may not accept them, and Shopify support may be unsure. The practical test is to connect the account and see whether it is accepted. Then check community groups for other sellers' experiences with that bank. Older advice (2021). [L1279.093]
- **Shopify subscription payment methods:** Credit card, debit card and PayPal are accepted. You can update the payment method later, for example when a business credit card is available. [L1304.016]
- **Email autoresponder billing:** Autoresponder subscriptions are billed through the Shopify account. The charge appears on the Shopify bill after the trial ends. Older advice (2021-22). [L1298.034]
- **Gemini API billing:** Usage is billed through a Google Cloud billing account that needs a credit or debit card. Charges go to the account linked to the key's project. If a tool runs on someone else's key, their account pays, so every user of a shared tool should enter their own key. [L0794.003]
- **Shared logins:** Sharing one login across several people usually breaches the terms of service of paid software such as ChatGPT Plus, Canva Pro or Helium 10. The provider can suspend or close the account, so read each tool's terms before you depend on shared access. [L1387.010]
- **Buying software bundles through Whop:** The bundle is sold through a third-party checkout platform, Whop, using an affiliate-tracked sign-up link. The seller is therefore not the software vendors themselves. Before paying, check:
  - who the seller is,
  - the refund terms,
  - whether the subscription renews automatically, and at what price. [L1387.017]

### Shipping costs and carriers

- **Shipping your own account:** Open a UPS or FedEx account online, which is free. Give the supplier your account number so they can ship and bill it to you. You then pay the shipping bill. [L0933.019]
- **Cutting shipping costs for large or heavy products.** Older advice (2021). [L0853.056]
  1. Ask the supplier whether they can give a shipping discount through their own carrier accounts.
  2. Get quotes from several freight brokers for recent shipments.
  3. Compare the quotes, because they can differ by hundreds of dollars.
  4. Use the cheapest reliable option.
- Pricing a large item with a fixed carrier rate can waste a lot of money over time. Get shipping quotes regularly and avoid relying on one carrier, since UPS quotes for large, heavy items can be very high. Older advice (2021). [L0853.057]
- Products that are unusually costly to ship or need special handling should be checked with the carrier. This course handles them as standard shipping with insurance, not as a separate cost line. Older advice (2022). [L1281.076]
- **Setting up shipping details in Smart SEO:**
  1. Turn on the shipping details option.
  2. Enter the country code for the country you ship to.
  3. Enter the minimum handling days, for example 0 if some products ship the same day.
  4. Enter the maximum handling days, for example 3 if orders cannot ship on weekends.
  5. Enter the minimum transit days, for example 1 to 5 days.
  6. Choose the shipping price type: minimum, maximum or fixed amount.
  7. For a fixed amount, enter the currency (USD in the example) and the rate. For free shipping, enter a rate of 0.
  8. Leave the setting turned on and save. [L1337.018]

### Shopify store setup: domains, SSL and licences

- **Connecting a domain in Shopify.** Older advice (2021-22). [L1292.012, L1292.013, L1292.014]
  1. In the Shopify admin, go to Online Store > Domains.
  2. Click Connect existing domain.
  3. Enter the domain name you purchased (no https://), then click Next.
  4. If the registrar is a major one such as GoDaddy, choose Connect automatically. Shopify asks you to authorize the connection in the registrar's window. Then click Connect and close it.
  5. Shopify verifies the connection and alters the domain's DNS settings. Verification can take a minute or two.
  6. For a smaller registrar that Shopify does not connect to automatically, choose Connect manually and follow the instructions Shopify shows to change the DNS settings.
  7. Once the domain shows as connected, wait for propagation, which can take up to 24 hours.
- Shopify detects and connects domains from major registrars such as GoDaddy automatically. Smaller registrars require a manual DNS connection. Older advice (2021-22). [L1292.014]
- **SSL:** Shopify provides a free SSL certificate for connected custom domains, so you do not need to buy one separately. The status shows SSL pending for a short period, around 30 minutes. After that, HTTPS is active. Older advice (2021-22). [L1292.015]
- HTTPS is essential for e-commerce. While the SSL status is pending, the site shows as not secure in the browser until the certificate is issued. Older advice (2021-22). [L1292.016]
- **Shrine licences:** One Shrine licence per store is enough. Buying more licences for a single store is unnecessary. [L1330.006]
- Shopify does not lock your store code. You can export all products as a CSV file and download image assets to move them elsewhere. A full rebuild on another platform is not a one-click process. Older advice (2021). [L0968.030]

### Software costs and subscriptions

- Shopify is the only paid software that is a must-have at the start. Canva's free version is enough for design. Stripe Atlas can help a founder operating from abroad set up a US company. Advice from 2023. [L0934.143]
- Klaviyo offers a free tier, but billing starts once the contact list grows past a set size. Check the contact count against the pricing before the list grows. Advice from 2023. [L0934.144]
- Canva's subscription cost about 11 GBP (roughly 14 USD) at the time of recording. It included a library of stock images, audio and stock videos that would otherwise cost extra. Check the current price and the licence terms before relying on this figure. [L0754.024]

### Marketplace fees and paid promotion

- **Amazon Associates commissions:** Rates range from about 4.5 percent to 8.5 percent, depending on the number of transactions sent per month. Lower-volume affiliates earn the lower rate, so commissions stay small until the volume grows. [L0923.022]
- **eBay fees:** Commission depends on the product category. The category used in the lesson had about 7 percent commission. Payment processor fees such as PayPal's come on top of this. Calculate the full fee per category before you set prices. [L1441.039]
- **Paid boosts (coins):** Coins can start at $5, but $10 per day for the first three days is the usual starting level. Avoid overspending on paid boosts, because heavy spending defeats the organic purpose. [L1429.045]

### Health insurance abroad

- Compare the total annual cost of a plan, meaning the premium plus the copay or deductible, rather than the monthly premium. One example US plan at about 500 USD per month carried a 7,000 USD annual copay. [L1515.019]
- Many long-term travellers combine travel insurance for large catastrophic events, such as helicopter evacuation from a hike, with paying cash for routine care. Private care abroad can be affordable. For example, an emergency visit with ultrasound in Thailand cost about 120 USD in cash. [L1515.020]
- SafetyWing offers health and travel cover built for digital nomads and expats. It is an alternative for people who need coverage without a home-country plan. [L1515.021]
